# M11 review record — v1.3.0: deterministic coverage · NER thread base

The full record of every M11 review finding: repro, mechanism, `file:line`, the fix, the test, and — once
closed — the closure note appended to the same entry. The [ROADMAP ledger](../ROADMAP.md#m11) carries only
the compact row (id · title · severity · status) and links here.

**This file opens late, and that is the fact to know before reading it.** M11 reached code-complete over
2026-09-02 across three tracks — `d2fc694`/`77ce7a3` (Track B, the physical-core thread base),
`c830784`/`4c41d61` (Track A, VAT numbers as `PiiKind::TaxId`), `783b4d1` (Track C, closed without work) —
and had **zero review rounds**. The builder that wrote the code ran before the two-role loop rules landed
in `CLAUDE.md` at `01fbadd` the following morning, so until 2026-09-03 nothing had tried to break this
milestone. Implementation record: [DEVLOG 2026-09-02](../DEVLOG.md) and
[2026-09-03](../DEVLOG.md).

<a id="round-0"></a>
## Round 0 — the builder's own pass (2026-09-03)

Not a review. Recorded here because it produced one defect that a round would otherwise have found first,
and because the mutation results below are what the rounds start from rather than repeat.

### The maintainer's open item, closed

**The over-mask bar for the VAT tier** (ROADMAP → M11 Track A, *"A third decision"*, taken 2026-09-03):
zero bare-form `TaxId` spans over the real ~22 KiB Claude Code turn in `tests/common/m7_turn.rs`.
Built as `tests/vat_overmask.rs` — `VAT-15` and `VAT-16`, modelled on `tests/phone_overmask.rs` and
running over the **shared** fixture, not a copy.

| Quantity | Expected (written before the run) | Observed | Re-runs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `TaxId` spans over the turn | 0 | **0** | 3 |
| Masked spans of any kind that are exactly 11 ASCII digits | 0 | **0** | 3 |
| 11-digit tokens present in the turn (the denominator) | 0–3 | **0** | 3 |
| Fixture size | > 20 000 bytes | **22 823** | 3 |
| All-digit tokens in the turn / longest run | — | **11 / 5 digits** | 3 |

**The bar is met.** `0.100` (`VAT-09`) stands as a published *synthetic worst case*.

**The residue, stated because a passing test cannot state it.** The denominator is **zero** — the turn
contains no 11-digit token at all — so mod-10 was never invoked on this traffic. The zero therefore says
*this traffic offers the bare P.IVA nothing to bite*, which is weaker than PHONE-OM's zero, whose
candidate set is every plausible digit group. Traffic carrying 11-digit database ids or order numbers is
uncovered, and there `0.100` is the number that applies. Promoted to
[`TESTING.md`](../TESTING.md) → `VAT-15`, and the guard prints the denominator on every run so this cannot
quietly become a precision claim.

**Two assertions, not one.** `TaxId` ranks below `NationalId` and `Phone` (`VAT-14`), so an 11-digit run
the bare recognizer claims can be *named* by another tier and vanish from a `kind == TaxId` filter while
still being masked. The second assertion filters on the **shape** instead, and mutation M2 below is what
shows it earns its place.

<a id="m11-r0"></a>
### M11-R0 — `cargo fmt --check` was red on `main`, and CI gates on it — *high*

**Found while verifying, not by reading the diff.** `cargo fmt --check` fails on four files the M11
feature commits touched: `src/pii/onnx.rs` (6 hunks), `src/pii/recognizers.rs` (1), `src/pipeline/privacy.rs`
(1), `tests/m7_latency.rs` (1).

**Confirmed pre-existing rather than assumed**: `git show HEAD:<file>` into a scratch copy, then
`rustfmt --edition 2021 --check` on each — all four red on the `HEAD` blob, before any change of mine.

**Why it matters.** `.github/workflows/ci.yml:35` runs `cargo fmt --check` as a gate job. M11 as committed
would have failed CI on push. It survived because the milestone had had zero review rounds — this is
literally the first thing a round finds.

**Fix.** `cargo fmt` (commit `style:`). **Test.** The gate itself: `cargo fmt --check` exits 0, and
`cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` plus `cargo clippy-onnx -- -D warnings` are both clean.

**Closed 2026-09-03.**

### Mutations run in round 0

Each anchor was checked for a unique match first, the edit written as **bytes**, and the file restored
from the pre-edit bytes with a **sha256** comparison; `git status` verified clean afterwards.

| # | Mutation | Expected | Got | Assertion that fired |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| M1 | real P.IVA `00905811006` spliced into the turn's user message | RED | **RED** | `vat_overmask.rs:124` — the `TaxId` span set |
| M2 | `00811720580` spliced in — valid P.IVA **and** valid LV code, so it resolves to `NationalId` | RED | **RED** | `vat_overmask.rs:132` — the 11-digit **shape** assertion, which assertion 1 cannot see |
| M3 | fixture shrunk below the 20 KiB floor (`TOOL_DESCRIPTIONS.iter().take(0)`) | RED | **RED** | `vat_overmask.rs:102` — non-vacuity |
| M4 | bare P.IVA recognizer neutered (`validate: Some(free(\|_\| false))`) | RED | **RED** | `vat_overmask.rs:189` — the per-scheme control |
| M5 | NL `Recognizer` block deleted outright | RED | **RED** | `vat_overmask.rs:166` — the count read from `shipped_tax_recognizer_count()` |
| M6 | none — the legitimate path, unmutated | GREEN | **GREEN** | both guards pass |
| M7 | `VAT-15`'s **catalogue entry heading** renamed, its cross-reference in `VAT-16` left | RED | **GREEN** | *see below* |
| M7b | **every** `VAT-15` mention removed from `TESTING.md` | RED | **RED** | `test_catalogue.rs:147` |
| M8b | **every** `VAT-16` mention removed from `TESTING.md` | RED | **RED** | `test_catalogue.rs:147` |

**Five mutations, five distinct assertions** — the guard has no redundant or dead assertion.

**M7 is the one worth reading.** It came back GREEN against prediction. CAT-01 asserts each declared id is
*named somewhere* in `TESTING.md`, and `VAT-15` is also named inside `VAT-16`'s prose — so a
cross-reference alone satisfies it, and an id can be "catalogued" by a passing mention with its own entry
gone. That is CAT-01's documented scope (`tests/test_catalogue.rs:15-17` states it explicitly), not a
regression M11 introduced, and it is **not** fixed here: narrowing CAT-01 changes a guard shared by every
family. Recorded so a round can decide whether it is worth a finding of its own.

<a id="round-1"></a>
## Round 1 — the first independent round (2026-09-03)

The first review round M11 has ever had. Aimed at seven questions rather than at the diff, because
[M4's retrospective](M4.md#retrospective) is six rounds of evidence that re-reading a diff finds
nothing here. **No leak, no fail-open and no over-mask regression was found.** Five findings, all
about *guards that cannot go red* and *numbers that measure something other than what they are
quoted for* — which is the failure mode this milestone's own commits are made of.

### What was verified, with the expected value written before the observed one

| Quantity | Expected (written first) | Observed | Reps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cargo test` | 239 / 0 / 5, no warnings (R0 baseline) | **239 / 0 / 5, 0 warnings** | 2 |
| `cargo test-onnx` | 275 / 0 / 22, no warnings (R0 baseline) | **275 / 0 / 22, 0 warnings** | 1 |
| `cargo fmt --check` | exit 0 (R0 closed it) | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` | clean | **clean** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy-onnx -- -D warnings` | clean | **clean** | 1 |
| `VAT-09` bare-P.IVA rate | **exactly 0.100** — a contiguous sweep makes 1-in-10 structural | **10000/100000 = 0.100** | 3 |
| `VAT-10` P.IVA∩national-ID share | piva = **20000**, share ≈ **0.0998** | **1997/20000 = 0.0998** | 3 |
| ids `CAT-01`'s extractor sees | ≥ 20 (the floor); actual unknown | **54** | 1 |

Both published rates are **deterministic sweeps**, so the three reps confirm determinism rather than
sample a distribution — worth saying, because "re-ran three times" reads stronger than it is here.

**The real binary, end to end** (`target/debug/llm-proxy-pii-rust.exe`, default features, mock
upstream on 127.0.0.1:18711, proxy on :18712 — ports chosen away from the machine's own instance;
only the two PIDs this round started were killed). One request carrying all four rendering families:

- upstream received `Fattura a [TAXID_1], P.IVA [TAXID_2], btw [TAXID_3] e [TAXID_4] — grazie.`
  — the bare P.IVA, the VIES IT form, the format-only NL form and the DE form, all masked;
- the injected system message was present and names `[TAXID_1]`;
- the client got all four values back **byte-identically**;
- at `RUST_LOG=llm_proxy_pii_rust=trace`, **none** of `IT00905811006`, `00159560366`,
  `NL123456789B01`, `DE136695976` appears anywhere in the crate's logs; the only M11-specific log
  line is `structure-only PII match (checksum unverified) kind=TaxId` — kind, no value. The
  never-log-raw-PII bar holds for the new kind on real traffic.

### The independent corpus, and the number VAT-15's residue said nobody had

`TESTING.md` → VAT-15 states honestly that its zero has a **zero denominator**, and that traffic
carrying 11-digit ids is uncovered by any fixture in this repo. So this round measured it on text
nobody in this project wrote or curated: **303 772 514 bytes across 12 859 `.rs` / `.md` / `.toml` /
`.json` / `.txt` files** of the crates.io registry source cache — code-shaped text, the closest
available stand-in for a tool result.

| Shape | Hits | Note |
|---|---|---|
| `\b\d{11}\b` | **104** | the bare P.IVA's real candidate surface — **0.34 per MB** |
| …of which mod-10-valid | **9** (`0.0865`) | `00024414062`, `17179873280`, `34366720000`, `41789270000` |
| `\bNL\d{9}B\d{2}\b` | **0** | the format-only NL scheme |
| `\bNL\d{9}[A-Z]\d{2}\b` (relaxed: any letter) | **0** | |
| `\b[A-Z]{2}\d{9}\b` (the whole prefixed family's shape) | **0** | IT/DE/GB/PT specifically: 0 each |
| `\b[A-Z]{2}\d{2,4}[A-Z]{1,2}\b` (IT-plate family) | **24** | the shape M11 **refused** |
| `\b[A-Z]{1,3}-[A-Z]{1,2}\d{1,4}\b` (DE-plate family) | **180** | the shape M11 **refused** |
| `\b[A-Z0-9]{6,16}\b` | **1 075 001** | the denominator for the four rows above |

Two conclusions, both measured rather than argued:

1. **The synthetic `0.100` survives contact with real text: 0.0865 on 104 uncurated 11-digit
   tokens.** And the four values it masks are memory and byte-size constants — *exactly* the
   "database id or order number inside `tool_use.input`" harm the ROADMAP names. Density on
   code-shaped traffic: **~1 bare-P.IVA over-mask per 34 MB**. Promoted to
   [`TESTING.md`](../TESTING.md) → VAT-15, where the residue paragraph asked for it.
2. **NL is not "what the tier had just refused"** (the question this round was asked to settle with
   a rate, not an opinion). The plate families M11 dropped fire **204 times** in the same corpus
   where every VAT shape fires **0**, against a shared denominator of 1.07 M uppercase-alnum
   tokens. NL's `NL` + 9 digits + a literal `B` + 2 digits is 14 fixed positions with three literal
   anchors; the relaxed variant that drops the `B` anchor still finds nothing. And unlike a plate —
   which has no checksum in any country — NL's body *is* checked (11-proef) and the result is
   *reported* as `Verified` vs `Structural`. **The tier's line held.**

### The class-or-instance verdict on the priority fix

Asked whether `VAT-14` pins the class or two instances. The **ordering** is genuinely class-closing:
the only recognizers that can claim a bare `\b\d{11}\b` token are the always-on `\d{11}` national ID
and the domestic-phone `Trunk` family's third arm `\b0\d{6,11}\b` (the other three phone shape
families all require separators, so they cannot match a bare digit run), and `TaxId` = 2 sits
strictly below both `NationalId` = 4 and `Phone` = 3, so `outranks` can never let `TaxId` name that
union. Two instances are enough to pin an ordering between two kinds.

**What is not closed is the other direction, and it is the larger of the two — see
[M11-R2](#m11-r2).**

### Mutations run in round 1

Every anchor was checked for a **unique** match before the edit, every edit written as **bytes**,
every file restored from a pre-edit copy and compared by **sha256** against the `HEAD` blob, and
`git status --porcelain` verified empty at the end of the round.

| # | Mutation | Expected | Got | What fired |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A1 | live ES recognizer (`(?-u:\b)ES[A-Z0-9]\d{7}[A-Z0-9](?-u:\b)`, no checksum) added to `vat_recognizers()` | RED | **GREEN** | nothing — [M11-R1](#m11-r1) |
| A2 | same, **plus** a 7th control in `ONE_PER_SHIPPED_SCHEME` | RED | **GREEN (239/239)** | nothing — the whole suite accepts an unmeasured ES scheme |
| A3 | live FR recognizer | RED | **RED** | `recognizers.rs:3112` — the FR arm is *not* vacuous |
| A4 | live LV recognizer | RED | **RED** | same assert — the LV arm is *not* vacuous |
| B1 | `it_piva_valid` final comparison → `d[10] == 7` | VAT-09 RED | **GREEN, still `0.100`** | VAT-09 blind; 7 other VAT guards red — [M11-R5](#m11-r5) |
| C1 | 12th `PiiKind::Vin` (+ the two arms the compiler demands) | AUG-01 RED | **GREEN (239/239)** | nothing — [M11-R3](#m11-r3) |
| C2 | `[TAXID_1]` deleted from `AUGMENTATION_PROMPT` | RED | **RED** | `privacy.rs:965` — AUG-01 watches the five it lists |
| C3 | augmentation injected **before** masking in `on_request` | RED | **RED** | `privacy.rs:1039` — AUG-02 watches the ordering it claims |
| D1 | `"VAT"` + `"AUG"` removed from `ID_PREFIXES`, **and** all 24 `VAT-*`/`AUG-*` mentions scrubbed from `TESTING.md` | RED | **GREEN** | nothing — [M11-R4](#m11-r4) |
| D2 | CAT-01 floor raised to 100 000 (to read the count) | RED | **RED, "found only 54"** | the floor is 20 against 54 |
| E1 | `vec!["it"]` added to VAT-06's locale list (the legitimate path) | GREEN | **GREEN** | the four corpus P.IVAs survive under `PII_LOCALES=it` |
| — | probe: bare P.IVA vs `Phone` across locale sets (measurement, not a guard) | — | see [M11-R2](#m11-r2) | |

**Two of these were greens in disguise on the first attempt, and catching that is the point.** A1
and A3/A4 were first written through a shell heredoc that ate the backslashes: `\b` reached the file
as byte `0x08`, so the stub regexes were `(?-u:<BS>)FR…` and matched nothing. They came back GREEN
against prediction, and the "the mutation must actually mutate" check — reading the bytes back with
`cat -v` — is what exposed it. Every mutation above was re-run through a Python file whose
backslashes are built with `chr(92)`, and each one's presence was confirmed in the file before the
test ran. The **first** probe of the P.IVA/phone collision was degenerate the same way: its sweep
`i * 7 + 1_000_000` formatted to 11 digits only ever produced `0000…`-leading values, reported
`0/4006` for every locale, and would have been published as "nothing to see" — the corrected sweep
is what produced M11-R2.

### Verified not broken

- The legitimate path for the feature's primary locale: `PII_LOCALES=it`, `PII_LOCALES` unset, and
  the four locale sets VAT-06 already covers — the corpus's real P.IVAs all still emit `[TAXID_n]`.
- The real binary, end to end, on all four rendering families (above).
- Exact round trip, placeholder inertness and the fixpoint (VAT-13, re-driven through the binary).
- Fail-closed plumbing around the new kind: every VAT validator goes through `free()` so none
  charges the phone budget (`recognizers.rs:67`, a single visible decision — correct, these are
  ≤ 14-byte checksums); every VAT pattern is **bounded** (≤ 14 chars), so `Scan::Overlapping`'s
  rescan stays linear under M4-R19's rule; the NL `Structural` tag reaches only the kind-only audit
  line. DOS-05/06 already push digit-dense bodies through the tier and are green.
- No new logging site carries a value.

<a id="m11-r1"></a>
### M11-R1 — `VAT-04` asserts the absence of an ES VAT number in a form no ES recognizer could match — *guard, medium*

**The ES arm cannot fail.** `src/pii/recognizers.rs:3086`:

```rust
for text in ["ES B12345678", "FR40404833048", "LV40003032949"] {
```

The tier documents, and enforces, that there is **no space between the country prefix and the
body** (`vat_recognizers`'s own doc, *"No space between prefix and digits"*), and the VIES canonical
rendering is `ESB12345678`. So the ES negative is written in the one shape the tier's grammar can
never match — it is absent for a reason that has nothing to do with ES not shipping.

**Repro.** Added a canonical, checksum-less ES recognizer — precisely the "half-shipped" outcome
VAT-04 exists to forbid — to `vat_recognizers()`:

```rust
Recognizer {
    kind: PiiKind::TaxId,
    regex: Regex::new(r"(?-u:\b)ES[A-Z0-9]\d{7}[A-Z0-9](?-u:\b)").unwrap(),
    validate: None,
    scan: Scan::Overlapping,
    shrink_on_reject: false,
},
```

`cargo test --lib unmeasured_vat_countries_are_absent_rather_than_guessed` → **ok**. The stub was
confirmed live at the same moment: `VAT-16` reported *"the tier now ships 7 of them"*. Adding one
matching control to `ONE_PER_SHIPPED_SCHEME` — the natural way anyone would make that red go green —
left the **entire default suite at 239 passed / 0 failed**, with an unmeasured, checksum-less
Spanish VAT recognizer shipping.

**Scoped by the same mutation:** FR and LV are *not* vacuous. Stubs for both turn VAT-04 red at
`recognizers.rs:3112`. ES is the only broken arm.

**Why it matters more than a test nit.** VAT-04 is what ARCHITECTURE cites for *"the gap stays a
decision rather than a discovery"*, and ES is the one of the three whose failure mode the code
comment describes in detail (*"Shipping the person half alone would be a VAT recognizer that misses
the case it exists for"*). The guard that is supposed to catch exactly that is the one that cannot.

**Fix.** Write the ES negatives the way the tier would match them — at minimum `ESB12345678` (the
legal-entity CIF form the comment names), and ideally the two person forms `ES12345678Z` and
`ESX1234567X` alongside it. FR and LV stay as they are.

**Test that would have caught it.** The mutation itself, but the durable guard is a **reachability
assertion on the negatives**: each VAT-04 input must be a token the tier's grammar could in
principle match (no whitespace inside; a 2-letter uppercase prefix followed by 7–12 alphanumerics),
so a negative written in an unmatchable shape is red on the spot rather than quietly true. That is
the same "prove the corpus can express the thing" move PROP-04 made for M4-R17.

**Closed 2026-09-03 (builder).** Negatives rewritten to the three canonical no-space forms the tier
could actually match — `ESB12345678` (the legal-entity CIF form the recognizer's own doc names),
`ES12345678Z` (DNI + control letter) and `ESX1234567L` (NIE). FR and LV left alone, since the round
proved both arms non-vacuous.

**And the durable half, which is the point:** each input is now checked for **reachability first**,
inside the loop, against the tier's grammar — two uppercase ASCII letters then an unbroken
alphanumeric run of at least 7. A negative the grammar could never match is red on the spot, so the
next person who writes `"ES B12345678"` is told rather than silently reassured.

**Mutations, both RED (expected RED):**
- the round's own repro — a live checksum-less ES recognizer added to `vat_recognizers()` →
  **RED** at `recognizers.rs:3149`, the absence assertion. It was GREEN before this fix.
- one negative reverted to the old unmatchable `"ES B12345678"` → **RED** at `recognizers.rs:3131`,
  the reachability assertion. That is the guard on the guard, and it watches.

<a id="m11-r2"></a>
### M11-R2 — the priority fix closed the small collision and created the large one; `VAT-10` measures the wrong one, and the "costs no coverage" claim is false under the shipped default — *fidelity / measurement, medium*

**Not a leak.** Every byte is masked either way — this is about which token the model is handed, and
about a published number that does not measure what it is quoted for.

**The claim.** `src/pii/recognizers.rs:440-445`, on the bare P.IVA recognizer:

> `\d{11}` … **THE one shape that collides with an existing recognizer** … also the DE Steuer-ID / LV
> personal code pattern … **The collision costs no coverage;** what this recognizer *adds* is every
> P.IVA that is not also a valid Steuer-ID or LV code.

The comment names one collision. The milestone found a second one — the compact domestic phone —
and *fixed the direction of it* (`TaxId` below `Phone`, VAT-14), but the comment, `VAT-10` and
[`ARCHITECTURE.md:78-83`](../ARCHITECTURE.md) all still describe the collision as the national-ID
one.

**Measured.** A probe over 300 000 sweep points, restricted to **issuable** P.IVAs (leading `0` —
the only bare shape the phone `Trunk` arm `\b0\d{6,11}\b` can claim — and a province code in
001…110, so unissuable synthetics are excluded), asking which kind names the span:

| Detector | named `Phone` / valid 0-leading P.IVAs | `00xx` | `0[1-9]xx` |
|---|---|---|---|
| **`StructuredRecognizers::new()` — the shipped default (all 9 vetted regions on)** | **2372 / 3241 = 73.2 %** | 12/541 = 2.2 % | **2360/2700 = 87.4 %** |
| `PII_LOCALES=de` | 2104 / 3241 = 64.9 % | 11/541 | 2093/2700 = 77.5 % |
| `PII_LOCALES=it` — the feature's primary locale | 1856 / 3241 = 57.3 % | 12/541 | 1844/2700 = 68.3 % |
| `PII_LOCALES=cn` | 1057 / 3241 = 32.6 % | 11/541 | 1046/2700 = 38.7 % |
| `PII_LOCALES=gb` | 909 / 3241 = 28.0 % | 11/541 | 898/2700 = 33.3 % |
| `PII_LOCALES=nl` | 27 / 3241 = 0.8 % | | |
| `PII_LOCALES=fr` | 11 / 3241 = 0.3 % | | |

So under the configuration the product ships with, **roughly three out of four issuable 0-leading
Partita IVAs are announced to the model as `[PHONE_n]`, not `[TAXID_n]`.** Example (issuable-shaped,
not a real company): `01032501239` → `Phone` under `new()`.

**Why every existing guard is green, and this is the part worth keeping.** The split by leading pair
is decisive: `00xx` is **2.2 %**, `0[1-9]xx` is **87.4 %** — a leading `00` reads to libphonenumber
as the international access code, so those are rejected as numbers. **All five real published
P.IVAs in this repo's corpus — `00905811006`, `00159560366`, `00488410010`, `00891030272`,
`00811720580` — are `00…`-leading**, i.e. entirely inside the immune sub-shape. VAT-01, VAT-03,
VAT-05, VAT-06, VAT-14 and VAT-16's controls are all drawn from it. *A corpus has a shape, and that
shape is a blind spot* — [M4's lesson 2](M4.md#retrospective), landing again, on a corpus assembled
in the milestone that quotes it.

**And `VAT-10` cannot see it by construction.** `recognizers.rs:3219` sweeps
`format!("{:011}", 10_000_000_000 + i)` — **every value starts with `1`**, and the only phone family
that can claim a bare digit run requires a leading `0`. The guard that ROADMAP presents as *"the
number a reader needs in order to know whether the naming rule matters in practice"* measures the
0.0998 national-ID collision and is structurally incapable of measuring the ~0.73 phone one. The
published statistic understates the labelling collision by about **7×**.

**What is and is not wrong.** The *ordering* is right and its justification (an assigned-number plan
lookup is better evidence than a mod-10 check) stands. Three things are wrong:
1. the magnitude was never measured, so nobody chose it;
2. *"The collision costs no coverage"* is false under the shipped default — what the recognizer adds
   is every P.IVA that is not also a Steuer-ID, an LV code, **or an assigned domestic number in any
   of the nine vetted regions**, and that last clause removes ~73 % of the issuable 0-leading space;
3. decision 1's entire purpose — a token that distinguishes a *business* identifier from a person's
   — is not delivered for the majority of the bare form's issuable space, which is the form the
   ROADMAP calls "the primary case".

**Fix.** Extend `VAT-10` to sweep the shape the phone collision actually lives in (leading `0`,
province code 001…110) and report the rate **against `StructuredRecognizers::new()`**, not only
against the validators; correct the `recognizers.rs:440-445` comment and
[`ARCHITECTURE.md`](../ARCHITECTURE.md)'s priority bullet to state both collisions and the measured
share. Then — and this is **the maintainer's call, not the builder's**, because it changes
product-visible labelling — decide whether `TaxId` should outrank `Phone` when the phone candidate
is reachable *only* through the separator-free `\b0\d{6,11}\b` arm (the weakest evidence in the
phone tier: no grouping, no context) while keeping `Phone` ahead on every separated rendering.

**Test that would have caught it.** VAT-14 with its instances drawn from `0[1-9]…` instead of `00…`
— or, better, a rate assertion beside VAT-10 quantifying over the *default* detector, so the number
that decides the naming rule is measured against the configuration that ships.

**Closed 2026-09-03 (builder) — the measurement and the two false claims. The ordering question is
NOT closed here, because it is not the builder's to close.**

**`VAT-17` is the missing measurement**, and it quantifies over `StructuredRecognizers::new()` — the
configuration that ships — rather than over the validators alone. It sweeps the *issuable* space on
purpose: a 0-leading serial and a province code in the assigned 001…110 band, which is the only bare
shape the phone `Trunk` arm can claim.

**Independently reproduced, on a different sample and a different construction from the round's
probe.** Expected around 0.73 (the round's number, written down before running); observed
**1163/1500 = 0.775**, with the split the round predicted: `00xx` **5/152 = 0.033**, `0[1-9]xx`
**1158/1348 = 0.859**. Both sub-rates are asserted, not just the total — the `00…` immunity is what
every *other* VAT guard silently rests on, so if it stopped holding those guards would quietly start
meaning something else.

**The two false claims are corrected where they were made**, not only in this archive:
- `src/pii/recognizers.rs`, on the bare recognizer — *"the collision costs no coverage"* named only
  the national-ID overlap. It now names **both** collisions with their measured sizes, and states
  what the recognizer adds correctly: every P.IVA that is not also a Steuer-ID, an LV code, **or an
  assigned domestic number in one of the nine vetted regions**.
- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`'s `TaxId` priority bullet now carries the 0.775 and the leading-pair split,
  and says plainly that the corpus every guard is built on sits inside the immune sub-shape.
- `VAT-10`'s doc comment now says what it *cannot* see — its sweep is `1`-leading — and points at
  `VAT-17`. `TESTING.md` carries all of it.

**Mutation, RED (expected RED):** `PiiKind::TaxId` raised above `Phone`, the regression VAT-14
forbids → **RED** at `recognizers.rs:3426`, the band assertion. So `VAT-17` watches the ordering it
measures, from the side a reintroducing change would be written.

**Answered by the maintainer 2026-09-03: the order stands.** Recorded as decision 4 in ROADMAP →
M11 Track A with its cost and both rejected alternatives, and summarised on `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s
priority bullet. The reasoning that settled it, and the part this round could not have supplied:
**the two strings `VAT-14` pins — `02079460958` and `03012345678` — are themselves
separator-free**, so yielding that arm to `TaxId` would not refine the rule, it would undo it. The
accepted cost is explicit: for ~77% of the bare form's issuable space, decision 1's business-vs-person
distinction is not delivered. `VAT-17` pins the number so a change to that trade has to edit it.

<a id="m11-r3"></a>
### M11-R3 — a twelfth `PiiKind` ships with the suite green: `AUG-01` and `from_label` are hand-kept lists the enum does not force you to revisit — *guard, medium-low*

**Repro.** Added `PiiKind::Vin` to `src/pii/mod.rs:35`, plus the only two arms the compiler demands
(`label()` → `"VIN"`, `priority()` → 2). `cargo test`: **239 passed, 0 failed**. `AUG-01` green.

Two hand-kept per-variant tables have no compile-time or test-time link to the enum:

1. **`AUG-01`** (`src/pipeline/privacy.rs:959`) iterates five literal tokens. Its own doc says
   *"Adding a `PiiKind` without teaching the model to read its token is a **silent** degradation …
   Nothing fails, so nothing tells you"* — and then nothing does. The guard watches the five kinds
   somebody typed, not the enum.
2. **`from_label`** (`src/pii/mod.rs:165`) ends in `_ => return None`, so `label()` can emit a token
   its inverse does not recognise. That is not cosmetic: `from_label` gates
   `anonymizer::is_placeholder_token`, which is what makes a placeholder **inert** to the next
   detection pass — the mechanism `Vault::mask_all`'s fixpoint depends on (M5-R4) — and it gates the
   M1.5 *"an unresolved known-kind placeholder is logged, never silently shipped"* warning. A kind
   added to `label` but not `from_label` degrades convergence and observability with no failing
   test.

`TaxId` was added to both correctly. Nothing makes the next one.

**Why this milestone in particular.** M11 built `shipped_tax_recognizer_count()`
(`recognizers.rs:520`) for exactly this reason, with exactly this argument — *"a hand-kept list of
six would silently stay six … Counting the set the recognizers are actually built from is the
chokepoint; a list somebody remembers to extend is not."* The same reasoning was not applied one
file over, to the guard whose subject is the enum itself.

**Fix.** One test with an **exhaustive `match kind`** over `PiiKind` — so a new variant is a compile
error, not a silent pass — that (a) classifies each variant as *exemplified in the prompt* or
*deliberately not*, and (b) asserts `PiiKind::from_label(k.label()) == Some(k)` for every variant.
Feed it from a `const ALL: &[PiiKind]` whose completeness the same exhaustive match proves.

**Test that would have caught it.** That test. The current one is satisfiable by any prompt
containing five fixed substrings.

**Closed 2026-09-03 (builder), at the chokepoint rather than the two instances.**

`PiiKind::ALL` is the new single list, and **`KIND-01` proves it cannot fall behind the enum** by
rebuilding it without reading it: a successor chain whose `match` the compiler checks for
exhaustiveness. Adding a variant is a compile error there; its author must place it in the chain; the
walk then reproduces `ALL` independently, and the comparison is what fails if `ALL` was not updated.
A variant wired to itself or into the middle of the chain is caught by a visited check rather than
hanging the test.

- **`KIND-02`** asserts `from_label(k.label()) == Some(k)` for every variant, case-insensitively,
  plus label distinctness — closing the `from_label` half, which gates placeholder inertness (the
  `mask_all` fixpoint, M5-R4) and the M1.5 unresolved-placeholder warning.
- **`AUG-01` is rewritten** to iterate `PiiKind::ALL` through an **exhaustive `match`** classifying
  each variant as exemplified-in-the-prompt or deliberately not. Either answer is fine; not being
  asked is not. The count of worked examples (5) is asserted too, since each one is paid for on
  every request.

**Mutations, both RED (expected RED):**
- `PiiKind::Vin` added to the enum alone → **does not compile** (`error[E0004]`, four sites). Red by
  construction, which is the strongest form available.
- `PiiKind::Vin` wired into *everything the compiler demands* — `label`, `priority`, `from_label`
  **and** `KIND-01`'s chain — but **not** into `ALL` → **RED** in
  `all_lists_every_variant_in_order`. That is exactly the residue the chain is claimed to close: the
  author satisfies the compiler and forgets the list. Before this fix the same variant left the whole
  suite at 239 passed / 0 failed.

<a id="m11-r4"></a>
### M11-R4 — `CAT-01`'s non-vacuity floor is 20 against 54 declared ids, so an entire guard family can go invisible — and `VAT-OM` already has — *guard, medium-low*

`tests/test_catalogue.rs:27` names this hole itself:

> a family that is *not* here is silently unchecked, which is the one way this guard can go quiet —
> so the non-vacuity floor below is what keeps that honest.

**The floor cannot keep it honest.** Measured by raising it to 100 000 and reading the failure
message: the extractor finds **54** declared ids. The floor is **20**, so **34 ids — 63 % of them —
can disappear before the guard notices**.

**Repro.** Removed `"VAT",` and `"AUG",` from `ID_PREFIXES` **and** scrubbed all **24** `VAT-*` /
`AUG-*` id mentions from `docs/TESTING.md`. `cargo test --test test_catalogue` → **ok**. The entire
guard family M11 added, gone from the catalogue and from the check, silently.

This goes past Round 0's M7 mutation: that showed a *cross-reference* keeps one id green, which is
CAT-01's documented scope. This shows a **whole family** can leave both the source-side prefix list
and the document with no floor able to notice.

**And there is a live instance already.** `VAT-OM` is declared at `tests/vat_overmask.rs:1` and
appears **nowhere** in `docs/TESTING.md`. Its model, `PHONE-OM`, is catalogued (5 mentions), so the
convention is real and M11's new file fell through it. The mechanism is a second blind spot in the
extractor: `declared_ids` only walks `///` doc comments **immediately above a `#[test]`**, so
module-level `//!` declarations are invisible. Four ids are declared that way — `CAT-01`, `DOS-01`,
`PHONE-OM`, `VAT-OM` — and `VAT-OM` is the one that is uncatalogued. Not fixed here on purpose: the
mutation proof would vanish with it, and the extractor is the builder's file.

**Fix (the chokepoint, not the instance).** Stop hand-keeping `ID_PREFIXES`: extract any
`[A-Z][A-Z0-9]+(?:-[A-Z0-9]+)*-[A-Za-z0-9]+`-shaped token out of doc comments and treat it as a
declaration, so a new family is in scope the moment it is written. Extend the walk to `//!` module
docs. Then add the `VAT-OM` entry to `TESTING.md` — with the extractor fixed, its absence is red
until someone does.

**Test that would have caught it.** A floor derived from the catalogue instead of a literal — e.g.
also extracting ids **from `TESTING.md`** and asserting the two sets agree — makes "a family
vanished from `ID_PREFIXES`" red while its entries are still in the document, and "an id was
declared but never catalogued" red while it is not.

**Closed 2026-09-03 (builder). The prefix list is gone, not extended.**

`ID_PREFIXES` was the defect, so it was deleted rather than grown. An id is now recognised by its
**shape** (`looks_like_guard_id`), so a family is in scope the moment somebody writes one — the
class, not the instance. Two structural exclusions carry the precision and neither is an enumeration:
**a first segment shorter than two characters** rules out all eight NER BIO tag names (`B-PER`,
`I-ORG`, `B-DATE`, …) quoted in the eval tests' module docs, and **a last segment that is neither
all digits nor all letters** rules out every review reference (`M4-R13`, `M10-R55`) — which matters
because those appear in nearly every doc comment in this repo.

**Measured on the way in, and this is the part worth keeping: adopting the shape rule brought nine
real families under the check that had never been in it** — `CC`, `DBG`, `NER-EP`, `PERF`,
`PHONE-COV`, `REG`, `THREAD` and two more. All were catalogued; none had ever been verified.

- The walk now reads **`//!` module docs**, which is how `VAT-OM` came to be declared and invisible.
- **`VAT-OM` is catalogued** in `TESTING.md`, as the over-mask family's entry alongside `PHONE-OM`.
- The **floor is 70 against a measured 73**, not 20 against 54. Its job changed: with no prefix list
  it can no longer be asked to notice a missing family, since that cannot happen — so it now only has
  to notice the extractor breaking, and for that it belongs just under reality.
- **`CAT-02`** pins the rule itself with a matrix — 18 real ids accepted against 15 look-alikes —
  because replacing a list with a rule moves the risk into the rule, and a rule that quietly narrowed
  would take `CAT-01` down while leaving it green.

**Mutations, all three RED (expected RED):**
- shape rule narrowed to digit-only suffixes, dropping `VAT-OM`, `DOS-BUD`, `PHONE-OM` and others →
  **RED** at `test_catalogue.rs:244` (`CAT-02`).
- `//!` walking removed → **RED** at `test_catalogue.rs:187`, the floor.
- every `VAT-17` mention removed from `TESTING.md` → **RED**, *"1 guard id(s) … named nowhere"*. Note
  it needed a replace-**all**: the id is named twice, and round 0's mutation M7 already showed a
  single-site rename leaves `CAT-01` green. That scope limit is unchanged and is now stated in the
  file's own header — closing it would mean pinning the catalogue's bullet format, which is a
  formatting rule pretending to be a correctness one.

**Residue, measured and accepted:** acronym-plus-number tokens such as `UTF-8` or `AGPL-3` satisfy
the shape rule. Neither occurs in any scanned doc comment today, and if one ever does the symptom is
a failure naming it rather than a silent pass. Written into `TESTING.md` → `CAT-02`, where it gets
read.

<a id="m11-r5"></a>
### M11-R5 — `VAT-09`'s band cannot go red: `0.100` is structural over a contiguous sweep, not a measurement of the checksum — *guard, low*

`src/pii/recognizers.rs:3193` sweeps **100 000 consecutive** 11-digit numbers and asserts the accept
rate lands in `0.08..=0.12`. For *any* scheme whose 11th digit is a check digit determined by the
first ten, exactly one value in each block of ten passes — so the rate is **exactly 0.100 by
construction**, whatever the arithmetic does.

**Repro.** Replaced the final comparison in `it_piva_valid` with `d[10] == 7`. VAT-09 printed
`bare P.IVA accepts 10000/100000 arbitrary 11-digit numbers (0.100)` and **passed**. (Seven other
VAT guards went red, so this is a claim-accuracy defect, not a coverage hole — the algorithm *is*
pinned, by VAT-01/VAT-02/VAT-03.)

The doc claim that does not hold is this one:

> A change that moved it would be a change to the shipped false-positive cost, and it should have to
> edit this number to land.

No change to the check-digit arithmetic can move it. What VAT-09 actually pins is the **shape** —
"eleven digits, the last of them a check digit, no context required" — which is a real and load-
bearing property, just not the one it is quoted for.

**Fix.** Restate what VAT-09 pins (one line in the doc comment and in
[`TESTING.md`](../TESTING.md) → VAT-09), and/or measure the **recognizer** over uncurated 11-digit
tokens rather than the validator over a contiguous range — Round 1's corpus figure above
(`9/104 = 0.0865`) is what that measurement looks like.

**Test that would have caught it.** A negative control on the guard itself: assert the rate over a
sweep whose last digit is **held constant**, where a correct mod-10 accepts ~1/10 of the *first ten*
digits' space and a constant-check-digit stub accepts either 0 or 1 — the two separate there and do
not separate over a contiguous run.

**Closed 2026-09-03 (builder).** `VAT-09` now runs **two** sweeps, and the second is the negative
control that makes the guard able to go red: the check digit is **held constant** while the first ten
digits vary. There a correct mod-10 still accepts about 1 in 10, while a comparison against a fixed
digit accepts **all or nothing** — 0.0 or 1.0, nowhere near the band. Two sweeps agreeing is evidence
about the checksum; one sweep was evidence about the format. Both fixed values (0 and 7) are run, so
neither can be the lucky one.

**The claim is restated rather than quietly kept.** The doc comment and `TESTING.md` → `VAT-09` now
say what sweep 1 actually pins — the *shape*: eleven digits, the last a check digit, no context
required — which is real and load-bearing, just not the arithmetic.

**And the rate is no longer only synthetic:** round 1's independent corpus figure is recorded beside
it — **9/104 = 0.0865** over 104 uncurated `\b\d{11}\b` tokens in ~304 MB of third-party source, about one
over-mask per 34 MB of code-shaped traffic, the masked values being memory and byte-size constants.
That is the real-traffic number `VAT-15`'s residue paragraph said no fixture in this repo could give,
and it now sits in `TESTING.md` under both ids.

**Mutation, RED (expected RED):** `it_piva_valid`'s final comparison replaced with `d[10] == 7` →
**RED** at `recognizers.rs:3294`, sweep 2. Before this fix the same mutation printed `0.100` and
**passed**.

<a id="round-2"></a>
## Round 2 — the product end to end, and two closures that did not hold (2026-09-03)

Aimed where Round 1 was not: **the product**. Round 1 returned five findings and all five were on the
test net, so this round spent its budget driving the **real compiled binary** over the surfaces M11's
own guards do not reach — the native Anthropic `/v1/messages` walk with VAT numbers in every
text-bearing field, the Anthropic SSE rewriter, `MAX_BODY_BYTES`, the DoS budget against a VAT-dense
body, JSON-encoded tool-call arguments, and multi-turn determinism.

**The product came back clean. No leak, no fail-open, no over-mask regression, no raw value in a log.**
That is the headline and it is not a formality: every one of those paths was driven, not read.

The four findings below are all on the **net**, and two of them are Round 1's own closures failing to
hold — [M11-R6](#m11-r6) (the `KIND-01` chokepoint does not close the class it claims) and
[M11-R7](#m11-r7) (`CAT-01`'s floor was set against a number that was never measured, and the mutation
its closure cites as proof is **green at HEAD**). Two further variants of already-closed families were
**not** filed: they are recorded as decided limits in [`TESTING.md`](../TESTING.md), per
`CLAUDE.md` → *a guard on a guard is worth one level, not four*.

### What was verified, with the expected value written before the observed one

| Quantity | Expected (written first) | Observed | Reps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cargo test` | 246 / 0 / 5, 0 warnings | **246 / 0 / 5, 0 warnings** | 2 (once at the start, once after every mutation was restored) |
| `cargo test-onnx` | 279 + 3 new guards = **282** / 0 / 22 | **282 / 0 / 22, 0 warnings** | 1 |
| `cargo fmt --check` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` | clean | **clean, 0 warnings** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy-onnx -- -D warnings` | clean | **clean, 0 warnings** | 1 |
| `VAT-17` | 1163/1500 = 0.775; `00xx` 5/152; `0[1-9]xx` 1158/1348 | **exactly that** | 1 (deterministic) |
| `VAT-09` sweep 1 / sweep 2 @0 / @7 | 0.100 / 0.100 / 0.100 | **0.100 / 0.100 / 0.100** | 1 (deterministic) |
| `VAT-10` | 1997/20000 = 0.0998 | **1997/20000 = 0.0998** | 1 (deterministic) |
| ids `CAT-01`'s extractor sees | **73** (what the floor's own comment and `TESTING.md` say) | **96** — and **90** at `f28631f`, the commit that wrote "73" | 3 commits sampled |
| `CAT-02` matrix size | 18 accepted / **15** rejected (what `TESTING.md` says) | 18 accepted / **17** rejected | 1 |

> **The task brief predicted `cargo test-onnx` at 279 + 5 = 284. It is 282, and nothing is missing.**
> Exactly **three** `#[test]` / `#[tokio::test]` functions were added since `6ba3620` (the diff shows 3
> added, 0 removed): PROP-05, VAT-18, VAT-19. Both feature sets moved by the same +3 (default 243 → 246,
> onnx 279 → 282), which is the cross-check that says the number is right rather than a test silently
> not running.

### The product, driven through the real binary

Two `.exe` instances, each on ports chosen away from the machine's own long-lived instance; **only PIDs
this round started were stopped.** (Worth writing down for the next round: **PID 27396 was already
running `target/debug/llm-proxy-pii-rust.exe` and is not ours.** It holds a lock on that file, so
`cargo test` cannot relink the bin target while it lives. The way through is `--target-dir target/review`,
**not** killing it.)

**1. Native Anthropic `/v1/messages` with a VAT number in every text-bearing field** — the largest
surface M11 shipped without any coverage. One request carrying six real VAT numbers across `system`
(string), a `text` block, a `document`'s `title` / `context` / `source.data`, a second document's
`source.type:content` nested block array, a `tool_use.input` (nested object **and** array leaf), a
`thinking` block, a `tool_result`'s nested content array, a plain-string `content`,
`tools[].description` and a nested `input_schema` description.

- **Expected: 0 occurrences of each raw value upstream. Observed: 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0 / 0.** Every field
  arrived as `[TAXID_n]`.
- Determinism held *across fields*: `IT00159560366` was `[TAXID_1]` in `system` and `[TAXID_1]` again in
  the `tool_result`; `DE136695976` was `[TAXID_5]` in three unrelated fields.
- The response walk restored `tool_use.input`'s string leaves — including one inside a nested array —
  byte-identically.
- At `RUST_LOG=llm_proxy_pii_rust=trace`, **0 raw values in either log stream**; the only M11-specific
  line is kind-only (`structure-only PII match … kind=TaxId`).

**2. Anthropic SSE with `[TAXID_1]` split across three `content_block_delta` frames** (`"[TAX"` / `"ID_"`
/ `"1] ok and [TAXID_2] too"`). Expected: rejoined and restored, no `[TAXID_` reaching the client.
Observed: both values restored, `[TAXID_` absent from the whole stream, frame order intact. `VAT-19`
covers the OpenAI rewriter; this is the *other* one.

**3. `MAX_BODY_BYTES`.** At `MAX_BODY_BYTES=4096`: a 196-byte body → 200; an 8 096-byte body → **413,
"length limit exceeded"**, and the upstream recorded **one** request (the small one) with **0** raw
P.IVAs. Fails closed, nothing forwarded.

**4. The DoS budget against a VAT-dense body.** `n` distinct 11-digit tokens, `n` in {200, 400, 800,
1600, 3200}: 0.075 / 0.034 / 0.051 / 0.089 / 0.172 s — the doubling ratios settle at **1.51 / 1.75 /
1.93**, i.e. linear, and every request 200. The same for the NL 14-character form at n in {200, 800,
3200}: 0.018 / 0.033 / 0.117 s (**1.85** and **3.53** for the two 4× steps). Six new `Scan::Overlapping`
recognizers did not reintroduce M4-R19 / M4-R24.

**5. OpenAI path — a VAT inside a JSON-encoded `tool_calls[].function.arguments` string**, restored
through `demask_json_string` alongside a literal quote escape: the client received
`{"vat": "00905811006", "note": "quote \" and NL111222333B01"}` and it **parses as JSON**.

**6. Multi-turn determinism with `TaxId`** across two independent stateless requests: same value → same
token → same restore, both turns.

**7. A real VAT next to a client-supplied `[TAXID_1]` literal.** The client's own token is restored to
the proxy's value. **This is not an M11 defect and was not filed** — the control run with `[EMAIL_1]` +
`bob@test.com` behaves identically, so it is the generic property of the tolerant de-masker (M1.5 CR-4),
older than this milestone and outside its scope. Nothing leaked in either case (0 raw values upstream).

### The independent measurement `VAT-17`'s lattice needed

The brief asked whether `VAT-17`'s sweep — `serial = (i * 661) % 1_000_000` for `i in 0..1500`, where the
modulo **never wraps** (max `1499 * 661 = 990 839`) so serials sit on an arithmetic progression, and
`province = 1 + (i % 110)` is locked to `i` — is representative or degenerate the way Round 1's first
probe was.

Measured out-of-tree, in a scratch crate that depends on this one by path and re-implements the P.IVA
check digit independently rather than calling the crate's validator: **a splitmix64 sample, not a
lattice**, over the same issuable space.

| Sample | n | named `Phone` | `00xx` | `0[1-9]xx` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `VAT-17`'s lattice | 1 500 | 0.775 | 0.033 | 0.859 |
| splitmix64 | 1 500 | **0.797** | 0.028 | 0.877 |
| splitmix64 | 20 000 | **0.793** | 0.028 | 0.878 |
| splitmix64 | 60 000 | **0.791** | 0.030 | 0.875 |

**The lattice is representative, and slightly conservative** — it under-reports by ~0.017, well inside
the asserted `0.55..=0.90` band, and the `00xx` immunity every other VAT guard rests on reproduces at
0.028–0.030 against the `< 0.15` assertion. No finding.

Two by-products worth keeping, both from the 60 000-point run:

- **`assert!(!entities.is_empty())` never fired across 81 500 issuable P.IVAs** — every one is masked
  under *some* kind. That is the no-leak claim measured at a scale no guard in the tree reaches.
- The resolved labels **partition exactly**: 47 437 `Phone` + 6 387 `TaxId` + 6 176 `NationalId` =
  60 000. The union-merge really does yield one naming kind per span, with nothing dropped and nothing
  double-counted.

### Mutations run in round 2

Every anchor was checked for a **unique** match first; every edit was written as **bytes** and read back
from disk (`cat -A`) to prove the replacement was really there and the LF line endings survived; every
file was restored from its pre-edit bytes and **sha256-compared against both the saved copy and the
`HEAD` blob**; `git status --porcelain` was verified empty after each restore and at the end of the
round; and the full suite was re-run afterwards and came back to **246 / 0 / 5, 0 warnings**.

| # | Mutation | Expected | Got | What fired |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | 12th `PiiKind::Vin` + **only what the compiler demands**: `label`, `priority`, `KIND-01`'s chain as `Vin => return None`, `AUG-01`'s classifier as `false`. **Not** in `ALL`, **not** in `from_label` | RED | **GREEN (149/149)** | nothing — [M11-R6](#m11-r6) |
| P1b | same, but the chain arm is `Vin => PiiKind::Email` (points *into* the chain; nothing points *to* `Vin`) | RED | **GREEN (149/149)** | nothing — the second of two minimal completions |
| P2 | `CAT-01`'s floor raised to 100 000 to read the count | RED, naming ~73 | **RED, "found only 96"** | the floor — the count is 96, not 73 ([M11-R7](#m11-r7)) |
| P3 | the `//!` module-doc walk disabled — **M11-R4's own closure records this as RED** | RED | **GREEN (2/2)** | nothing — [M11-R7](#m11-r7) |
| P4 | `it_piva_valid`'s doubling parity swapped — **wrong** arithmetic, still body-dependent | VAT-09 GREEN, real-number guards RED | **VAT-09 GREEN; 9 tests RED** | `italian_piva_accepts_real_published_numbers` + 8 others — a *decided limit*, not a finding |
| — | the legitimate path, unmutated: `cargo test`, `cargo test-onnx`, `cargo fmt --check`, both clippy legs | GREEN | **GREEN** | — |

**P3 is a mutation Round 1's closure already recorded, and re-running it was deliberate.** Its arithmetic
did not add up: the `//!` walk contributes only a handful of ids, so it could not plausibly drop a count
of "73" below a floor of 70. Before contradicting the recorded red, the same instant was checked — and
the recorded red was **honest at its own commit**: at `f28631f` the walk-less count is **64**, below 70.
It is **96 → 70** at HEAD, and `70 >= 70` passes. The guard did not fail then; it has stopped watching
*since*, inside this same milestone, because three guards were added and the floor was not raised. That
is the finding, and it is only visible because the mutation was re-run rather than trusted.

### Verified not broken

- Both ordinary gates a person actually runs: `cargo test` and `cargo test-onnx` green with **0
  warnings**, `cargo fmt --check` exit 0, `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` and
  `cargo clippy-onnx -- -D warnings` both clean.
- The real binary boots, serves `/healthz` 200, and round-trips on **both** wire schemas
  (`/v1/chat/completions` and native `/v1/messages`), buffered **and** streamed.
- Every VAT rendering family (bare IT, `IT…`, `DE…`, `GB…`, `PT…`, `NL…B..`) masks and restores over
  HTTP.
- **Suite cost is not a problem, contrary to the brief's worry.** Measured directly against the lib test
  binary: `VAT-17` **2.06 s**, `VAT-09` **0.32 s**, `VAT-10` **0.23 s** — ~2.6 s for all three
  measurement guards. `PROP-05` costs **17.2 s** of single-threaded CPU but its **marginal wall-clock
  cost in `tests/pii_properties.rs` is about zero** (three reps: +0.02 s, +4.06 s, −2.55 s, against a
  file whose own run-to-run spread is 24.6–28.7 s) — it runs in parallel with the phone proptest, which
  is the long pole. M10-R2's class is not reproduced.
  > **A measurement of my own that was a green in disguise, recorded because it is the same trap.** The
  > first attempt at these timings ran `cargo test --lib <name> -- --exact`. `--exact` needs the *full
  > module path*, so the filter matched **nothing** and the harness printed `test result: ok … finished
  > in 0.00s` — a pass that ran zero tests. It was caught by reading `0 passed; … 149 filtered out`
  > rather than the `ok`. Only the full-path re-run produced the numbers above.

### What this round could not break

- The masking path itself. Every text-bearing field of the native Anthropic schema is reached; every
  unmodeled shape still fails closed; the six new recognizers are all bounded and all take `free()`, so
  neither the rescan nor the budget changed character.
- `looks_like_guard_id`'s `first[1..]`. It is **unreachable** with a multi-byte first character:
  `first.starts_with(|c: char| c.is_ascii_uppercase())` on the line above guarantees a one-byte first
  char, so the slice is always on a boundary. `"VÄT-15"` and `"É-01"` are in `CAT-02`'s matrix and both
  reject for the right reason. No panic, no finding.
- `KIND-01`'s successor chain cannot **hang**: a cycle reachable from `Email` is caught by the visited
  check, and one that is not reachable is never walked. (That it is never walked is the *other* problem —
  see [M11-R6](#m11-r6).)
- `vat_grammar_could_match`'s floor does not call any real form unreachable (the shortest shipped
  prefixed token is 11 characters, the floor is 9). Its *stated reason* is wrong and its slack is
  recorded as a decided limit in `TESTING.md` → VAT-04 rather than filed.

### Promoted to `TESTING.md` instead of filed — two variants of families already closed

`CLAUDE.md` says a further variant of a closed family is a **decided limit**, not a finding. Both of
these are, and both are now written next to the guard they govern:

1. **`VAT-04`'s reachability floor is 9, but no shipped pattern can match a token shorter than 11.**
   A negative written 9 or 10 characters long would be judged *reachable* and its absence believed —
   the exact failure M11-R1 filed, two characters out of reach. Latent today: every VAT-04 negative is
   at least 11 characters. Same family as M11-R1; recorded, not chased.
2. **`VAT-09`'s two sweeps are both structurally 0.100, so together they pin "the check digit depends on
   the body" and not the arithmetic.** Measured (P4): swapping the doubling parity — wrong arithmetic,
   still body-dependent — turns **9 tests red and leaves `VAT-09` green at 0.100 on both sweeps**. The
   arithmetic *is* pinned, by the eight real-published-number guards that went red; what overstates its
   reach is the doc claim. Same family as M11-R5; recorded, not chased.

<a id="m11-r6"></a>
### M11-R6 — `KIND-01`'s chain forces an *arm*, not a place in the walk: a twelfth `PiiKind` still ships with the suite green — *guard, medium-low*

**M11-R3 was closed with a chokepoint claim, and the claim is false.** Three places state it:

- `src/pii/mod.rs:76-82` — *"a new variant is a compile error there, its author has to say where in the
  chain it goes, and once it is in the chain it appears here automatically"*;
- [`TESTING.md`](../TESTING.md) → KIND-01 — *"A new variant is a compile error there, **its author has to
  place it in the chain**"*;
- [M11-R3](#m11-r3)'s closure note — *"Adding a variant is a compile error there; its author must place
  it in the chain."*

The compiler demands an **arm**. It does not demand that anything **point to** the new variant, and the
walk only ever visits what the chain reaches from its hard-coded start (`src/pii/mod.rs:525`,
`let mut walked = vec![PiiKind::Email];`).

**Repro — two of the three minimal ways to satisfy `error[E0004]` leave the suite green.** A twelfth
`PiiKind::Vin` was added to the enum with **only** what the compiler refuses to build without: `label`
(`src/pii/mod.rs:114`), `priority` (`:182`), `KIND-01`'s `next` (`:526`), and `AUG-01`'s `exemplified`
(`src/pipeline/privacy.rs:990`). It was **not** added to `PiiKind::ALL` and **not** to `from_label`.

| chain arm written for `Vin` | outcome |
|---|---|
| `PiiKind::Vin => return None,` (P1) | **149 passed / 0 failed** |
| `PiiKind::Vin => PiiKind::Email,` (P1b) | **149 passed / 0 failed** |
| slotted between two existing variants | RED — this is the one M11-R3's closure tested |

`all_lists_every_variant_in_order`, `every_kind_round_trips_through_its_label` and
`the_augmentation_prompt_names_every_placeholder_kind_it_should` are **all three green** in the first two
rows. `ALL` stays at 11, so `AUG-01`'s `PiiKind::ALL.len() == 11` non-vacuity check passes too — the
guard designed to notice `ALL` shrinking cannot notice `ALL` failing to grow.

**Why it is not cosmetic, and it is the same consequence M11-R3 named.** `KIND-02` iterates
`PiiKind::ALL`, so a variant missing from `ALL` is a variant `KIND-02` never asks about — and in both
rows above `Vin` is in `label` but **not** in `from_label`. `from_label` gates
`anonymizer::is_placeholder_token` (`src/pii/anonymizer.rs:335`), which is what makes `[VIN_1]` **inert**
to the next detection pass — the mechanism `Vault::mask_all`'s fixpoint rests on (M5-R4) — and it gates
the M1.5 warning that an unresolved known-kind placeholder is logged rather than silently shipped. The
failure is fail-*closed* (a non-converging `mask_all` blocks, M4-R20) rather than a leak, so this is
availability and observability, not privacy. But it is precisely the harm the closure claimed to have
made impossible.

**Fix — and it must be *one* level, not a fourth.** Do not add a guard that watches `KIND-01`. Delete the
hand-written list instead: declare the variants **once**, in a `macro_rules!` that emits both the `enum`
and `ALL` from the same token list. Then a variant absent from `ALL` is not a test failure, it is a
variant that does not exist — and `KIND-01` and its successor chain can both be deleted, which is the
mark of a real chokepoint rather than another layer. If the macro is judged too much, the honest
alternative is to keep the chain and **correct the three claims above** to what it actually does: *the
chain proves `ALL` agrees with the chain; it does not prove either agrees with the enum.*

**Test that would have caught it.** The mutation in row 1 — the laziest edit that compiles. M11-R3's own
mutation wired `Vin` into the chain, which is the *diligent* author's edit; the guard exists for the
other one.

**Closed 2026-09-03 (builder) — by deleting the guard, which is what the finding asked for.**

The round is right that no test can close this: Rust cannot enumerate an enum's variants, so any
guard is reduced to checking a *list against a list*, and the compiler can only be made to demand
an **arm**. `Vin => return None` is a legal arm. So the chain is gone and the question is removed
instead of watched.

**`pii_kinds!` generates the enum, `PiiKind::ALL`, `label` and `from_label` from one list.** Adding
a kind is one line and all four move together; declaring a variant outside that list is a *parse
error*. Deliberately **not** generated: `priority` and `is_structured` — those are judgements about
a new kind rather than restatements of it, and the compiler already stops you at their exhaustive
`match`, which is the right place to be stopped. One level, as the finding prescribed: the chain
was deleted rather than joined by a second guard.

**Four mutations, every remaining route, all RED:**

| # | Route | Result |
|---|---|---|
| R6a | 12th kind added to the macro list | **RED** — `error[E0004]` at `priority`/`is_structured`. The list can grow; the judgement cannot be skipped. |
| R6b | variant declared **without** a label — *this round's P1 route* | **RED** — `error: no rules expected` \`,\`. The route that beat the chain is now a parse error. |
| R6c | two kinds share a label | **RED** — `KIND-02`, plus two callers |
| R6d | a label that is not uppercase (unreachable after `to_ascii_uppercase`) | **RED** — `KIND-02`, plus three callers |

`KIND-01` survives with a narrowed and honest claim: `ALL` is the eleven-entry list every other
guard is driven from, and the priority judgement was actually made rather than defaulted.

<a id="m11-r7"></a>
### M11-R7 — `CAT-01`'s floor was justified by a number that was never measured, and the mutation its closure cites as proof is **green at HEAD** — *guard, medium*

Two halves, and the second follows from the first.

**Half 1 — the number is fabricated.** `tests/test_catalogue.rs:188-195` sets the floor at 70 and says
*"Measured 73 when written"*; the failure message says *"there were 73 when this floor was set"*;
[`TESTING.md`](../TESTING.md) → CAT-01 says *"the floor now sits at **70 against a measured 73**"*; and
`looks_like_guard_id`'s own doc (`tests/test_catalogue.rs:55`) says *"Measured against the whole tree when
it was written: 73 ids accepted"*.

Measured by raising the floor to 100 000 and reading the failure message (P2): the extractor finds **96**.
Reconstructing the tree at each M11 commit with `git archive` and running a Python replica of
`declared_ids` + `looks_like_guard_id` — validated by reproducing the Rust count of 96 on HEAD exactly:

| commit | extractor count |
|---|---|
| `f28631f` — **the commit that wrote "Measured 73"** | **90** |
| `6ba3620`, `178569d`, `41c9e42` | 90 |
| `57ab085`, `f3a6b18`, HEAD | **96** |

So 73 was wrong by 17 the day it was committed. It is not a sub-count either: at `f28631f` the tree gives
49 from `tests/`, 41 from `src/`, 90 together, 76 distinct ids. Nothing there is 73. (The assertion counts
`(id, file)` **pairs**, so an id declared in two files counts twice — worth stating, because the message
calls them "declared guard ids".)

**Half 2 — and this is why the number mattered.** M11-R4's closure justifies the floor with *"its job
changed … it now only has to notice the extractor breaking, and for that it belongs just under reality"*,
and proves it with a mutation: *"`//!` walking removed → **RED** at `test_catalogue.rs:187`, the floor."*

**That mutation is GREEN at HEAD.** Disabling the `//!` module-doc walk (P3) leaves the extractor at
**exactly 70**, and the assertion is `declared.len() >= 70`:

```
test the_guard_id_shape_rule_separates_ids_from_prose ... ok
test every_declared_guard_id_appears_in_the_test_catalogue ... ok
test result: ok. 2 passed; 0 failed
```

**The recorded RED was honest at its own commit** — at `f28631f` the walk-less count is **64**, below 70 —
and this round verified that before contradicting it. What happened since is inside this same milestone:
`41c9e42` / `57ab085` added VAT-18, VAT-19 and PROP-05, the walk-less count went 64 → 70, and the floor
was not raised. The floor's own comment says *"raise this deliberately when guards are added"* and the
three guards added two commits later did not raise it. Seventeen ids — `VAT-OM`, `DBG-01` / `DBG-02`,
`CLI-01` / `CLI-05`, `E2E-01` / `E2E-03`, `E2E-INT` / `E2E-INT-01` / `E2E-INT-02`, `INT-01`, `DOS-03`,
`NER-EP`, `PERF-01`, `PROP-01`, `REG-03`, `SMOKE-GLINER` — are declared only in `//!` module docs, and
losing the whole `//!` branch is now free.

More generally: **26 of 96 declarations (27%) can disappear before the floor notices.** M11-R4 filed
exactly this shape at 20-against-54 (63%); the fix moved the ratio and left the mechanism.

**Fix.** Correct all four statements of "73" to the measured value, and set the floor **just under
reality** the way the comment says — with the count at 96 and the walk-less count at 70, a floor of 70
does not distinguish them, and something like 88–90 does. Better, and it is what M11-R4's own *"test that
would have caught it"* proposed and the closure did not build: **derive the floor instead of writing it
down** — extract the ids from `TESTING.md` as well and assert the two sets agree, so "a family vanished
from the source" is red while its entries are still in the document, and no literal has to be maintained
at all. That is one level, and it retires the treadmill rather than resetting it.

**Test that would have caught it.** Re-running the closure's own `//!` mutation after the three guards
landed. It is the cheapest possible regression check and it is the one the milestone skipped.

**Closed 2026-09-03 (builder). The number was wrong and so was the shape of the check.**

The round is exactly right about the arithmetic, and the error is worth naming precisely: the
extractor collects **(id, file) pairs**, while the "measured 73" came from a Python probe of mine
that counted **distinct candidate ids**. Two different quantities, and I compared one against the
other. Re-read from the extractor itself: **96 pairs, 79 distinct** today. Confirmed the round's
consequence rather than taking it on trust — deleting the `//!` walk leaves **exactly 70** pairs
against `>= 70`, green by one. (My own first re-measurement said 64, which was the wrong
subtraction: it removed pairs that a `///` block *also* declares. 70 is the right number.)

**A bigger literal was not the fix.** One total cannot notice one of two independent mechanisms
dying while the other grows — that is structural, not a matter of margin. So each walk asserts its
own liveness and the aggregate is left only the job it can do:

- `>= 4` ids from `//!` module docs — several files name their whole family at the top
  (`CAT-01`, `DOS-01`, `PHONE-OM`, `VAT-OM`), and that walk dying is precisely this finding;
- `>= 60` ids from `///` blocks above a `#[test]` — the main site;
- `>= 90` pairs overall, for the case both walks survive and the *shape rule* narrows.

**Three mutations, three distinct assertions, all RED** — `//!` walk deleted → line 210 (green
before this fix), `///` walk deleted → line 217, shape rule narrowed to digit-only suffixes →
line 225 plus `CAT-02`. Every number above is read from this extractor on this tree at this commit,
which is the discipline whose absence caused the finding.

<a id="m11-r8"></a>
### M11-R8 — the maintainer's decision 4 is recorded as still open in two of the three places that state it — *docs, low-medium*

`f3a6b18` recorded the answer: **the order stands**, with its cost and both rejected alternatives
(ROADMAP → M11 Track A, decision 4), and [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](../ARCHITECTURE.md)'s priority bullet was
updated to match. Two places were not, and both still put the question to the reader as open:

- [`TESTING.md`](../TESTING.md), the `VAT-17` entry — *"Whether the separator-free arm alone should
  yield to `TaxId` **is open** and **product-visible**, so it is the maintainer's call; what this guard
  ends is the number being unknown."*
- `src/pii/recognizers.rs:3437-3439`, `VAT-17`'s own doc comment — *"Whether the ordering should change
  for the separator-free arm specifically is the maintainer's call, not this guard's."*

`TESTING.md` is one of the two files `CLAUDE.md` designates as **the design** — the file a reader
consults instead of the archive. Someone reading `VAT-17`'s catalogue entry today is told a settled trade
is still awaiting a decision, and the specific reason it was settled (the two strings `VAT-14` pins are
*themselves* separator-free, so yielding that arm undoes the guard rather than narrowing it) is the one
thing a future reader most needs, because it is the argument that stops the question being re-opened.

**Not a defect:** [`DEVLOG.md`](../DEVLOG.md) says the same thing and should **stay** — it is
chronological history and was true when written.

**Fix.** Replace both sentences with the decision and its one-line reason, pointing at ROADMAP →
M11 Track A decision 4. `TESTING.md` is docs; the `recognizers.rs` comment is the builder's.

**Test that would have caught it.** None available, and worth saying rather than inventing one: no guard
can know a maintainer answered a question. What *would* have caught it is the habit `CAT-01` encodes one
level down — when a decision lands, grep for every place that states the question, not just the one being
edited. `grep -rn "separator-free arm" docs/ src/` returns four hits and two were updated.

**Closed 2026-09-03 (builder).** Both sites now state decision 4 as decided:
`docs/TESTING.md` → `VAT-17` and the `VAT-17` doc comment in `src/pii/recognizers.rs`. Each carries
the reason the obvious refinement was rejected, because the recognizer's doc comment is where
somebody would try it: yielding the separator-free arm to `TaxId` relabels `02079460958` and
`03012345678`, which **are themselves separator-free**, so it undoes `VAT-14` rather than narrowing
it. DEVLOG left as written — it is the record of what was open on the day, and the round is right
that rewriting it would be falsifying history rather than correcting a claim.

<a id="m11-r9"></a>
### M11-R9 — `TESTING.md` states `CAT-02`'s matrix as 18 against 15; it is 18 against 17 — *docs, low*

[`TESTING.md`](../TESTING.md), the CAT-02 entry — *"the rule is pinned directly, by a matrix of the cases
it must separate — 18 real ids accepted … against **15** things that look like them and are not."*

`tests/test_catalogue.rs` holds **18** positives and **17** negatives. The two extra are `"VÄT-15"` and
`"É-01"`, added in `41c9e42` for the multi-byte `first[1..]` question, and the count beside them was not
moved.

Small, and filed anyway for the reason M6-R3 was: this repo's catalogue entries are quoted as
measurements, and a count that drifts by two on the commit that changes it is the same drift the
catalogue exists to prevent — one level in.

**Fix.** `15` → `17` in that sentence.

**Test that would have caught it.** Nothing cheap and nothing worth building — pinning a prose count
against a source array is a formatting rule pretending to be a correctness one, which is exactly the line
M11-R4's closure drew for `CAT-01`'s scope. Recording it as review's job is the right answer.

**Closed 2026-09-03 (builder).** `TESTING.md` now reads 18 against **17**. The count moved when
`178569d` added the two non-ASCII negatives (`VÄT-15`, `É-01`) and the catalogue entry was not
re-counted — the same drift `CAT-01` exists to catch one level up, and one `CAT-01` cannot see
because it checks that an id is *named*, not that a sentence about it is true.
