<a id="round-3"></a>
## Round 3 — the case axis, and the leak nobody had looked for (2026-09-03)

Rounds 1 and 2 returned nine findings and **every one was on the test net or the docs**. That
ratio is what `CLAUDE.md` → *a guard on a guard is worth one level, not four* exists to stop, so
this round was told to file only a **chokepoint still open** or something in a **different
class**, and to prefer an empty round to a tenth net finding.

**It is not empty, and the one finding is in the product: a leak.** Every one of this repo's
letter-bearing structured recognizers has a **case-sensitivity** axis, nobody has ever decided it,
and the two families that landed on "uppercase only" — **IBAN** (M1) and all six **VAT** patterns
(M11) — forward a lower- or mixed-case rendering **in clear**. Driven through the real `.exe`:
7 of 13 renderings of real published VAT numbers, and an IBAN with a *single* lowercase letter in
it. [M11-R10](#m11-r10).

Two further variants of already-closed families were **not** filed and are recorded as decided
limits in [`TESTING.md`](../TESTING.md) instead, per the same rule.

### What was verified, with the expected value written before the observed one

| Quantity | Expected (written first) | Observed | Reps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cargo test` | 246 / 0 / 5, 0 warnings | **246 / 0 / 5, 0 warnings** | 2 (start of round; after every mutation was restored) |
| `cargo test-onnx` | 282 / 0 / 22, 0 warnings | **282 / 0 / 22, 0 warnings** | 1 |
| `cargo fmt --check` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` | clean | **clean, exit 0** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy-onnx -- -D warnings` | clean | **clean, exit 0** | 1 |
| `VAT-17` | 1163/1500 = 0.775; `00xx` 5/152; `0[1-9]xx` 1158/1348 | **exactly that** | 1 (deterministic) |
| `VAT-09` sweep 1 / sweep 2 @0 / @7 | 0.100 / 0.100 / 0.100 | **0.100 / 0.100 / 0.100** | 1 (deterministic) |
| `VAT-10` | 1997/20000 = 0.0998 | **1997/20000 = 0.0998** | 1 (deterministic) |
| `CAT-02` matrix | 18 accepted / 17 rejected (what `TESTING.md` now says) | **18 / 17** — counted in `test_catalogue.rs` | 1 |
| `CAT-01`, what the aggregate assertion counts | module + test **must equal** total; total **> 96** — predicted **32 / 70 / 102**, because the fix added `Origin` to the tuple and 70 was M11-R7's own walk-less pair count | **module 32 · test 70 · total 102** | 1 |
| `CAT-01`, distinct | 79 distinct ids, 96 distinct (id, file) pairs — the two figures the closure quotes | **79 / 96** | 1 |
| every prefixed VAT scheme, **case-insensitive**, over 341.1 MB of third-party source | 0–20 raw hits per scheme, 0–2 checksum-valid | **0 raw, 0 valid — for all five, uppercase and case-insensitive alike** | 1 |
| bare `\b\d{11}\b` over the same corpus | round 1 got 104 raw / 9 valid over 304 MB → scaled, ~115–160 / ~10–16 | **128 raw / 12 valid = 0.094** | 1 |
| IBAN continuous arm, case-folded | direction predicted (hex digests would flood it); **magnitude not predicted** | **2 → 933 hits, +931** | 1 |
| …of those 931, how many pass mod-97 **and** the ISO 13616 length | ~1/97 ≈ **9–10** | **0** (only 26 of the 931 even carry a known country code) | 1 |

The three published VAT rates are deterministic sweeps, so one rep confirms determinism rather
than sampling a distribution. The filter that produced them was checked against the
`filtered out` count — **3 passed, 146 filtered out** — because a filter matching nothing prints
`ok … 0 passed` (round 2's own trap).

### The product, driven through the real binary

Round 2 drove the native Anthropic walk, the Anthropic SSE rewriter, `MAX_BODY_BYTES`, the DoS
budget, JSON-encoded tool arguments, multi-turn determinism and the client-supplied-placeholder
case; those are recorded there and were **not** re-run. This round drove the axis none of them
varies: **letter case**.

Two `.exe` instances, on ports chosen away from the machine's own long-lived proxy; only the PIDs
this round started were stopped. Built and run with `--target-dir target/builder` so the locked
`target/debug/llm-proxy-pii-rust.exe` could not block a relink — see the note this round added to
[`SETUP.md`](../SETUP.md).

**Run 1 — thirteen renderings of six real published VAT numbers in one request.** Expected: the
five uppercase VIES forms and the bare digits masked; the rest unknown, which is the hypothesis.

```
IT upper glued   IT00905811006    masked      IT lower glued  it00905811006   RAW UPSTREAM
IT mixed glued   It00905811006    RAW         IT bare digits  00905811006     masked ([TAXID_2])
DE upper         DE136695976      masked      DE lower        de136695976     RAW UPSTREAM
GB upper         GB220430231      masked      GB lower        gb220430231     RAW UPSTREAM
PT upper         PT524287244      masked      PT lower        pt524287244     RAW UPSTREAM
NL upper         NL111222333B01   masked      NL lower        nl111222333b01  RAW UPSTREAM
                                              NL mixed `b`    NL111222333b01  RAW UPSTREAM
```

**Run 2 — is it the VAT tier's, or the whole structured tier's?** The latter. Same binary, one
value per family, upper and lower:

```
IBAN IT       IT60X0542811101000000123456  masked   it60x0542811101000000123456  RAW
IBAN DE       DE89370400440532013000       masked   de89370400440532013000       RAW
IBAN, one letter lowered                            IT60x0542811101000000123456  RAW
IBAN, grouped, lowercased                           it60 x054 2811 … 456         RAW
Codice Fiscale MRTMTT25D09F205Z            masked   mrtmtt25d09f205z             masked
ES DNI        12345678Z                    masked   12345678z                    masked
GB NINO       AB123456C                    masked   ab123456c                    masked
CN resident   11010519491231002X           masked   11010519491231002x           masked
VAT GB, mixed prefix                                Gb220430231                  RAW
```

So the national-ID tier folds case and masks either way; **IBAN and the six VAT patterns are the
only ones that do not**, and for IBAN a single lowercase character anywhere in the BBAN drops the
whole span. `AKIA…` lowercased is also unmatched and that one is **correct** — an AWS access key
id is uppercase by format, so `akia…` is not a key.

Everything that *was* masked round-tripped byte-identically to the client, and at
`RUST_LOG=llm_proxy_pii_rust=trace` the only kind-bearing log line remains value-free. The raw
values that do appear in the trace are the ones the masker **missed**: the `trace!` of the
outbound body is only as safe as the masking it follows (M2.6's boundary is unchanged and still
correct — this is a consequence of the leak, not a second defect).

### The independent corpus measurement

Same stand-in Round 1 used — the crates.io source cache, text nobody in this project wrote —
re-walked at **341.1 MB across 16 380** `.rs` / `.md` / `.toml` / `.json` / `.txt` files. The
checksum replicas were written from the specs rather than called from the crate, and validated
first against all six real published numbers plus four altered ones (12/12 correct) before any
count was believed.

| Pattern | uppercase (as shipped) | case-insensitive | marginal cost of folding case |
|---|---|---|---|
| `IT\d{11}` / `DE\d{9}` / `GB\d{9}` / `PT\d{9}` / `NL\d{9}B\d{2}` | 0 hits each | **0 hits each** | **0** |
| bare `\d{11}` | 128 raw, **12** mod-10-valid (0.094) | — | — (cross-checks round 1's 9/104 = 0.0865 and the synthetic `VAT-09` 0.100) |
| IBAN, continuous arm | **2** | **933** | **+931**, all masked — IBAN has no hard checksum gate |
| …IBAN, if a case-folded rendering had to be `Verified` | — | — | **0 of the 931 survive** mod-97 + ISO 13616 length |

That is the whole finding in four rows: for the VAT tier the case rule buys **nothing measurable**
and costs seven renderings; for IBAN it buys a great deal, is undocumented, and there is a cheap
gate that would keep it while closing the miss.

### Mutations run in round 3

Every anchor was checked for a **unique** match first, every edit written as **bytes** through a
Python helper whose backslashes are built with `chr(92)` (M11's own heredoc trap), read back from
disk with `cat -A`, refused outright if the substitution was empty, and restored from the pre-edit
bytes with a **sha256** comparison against both the saved copy and the `HEAD` blob.
`git status --porcelain` was verified empty after each restore and at the end of the round. Both
files are LF-only, confirmed before matching.

| # | Mutation | Expected | Got | What fired |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| R3-A | the NL btw-id's literal `B` separator → `[Bb]` | GREEN — nothing in the catalogue mentions that character's case | **GREEN (246 / 0 / 5)** | nothing — the case of the `B` is unpinned in **both** directions |
| R3-B | the prefixed IT VAT pattern → `(?i:IT)\d{11}` | RED, at `VAT-05` only | **RED — 148 passed / 1 failed** | `recognizers.rs:3226`, `kinds("it00905811006").is_empty()`. **Line 3221 — the assertion that carries the published rationale — stayed green under the very change it is quoted as forbidding** |
| R3-C | `CAT-01`'s module-doc floor replaced by a panic printing all three live counts | RED, naming them | **RED — `module=32 test=70 total=102`** | the probe. `102 ≠ 96`, the number in the comment and in `TESTING.md` |
| R3-D | same probe extended to distinct ids and distinct (id, file) pairs | RED, naming them | **RED — `distinct_ids=79 distinct_pairs=96`** | the probe — the closure's two figures are right about the *tree* and wrong about the *assertion* |
| — | the legitimate paths, unmutated: `cargo test`, `cargo test-onnx`, `cargo fmt --check`, both clippy legs, the real binary on both wire schemas | GREEN | **GREEN** | — |

**R3-B is the one worth reading.** It is the inverse of the usual mutation result: the guard did
go red, but the *wrong half* of it did. `VAT-05` holds two assertions; only the second depends on
case at all, because the first (`"call it 00905811006 back"`) contains a **space**, and the tier
separately forbids any space between prefix and digits. The published rationale for the whole
uppercase-only rule is built on that unreachable example.

### What this round could not break

- **The `pii_kinds!` macro is a faithful replacement, checked by comparing the expansion against
  the pre-macro source at `5fa9c1f^` rather than by reading the commit message.** Same eleven
  variants in the same order, same eleven labels, same `ALL` order, same derives
  (`Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, Serialize, Deserialize`), and a `from_label` body
  that is semantically identical line for line — `to_ascii_uppercase()`, the same eleven arms, the
  same fallback. **No alias was lost and no variant renamed**, so the serde encoding
  (variant names verbatim) that `tests/corpus/pii_cases.json` depends on is unchanged; the corpus
  names seven of the eleven and its tests are green. `from_label` on an empty string, on non-ASCII
  (`to_ascii_uppercase` leaves it untouched) and on mixed case behaves exactly as before. Nothing
  in `src/` serialises or deserialises a `PiiKind` at runtime — the derive is test surface only,
  so the blast radius of a serde change is the corpus, and the corpus is green.
- **No panic path in the new validators on non-ASCII input.** The bare recognizer's `\d{11}` is
  Unicode-aware (only the `\b` is `(?-u:)`), so a run of Arabic-Indic or fullwidth digits *can*
  reach `it_piva_valid` — which filters `is_ascii_digit` and demands exactly eleven, so it returns
  `false` rather than indexing a multi-byte char. Every VAT validator filters to ASCII bytes
  before any arithmetic. `VAT-07` already pins the M4-R13 direction (the tier is not inert in CJK).
- **No prefixed VAT pattern can bite inside an IBAN**: the trailing `(?-u:\b)` fails against the
  IBAN's continuing alphanumeric run, and where two spans did coincide the union is named `Iban`
  (priority 6 over `TaxId`'s 2), so nothing is lost either way.
- Every published rate reproduces exactly, and `CAT-02`'s matrix really is 18 against 17.

### Promoted to `TESTING.md` instead of filed — two variants of families already closed

1. **`CAT-01`'s aggregate floor is stated against a number that is not what it counts — again.**
   The fix for [M11-R7](#m11-r7) added an `Origin` component to the extracted tuple, so
   `declared.len()` counts **(id, file, origin) triples**: it is **102**, while the code comment,
   the failure message and `TESTING.md` all say **96** (the distinct-pairs figure, which is
   correct about the tree). Real slack is 12, not 6. **Third statement of the same shape in one
   milestone** — M11-R4's "20 against 54", M11-R7's "70 against a measured 73", this one — so it
   is recorded rather than chased, with the rule that comes out of it: *a guard's non-vacuity
   number belongs in its **failure message**, computed, never in prose beside the literal.*
   Written next to `CAT-01`.
2. **One of the four "73"s M11-R7 named is still standing** — `tests/test_catalogue.rs:55`,
   `looks_like_guard_id`'s own doc: *"Measured against the whole tree when it was written: 73 ids
   accepted"*. Same family, and a one-line source edit rather than a finding. Two more one-line
   source edits of the same kind, handed over rather than filed: `KIND-01`'s doc comment
   (`src/pii/mod.rs`) says it asserts *"`ALL` agrees with `label` on every entry, and every
   variant is reachable through the public constructor"* — the body checks that no label is empty
   and that priorities are not all equal, neither of which is either claim. `TESTING.md`'s
   `KIND-01` entry describes the body correctly, so the design doc is right and only the source
   comment overstates.

### Handed over, not filed

`docs/SETUP.md` gained the missing companion to `CLAUDE.md`'s "never kill the machine's own
proxy": what to do when that instance holds `target\debug\llm-proxy-pii-rust.exe` and the link
step fails. The rule as written left `taskkill` as the only obvious move, which is the one thing
it forbids; the answer is `--target-dir target/mine`, the same "give the new thing its own path"
move the `-onnx` aliases already make one section below. Docs-only, so it is fixed rather than
filed.

<a id="m11-r10"></a>
### M11-R10 — a lower- or mixed-case IBAN or VAT number is forwarded **in clear**: the structured tier's case axis was never decided — *leak, high*

**Reproduced through the real binary, not read off the regexes.** With the proxy pointed at a
recording mock upstream, these reach the provider **unmasked**, byte for byte:

| rendering | value | what the provider receives |
|---|---|---|
| lowercase VIES prefix | `it00905811006`, `de136695976`, `gb220430231`, `pt524287244`, `nl111222333b01` | raw |
| mixed-case prefix | `It00905811006`, `Gb220430231` | raw |
| uppercase prefix, lowercase internal `B` | `NL111222333b01` | raw |
| lowercase IBAN | `it60x0542811101000000123456`, `de89370400440532013000` | raw |
| **one** lowercase letter in an otherwise uppercase IBAN | `IT60x0542811101000000123456` | raw |
| space-grouped lowercase IBAN | `it60 x054 2811 1010 0000 0123 456` | raw |

Every value above is one this repo already treats as a positive in its own corpus; only the letter
case changes. The uppercase renderings of all of them mask correctly.

**Mechanism.** `src/pii/recognizers.rs:465-521` (the six VAT patterns) and `:238` (IBAN) spell
their letters as uppercase-only character classes — `IT`, `DE`, `GB`, `PT`, `NL…B`, and
`[A-Z]{2}\d{2}[A-Z0-9]{11,30}`. Lowercasing any of them does not merely lose the *prefix*: the
letters are ASCII word characters, so there is no `(?-u:\b)` between them and the digits for the
bare `\d{11}` recognizer (or the always-on 9-digit national-ID recognizer) to fall back on. The
span is not shortened, it **disappears**.

**This is an axis, not an instance, and the tier is inconsistent along it.** Driven in the same
run: the Codice Fiscale (`[A-Za-z]{6}…`), ES DNI/NIE (`[XYZxyz]…[A-Za-z]`) and Chinese resident-id
(`\d{17}[0-9Xx]`) recognizers all fold case and mask either way. Two families do not, and one of
them is from **M1**: this half of the finding predates M11 by ten milestones and has survived
every review this repo has run. `iban_mod97`'s own doc comment says *"letters are folded to
uppercase"* — the **validator** was written for input the **regex** can never deliver, which is
the clearest evidence available that nobody chose this.

**The published reason for the VAT half does not survive the tier's own grammar.**
`recognizers.rs:411-417` and `TESTING.md` → `VAT-05` both argue:

> Lowercase would make `it` a matchable prefix — and `it` is one of the commonest words in English
> prose. `"call it 12345678901"` with a mod-10-valid tail would then produce a **14-character**
> span swallowing an ordinary English word.

The very next section of that same doc comment says there is **no space between prefix and
digits**. `it 12345678901` therefore cannot match under *any* case rule, and the mutation confirms
it: making the `IT` pattern case-insensitive turns exactly **one** assertion red in 149 lib tests —
`kinds("it00905811006").is_empty()`, the one that pins the miss. `kinds("call it 00905811006
back")`, the assertion the rationale is attached to, stays **green**. And the `NL…B…` separator's
case is covered by no argument at all and pinned by no test: changing `B` to `[Bb]` leaves the
suite at **246 / 0 / 5**.

**Measured, because the two halves do not resolve the same way** — 341.1 MB / 16 380 files of
uncurated third-party source, the same stand-in Round 1 used:

- **VAT: folding case costs 0.** All five prefixed schemes fire **0** times uppercase and **0**
  times case-insensitively. Each is checksum-gated besides (NL is fourteen fixed positions with
  three literal anchors), so the marginal false-positive cost is zero on this corpus and bounded
  by a checksum everywhere else.
- **IBAN: folding case costs a lot, and the rule is load-bearing.** The continuous arm goes from
  **2** hits to **933** — hex digests, base64 blobs, `Ed25519PublicKey` — and IBAN has **no hard
  checksum gate** (mod-97 is a confidence signal, M4), so all 931 extra would be masked. Masking a
  hex digest inside a `tool_use.input` is exactly the harm M10 spent nine rounds bounding.
- **But the cheap gate closes it anyway: 0 of the 931 survive mod-97 + the ISO 13616 length.**
  (Only 26 of them even carry a country code the length table knows.)

**Fix — one decision at the recognizer table, not five patches.** The axis is *"for each
letter-bearing pattern, does letter case matter, and what does the answer cost?"*, and it should be
answered once, per pattern, with the number beside it:
1. **VAT (five schemes + the NL `B`): fold case.** Measured cost 0; closes seven of the thirteen
   renderings above. Replace the refuted rationale in `recognizers.rs` and `VAT-05` with the
   measurement, and keep `VAT-05` — but as a guard on the *span boundary* (`"call it <digits>"`
   must still yield the digits alone), which is the real property it protects and the only one its
   first assertion actually tests.
2. **IBAN: fold case, and require a non-canonical-case rendering to be `Verified`** (mod-97 **and**
   `iban_country_length`) rather than `Structural`. Measured residue **0/931**, and the uppercase
   path keeps M4's deliberate "a structurally valid IBAN is masked even when mod-97 fails" rule
   untouched.
3. **`Secret`'s `AKIA…` stays uppercase-only** — that one is a format, not a convention.

Both are **product-visible coverage changes**, so per `CLAUDE.md` the decision is the maintainer's,
not the builder's; the numbers to decide on are above. Doing nothing is a legitimate third option —
but then it has to be *written down as a decided limit with these numbers*, because today the docs
assert a reason that the code refutes.

**Test that would have caught it, and it is one test, not seven.** A **case matrix over the
recognizer table**: for every recognizer whose pattern contains a letter, assert that a known
positive in `to_lowercase()`, in `to_uppercase()` and with one letter case-flipped either **all
mask** or **all miss**, and that the answer for each pattern is the one recorded in a table beside
it. A new letter-bearing recognizer then cannot ship without an answer, which is what
`shipped_tax_recognizer_count` does for the count and what the `pii_kinds!` macro does for the
enum. The instance-shaped version — adding `it00905811006` to a corpus — would close IT and leave
DE, GB, PT, NL and IBAN open, which is the move [M4's retrospective](M4.md#retrospective) is six
rounds of warning against.

**Closed 2026-09-03 — maintainer's decision: option 1, both halves.** Put to the maintainer with
both costs re-measured independently of this round (same 341.1 MB / 16 380 files; distinct values
rather than occurrences, hence 149 where the round counted 933 hits). **The leak was reproduced
first**: `it00905811006`, `IT60x0542811101000000123456` and the rest yield *no detection at all*,
while `RSSMRA85T10A562S`/`rssmra85t10a562s` and `12345678Z`/`12345678z` both mask — the
inconsistency is exactly as reported.

- **VAT: folded.** All five prefixed schemes plus the NL literal `B` now spell their letters as
  explicit ASCII classes (`[Ii][Tt]`, `[Nn][Ll]…[Bb]`) rather than `(?i)`, so no Unicode case
  folding can widen them. Re-measured cost: **0 added matches** for every scheme.
- **IBAN: folded, with `iban_case_gate`.** Canonical uppercase keeps M4's rule untouched
  (structurally valid is masked even when mod-97 fails); a rendering carrying **any** lowercase
  letter must be fully verifiable. **One correction to the round's arithmetic, in the safe
  direction:** `iban_length_ok` returns `true` for an unknown country code, so for 145 of the 149
  the gate is mod-97 *alone* — weaker than the round modelled. Re-measured under that weaker
  reading: still **0 survivors**.
- `confidence_of`'s NL arm folded too, or a lowercase btw-id would have been mis-tagged.

**`VAT-05` was rewritten rather than deleted.** Its rationale was refuted, but the property under
its first assertion is real and independent of the case rule — a VAT number after an English word
must still yield the digits alone. It now guards that, in both cases, plus the leak itself.

**`CASE-01` and `CASE-02` are the chokepoint**, per the finding's own prescription: one matrix over
the recognizer table asking each letter-bearing pattern the same question, including the
single-letter-flipped rendering that a lowercase corpus would miss. `CASE-02` pins both halves of
the IBAN split, since getting either wrong is silent.

**Verified:** 248/0/5, fmt and both clippy legs clean. `CAT-01` caught the two new ids as
uncatalogued before they were written up — the guard working on its own author.

<a id="m11-r11"></a>
### M11-R11 — `CASE-01` is a hand-written list wearing a chokepoint's doc comment: four letter-bearing recognizers have no answer, and NINO can be made uppercase-only with the suite green — *guard, high*

**The guard's own doc comment states the property it does not have.** `src/pii/recognizers.rs:3284`
promises, in two places:

> every letter-bearing recognizer has a recorded answer on the case axis
>
> A new letter-bearing recognizer cannot ship without an entry here, and an entry cannot be added
> without stating the answer — the same move `shipped_tax_recognizer_count` makes for the count and
> `pii_kinds!` for the enum.

Neither move is made. `shipped_tax_recognizer_count` **counts `vat_recognizers()`**, the live set;
`pii_kinds!` **generates** the enum. `MATRIX` is a nine-line `const` that derives nothing from the
recognizer registry, so the set it quantifies over is "the values someone typed", not "the
recognizers this build ships". It is exactly the instance-shaped fix
[M4's retrospective](M4.md#retrospective) warns about, wearing the vocabulary of the chokepoint
that finding asked for — which is how it passed review inside its own closure.

**Derived from the registry rather than counted by eye, this build ships 12 letter-bearing
recognizers.** `MATRIX`'s nine rows name eight of them (IBAN twice). **Four have no answer:**

| recognizer | pattern | case answer it actually has today |
|---|---|---|
| `Secret` | `(?:sk-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{6,}\|AKIA[0-9A-Z]{16})` | **does not fold, deliberately** — `sk-` and `AKIA` are formats |
| `Email` | `[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[A-Za-z0-9.-]+\.[A-Za-z]{2,}` | folds |
| `NationalId` (GB NINO) | `[A-Za-z]{2}\d{6}[A-Da-d]` (+ the spaced arm) | folds |
| `NationalId` (CN resident id) | `\d{17}[0-9Xx]` | folds |

**Reproduced by mutation, not by reading — four mutations, one per unanswered recognizer.** Each
was paired with a temporary probe asserting the case rendering the mutation removes, so a green
result cannot be a mutation that mutated nothing. At HEAD, with the four probes added: **155
passed / 0 failed** — every one of these renderings really is detected today, so each mutation
removes real coverage.

| mutation | probe | `CASE-01` | whole `--lib` suite |
|---|---|---|---|
| NINO → `[A-Z]{2}\d{6}[A-D]` (both arms) | red | **green** | 154 / 1 — the probe is the only red |
| CN resident id → `\d{17}[0-9X]` | red | **green** | 154 / 1 — the probe is the only red |
| `Email` → `[a-z0-9._%+-]+@[a-z0-9.-]+\.[a-z]{2,}` | red | **green** | 151 / 4 — the probe **plus three unrelated tests** |
| `Secret` → `sk-[a-z0-9_-]{6,}` | red | **green** | 154 / 1 — the probe is the only red |

So for **three of the four**, a letter-bearing recognizer can be narrowed to uppercase-only and the
entire library suite stays green: `ab123456c` — a real NINO rendering, and one `nino_prefix_valid`
accepts, since it upper-cases before checking — goes from masked to **forwarded in clear** with
nothing red. That is M11-R10's class, alive inside the fix that closed it. `Email` is caught, but
**not by `CASE-01`**: three tests that happen to carry an uppercase letter in an address
(`a_vat_inside_an_email_keeps_the_email_label`, `email_beats_a_card_iban_or_secret_local_part`,
`grouped_forms_attached_to_a_domain_do_not_leak`) fail incidentally. Incidental coverage is not a
recorded answer, and it moves the moment those fixtures are rewritten.

**Second half — the guard cannot express the answer M11-R10's own decision 3 gave.** The doc
comment closes with:

> **`MUST_MATCH` is not decoration: `Some(kind)` means the lowercase form must be detected *as that
> kind*.**

There is no `MUST_MATCH` and no `Option` in this file: the const is `MATRIX: &[(&str, PiiKind)]`,
and every row asserts *folds*. The paragraph describes a shape that would have carried a
"deliberately does not fold" answer — and that answer is precisely M11-R10's **decision 3**,
*"`Secret`'s `AKIA…` stays uppercase-only — that one is a format, not a convention"*. The decision
is recorded in the review record and is unrepresentable in the matrix that exists to record it,
which is why `Secret` is one of the four missing rows rather than a row saying *no*.

**Fix — the chokepoint, and adding four rows is not it.** Nine hand-written rows becoming thirteen
is the same defect with a different number. `StructuredRecognizers` already holds an enumerable
`Vec<Recognizer>` and every `Recognizer` carries its compiled `Regex`, so the pattern is
interrogable: **derive** the letter-bearing set from the shipped registry, and require every member
to carry a declared answer — one that can also say *does not fold, on purpose*, or `Secret` does not
fit. A new letter-bearing recognizer must then turn the guard red until someone writes its answer
down.

**And it needs two proofs, not one.** The matrix proves the answers are **right**; a synthetic
recognizer injected into the registry proves the guard is **reached**. The second is the one that
gets skipped — and skipping it is how a nine-row const came to be described as a chokepoint.

**Closed 2026-09-04 — the set is derived, and the answer table only says what the answer is.**

`StructuredRecognizers::shipped_patterns()` exposes every recognizer the scan is actually built
from as `(kind, pattern)`; `pattern_can_match_a_letter` decides which of them carry letters by
parsing the pattern to the same HIR `regex` compiles and asking each literal and character class
whether it covers `A-Za-z`. **A textual scan cannot answer this** — a word boundary and a digit
class are both spelled with letters that match none, so every pattern in the file "contains a
letter" and none of that is the question. The parse costs a test-only `regex-syntax`
dev-dependency, which is the crate `regex` itself already pulls: no new crate, no build script, no
native code, and `tests/dependency_footprint.rs` asserts the **default** tree, which
dev-dependencies do not enter.

`audit_case_axis(shipped, answers)` is the pure decision, and it reports **three** disagreements,
not two:

- `unanswered` — a letter-bearing recognizer this build ships that no answer covers;
- `stale` — an answer naming a `(kind, pattern)` no longer shipped, so a deleted recognizer cannot
  leave its answer behind asserting a closed question;
- `not_letter_bearing` — **the field that stops the guard passing vacuously.** An empty
  `unanswered` list is exactly what a derivation answering *no* to everything would also produce.
  Requiring every recorded answer to be about a pattern the derivation *does* call letter-bearing
  turns the two into an equality — the letter-bearing recognizers this build ships are precisely
  the ones the answers name — **with no count for anyone to keep current**. That is deliberately
  not the shape `CAT-01`'s floor had (M11-R4 / M11-R7): a number somebody has to re-measure is a
  number that goes stale, and this one derives itself.

**`CaseRule` is an enum because decision 3 is an answer.** `Folds` asserts the lowercase, uppercase
and one-letter-flipped renderings are all detected as the same kind. `Fixed { variant, why }`
asserts the canonical rendering *is* detected and a **named** case rendering of the same characters
is **not** — with `variant.eq_ignore_ascii_case(positive)` checked, so the row cannot cheat by
naming an unrelated undetectable string. That is what makes M11-R10's decision 3 — `Secret`'s
`sk-` and `AKIA` are formats, not conventions — expressible in the matrix that exists to record it.
`Secret`'s positive is `sk-AbCdEf123456`, mixed-case **on purpose**: the prefix is case-fixed but
the key body is opaque, and an all-lowercase positive would have left the body unpinned — which was
one of the four green mutations below.

**The answer table holds each pattern verbatim rather than sharing a constant with the recognizer.**
That is the forcing function: editing a letter-bearing pattern invalidates its recorded answer and
the guard demands the answer be re-decided, printing the new pattern in the failure. A shared
constant would let the key follow the edit silently and leave only the behavioural assertion.

**Answers now recorded: 14 rows over 12 letter-bearing recognizers** (IBAN and `Secret` carry two
each — two countries, two formats). The four M11-R11 named — `Secret`, `Email`, the GB NINO, the CN
resident id — are among them, and the count is not asserted anywhere: the equality above is.

### Mutations — the four that were green, and the two the new shape adds

Every one was applied to the **product**, `cargo test --lib` run in full, and reverted from a
pristine copy. Before the fix all four of the first group left `CASE-01` green (the finding above).

| # | mutation | `CASE-01` before | `CASE-01` after | suite after |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | NINO -> `[A-Z]{2}\d{6}[A-D]`, product only | green | **red** | 150 / 2 |
| A2 | ...and the answer table updated to match, so the key still binds | — | **red**, naming `ab123456c` | 151 / 1 |
| B | CN resident id -> `[0-9X]`, product only | green | **red** | 150 / 2 |
| C | `Email` -> `[a-z0-9._%+-]+@...` | green (3 unrelated tests caught it) | **red** | 147 / 5 |
| D | `Secret` -> `sk-[a-z0-9_-]{6,}` | green | **red** | 150 / 2 |
| E | **a real letter-bearing recognizer injected into `universal_recognizers()`** | — | **red**, printing `NationalId :: (?-u:\b)QQ\d{7}[A-Za-z](?-u:\b)` | 150 / 2 |
| F | `pattern_can_match_a_letter` forced to `false` | — | **red** via `not_letter_bearing` | 150 / 2 |

**E is the proof that matters and the one that gets skipped.** It is not a synthetic list handed to
the pure function — it is a `Recognizer` added to `universal_recognizers()`, built by
`StructuredRecognizers::new()` like every other, and the guard names it and refuses to pass until
someone writes its answer down. That is the property `CASE-01`'s doc comment claimed for a full
review round without having it. Removed after being seen red.

**A2 matters for a different reason.** An author who narrows a pattern *and* updates the table's
copy defeats the key check — and `CASE-01` is still red, on the behaviour, with the message naming
`ab123456c`. The key and the assertion cover each other; neither alone is enough.

**Verified:** **249 / 0 / 5** over 24 test binaries, re-run twice with identical counts (round 3
closed at 248/0/5; the +1 is `CASE-03`). `cargo fmt --check` clean, `cargo clippy --all-targets -D
warnings` clean. `CAT-01` caught `CASE-03` as uncatalogued before it was written up — the guard
working on its own author for the second round running.

**Promoted out of the review archive**, per `CLAUDE.md`: `TESTING.md` -> `CASE-01` now carries the
derivation, both decided limits (the ASCII-only axis measured at **0 patterns** of residue, and the
non-vacuity equality) and `CASE-03`, because a lesson left only here is one the next builder
re-learns.

<a id="m11-r12"></a>
### M11-R12 — the M11 review record was truncated to a single finding: `M11-R0`…`M11-R9` are gone and all ten ledger links are dead — *docs, high*

`docs/reviews/M11.md` is **120 lines and contains one `<a id=…>` anchor**, `m11-r10`. The ledger in
[`ROADMAP.md`](../ROADMAP.md) carries **eleven** rows, ten of which link into anchors that no longer
exist. The file also lost its title, so it now opens mid-record with a bare `<a>` tag.

**Mechanism — measured over the file's history, not inferred:**

| commit | lines | anchors present |
|---|---|---|
| `17ec2e5` round 2 filed | 983 | `r0`…`r9` |
| `382a5c4` round 2 closed | 1046 | `r0`…`r9` |
| `c8b8aef` round 3 filed | 1330 | `r0`…`r10` |
| `33eb159` **round 3's fix** | **120** | `r10` |

`33eb159` — the commit that closed the case-axis leak — rewrote the file whole instead of appending
R10's closure note to it, dropping 1210 lines and ten finding records. Its message says nothing
about the record, so the loss is silent: nothing in the working tree indicates the ledger's links
are broken, and `cargo test` cannot see it.

**Why it matters beyond tidiness.** `CLAUDE.md` makes the record *the* home of a finding for its
whole life — the ledger deliberately carries only id · title · severity · box, so the repro,
mechanism, `file:line`, fix and test for R0–R9 existed **only** here. Among them are the two
closures Round 2 found had not held (`M11-R6`, `M11-R7`): the evidence for what was re-opened and
why is exactly what was deleted.

**Fix.** Restore R0–R9 from `c8b8aef` and keep HEAD's R10 (which carries the closure note the
truncating commit added), so the file is the union rather than either side.

**Closed 2026-09-04 — restored as the union of the two sides, not by picking one.** `R0`…`R9` come
from `c8b8aef` verbatim (its bytes up to the `m11-r10` anchor, title and round-0 preamble included);
`R10` is kept as HEAD had it, because HEAD's copy is `c8b8aef`'s entry **plus** the closure note
`33eb159` appended. Verified by diffing the two renderings of `R10` against each other: the only
difference is the 30 closure lines, so the splice loses nothing from either side. The file is back
to **13 anchors** (`r0`…`r12`) and every ledger link resolves.

**What outlives this finding is a rule, and it is written where it gets read** — `CLAUDE.md`
already says a finding is *"never copied, and never moved"* and that closing one is *"flip the box
in the ledger + append the closure note to its entry"*. The operative word is **append**: this file
is 1400+ lines, and a fix commit that regenerates it rather than appending to it destroys the
record silently, because no test and no lint can see it. `33eb159` was otherwise a good commit — it
closed a real leak — which is exactly why the loss went unnoticed for a full round.

<a id="round-4"></a>
## Round 4 — the fix that relocated the leak (2026-09-04)

Round 3 found the case axis and closed it; round 4 was told to look for a **chokepoint still open**
or something in a **different class**, and to prefer an empty round to another finding on the net.

**It is not empty, and the sharpest finding is in the product: the M11-R10 fix introduced a leak of
its own.** `iban_case_gate` gave the IBAN recognizer a validator that can *reject* — the first one it
has ever had — and the recognizer kept `shrink_on_reject: false`. The IBAN pattern's grouped arm has
always over-reached into a following short token; while `validate` was `None` that was an
**over-mask** (the guard `IBAN-04` was written about exactly that), and it is now a **dropped span**.
Driven through the real `.exe`, an ordinary English sentence sends a real Spanish IBAN's country
code, check digits and final group to the provider **in clear**:

```text
client sends : Please wire the deposit to ES91 2100 0418 4502 0005 1332 for the invoice we discussed.
provider gets: Please wire the deposit to ES91 2100 [PHONE_1] 1332 for the invoice we discussed.
```

[M11-R13](#m11-r13). It is a **regression**, proved rather than argued: with `iban_case_gate`
neutralised — the pre-M11-R10 posture — all 40 cases of the matrix mask and none leak.

Four further findings, all downstream of the same fix not reaching everything that states its rule:
the refuted rationale still standing in three places ([M11-R14](#m11-r14)), the reachability helper
that still describes the old grammar ([M11-R15](#m11-r15)), the one half of the fix nothing pins
([M11-R16](#m11-r16)), and the two files outside this archive where the decision never arrived
([M11-R17](#m11-r17)).

### What was verified, with the expected value written before the observed one

| Quantity | Expected (written first) | Observed | Reps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cargo test` | 249 / 0 / 5 over 24 binaries, 0 warnings — round 3 closed at 248, +1 for `CASE-03` | **249 / 0 / 5, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 2 (start of round, and after every mutation was restored) |
| `cargo test-onnx` | 285 / 0 / 22 — round 3's 282 plus the three new `CASE-*` guards | **285 / 0 / 22, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 1 |
| `cargo fmt --check` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` | clean | **clean, exit 0** | 1 |
| `CASE_ANSWERS` rows / distinct patterns | 14 over 12, per the M11-R11 closure | **14 rows, 12 distinct patterns** | 1 (counted from source) |
| M11-R10's 13 leaking renderings, through the real `.exe` | all masked | **all 13 masked**, round-trip byte-exact | 1 |
| IBAN case-fold cost on uncurated third-party source | ~0 survivors of the mod-97 + length gate | **0 of 1 050 added matches**, 435.6 MB / 23 077 files | 1 (deterministic) |
| VAT case-fold cost, same corpus | 0 added matches per scheme | **0 for all five** (IT-VIES · DE · GB · PT · NL) | 1 (deterministic) |

> **A `cargo test` run from the Bash tool reported five link failures that were not test results at
> all**, and it is worth recording because it is the *red* version of the trap this brief warns
> about. Git Bash puts `/usr/bin/link.exe` — the MSYS coreutils `link` — ahead of the MSVC linker on
> `PATH`, so every mutation "failed" identically and told me nothing. Every measurement in this round
> was re-run from PowerShell. **A mutation harness that cannot distinguish "the guard went red" from
> "the build never happened" is not a harness.**
>
> The machine's own long-lived proxy holds `target/debug/llm-proxy-pii-rust.exe`, so nothing was
> killed and nothing was rebuilt in `target/debug`: the whole round ran in
> `--target-dir target/review`.

### The product, driven through the real binary

A recording mock upstream plus the real `.exe`, so what is quoted is what the provider would have
received.

- **All thirteen M11-R10 renderings mask.** `it00905811006`, `It00905811006`, `de136695976`,
  `gb220430231`, `pt524287244`, `nl111222333b01`, `NL111222333b01`, `it60x…123456`,
  `de89370400440532013000`, `IT60x…123456`, the space-grouped lowercase IBAN, `ab 12 34 56 c` and
  the rest — every one masked, and the client got the original bytes back unchanged.
- **The two `Fixed` answers behave as decided.** `SK-AbCdEf123456` and `akiaiosfodnn7example` reach
  the provider in clear, which is M11-R10's decision 3 working, not a defect.
- **The `Structural` audit line is emitted for the lowercase Dutch btw-id.** Two 11-proef failures
  in, two `structure-only PII match (checksum unverified) kind=TaxId` lines out, kinds only — and
  **no raw value anywhere in the log** for any of the four renderings.
- **The leak.** Five countries × two cases × four trailing contexts, plus five sentences a person
  would actually type. See [M11-R13](#m11-r13).

### The independent corpus measurement

435.6 MB / 23 077 files of uncurated third-party Rust source (`~/.cargo/registry/src`), a different
corpus and a different implementation from round 3's. It reproduces both halves of the M11-R10
closure's arithmetic:

| | uppercase pattern | case-folded | added by the fold | survive `iban_case_gate` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| IBAN (both arms) | 133 hits | 1 183 hits | 1 050 (223 distinct) | **0** |
| IT-VIES · DE · GB · PT · NL VAT | 0 each | 0 each | **0 each** | — |

So the fold's **false-positive** cost is confirmed at zero on a corpus 28% larger than the one the
closure used. What a false-positive measurement cannot see — and did not — is the **false-negative**
cost, which is [M11-R13](#m11-r13): the gate does not only refuse hex digests, it also refuses the
over-long match that used to carry a real IBAN's bytes.

### Mutations run in round 4

Every one applied to the **product**, `cargo test --lib` run in full from PowerShell, restored from a
pristine byte copy in a `finally`, sha256 re-checked against the `HEAD` blob afterwards. Two of the
five (G, M) are renderings the round-3 mutation set did not cover.

| # | mutation | result | suite |
|---|---|---|---|
| G | IBAN pattern back to `[A-Z]{2}\d{2}[A-Z0-9]{11,30}…` — **product only**, the answer table's copy left alone | **red** — `CASE-01`, `CASE-02` *and* `CASE-03` | 149 / 3 |
| H | `[Ii][Tt]` back to `IT` — product only | **red** — `VAT-05`, `CASE-01`, `CASE-03` | 149 / 3 |
| M | `[Ii][Tt]` to `(?:IT|it)`: accepts all-upper and all-lower, **rejects the flipped `It`** | **red** — `CASE-01`, `CASE-03` | 150 / 2 |
| I | `iban_case_gate` always accepts | **red** — `CASE-02` | 151 / 1 |
| L | `confidence_of`'s NL arm stops folding case | **GREEN** | **152 / 0** → [M11-R16](#m11-r16) |

**M is the one worth repeating.** `CASE-01`'s doc calls the one-letter-flipped rendering "the
sharpest case and the one a corpus of lowercase strings would miss"; M is the mutation that leaves
lowercase and uppercase both working and breaks only the flip. It goes red. That arm is
load-bearing, not decoration.

**G matters because the closure's own set did not cover it.** M11-R11's mutations exercised
`Secret`, `Email`, the NINO and the CN id — the four recognizers that finding named — and never the
two families M11-R10 was actually about. Both go red, and `CASE-03` fires alongside `CASE-01` in each
case, so the `stale`/`unanswered` equality is doing work rather than riding along.

### What this round could not break

- The 13 renderings M11-R10 listed: all masked, round-trip byte-exact, no raw value in any log line.
- The fold's false-positive cost on 435.6 MB of third-party source: 0 for IBAN after the gate, 0 for
  every VAT scheme before it.
- `pattern_can_match_a_letter`'s HIR walk is exhaustive over `regex_syntax`'s eight `HirKind`
  variants; there is no arm through which a letter-bearing pattern can answer *no*.
- `shipped_patterns()` reads `self.recognizers`, and `StructuredRecognizers::new()` is the
  **maximal** build — `with_locales` can only narrow the phone tier, whose patterns carry no letters
  — so a locale-gated letter-bearing recognizer cannot hide from `CASE-01` today. Recorded as an
  observation, not filed: it is a property of the tiering, and it stops holding the day an FP-prone
  recognizer with letters ships.
- The streaming hold-back is kind-agnostic — `split_demaskable` matches placeholder *shape*, not a
  list of kinds — so `[TAXID_1]` needed no change there and none was missed.
- The fixed-length VAT and national-ID patterns cannot over-reach the way the IBAN grouped arm does:
  every one is anchored `(?-u:\b)…(?-u:\b)` around a fixed digit count, so M11-R13's mechanism does
  not reach them. The card recognizer's `\d{13,19}` arm is variable-length with a validator and
  `shrink_on_reject: false`, but its over-long form has no interior word boundary to restart from and
  is covered by the deliberate *"an ID never fires inside a longer token"* rule — pre-M11 and out of
  scope here, stated so the audit's boundary is on the record.

<a id="m11-r13"></a>
### M11-R13 — the case gate turned a benign over-match into a dropped span: a real IBAN reaches the provider **in clear** — *leak, high*

**Reproduced through the real `.exe` against a recording mock upstream, in a sentence nobody had to
construct:**

```text
client sends : Please wire the deposit to ES91 2100 0418 4502 0005 1332 for the invoice we discussed.
provider gets: Please wire the deposit to ES91 2100 [PHONE_1] 1332 for the invoice we discussed.
```

Twelve of the IBAN's twenty-four characters — the country code, both **check digits** and the final
group — are forwarded verbatim, and the middle is announced to the model under the wrong kind. The
Italian equivalent behaves identically (`Il mio IBAN e' ES91 2100 [PHONE_1] 1332 e il conto…`).

**Mechanism — three lines of `src/pii/recognizers.rs` that were never all true at once before M11.**

1. `:238` — the grouped arm is `(?: [A-Za-z0-9]{4}){2,7}(?: [A-Za-z0-9]{1,4})?`, greedy. When an
   IBAN's grouped rendering **ends on a full four-character group** the optional `{1,4}` tail is
   still free, so the match runs on into the next token: `ES91 2100 0418 4502 0005 1332 for`.
2. `:242` — `validate: Some(free(iban_case_gate))`, **added by M11-R10**. The over-long span contains
   a lowercase byte (here from the swallowed word `for`; in a lowercase rendering, from the IBAN
   itself), so the gate demands mod-97 **and** the ISO 13616 length. The over-long string fails both.
   **Rejected.**
3. `:244` — `shrink_on_reject: false`, so `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` returns `None` on the spot. And
   `Scan::Overlapping` resumes one `char` past the match's *start*, where there is no ASCII word
   boundary at which `[A-Za-z]{2}\d{2}` can begin again — the next viable start is past the IBAN.

So **no IBAN candidate is produced at all.** What reaches the resolver is whatever another recognizer
happened to grab of the digits — the ES `Groups` phone family here, Luhn elsewhere — and everything
outside it goes upstream raw. This is M10-R1's shape exactly, on the recognizer that never received
M10-R1's fix: *the fixpoint recovers a value it did not touch; it can never recover one the mask ate.*

**It is a regression, and the proof is a build rather than an argument.** With `iban_case_gate`
replaced by `true` — precisely the pre-M11-R10 posture, `validate: None` — the same binary, the same
matrix:

| build | leaking rows / 40 |
|---|---|
| HEAD | **6** |
| `iban_case_gate` neutralised (pre-M11-R10 behaviour) | **0** |

Before the fix the over-long match was *accepted*, so the union covered the IBAN and merely swallowed
the next word: an over-mask, which is the direction this project errs in on purpose. **The fix turned
the same over-match into a leak.**

**The matrix, five countries × two cases × four trailing contexts, through the binary.**

| IBAN | length | + lowercase word | + `2026` | + a 6-letter word | alone |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `ES91 2100 0418 4502 0005 1332` **uppercase** | 24 | **LEAK** | ok (span all-uppercase → gate passes → over-mask) | ok | ok |
| same, lowercase | 24 | **LEAK** | **LEAK** | ok | ok |
| `BE68 5390 0754 7034` uppercase | 16 | **LEAK** | ok | ok | ok |
| same, lowercase | 16 | **LEAK** | **LEAK** | ok | ok |
| `PL61 1090 1014 0000 0712 1981 2874` | 28 | partial — `PL61` in clear, the rest taken by `[CARD_1]` | ok | ok | ok |
| `AT61 1904 3002 3457 3201` | 20 | partial — `AT61` in clear (`Konto AT61 [CARD_1] bei der Bank`) | — | — | ok |
| `DE89 3704 0044 0532 0130 00` | 22 | ok | ok | ok | ok |
| `IT60 X054 2811 1010 0000 0123 456` | 27 | ok | ok | ok | ok |

**The predicate, stated so the exposure is a number and not an anecdote.** The span is lost when all
three hold:

- the IBAN's **compact length is a multiple of 4**, so its canonical grouping leaves the optional
  `{1,4}` tail unused — **14 of the 36 countries `iban_country_length` knows** (AD · AT · BE · CY ·
  CZ · EE · ES · HU · LT · LU · PL · RO · SE · SK), plus any country outside the table whose length
  is a multiple of 4, where `iban_length_ok` returns `true` and the gate is mod-97 alone;
- the **next token is 1–4 alphanumeric characters** — an English or Italian short word, a 4-digit
  year, a currency code, the next column of a CSV;
- the resulting span holds **at least one lowercase ASCII letter** — supplied by the swallowed word
  even when the IBAN itself is canonical uppercase, which is why this is not confined to the
  rendering M11-R10 added.

DE, IT, FR, GB, NL, PT and the rest of the ≢ 0 (mod 4) countries are immune, because their canonical
grouping already spends the optional tail on a 2- or 3-character last group.

**Why every guard missed it — and it is M4's lesson 2 landing on the guard itself.** `IBAN-04`
(`iban_does_not_absorb_a_following_word`) is the guard named for this exact behaviour, and its two
fixtures are **`IT60X…` (27) and `DE89 3704 … 00` (22)** — the two countries that *cannot*
over-reach. `CASE-02` exercises the IBAN alone, never followed by anything. `PROP-03` quantifies over
**accepted** candidates and these bytes belong to a **rejected** one; `masking_leaves_nothing_detectable`
(PROP-04) passes because the orphan `ES91 2100 … 1332` is not detectable — word for word the note
M10-R1 left behind about the orphaned `912`, in the one place its prescription was never applied. The
repo's whole IBAN corpus is DE / IT / FR / LV / NL / ZZ: **not one vulnerable length is in it.**

**Fix — the chokepoint is the rejection, not the arm.** Two things are true and both need saying:

1. **`shrink_on_reject: true` for the IBAN recognizer.** This is M10-R1's fix, and it applies here
   for M10-R1's reason: IBAN is now an un-anchored family with a rejecting validator. Cutting at the
   last separator inside the span turns `ES91 … 1332 for` into `ES91 … 1332`, which verifies. It
   closes the grouped arm, where every reachable instance above lives.
2. **It does not close the continuous arm**, and that residue must be measured rather than assumed:
   `es9121000418450200051332abcd` has no separator to cut at, and it leaks today (verified through
   the binary). That is an evasion-shaped input rather than a natural one, so it may well be an
   accepted limit — but it is a *decision*, and the general rule underneath it is the one to write
   down: **a validator added to a recognizer that previously accepted everything must not be able to
   reduce coverage.** Whatever `iban_case_gate` refuses, the bytes it refuses must still reach the
   resolver in some form, or the gate is a coverage switch wearing a precision argument.

**Test that would have caught it, and it is not another example.** The predicate M10-R1 arrived at —
***no byte of a real value survives masking***, not *nothing detectable survives* — has a guard for
the phone tier (`PHONE-NAT-09`) and none for IBAN. A property test over the IBAN corpus × a trailing
token of 1–5 characters × {lowercase, uppercase, one-letter-flipped}, asserting that no 4+-character
run of the original IBAN appears in the masked output, fails on the first case here and on the
continuous arm too. The instance-shaped version — adding an ES IBAN to
`iban_does_not_absorb_a_following_word` — closes ES and leaves thirteen countries and the whole
continuous arm open.

**Closed 2026-09-04 — `shrink_on_reject: true` on the IBAN recognizer, and the guard is the
predicate rather than another example.**

**Reproduced first, then the regression proved by build rather than argued.** Three runs of the same
probe, each a real detection through `StructuredRecognizers::new()`:

| build | `…to ES91 2100 0418 4502 0005 1332 for the invoice.` |
|---|---|
| HEAD | `[(Phone, "0418 4502 0005")]` — country code, both check digits and the last group **in clear** |
| `iban_case_gate` neutralised (its `if` forced true) | `[(Iban, "ES91 … 1332 for")]` — the benign over-match, an over-mask |
| the **exact** pre-M11-R10 recognizer (uppercase-only pattern, `validate: None`) | `[(Iban, "ES91 … 1332")]` |

The third row is the one that settles what kind of defect this is. Pre-M11-R10 the pattern could not
match a lowercase byte **at all**, so the greedy `{1,4}` tail could never swallow an English word:
the span was exactly the IBAN. Folding case made the over-reach possible and the gate then deleted
the result. **A regression, introduced by M11-R10, in the direction this project never errs in.**

**The fix.** `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` already existed for precisely this shape (M10-R1) and IBAN
was excluded from it by a doc comment about *checksum* recognizers. IBAN is not one: `iban_case_gate`
**accepts unconditionally** unless the span carries a lowercase byte, so a rejection only ever
happens on a rendering the pattern could not match before M11-R10 — and every shortened prefix it
then tries either carries no lowercase (accepted structurally, exactly what shipped for ten
milestones) or must pass mod-97 **and** the ISO 13616 length. **The shrink therefore cannot admit
anything the pre-M11-R10 build did not already admit.** That argument is now written on the
`shrink_on_reject` field itself, replacing the sentence that excluded IBAN.

Result, same probe: `[(Iban, "ES91 2100 0418 4502 0005 1332")]` — and note it is **better than
pre-M11-R10**, which masked the IBAN but also swallowed nothing extra only because it could not; the
shrink cuts the span to the value itself in both letter cases.

**The guard — `IBAN-05`, `no_window_of_an_iban_survives_masking`.** M10-R1's predicate (*no byte of
the value survives*, not *nothing detectable survives*), which the phone tier has had as
`PHONE-NAT-09` and IBAN never got. The corpus is **derived, not collected**: all 676 two-letter codes
are probed against `iban_country_length`, every country it knows is synthesised into a structurally
valid IBAN with **real** check digits (solved for, then re-checked with `iban_mod97`), and each is
driven in both renderings × three letter cases × six trailing contexts × two carrier sentences. It
asserts a non-vacuity floor in the dimension the defect lives in — at least ten countries whose
length is divisible by 4, of which there are **14** — because a corpus of immune countries is exactly
how `IBAN-04` stayed green through this.

**Mutations.**

| mutation | outcome |
|---|---|
| `shrink_on_reject: true` -> `false` (the fix removed) | **red**, and on a *worse* case than the one reported: with the carrier `" and confirm."` the AD IBAN is masked **not at all** — `AD49 1852 9630 7418 5296 3074` reaches the provider whole |
| `iban_case_gate`'s `if` forced true | red (`CASE-02`) |
| IBAN pattern back to uppercase-only | red (`CASE-01`, `CASE-02`, `CASE-03`) |

**Decided limit, measured not assumed, and it is not a regression.** A lowercase IBAN **glued** to
1-4 alphanumerics (`es9121000418450200051332abcd`) still yields no candidate: the continuous arm
carries no separator for the shrink to cut at. Verified against the pre-M11-R10 build in the table
above — it produced **no candidate for that string either**, its uppercase-only pattern being unable
to match it at all. So this is a residue of the M11-R10 fix's reach, not damage it did. Closing it
would need a **length-derived** cut rather than a separator-derived one, whose false-positive cost is
unmeasured and whose shape is IBAN-specific logic inside a generic mechanism. Recorded in
`docs/TESTING.md` -> `IBAN-05` and raised to the maintainer rather than decided here, per `CLAUDE.md`
— it is product-visible coverage.

**Promoted out of this archive.** `ARCHITECTURE.md` now carries the rule as an invariant beside the
ASCII-word-boundary one: ***a validator that rejects must not be able to delete a value*** —
whatever a newly added validator refuses, those bytes must still reach the resolver in some form, or
the gate is a coverage switch wearing a precision argument.

**Verified:** **250 / 0 / 5** over 24 binaries (round 4 measured 249; the +1 is `IBAN-05`), re-run
twice with identical counts; `cargo test-onnx` **286 / 0 / 22** (round 4: 285). `fmt` and `clippy
--all-targets -D warnings` clean.

---

<a id="m11-r14"></a>
### M11-R14 — the refuted rationale is still standing in three places, two of them still marking M11-R10 **open** — *docs, medium*

M11-R10's own prescription was *"replace the refuted rationale in `recognizers.rs` and `VAT-05` with
the measurement"*. The code changed; three statements of the old rule did not, and they sit in the
two files `CLAUDE.md` calls the design plus the source comment beside the patterns:

| site | what it still says | what ships |
|---|---|---|
| `docs/TESTING.md:227-244` | *"**The IBAN pattern is uppercase-only, and nothing here says so or checks it (M11-R10, open).** … Recorded here rather than fixed, because it changes product-visible coverage."* | folded since `33eb159`, and `CASE-02` — twenty lines further down the same file — checks it |
| `docs/TESTING.md:332-354` | *"**VAT-05 …: the country prefixes are uppercase-only.** The recognizers match `IT`/`DE`/`GB`/`PT`/`NL` in uppercase only, so a lower- or mixed-case rendering matches **nothing at all**"*, with the finding marked *(M11-R10, open)* and left as "the maintainer's call" | `[Ii][Tt]`, `[Dd][Ee]`, `[Gg][Bb]`, `[Pp][Tt]`, `[Nn][Ll]…[Bb]`; the call was made and the ledger box is `[x]` |
| `src/pii/recognizers.rs:411-417` | a whole doc section headed *"## The country prefix must be UPPERCASE, and that is an anti-false-positive decision"*, ending *"Pinned by `lowercase_country_prefix_is_not_a_vat_number`"* | that test exists nowhere in the tree — it was rewritten as `a_vat_span_never_swallows_the_word_before_it` |

**Why this is worse than untidiness.** These three texts are not stale trivia; they are the
**argument for reverting the fix**, written in the voice of a decision, sitting where the next person
to touch the patterns will read them. Two of them state the leak as current behaviour and the finding
as open, so a reader reconciling `TESTING.md` against itself finds the file asserting both that a
lowercase IBAN matches nothing and that `CASE-02` proves it is masked.

**`CAT-01` cannot see it, by design and correctly.** Its module doc scopes itself: *"It proves each
declared id is named in `TESTING.md` — not that its description is accurate … **Accuracy is review's
job.**"* `VAT-05` is still named, so the guard is green while its entry describes a deleted test and
the opposite behaviour. Recorded here rather than proposed as a new guard — pinning a catalogue
entry's prose is a formatting rule pretending to be a correctness one, and *a guard on a guard is
worth one level, not four*.

**Fix.** Rewrite all three against `33eb159`'s measurements: `TESTING.md`'s IBAN note becomes the
`iban_case_gate` split (or a cross-reference to `CASE-02`), `VAT-05`'s entry describes the guard that
now exists, and `recognizers.rs:411-417` states the measured cost — **0 added matches on 435.6 MB**,
reproduced this round — in place of the `"call it 12345678901"` argument the tier's own no-space rule
refutes.


**Closed 2026-09-04.** All three sites now say what ships. `docs/TESTING.md`'s IBAN block describes
the `iban_case_gate` split and states the operator-visible asymmetry outright — *a lowercase IBAN
whose mod-97 fails is not masked while its uppercase twin is* — and points at `IBAN-05` for what the
gate cost on the way in. The `VAT-05` entry now names the test that exists
(`a_vat_span_never_swallows_the_word_before_it`), says plainly that the entry used to describe a
different, deleted test whose rule was refuted, and keeps the measurement rather than the argument.
`recognizers.rs`'s doc section is retitled *"the country prefix folds case, and the argument for
uppercase-only was refuted"*, quotes the old rationale so the next reader recognises it, and states
why the tier's own grammar defeats it. Neither file says "M11-R10, open" anywhere any more.
---

<a id="m11-r15"></a>
### M11-R15 — `vat_grammar_could_match` still states the grammar as `[A-Z]{2}`, and its own doc names that as the thing that would make it wrong — *guard, low-medium*

`src/pii/recognizers.rs:1594`:

```rust
bytes[..2].iter().all(u8::is_ascii_uppercase)
    && bytes[2..].iter().all(u8::is_ascii_alphanumeric)
```

and, at `:1569`, the doc it is supposed to be a statement of:

> every prefixed pattern is `[A-Z]{2}` immediately followed by an unbroken run of ASCII
> alphanumerics […] It lives here, beside the patterns, rather than in the test module, because it is
> a statement about the grammar: **a pattern that stops being `[A-Z]{2}`-prefixed makes this function
> wrong, and this is where somebody making that change will be looking.**

The patterns stopped being `[A-Z]{2}`-prefixed in `33eb159`. The function did not move, and neither
did the person making the change. **The condition the doc comment names as its own invalidation
happened, in the commit that made it happen.**

This is M11-R1's helper: it exists so that `VAT-04`'s *absence* assertions are checked for
**reachability** before they are believed. It now **under-approximates** the tier's grammar, so it
answers *unreachable* for a shape the recognizers really could match. The matrix at `:3223` encodes
that as a fact —

```rust
"es12345678z",  // lowercase prefix: the patterns are uppercase-only (VAT-05)
```

— asserting `!vat_grammar_could_match("es12345678z")` with a stated reason that is no longer true.
Nothing is red today, because every live negative in the loop is uppercase; the cost is latent and it
points the wrong way. An author adding a lowercase ES negative once ES ships would be told their
negative is unreachable, "fix" it by uppercasing, and the lowercase absence would go untested — which
is the exact failure M11-R1 was filed about, arriving through the helper written to prevent it.

**Fix.** `bytes[..2].iter().all(u8::is_ascii_alphabetic)`, and rewrite the doc sentence and the
`es12345678z` comment to say what actually excludes that string (there is no ES recognizer at all —
which is `VAT-04`'s whole subject). The row itself belongs in the *reachable* list, since it now is.


**Closed 2026-09-04.** `vat_grammar_could_match` reads `is_ascii_alphabetic` on the prefix bytes,
and its doc states the grammar as *two ASCII letters, in either case* — plus a paragraph naming what
happened, because the sentence that was supposed to catch this change was already there and did not.
The matrix moved `es12345678z` into the **reachable** list, where it now belongs, alongside a new
mixed-case row `Es12345678Z`; the comment that read *"the patterns are uppercase-only (VAT-05)"* is
gone, replaced by the true reason a Spanish VAT number is absent — **no ES recognizer ships at all**,
which is `VAT-04`'s entire subject. A `1S12345678Z` row went into the unreachable list so the
weakened prefix rule still has a floor: two ASCII **letters** remains the rule, and a digit there
must stay unreachable.
---

<a id="m11-r16"></a>
### M11-R16 — `confidence_of`'s NL case fold is a named half of the M11-R10 fix that nothing pins — *guard, low-medium*

The M11-R10 closure note lists it explicitly: *"`confidence_of`'s NL arm folded too, **or a lowercase
btw-id would have been mis-tagged**."* Nothing checks it.

**Mutation, product-side, suite run in full and restored from a pristine byte copy:**

```diff
  src/pii/recognizers.rs:1309
- PiiKind::TaxId if text.len() == 14 && text[..2].eq_ignore_ascii_case("NL") =>
+ PiiKind::TaxId if text.len() == 14 && &text[..2] == "NL" =>
```

`cargo test --lib`: **152 passed / 0 failed** — completely green, where the four other product
mutations this round each turned at least one guard red.

**And the consequence is visible in the real binary, which is how it was confirmed rather than
argued.** Same mutation, rebuilt, driven end to end with `RUST_LOG=debug` over the pair
`btw NL123456789B01` / `btw nl123456789b01` (both fail the 11-proef):

| build | `structure-only PII match (checksum unverified)` lines |
|---|---|
| HEAD | **2** — one per rendering |
| NL arm un-folded | **1** — the lowercase one is silently promoted to `Verified` |

No leak: the value is masked either way, because the NL recognizer accepts on format. What is lost is
the honesty `VAT-11` exists to protect — the audit log stops reporting a btw-id whose arithmetic did
not confirm it, and the `Confidence` a downstream consumer reads becomes a claim the scheme cannot
support.

**Why the guard missed it.** `VAT-11` (`nl_vat_confidence_splits_verified_from_format_only`) drives
`"btw NL111222333B01 en NL123456789B01"` — uppercase only. `CASE-01`'s NL row asserts the *kind* and
the *span* across renderings, never the `Confidence`.

**Fix.** `VAT-11` should run its pair in both renderings — the fixture is one `to_lowercase()` away —
or `CaseRule::Folds` should assert `Confidence` alongside kind and span, which closes it for every
recognizer that has a confidence split rather than for NL alone. The second is the chokepoint; NL is
the only current instance, which is precisely the argument for doing it once.


**Closed 2026-09-04, at the chokepoint rather than on the fixture.** `CaseRule::Folds` now asserts
the full grade — kind, span **and `Confidence`** — via a `graded()` helper beside `kinds()`, so any
recognizer with a confidence split is covered rather than NL alone.

**And the first attempt at that was green, which is the part worth recording.** With the answer
table's only NL positive being `NL111222333B01`, whose 11-proef **passes**, the mutation stayed
green: on the `Verified` side both branches of `confidence_of` return the same thing, so a fold that
had stopped working was invisible. A second NL row carrying `NL123456789B01` — the same pattern, the
**`Structural`** side of the split — makes the mutation red, naming the rendering:

    nl123456789b01 must be graded exactly as NL123456789B01 is — same kind, same span,
    same `Confidence` (M11-R16)

The rule that generalises, and it is written into the answer table beside the two rows: **an answer
must carry a positive from each branch of any confidence split, or the fold is only half asserted.**
That is not enforceable by a further guard without a third level of guard-on-guard, so it is a stated
rule at the point of use, deliberately.

<a id="m11-r17"></a>
### M11-R17 — the case-axis decision reached the code and this archive, and neither of the two files a reader outside the review loop would open — *docs, medium*

`CLAUDE.md`: *"**Promote what outlives the review.** If a finding taught the codebase a rule — an
invariant, a guard, a tradeoff we now accept on purpose — write it into `ARCHITECTURE.md` or
`TESTING.md`, **next to the thing it governs**. […] A lesson left only in the archive is a lesson the
next builder will re-learn the hard way."* And, for a product-visible change, `CHANGELOG.md` is what
the release page publishes.

**`ARCHITECTURE.md` does not mention letter case anywhere.** Measured:
`grep -in "case-fold\|letter case\|uppercase\|lowercase\|case axis\|iban_case_gate\|CASE-01"` over
`ARCHITECTURE.md`, `README.md` and `README.it.md` returns **zero lines**. Yet the file carries, as a
numbered invariant of the detection layer:

> **Word boundaries are ASCII — `(?-u:\b)`, never a bare `\b` (M4-R13).**

M11-R10 is that paragraph's sibling in every respect — a property of how the patterns are *spelled*,
undecided for ten milestones, discovered as a total detection failure for a whole rendering class —
and the rule it produced (*every letter-bearing recognizer carries a decided answer; IBAN folds only
under a verification gate; `sk-`/`AKIA` are formats, not conventions*) lives only in `TESTING.md`
beside the guard and in this file. `TESTING.md` is the right home for **how it is checked**;
`ARCHITECTURE.md` is the missing home for **what the rule is**.

**`CHANGELOG.md`'s `## [Unreleased]` section says nothing about it either.** It describes the VAT
tier and the thread-base change in detail, and the case fold — a closed **leak** and a
product-visible coverage change — appears in neither `### Added`, `### Changed` nor a `### Fixed`
section (there is none). `v1.2.1`'s entry set the precedent in the words this needs: *"**A partial
leak.** A real domestic number could be left partly in clear…"*. The file's own preamble says the
`### Changed` section *"is where a change that can surprise you is named, and it is the part to read
before upgrading"*, and the release guard matches the **heading** only, so nothing mechanical can
notice the omission.

**Fix.** Two edits, one each: a paragraph in `ARCHITECTURE.md` beside the ASCII-word-boundary rule,
and a `### Fixed` entry in `CHANGELOG.md`'s `[Unreleased]` naming the closed leak, the renderings now
covered, and the uppercase/lowercase asymmetry `iban_case_gate` introduces — which an operator
reading the IBAN row needs, because a lowercase IBAN that fails mod-97 is *not* masked while its
uppercase twin is. **Both should wait on [M11-R13](#m11-r13)**, since its resolution changes what the
rule says.

**Closed 2026-09-04, and waiting on M11-R13 was the right call — its resolution changed what the
rule says.** `ARCHITECTURE.md` gains two paragraphs beside the ASCII-word-boundary invariant, which
is exactly the sibling this round argued it was:

- ***Every letter-bearing recognizer carries a decided answer on letter case (M11-R10)*** — the
  three-part rule (fold a convention; keep a format, `sk-`/`AKIA` being the instance; IBAN folds only
  under a verification gate), each with its measured cost, and the operator-visible asymmetry stated.
- ***A validator that rejects must not be able to delete a value (M11-R13)*** — the corollary this
  round paid for, which would not have been written a day earlier. Whatever a newly added validator
  refuses, those bytes must still reach the resolver in some form, or the gate is a coverage switch
  wearing a precision argument; mechanically `shrink_on_reject`, proved by M10-R1's predicate.

`CHANGELOG.md`'s `[Unreleased]` gains a `### Fixed` section with both entries in the register
`v1.2.1` set — the closed leak with the renderings now covered and the IBAN asymmetry an operator
needs before upgrading, and M11-R13 named as *found and fixed inside this release, so no shipped
version carries it*, with the fourteen affected country flags listed.
