<a id="round-5"></a>
## Round 5 — the cost of the fix, and a published zero that is a one (2026-09-04)

Round 4 found that the M11-R10 fix had *relocated* a leak, and closed it with
`shrink_on_reject: true`. Round 5 asked the two questions that closure invites and that the M4
retrospective says are the ones that get skipped: **does the fix hold on the axis its own guard
holds constant**, and **what does it now cost on the worst input an attacker can send** (M4
retrospective, lesson 5 — *a safety fix has a cost, and the cost is part of the fix*).

**The fix holds, and holds well.** On a 5 280-case matrix that varies the axis `IBAN-05` does not —
the token *before* the value — every separated rendering is masked: **0 leaks in 3 360 separated
cases**, and HEAD is strictly better than either build behind it (1 600 leaking at HEAD against
2 599 without the M11-R13 fix and 3 840 pre-M11-R10, and all 1 600 of HEAD's are the glued residue
already recorded). The M11-R13 repro masks correctly through a rebuilt library, and HEAD's leaking
set is a strict subset of the pre-M11 one.

**What it cost was never measured, and it is large.** The IBAN validator is declared `free` — a
wrapper whose doc comment states its premise as *"a checksum over at most 18 bytes"* — and
`shrink_on_reject: true` now runs it **up to eight times per rejected match**, on spans up to 44
characters, each call allocating inside `iban_mod97`. Driven through the **real release `.exe`**
against a mock upstream, one legal 14 640 KiB request costs **8.2–10.3 s** where the byte-identical
body uppercased costs **2.1–3.1 s** in the same session. `ARCHITECTURE.md` publishes *"across every
shape measured, a request costs at most about 3 s"*. [M11-R18](#m11-r18).

**And the number that justified the gate does not reproduce.** `iban_case_gate`'s residue is
published as **0** in five places. Measured on 304.9 MB of uncurated third-party Rust source it is
**1 of 936** — a matrix-multiply kernel's register list, `ab22 ab23 ab44 ab45 ab66 ab67`, masked as
`[IBAN_1]`. [M11-R19](#m11-r19). Separately, the residue M11-R13 escalated to the maintainer is
written as affecting a *lowercase* IBAN; measured, a **canonical uppercase** one glued to a
lowercase token has the identical fate. [M11-R20](#m11-r20).

### What was verified, with the expected value written before the observed one

| Quantity | Expected (written first) | Observed | Reps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cargo test` | 250 / 0 / 5 over 24 binaries, 0 warnings — the M11-R13 closure's count | **250 / 0 / 5, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 2 (start of round; after every mutation was restored) |
| `cargo test-onnx` | 286 / 0 / 22 over 24 binaries | **286 / 0 / 22, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 1 |
| `cargo fmt --check` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| M11-R13's sentence through a rebuilt library | one `Iban` span over the whole IBAN | **`[(Iban, "ES91 2100 0418 4502 0005 1332")]`** | 3 builds |
| leading-context matrix, separated trailings | 0 leaks, if the fix is a fix | **0 of 3 360** | 1 (deterministic) |
| IBAN spans on 304.9 MB of third-party source, gate **open** | ~900, matching round 3's +931 | **938 (936 lowercase-bearing)** | 1 (deterministic) |
| the same at HEAD | **0** lowercase-bearing, per the published residue | **1** — and it is a real over-mask | 1 (deterministic) |
| 14 640 KiB legal request, real `.exe`, uppercase control | under the published ~3 s | **2.41 / 2.07 / 2.08 s** and **3.13 / 2.59 / 2.59 s** | 2 sessions x 3 |
| the same body lowercased | the same, if the published cost model holds | **9.39 / 8.19 / 10.34 s** and **8.55 / 9.23 / 9.11 s** | 2 sessions x 3 |

> **The absolute is the machine; the ratio is the code — this project's own M7-R9/R12 lesson,
> applied on purpose.** The two end-to-end sessions disagree on the absolute (a first pair of runs
> gave 4.9–5.4 s for the lowercase body), so every number above was taken **paired with an in-run
> uppercase control on the byte-identical payload**. The ratio held at **3.2x and 4.2x** across the
> two sessions while the absolute moved by a factor of two. The ratio is what
> [M11-R18](#m11-r18) quotes.

> **A restore that does not rebuild is a mutation still running, and it caught me once.** After
> restoring `recognizers.rs` from a byte copy, `cargo` did **not** rebuild the library — the
> restored file's fingerprint matched a cached one — and the next four measurements were the
> *mutated* build wearing HEAD's name. It was visible only because the repro string still leaked
> when it should not have. Every mutation and restore in this round therefore stamps
> `LastWriteTime` and is confirmed by re-running the repro before any number is read. **The
> counterpart of "check that the mutation actually mutated" is "check that the restore actually
> restored".** It belongs beside the four traps in
> [`mutation-harness-greens-in-disguise`](M10.md), because the failure mode is the same one wearing
> the other colour: a red that is really a green, and a green that is really a red.
>
> The machine's own long-lived proxy holds `target/debug/`, so nothing was killed and nothing was
> built there. The library measurements ran from a scratch crate with a path dependency — so
> `src/` and `tests/` were never edited to *take* a measurement, only to run a mutation — the suite
> in `--target-dir target/review`, and the end-to-end runs against a release build in
> `target/review/release`, on OS-assigned free ports, killed only by the PID the run started.

### The product, driven through the real binary

A mock upstream plus the real release `.exe`.

- **A 14 640 KiB body of four-character alphanumeric groups is masked and answered `200`** — no
  refusal, and no budget involved: the IBAN validator is `free`, so
  `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST` never sees it. What varies is only how long it takes, which is
  [M11-R18](#m11-r18).
- **The uppercase control answers in a third of the time on the byte-identical payload**, which is
  what attributes the cost to the case-fold path rather than to body size.

### Mutations run in round 5

Every one applied to `src/pii/recognizers.rs`, written as bytes from a pristine copy, restored in a
`finally`, sha256 re-checked against the `HEAD` blob (`970556fb…`; file `F9FC1149…`) afterwards, and
**confirmed by re-running the repro** — see the note above about the restore that did not rebuild.
The mutation script refuses to write when the substitution changes nothing, so an empty mutation
cannot be mistaken for a green.

| # | mutation | result |
|---|---|---|
| A | `shrink_on_reject: true` -> `false` on the IBAN recognizer (the M11-R13 fix removed) | **red** — the repro returns `[(Phone, "0418 4502 0005")]`, the reported leak exactly, and the leading matrix goes 1 600 -> 2 599 |
| B | the **exact** pre-M11-R10 recognizer (uppercase-only pattern, `validate: None`) | leading matrix **3 840** / 5 280 — worse than both, which is what makes HEAD's residue a residue and not a regression |
| C | `iban_case_gate`'s verification arm forced `true` (the gate open) | repro returns the benign over-match `[(Iban, "ES91 … 1332 for")]`; the corpus goes 3 -> 938 spans, which is the denominator [M11-R19](#m11-r19) needed |

Mutation C is the one worth repeating: it is the only way to obtain the *denominator* the published
residue is a fraction of, and running it is what turned "the residue looks wrong" into "the residue
is 1 of 936".

### What this round could not break

- **The M11-R13 fix, on every separated rendering.** 10 countries x 2 renderings x 3 letter cases x
  8 leading tokens x 7 separated trailing contexts = 3 360 cases, **0 leaks**, under M10-R1's
  predicate (no eight-character window of the value survives masking). The leading tokens include
  six that can themselves start an IBAN match (`ab12`, `AB12`, `Ab12`, `ref9`, `x1`, `R2D2`) — the
  shape `IBAN-05`'s two carriers cannot produce, since both end in a word no recognizer can start
  on.
- **`IBAN-05`'s predicate is stronger than the one I started with.** My first harness skipped
  windows containing a space, and with it the M11-R13 leak itself is **invisible**: `ES91 2100
  [PHONE_1] 1332` leaves no eight-character space-free run of the value. `IBAN-05` takes its windows
  over the rendering *including* spaces, so it sees it. Recorded because the weaker predicate is the
  natural one to write, and it would have reported this round's mutation A as green.
- **No other recognizer can repeat M11-R13's mechanism today.** Checked mechanically rather than
  taken on trust: of the recognizers carrying a validator that can reject, every VAT and
  national-ID pattern is a fixed digit count anchored `(?-u:\b)…(?-u:\b)` at both ends, and the
  card's variable `\d{13,19}` arm cannot over-reach a *following* token because a longer digit run
  has no interior word boundary to end on. **IBAN is the only variable-length pattern with a
  rejecting validator**, and it now shrinks.
- **The Unicode-`\d` axis.** `\d` is Unicode-aware in `regex` (M4-R13 turned off Unicode for `\b`,
  not for `\d`), so a candidate can carry Arabic-Indic digits. Every validator that byte-indexes
  checks its length first and rejects non-ASCII — `cf_check_valid` via `even_val`,
  `zh_resident_id_valid` via `char::to_digit`, `de_steuerid_valid` via `is_ascii_digit` — and
  `iban_mod97` returns `false` on any non-ASCII character. Worst case is an over-mask; never a
  panic, never a miss.
- The suite on both feature sets, `fmt`, and `clippy --all-targets -D warnings`.

### Where the chokepoint is, said rather than filed

M11-R13's promoted invariant — *a validator that rejects must not be able to delete a value* — now
lives in `ARCHITECTURE.md`, and **nothing enforces it**. `shrink_on_reject` is a plain `bool` set
independently at each construction site; a future variable-length recognizer with a rejecting
validator can set it `false` with the whole suite green, which is precisely how M11-R13 shipped. The
case axis has `CASE_ANSWERS` — a registry that makes *not deciding* a test failure — and this axis
has no equivalent.

This is deliberately **not filed as a finding**. `CLAUDE.md`'s rule is that a guard on a guard is
worth one level and that another variant of the same family should be recorded rather than chased,
and M11's ratio is already lopsided: **13 findings on the guards and the docs against 5 on the
product**. It is recorded here so that the next builder adding a recognizer with a validator has the
question in front of them, and so that leaving it unenforced is a decision rather than an oversight.

---

<a id="m11-r18"></a>
### M11-R18 — the M11-R13 fix multiplies an **unbudgeted** scan cost: a legal 14.6 MiB request now costs 8–10 s against a published ceiling of ~3 s — *hardening / availability, medium*

**Availability is a privacy property here**, in this repo's own words: *a proxy too slow to keep
switched on protects nothing.* This is not a leak and not a super-linear blow-up — the path stays
linear — but it is a constant factor of **3–4x** on an unauthenticated path, landing above the
number `ARCHITECTURE.md` publishes as the ceiling.

**Reproduced through the real release `.exe`** against a recording mock upstream: two sessions,
three reps each, every treatment paired with an in-run control on the byte-identical payload.

| body (15 360 059 bytes of JSON, one `messages[0].content`) | session 1 | session 2 |
|---|---|---|
| four-character groups, **UPPERCASE** — the gate short-circuits, no shrink runs | 2.41 / 2.07 / 2.08 s | 3.13 / 2.59 / 2.59 s |
| the same body **lowercased** — fold, gate, shrink | **9.39 / 8.19 / 10.34 s** | **8.55 / 9.23 / 9.11 s** |

Both answer `200`. The ratio is **3.2x–4.2x**; the absolute moved between sessions and the ratio did
not, so the ratio is the claim (M7-R9's rule).

**Attribution, at the library level — three builds x three reps on a 4 MiB field**, with an
uppercase control that is flat across all three, which is what proves the difference is the
case-fold path rather than the measurement:

| build | lowercase groups | UPPERCASE groups (control) |
|---|---|---|
| the exact pre-M11-R10 recognizer | **145 / 147 / 159 ms** (0 spans) | 414 / 424 / 447 ms |
| M11-R10 as it landed (`shrink_on_reject: false`) | **422 / 475 / 432 ms** | 474 / 425 / 411 ms |
| **HEAD** (with the M11-R13 fix) | **1 421 / 2 306 / 2 643 ms** | 662 / 641 / 489 ms |

So M11 took this shape from ~150 ms to ~440 ms (the fold plus the gate) and then to 1.4–2.6 s (the
shrink): **~10-17x pre-M11**, of which the M11-R13 fix is ~3-6x. At 16 MiB a single `try_detect`
measures **5 663 / 5 380 / 6 010 ms** at HEAD against **2 139 / 2 502 / 2 510 ms** without the
shrink.

**Mechanism — three lines of `src/pii/recognizers.rs` that only became true together in `5844a09`.**

1. `:253` — `shrink_on_reject: true`, the M11-R13 fix. A rejected IBAN match is now retried once per
   interior separator: up to **seven** extra validator calls for the grouped arm's widest form, on
   top of the one that rejected it.
2. `:1087-1108` — `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` calls `check(prefix, budget)` for each attempt. The
   per-scan memo keys on the matched bytes, so on a body of **distinct** groups every one is a miss —
   the exact axis DOS-06 exists for, on a recognizer DOS-06 does not exercise.
3. `:67` — `free()` wraps `iban_case_gate`, so **none of it is charged to the request budget**. Its
   doc comment states the premise the exemption rests on: *"A validator whose cost is negligible — a
   checksum over at most **18 bytes**"*. An IBAN span is up to **44** characters and `iban_mod97`
   allocates a `String` per call, so after the fix one rejected match can cost eight allocations and
   ~350 character-operations where the premise assumed one and ~44.

`MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST` cannot bound this — it is spent by the phone validator alone,
and `iban_case_gate` is `free`. The guarantee `ARCHITECTURE.md` derives from the budget does not
cover this term at all.

**Why no guard saw it — M4's lesson 6 on the DoS guards, again.** `tests/complexity.rs` varies field
size (DOS-01…03), entity count (DOS-04), alphabet (DOS-05), periodicity (DOS-06), field count
(DOS-07) and fixpoint charging (DOS-08/09) — and **every one of them holds the alphabet at digits,
`sk-` runs, or `a@b.co`**. Not one produces a body of mixed alphanumeric groups, which is the only
shape that makes the IBAN pattern match at every position *and* reject at every position. DOS-05 is
explicitly *"the third axis: what the bytes are"*, and its bytes are digit groups. `within_budget`
allows ~30 s in the debug profile, so this shape would have to get several times worse again before
an existing guard noticed.

**The claim this falsifies**, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`, under the budget table: *"Two terms make up a
request's CPU: `units x ~3 µs`, which the allowance caps at ~1.5 s, **plus** regex scanning and the
mask rewrite, linear in body size and entity count … Across every shape measured, a request costs at
most about **3 s**."* There is a **third term** — unbudgeted `free()` validator work, which is per
*candidate* rather than per byte — and the M11-R13 fix made it the largest of the three for a legal
body. The sentence was true when it was written: the pre-M11 build answers this shape in ~0.6 s.

**Fix — the choice is the maintainer's, because every option is product-visible.** Stated with what
each costs:

1. **Charge `iban_case_gate` to the budget** (drop it out of `free`; spend one unit per `iban_mod97`
   call). The bound then exists and is the one the architecture already describes. Cost: a
   pathological IBAN-shaped body starts being **refused** rather than slow — a new refusal on
   legal-looking traffic, which is precisely what M10 chose `500 000` over `50 000` to avoid, so the
   rate has to be measured against an ordinary payload before the number is picked.
2. **Bound the shrink rather than the validator** — cap the retries at, say, two separators instead
   of seven. Cheap, and invisible to correctness *if* every real rendering needs at most two cuts
   (the reported case needs one). That is measurable and must be measured: a cap derived from what
   the validator accepts is sound, one derived from what someone expects is M10-R13 again.
3. **Make the shrink not pay for what it already knows.** `iban_case_gate` returns `true` without
   arithmetic when the span carries no lowercase byte; the expensive path is only the lowercase one.
   Checking the ISO 13616 length before computing mod-97 is derived from the **validator** rather
   than from metadata, so it does not repeat M10-R13. Lowest product risk, smallest win.
4. **Accept it and republish the ceiling.** Legitimate — nothing here is unsafe and the shape is
   adversarial — but then the two-term model and the ~3 s figure must be rewritten in
   `ARCHITECTURE.md`, because as published they are now false.

I would take **3, then 1**: 3 carries no product risk and may close most of the gap, and 1 is the
only option that makes the architecture's own bound true. 4 alone leaves a privacy tool's
availability guarantee resting on a number that does not hold.

**Test that would have caught it.** `tests/complexity.rs` has no case whose alphabet is
**alphanumeric groups**, and that is the whole gap. A `DOS-10` built like DOS-06 — distinct,
non-repeating four-character `[a-z]{2}[0-9]{2}` groups, so the memo is inert — asserts that
`try_detect` on that shape is linear *and affordable*, and it fails on the commit that introduced
the fix. The stronger form asserts the **ratio** against an uppercase body of identical size, so it
measures the case-fold path rather than the machine — the same construction `PERF-M7` already uses
for its calibration leg.

**Closed 2026-09-06 — option 1: the gate is charged (`28f0b9e`).**

`iban_case_gate` leaves `free()` and spends a unit, **on its arithmetic branch only**. The
short-circuit for a span with no lowercase byte does no arithmetic and pays nothing, so an uppercase
body of identical bytes still spends **zero** — a charge that fired on both would be a charge on
candidate count, and it would refuse legal uppercase bodies for work nobody did.

`free` gained a named counterpart, **`metered`**, and between them there is no third way to build a
`Validator`. Charging, like not charging, is now a word a reader and `grep` can both find rather than
an anonymous `Box::new` at four call sites, so a tenth recognizer cannot join the cheap tier by
omission — it has to pick.

**What it costs, measured rather than asserted.** The shapes that come near the allowance are the
dense ones, and they are the adversarial ones: 4 MiB of distinct lowercase `[a-z]{2}[0-9]{2}` groups
spends **708 648** units and is refused; 4 MiB of lowercase hex (`abcd 1234 …`) spends ~38 000, an
ordinary JSON export ~4 500, base64 and source code **zero**, and the M7 22 KiB turn zero. At
1.3-1.8 µs a unit the charge over-prices that work by about 2-3× against the nominal, which for a
fail-closed bound is the safe direction. `CHANGELOG.md` names the new refusal under `### Changed`.

**`DOS-10` is the guard, and it asserts that the work is *charged*, not that it is fast** — a
wall-clock bar here would be a claim about one box, while what must never come back is the term being
invisible to the budget, which is a property of the code. Three assertions plus a non-vacuity floor;
verified by mutation: put the gate back inside `free()` and the lowercase body's spend drops to **0**,
red with the finding's own message.

*The exemption's own doc comment was the tell, and it stayed true for exactly as long as its premise
did:* **"a checksum over at most 18 bytes"** was accurate while a validator ran once per match, and
false from the commit that made a rejected span retry at every interior separator. **A cost exemption
is an assertion about a call rate, and nothing was watching the call rate.**

<a id="m11-r19"></a>
### M11-R19 — `iban_case_gate`'s residue is published as **0** in five places; measured it is **1 of 936**, and the survivor is a real over-mask — *precision / docs, low*

**The claim.** `iban_case_gate` exists because folding IBAN's case sweeps in hex digests and base64
blobs, and the gate's justification everywhere is that it lets **none** of them through:

- `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` — *"Residue: **0 of the 931**."*
- `docs/TESTING.md`, the CASE-02 note — *"Measured residue: **0 of the 931**."*
- `docs/TESTING.md`, the CASE-02 catalogue entry — *"**Measured residue: 0 of the 149 added.**"*
- `src/pii/recognizers.rs`, `iban_case_gate`'s doc — *"Measured residue of that gate on the same
  corpus: **0 of the 149**."*
- `src/pii/recognizers.rs`, CASE-02's doc — *"Measured residue: **0 of the 149 added**."*
- and the operator-facing form in `CHANGELOG.md` — *"a lowercase IBAN-shaped string that verifies as
  nothing is not [masked]"*.

Round 4 re-measured independently on a larger corpus and published **0 of 1 050**.

**Measured on 304.9 MB / 13 998 `.rs` files of uncurated third-party source
(`~/.cargo/registry/src`), through the shipped recognizer rather than a re-implementation of it:**

| build | IBAN spans | lowercase-bearing |
|---|---|---|
| `iban_case_gate`'s verification arm forced `true` (mutation C — the denominator) | 938 | **936** |
| **HEAD** | 3 | **1** |

So the residue is **1 of 936**, not 0. The survivor:

```text
ab22 ab23 ab44 ab45 ab66 ab67
    in  matrixmultiply-0.3.10/src/sgemm_kernel.rs
```

Six SIMD accumulator register names, masked as `[IBAN_1]`. It walks through the hole the source
comment beside the claim **already names**: `iban_length_ok` returns `true` for a country code it
does not know, so for a two-letter prefix like `ab` the gate is **mod-97 alone**, which one
arbitrary span in 97 passes. The comment concludes *"the zero survives that weaker reading, which is
the one the code actually implements"*. It does not.

**Why this is worth a row rather than a shrug.** It is the exact functional harm the gate was
introduced to prevent — *masking a hex digest inside a `tool_use.input`* — and the instance is a
matrix kernel's register list inside a source file, which is ordinary agent traffic. The rate is low
(≈1 per 300 MB) and the direction is safe (over-mask, restored byte-identically), so the finding is
not the behaviour. **It is that a number used to justify a design decision in five places does not
reproduce, and that the one place which reasoned about how it could fail concluded that it could
not.**

**Fix.** Two parts, and the second is the one that lasts.

1. Re-measure and republish it as a **rate with its corpus** rather than as a zero — *"1 of 936
   added matches over 304.9 MB"*, with the survivor quoted — in all five places, plus the CHANGELOG
   sentence, which currently promises an operator strictly more than the code delivers.
2. **State the mechanism as a bound rather than as an observation.** A span whose country code is
   not in `iban_country_length` is gated by mod-97 alone, so the *expected* residue is
   `added_matches / 97` — 936/97 ≈ 9.6, of which 1 was observed. **A measurement whose expected
   value is ~10 should never have been published as 0 without asking why**, and computing that
   expectation is what turns the figure from a corpus artefact into a property of the gate.

**Test that would have caught it.** `CASE-02` asserts the gate on hand-written strings, so it can
only ever confirm the cases somebody thought of. The invariant is a property test over generated
`[a-z]{2}[0-9]{2}(?: [a-z0-9]{4}){2,7}` spans: *a span the gate accepts whose country code is
unknown must pass mod-97*. The residue **rate** then belongs to the same corpus harness that already
produces the +931 figure — run in both directions, rather than only in the one that produced the
pleasing number.

**Closed 2026-09-04 (`831f916`).** The residue is published as **1 of 936 over 304.9 MB** in all
five places that carried `0` — `iban_case_gate`'s doc, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `TESTING.md`, and the
`CHANGELOG.md` bullet that had promised operators a zero. The mechanism is named with it, because a
rate without its cause invites the next reader to round it back down: `iban_length_ok` answers `true`
for a country code it does not know, so a span prefixed with an unrecognised pair is gated by mod-97
alone and about one in 97 passes. *A measured residue is a fact about the corpus; the mechanism is
what makes it a bound.*

<a id="m11-r20"></a>
### M11-R20 — the residue escalated to the maintainer is scoped to *lowercase*; measured, a canonical **uppercase** IBAN glued to a lowercase token has the identical fate — *docs, low (an open decision)*

**The statement**, in `docs/TESTING.md` -> `IBAN-05`, in this file's [M11-R13](#m11-r13) entry, and
in `5844a09`'s commit message, all in the same words:

> A **lowercase** IBAN glued to 1-4 alphanumerics (`es9121000418450200051332abcd`) still yields no
> candidate … That is **not a regression**: verified against a build of the exact pre-M11-R10
> recognizer, which produced no candidate for it either.

The *not a regression* half is correct, and I reproduced it: pre-M11-R10 leaks **3 840** of the same
5 280-case matrix against HEAD's **1 600**. **The scope is not.** The same fate befalls a
**canonical all-uppercase** IBAN glued to a lowercase 1-4-character token — a rendering M11-R10 did
not introduce and nobody has to contrive:

```text
in    : Please wire to ES6518529630741852963074for now.
masked: Please wire to ES6518529630741852963074for now.      (nothing masked at all)
```

At HEAD **1 600 of 5 280** matrix cases leak, and **every one is a glued trailing token**: broken
down, each of the four glued trailings contributes exactly 400, spread evenly across all three
letter cases — uppercase included. The mechanism differs by one step and the outcome is identical:
pre-M11 the pattern could not match `…3074for` at all; at HEAD it matches, and `iban_case_gate`
deletes it for the lowercase byte the glued word contributed.

**Why it matters, given that nothing changed.** This residue is the one item M11 deliberately **did
not decide** and handed to the maintainer as product-visible coverage. The framing offered to them —
*"an evasion-shaped input rather than a natural one"* — rests on the value being written in
lowercase, which nobody does by accident. *"An ordinary uppercase IBAN followed immediately by a
word"* is a different question, and it is the one actually on the table.

**Fix.** Restate the limit in `docs/TESTING.md` -> `IBAN-05` and in [M11-R13](#m11-r13)'s closure
note as: **any** IBAN — canonical uppercase included — **glued** to a 1-4-character token containing
a lowercase letter yields no candidate, because the continuous arm has no separator for the shrink
to cut at. Keep the *not a regression* conclusion, which is measured and correct, and give it the
number it now has: 3 840 -> 1 600 of 5 280.

**Test that would have caught it.** `IBAN-05`'s `TRAILING` list is `["", " for", " EUR", " x",
" abcd", " alone"]` — **every entry begins with a space**. Adding the glued forms turns it red, which
is why they are not there. The honest shape is a second assertion that pins the residue *as* the
residue — *a glued trailing token yields no candidate, in every letter case* — so that the day it
stops being true the guard says so, instead of staying quietly green. A limit nobody asserts is
indistinguishable from a limit nobody noticed.

**NOT closed — escalated to the maintainer 2026-09-04, which is what `CLAUDE.md` requires of it.**
All four options are product-visible: a new refusal on legal traffic (1), a retry cap that changes
what is detected (2), or republishing an availability ceiling (4). Those are not the builder's.

**What was done meanwhile, because it depends on no answer.** `iban_mod97` and `iban_length_ok` are
now **allocation-free** — the first used to build a compacted `String` and then a `format!`ed
rearrangement, two allocations per call, a fair price while the validator ran once per match and not
after `shrink_on_reject` made it run up to eight times. The arithmetic and every verdict are
unchanged.

Measured on this round's own shape — 4 MiB of distinct lowercase `[a-z]{2}[0-9]{2}` groups, **median
of 9**, with the uppercase control that proves the difference is the case-fold path and not the
machine:

| build | lowercase median | min | UPPERCASE control (median) | spans |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| HEAD, allocating | 2 573 ms | 2 012 | 694 ms | 56 729 |
| allocation-free | **2 108 ms** | **1 624** | 671 ms | 56 729 |

**~18% off, and the span counts are byte-identical** (56 729 and 1), which is the differential proof
that no verdict moved — the rule M10-R13 left behind about optimisations in front of validators.
**It does not close this finding**: the lowercase path is still ~3x its own uppercase control, and
term 3 is still unbudgeted. Option 3 as the round framed it — checking the length before mod-97 —
was measured and **rejected**: `iban_length_ok` answers `true` for a country code it does not know,
so on exactly the adversarial shape (random two-letter prefixes, ~95% unknown) it rejects nothing
earlier and buys nothing.

**One number this round did not have, measured while attributing the cost.** The shrink's price is
not only time. On the same 4 MiB body it takes the masked-span count from **7 943 to 56 729** —
**7.1x more over-masking** on an adversarial shape. Every one is an over-mask, restored
byte-identically, so the direction is safe; it is stated because option 2 (capping the retries)
would buy that back as well as the time, which changes the balance between the options.

**Re-priced again by M11-R51 (2026-09-05).** Making the separator a bounded *run* — the fix for a
leak — widened term 1 by a measured **+22% per row** on `DOS-BUD`'s verdict grid: the valid phone
column goes 8 -> 9.8 units/row and the rejected id column 12 -> 13.8, so the allowance is reached
about 18% sooner. Wall-clock per row is unchanged. Whichever option is taken here, the number it is
taken against is now this one.

**`ARCHITECTURE.md` has been corrected regardless of the decision**, because the sentence it
published was false: the CPU model now names **three** terms, states the measured 8–10 s and the
2.1 s/671 ms library pair, says plainly that "at most about 3 s" no longer holds, and points here
for the open decision. Leaving a knowingly false availability guarantee standing is M11-R14's defect,
and correcting a number is not choosing an option.

**Closed 2026-09-04 — republished as a rate with its corpus, and as a *bound* rather than an
observation.** All five places plus the CHANGELOG sentence now read **1 of 936 added matches over
304.9 MB**, with the survivor named (`ab22 ab23 ab44 ab45 ab66 ab67`, a matrix kernel's SIMD
register list). More important than the number: each one now states **why zero was never the right
answer** — `iban_length_ok` answers `true` for a country code the table does not know, so such a
span is gated by mod-97 alone and one in 97 passes, making the expected residue `added / 97` ≈ 9.6.
The bound is that rate.

The CHANGELOG sentence promised an operator strictly more than the code delivers (*"a lowercase
IBAN-shaped string that verifies as nothing is not [masked]"*); it now says *almost never*, gives
the measured rate, and says the value is masked rather than leaked and restored byte-identically.
`iban_case_gate`'s own doc — the one place that reasoned about this hole and concluded the zero
survived it — now says the opposite in the same sentence, so the reasoning and the number cannot
drift apart again. The neighbouring claim *"the rule is split by rendering, and it costs nothing"*
was corrected too: it costs very little, not nothing.

`docs/DEVLOG.md`'s 2026-09-03 entry still carries the 0. That is a running log of what was believed
on the day and is not rewritten; the correction is in this round's entry, which is how a log carries
one.

**Closed 2026-09-04.** The limit is restated in `docs/TESTING.md` -> `IBAN-05` without the
lowercase scoping: an IBAN **glued** to 1-4 alphanumerics yields no candidate whether the IBAN is
`es9121000418450200051332abcd` or a canonical uppercase `ES9121000418450200051332abcd` — one
lowercase byte anywhere in the glued token is enough to make the gate demand verification of the
over-long span, whatever case the IBAN itself is written in. The *not a regression* half is
unchanged and still verified against the pre-M11-R10 build, which matched neither string.

What goes to the maintainer alongside [M11-R18](#m11-r18) is therefore the wider statement, and the
same entry now carries the number that was missing from the trade: capping or removing the shrink
would buy back **7.1x** of over-masking on an adversarial body as well as the time.

<a id="round-6"></a>
## Round 6 — the axis under the alphabet: a Unicode digit, and a prefix nobody verifies (2026-09-04)

Round 5 closed on numbers. Round 6 went after the **alphabet** the whole milestone has held constant.
Every corpus M11 has measured — the case matrix, the leading matrix, the 4 MiB adversarial bodies, the
304.9 MB of third-party source, the glued residue — is **ASCII**. Rust `regex`'s `\d` is not:
[M4-R13](M4.md#m4-r13) turned Unicode off for `\b` and left it **on** for `\d`, so an IBAN candidate
can carry an Arabic-Indic, Thai, fullwidth, Osmanya or mathematical-bold decimal digit. Round 5 looked
at that axis and concluded *"worst case is an over-mask; never a panic, never a miss"*.

**It was a panic, and it has been one in every release this project has ever cut.**
`iban_mod97` sliced its compacted string at **byte** index 4 (`&compact[..4]` / `&compact[4..]`) while
the first four *characters* can be multi-byte — so `AB𝟎𝟏ABCDEFGHIJK`, 17 characters inside a legal
`messages[0].content`, killed the masking task. Driven through the real **v1.2.1 `.exe`**: **HTTP 500,
nothing forwarded, `panicked at src\pii\recognizers.rs:1423:46`**. It is **fixed at HEAD** — as an
undocumented side effect of `831f916`'s allocation-free rewrite, which describes itself as *"same
arithmetic, same verdicts"*. [M11-R21](#m11-r21).

The round's second half asked what `shrink_on_reject: true` **admits**, rather than what it costs.
`recognizers.rs`, the DEVLOG and this file all carry the same sentence: *"the shrink therefore cannot
admit anything the pre-M11-R10 build did not already admit."* It can: `Registers AB12 cafe babe dead
beef are clobbered` is untouched by v1.2.1 and comes back from HEAD as `Registers [IBAN_1] cafe babe
dead beef are clobbered`. [M11-R22](#m11-r22). And the glued residue the maintainer is being asked to
decide is described as *"yields no candidate"*; for the **grouped** rendering — the one banks print —
a candidate is produced and up to **ten consecutive bytes** of a real IBAN survive.
[M11-R23](#m11-r23). Neither of those two is a regression — v1.2.1 is byte-identical on the second and
strictly less protective on the first — what is wrong is that both are stated in the docs as the
opposite of what they do.

**No leak was found in `src/`, and the M11 guards are alive** — `shrink_on_reject` and
`iban_case_gate`'s short-circuit were each removed and each turned the suite red.

### What was verified, with the expected value written before the observed one

| Quantity | Expected (written first) | Observed | Reps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cargo test` | 250 / 0 / 5 over 24 binaries, 0 warnings — round 5's count, unchanged by a docs+perf commit | **250 / 0 / 5, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 2 (start of round; again after every mutation was restored) |
| `cargo test-onnx` | 286 / 0 / 22 over 24 binaries, 0 warnings | **286 / 0 / 22, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 1 |
| `cargo fmt --check` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `iban_case_gate` residue on `~/.cargo/registry/src`, at HEAD | **1 of 936**, per M11-R19's closure | **3 spans, 1 lowercase-bearing — `ab22 ab23 ab44 ab45 ab66 ab67` in `matrixmultiply-0.3.10`**, over 13 998 files / 319.7 MB | 1 (deterministic) |
| HEAD vs `831f916^` on 400 000 random Unicode-bearing inputs | byte-identical verdicts, if *"same arithmetic, same verdicts"* holds | **identical on 399 968; the older build PANICS on the other 32** | 1 (deterministic, seed `0xDEADBEEF`) |
| the panic through the real **v1.2.1** `.exe` | — (no expectation; this is the question) | **HTTP 500, nothing forwarded, panic logged** | 1 |
| the same payload through the real **HEAD** `.exe` | 200, masked | **200, upstream saw `Account [IBAN_1] please`** | 1 |
| `mask_all` → `demask` round-trip fuzz, 2 × 200 000 inputs | 0 panics, 0 mismatches | **0 panics, 0 mismatches**, 95 701 / 96 155 inputs actually masked (non-vacuity) | 2 seeds |
| glued-trailing matrix, 36 real ISO 13616 countries × 2 renderings × 2 cases × 4 glues | *"yields no candidate"*, per `IBAN-05`'s decided limit | **true only of the continuous arm; the grouped arm produces a candidate in 288/288 and leaves ≥ 4 bytes of the IBAN in 236** | 1 (deterministic) |
| the same matrix on **v1.2.1** | identical, if it is not a regression | **byte-identical, every cell** | 1 |
| `AB12 cafe babe dead beef` at HEAD vs v1.2.1 | identical, per *"cannot admit anything the pre-M11-R10 build did not already admit"* | **HEAD masks `AB12`; v1.2.1 masks nothing** | 3 (library, real `.exe` ×2) |
| Iban/TaxId spans over the repo's own 22 823-byte Claude Code turn | 1 `Email` + 1 `Iban`, the two planted values | **exactly those two** | 1 |

### Mutations run in round 6

`src/pii/recognizers.rs` only. Each was written as **bytes** from a pristine copy taken at the start
of the round (sha256 `4dc85764…`), had its `LastWriteTime` stamped so cargo could not reuse a cached
fingerprint (round 5's trap), was **refused if its sha256 matched the pristine one** — so an empty
substitution cannot be mistaken for a green — and was restored in a `finally` with the sha256
re-checked afterwards. `git status --porcelain` was empty after every one.

| # | mutation | expected | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | `iban_mod97` + `iban_length_ok` reverted to the **exact** pre-`831f916` bodies (verified `diff`-identical to `831f916^`) | red, if anything pins the panic fix | **GREEN — 250 / 0 / 5.** Nothing in the suite watches it. [M11-R21](#m11-r21) |
| B | `shrink_on_reject: true` → `false` on the IBAN recognizer (the M11-R13 fix removed) | red | **red** — `no_window_of_an_iban_survives_masking` (`IBAN-05`), 152 passed / 1 failed |
| C | `iban_case_gate`'s no-lowercase short-circuit deleted (always verify) | red | **red** — `a_lowercase_iban_is_masked_only_when_it_verifies` (`CASE-02`) **and** `structural_iban_is_masked_but_flagged`, 151 / 2 |

Mutation A is the one that matters, and its counterpart is not a mutation at all: the **actual
`831f916^` build**, compiled from `git archive` into a scratch crate, panics on 32 of the same 400 000
inputs HEAD answers. So the harness demonstrably detects the failure the suite reports green for — the
gap is in the *suite*, not in the instrument.

### The product, driven through the real binary

Two real `.exe`s (HEAD in `target/review`, v1.2.1 built from `git archive` into a scratch target dir)
against a recording mock upstream on OS-assigned free ports; only PIDs this round started were ever
killed, and `target/debug/` was never written.

- **v1.2.1** — `{"messages":[{"role":"user","content":"Account AB𝟎𝟏ABCDEFGHIJK please"}]}` → **500**,
  the mock recorded **nothing**, and the proxy logged
  `panicked at src\pii\recognizers.rs:1423:46: start byte index 4 is not a char boundary; it is inside '𝟎'`.
  The process survived; that request did not.
- **HEAD** — the identical payload → **200**, and the mock received `Account [IBAN_1] please`.
- **HEAD** — `Registers AB12 cafe babe dead beef are clobbered` → **200**, upstream saw
  `Registers [IBAN_1] cafe babe dead beef are clobbered`. **v1.2.1**, same request: upstream saw the
  sentence unchanged.
- **HEAD** — `Please wire to ES91 2100 0418 4502 0005 1332abcd now` → upstream saw
  `Please wire to [IBAN_1] 1332abcd now`. **v1.2.1**: byte-identical.

### What this round could not break

- **The round-trip.** 400 000 fuzzed inputs through `Vault::mask_all` → `demask`, built from twelve
  real PII fragments glued with ASCII **and** multi-byte Unicode noise: **0 panics, 0 mismatches**,
  with ~96 000 per run actually masked so the assertion is not vacuous.
- **Any panic at HEAD.** 400 000 inputs over `try_detect` and 400 000 more over the full round-trip,
  across five multi-byte `\p{Nd}` blocks (2-, 3- and 4-byte) plus `é`, `中`, `€`, NBSP and tab: none.
  Every other byte-indexing validator checks its length **after** filtering to ASCII bytes
  (`es_dni_nie_valid`, `fr_nir_valid`, `cf_check_valid`, `nl_bsn_valid`, `zh_resident_id_valid`,
  `de_steuerid_valid`), so a multi-byte character makes them return `false`, never index.
- **M11-R19's number.** Re-measured independently on the same registry with a line-oriented sweep
  rather than a whole-file one: 3 spans, 1 lowercase-bearing, and it is the same `matrixmultiply`
  register list. The closure's rate reproduces.
- **The `831f916` rewrite's correctness.** 400 000-input differential against the exact previous
  commit: **zero verdict differences** (kind, text and `Confidence`), the panics excepted. The perf
  claim holds; it is the *completeness* of the claim that does not.
- **A truncating shrink.** Hunted specifically for an accepted prefix `P` strictly inside a real IBAN
  `V` whose remainder goes upstream: it needs `V` to be both mixed-case *and* mod-97-failing, which is
  already the escalated residue, or a country code the ISO table does not know. No new shape found.
- **`confidence_of`'s `PiiKind::TaxId` arm** (`text.len() == 14 && text[..2]`) — a byte-length gate in
  front of a byte slice. Reachable only with two ASCII `NL` letters at bytes 0-1, so the slice is
  always on a boundary; a fullwidth-digit `NL１２３…B01` merely falls through to `Verified` without the
  11-proef. It mislabels an audit line and masks the value either way. **Recorded here as a decided
  limit, not filed** — `CLAUDE.md`'s rule about the third variant of a family.

### Guard-vs-product tally

Round 6: **3 in the product** ([R21](#m11-r21) a panic, [R22](#m11-r22) a false invariant with a
measurable behaviour behind it, [R23](#m11-r23) a mis-stated residue), **1 on the docs**
([R24](#m11-r24)). That is the first round of M11 whose findings are mostly *in* `src/` since round 4,
and the reason is the one thing this milestone had never varied: **the alphabet**.

---

<a id="m11-r21"></a>
### M11-R21 — a Unicode decimal digit in an IBAN candidate **panicked** the masking path in every release ever cut; fixed at HEAD by accident, pinned by nothing — *hardening / availability, medium*

**The mechanism, three lines that were only ever true together.**

1. `src/pii/recognizers.rs:246` — the IBAN pattern is `(?-u:\b)[A-Za-z]{2}\d{2}(?:…)`.
   [M4-R13](M4.md#m4-r13) turned Unicode **off for `\b`** and deliberately left it **on for `\d`**, so
   `\d{2}` matches `\p{Nd}` — Arabic-Indic `٠١` (2 bytes), Thai `๐๑` and fullwidth `０１` (3 bytes),
   Osmanya `𐒠𐒡` and mathematical-bold `𝟎𝟏` (4 bytes).
2. `confidence_of` calls `iban_mod97` on **every** accepted `Iban` span (since M1.5, `bb68707`), and
   since M11-R10 `iban_case_gate` calls it again.
3. `iban_mod97`, from the first commit of the project (`a74c78f`) until `831f916`, compacted its input
   into a `String` and then took **byte** slices at index 4:

   ```rust
   let rearranged = format!("{}{}", &compact[4..], &compact[..4]);
   ```

   Byte 4 is a `char` boundary only when the first four characters are all 1- or 2-byte. With a 3- or
   4-byte digit it is not, and `str` indexing **panics**.

**Repro, through the real `v1.2.1` `.exe`** against a recording mock upstream:

```text
POST /v1/chat/completions
{"model":"m","messages":[{"role":"user","content":"Account AB𝟎𝟏ABCDEFGHIJK please"}]}

client   : HTTP 500 Internal Server Error
upstream : (nothing — the mock recorded no request)
proxy log: thread 'tokio-rt-worker' panicked at src\pii\recognizers.rs:1423:46:
           start byte index 4 is not a char boundary; it is inside '𝟎' (bytes 2..6 of string)
```

The IBAN pattern is byte-identical at `v0.4.0`, `v1.0.0`, `v1.1.0`, `v1.2.0` and `v1.2.1`, and
`iban_mod97` carried the slice throughout, so **every release this project has published has this
path**. The payload is 30 bytes of legal JSON on an unauthenticated endpoint; no key, no size, no
streaming needed.

**Severity, stated precisely.** It is **not a leak** — the request dies before anything is forwarded,
which is the direction this project errs in. It is an availability defect, and this repo's own words
are why it earns a row: *availability is a privacy property here — a proxy too slow (or too brittle)
to keep switched on protects nothing.* One 30-byte request is enough to fail that request, and a
client that retries the same content gets 500 forever.

**It is fixed at HEAD, and that is the second half of the finding.** `831f916` rewrote `iban_mod97`
and `iban_length_ok` to be allocation-free, iterating `chars()` instead of slicing bytes. The commit
message, the DEVLOG entry and `ARCHITECTURE.md` all describe it as a pure optimisation — *"Same
arithmetic, same verdicts"* — and its differential proof is *"span counts byte-identical at 56 729"*
on **4 MiB of `[a-z]{2}[0-9]{2}` ASCII groups**. That corpus cannot contain a multi-byte character, so
the proof was structurally unable to notice that the rewrite also removed a panic. *A corpus has a
shape, and that shape is a blind spot* — [M4-R13](M4.md#m4-r13)'s lesson, landing this time on the
verification of a performance fix.

**Measured differentially, 400 000 random inputs drawn from an alphabet of ASCII plus five multi-byte
`\p{Nd}` blocks, HEAD against a `git archive` build of `831f916^`:** verdicts identical on 399 968
(kind, text **and** `Confidence`); on the remaining **32** the older build panics and HEAD returns a
result. Zero verdict differences otherwise — so the perf claim is true and *incomplete*.

**Mutation.** Reverting `iban_mod97` + `iban_length_ok` to the exact pre-`831f916` bodies
(`diff`-identical to `831f916^`) leaves `cargo test` at **250 / 0 / 5**. Nothing in the suite watches
this.

**Fix.** Three parts, and the third is the one that lasts.

1. **Pin it.** A unit case beside `iban_mod97_accepts_valid_rejects_invalid` asserting
   `!iban_mod97("AB𝟎𝟏ABCDEFGHIJK")` is the minimum, and it is *not* enough on its own — it pins the
   instance. The chokepoint is a property test: **`try_detect` must not panic on any input**,
   quantified over a generator whose alphabet includes multi-byte `\p{Nd}` characters. The `PROP-*`
   family already has that shape; what it lacks is the alphabet. One generator change covers every
   current and future validator, which is the difference between closing the class and closing this
   line.
2. **Say it in `CHANGELOG.md`.** The `[Unreleased]` `### Fixed` section describes two IBAN defects and
   not this one. An operator on `1.2.x` is running a build that answers 500 to a legal request, and
   the release page is where they would find that out. *"A request whose text contained a non-ASCII
   decimal digit next to an IBAN-shaped token could crash the masking task and return 500; nothing was
   ever forwarded"* is the sentence.
3. **Correct the two places that call `831f916` verdict-preserving** — `docs/DEVLOG.md`'s round-5
   entry and this file's [M11-R20](#m11-r20) closure note. It changed one verdict class: *panic* to
   *answer*. Saying so costs a clause and buys the next reader the knowledge that the rewrite is not
   revertible for performance reasons alone.

**Test that would have caught it.** Not `CASE-01`/`CASE-02` — the case axis is about *ASCII* letters
and says so. The generators behind `PROP-03`/`PROP-04` draw from an ASCII pool, and `IBAN-05`
synthesises its IBANs from `iban_country_length` with ASCII digits. **Every corpus in the repo is
ASCII except the `non_ascii_scripts` cases M4-R13 added — and those carry non-ASCII *letters* around
ASCII values, never a non-ASCII *digit inside* one.** That is the gap in one sentence.

**Closed 2026-09-04 (`a19206d`).** The panic was already gone at HEAD as an unremarked side effect of
`831f916`'s allocation-free rewrite — which is exactly why it needed closing rather than noting:
reverting that one function left the suite at **250/0/5**. `UTF8-01` pins it now, with its corpus
derived from a registry rather than hand-written: four `\p{Nd}` digits of 2, 3, 3 and 4 bytes into
every character position of every recorded positive. Reverting `iban_mod97` byte for byte from
`git show 831f916^` turns it red with the finding's own message. The `[Unreleased]` changelog gained
the entry an operator wants ([M11-R28](#m11-r28)): a 30-byte request returned `500` on every release
ever cut.

<a id="m11-r22"></a>
### M11-R22 — the shrink **can** admit what the pre-M11-R10 build could not: an unverified `XX99` prefix is masked, and the invariant that says otherwise is stated in three places — *precision, low*

**The claim.** `src/pii/recognizers.rs:99-105` (the `shrink_on_reject` field doc),
`docs/DEVLOG.md:121-124` and this file's [M11-R13](#m11-r13) entry all carry the same argument, word
for word:

> Its validator is [`iban_case_gate`], which *accepts unconditionally* unless the span carries a
> lowercase byte — so … every prefix the shrink then tries either carries no lowercase (**accepted
> structurally, which is exactly what shipped for ten milestones**) or must pass mod-97 **and** the
> ISO 13616 length. **The shrink therefore cannot admit anything the pre-M11-R10 build did not
> already admit.**

**It is false, and the counter-example needs no PII in it.** `iban_case_gate` short-circuits `true` on
any span with no lowercase byte — *without arithmetic*. The shrink walks a rejected match back one
separator at a time and stops at the **first** prefix the gate accepts. When the lowercase lives in
the later groups, that prefix is the bare country code + check digits:

```text
in       : Registers AB12 cafe babe dead beef are clobbered
v1.2.1   : Registers AB12 cafe babe dead beef are clobbered       (nothing masked)
HEAD     : Registers [IBAN_1] cafe babe dead beef are clobbered   (`AB12` masked, Confidence::Structural)
```

Verified three ways: at the library, and through **both** real `.exe`s against a mock upstream — the
v1.2.1 upstream received the sentence unchanged, the HEAD upstream received the placeholder. A second
shape stops one group later: `DE12 3456 789a bcde f012` → `[IBAN_1]` over `DE12 3456`.

**Where the argument goes wrong** is instructive, and it is [M4](M4.md#retrospective)'s third lesson
verbatim: *an invariant is only as strong as the set it quantifies over.* The sentence quantifies over
the **validator's verdict on a prefix** — and on that set it is true; `AB12` is exactly the
"structurally valid, mod-97 unchecked" span M4 decided to mask. But `shrink_on_reject` also changes
the **set of spans the recognizer can emit**: pre-M11-R10 that set was *"the spans this regex
matches"*, and the regex can match neither `AB12` (it demands 11–30 more characters, or two more
groups) nor `AB12 cafe babe dead beef` (uppercase-only classes). HEAD's set is that one **union its
group-boundary prefixes**, and `AB12` is in the second and not the first.

**Measured, so the row is not larger than the thing.** Over the whole third-party corpus M11-R19 uses
— 13 998 `.rs` files, 319.7 MB of `~/.cargo/registry/src` — the shipped recognizer emits **3** `Iban`
spans, **none** of them a shrink artefact (0 spans of exactly four characters), and over the repo's
own 22 823-byte real Claude Code turn it emits exactly the two planted values. So the *rate* on real
traffic is 0 in everything this repo can measure, and the direction is safe — over-mask, restored
byte-identically. **What is wrong is the invariant**, and a false invariant is worse than an absent
one, because the next person adding a rejecting validator to a variable-length recognizer will read it
and conclude the shrink is free.

**Fix.**

1. Correct the sentence in all three places to what the code does: *the shrink can accept a prefix the
   gate waves through structurally, and such a prefix — down to the bare `XX99` — is a span the
   pre-M11-R10 pattern could not emit. The cost is an over-mask, measured at 0 spans over 319.7 MB of
   third-party source and 0 over the M7 turn.*
2. If that cost is not wanted, the chokepoint is one line: make the shrink's acceptance **stricter
   than the scan's** — require a shrunk prefix to verify (`iban_mod97 && iban_length_ok`) rather than
   reusing `iban_case_gate`, whose short-circuit exists to preserve M4's rule for spans the *regex*
   produced. That is product-visible (it drops the structural-uppercase shrink path), so it is the
   maintainer's call, not the builder's — and [M11-R18](#m11-r18) option 2 edits the same line, so the
   two should be decided together.

**Test that would have caught it.** `CASE-02` asserts the gate on whole spans and `IBAN-05` asserts a
predicate about **real** IBANs; neither asks what the shrink emits on a span that is not an IBAN at
all. The assertion is one line and it is the invariant itself: *every masked `Iban` span must be a
span the recognizer's own regex matches* — differential against the pattern, so it cannot be satisfied
by an example somebody chose.

**Closed 2026-09-04 (`a19206d`), by making the sentence true rather than by softening it.** A claim
that the shrink is free is what the next person adding a rejecting validator will read, so
`shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` now requires each prefix to be a **full match of the recognizer's own
pattern**. The emitted set is back to *"the spans this regex matches"*, `AB12` is no longer among
them, and the invariant is true by construction instead of by argument. `SHRINK-01` checks it
differentially — it cannot be satisfied by an example somebody chose. The phone tier shares the code
path, so that was measured rather than assumed.

<a id="m11-r23"></a>
### M11-R23 — the glued residue escalated to the maintainer is published as *"yields no candidate"*; for the **grouped** rendering a candidate is produced and up to **ten** consecutive bytes of a real IBAN survive — *precision / escalation accuracy, medium*

**The statement**, in `docs/TESTING.md` → `IBAN-05`, in `src/pii/recognizers.rs`'s `IBAN-05` doc and
in [M11-R20](#m11-r20)'s closure note, all restated on 2026-09-04 to remove the *lowercase* scoping:

> An IBAN **glued** to 1-4 alphanumerics still yields **no candidate**, because the continuous arm
> carries no separator for the shrink to cut at.

The reason given is a property of the **continuous** arm; the statement is made about *an IBAN*.
[M11-R20](#m11-r20) fixed the scope on the **case** axis and left it wrong on the **rendering** axis —
the same sentence, mis-scoped a second time, in the same edit.

**Measured over the 36 countries in `iban_country_length`**, each synthesised with real check digits,
in both renderings, both letter cases, against four glued trailing tokens (`abcd`, `x`, `EUR`,
`ABCD`), inside the carrier `Please wire the deposit to …{glue} and confirm.`:

| rendering / case | rows | nothing masked | candidate produced, **≥ 4 bytes of the value survive** | fully covered |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| continuous / UPPER | 144 | 73 | 0 | 71 |
| continuous / lower | 144 | **144** | 0 | 0 |
| **grouped / UPPER** | 144 | 0 | **92** | 52 |
| **grouped / lower** | 144 | 0 | **144** | 0 |

The published statement describes row 2. Rows 3 and 4 are the rendering banks print — the guard's own
helper in the same file is called `in_groups_of_four` and its doc says *"the rendering banks print"*.

```text
grouped / UPPER
  in     : Please wire the deposit to AD87 0123 4567 8901 2345 6789abcd and confirm.
  masked : Please wire the deposit to [IBAN_1] 6789abcd and confirm.
           → the final group ` 6789` (5 bytes) upstream in clear

grouped / lower
  in     : Please wire the deposit to ad87 0123 4567 8901 2345 6789abcd and confirm.
  masked : Please wire the deposit to ad87 [PHONE_1] 2345 6789abcd and confirm.
           → `ad87` and ` 2345 6789` — country code, both check digits and two whole
             groups, 10 consecutive bytes, upstream in clear
```

**The second one is the output shape [M11-R13](#m11-r13) was filed as a LEAK for**, word for word
(*"country code, both check digits and the last group in clear"*, the middle announced as
`[PHONE_1]`). The only difference is that R13's trailing token is **separated** and this one is
**glued**.

**It is not a regression, and that half is confirmed rather than assumed.** The identical matrix run
against a `git archive` build of **`v1.2.1`** is **byte-identical in every one of the 576 cells**. The
behaviour predates M11 entirely — the grouped arm has always stopped at the last complete group, and
the case fold changed nothing about it.

**Why it still earns a row.** This residue is *the* item M11 deliberately did not decide and handed to
the maintainer as product-visible coverage. The framing they were given — *"a glued token yields no
candidate; an evasion-shaped input rather than a natural one"* — reads as *nothing is masked, so the
operator sees the value and knows*. What actually happens for the printed rendering is that
**something is masked and a piece is not**, which is the failure mode nobody inspects. A decision
taken on the first description is not the same decision.

**And `IBAN-05` cannot see it, by construction.** The guard's title is *"no **eight-character window**
of an IBAN survives masking"* while its doc and `TESTING.md` both present it as M10-R1's predicate,
*no **byte** of the value survives*. Those differ, and the whole glued residue lives in the difference:
the surviving runs above are 5 and 10 bytes, and every 5-byte one is invisible to an 8-byte window.
The 8 was chosen for a good reason — *"deliberately digit-free, so an eight-character window cannot
coincide with the carrier text"* — but the relaxation's cost has never been written down beside it.

**Fix.**

1. Restate the limit on **both** axes: *a **continuous** IBAN glued to 1-4 alphanumerics yields no
   candidate; a **grouped** one yields a candidate that stops at the last complete group, leaving the
   final group — and, when the rendering carries lowercase, the country code and check digits too —
   in clear.* With the 576-cell table, and with the note that v1.2.1 does the same.
2. Re-put the escalation to the maintainer in that wording and with those numbers, beside
   [M11-R18](#m11-r18)'s options, since a length-derived cut would close both.
3. Write **[M11-R20](#m11-r20)'s own recommended assertion**, which its closure did not: a second
   assertion in `IBAN-05` pinning the residue **as** the residue, per rendering. R20 said it in one
   line — *"a limit nobody asserts is indistinguishable from a limit nobody noticed"* — and the proof
   that it was needed is that the restatement written instead of the test is wrong again. Say the
   window relaxation out loud in the same place: *`IBAN-05` implements M10-R1's predicate at a
   granularity of eight bytes; a residue shorter than that is outside what it can report.*

**Closed 2026-09-04 (`a19206d`).** The escalation the maintainer was reading now describes what the
grouped rendering actually does — a candidate **is** produced and up to ten bytes of a real IBAN can
survive — instead of *"yields no candidate"*. Both halves of the correction matter: the description
was wrong for one rendering of two, and a decision taken on the wrong description is not the decision
anyone thought they were taking.

<a id="m11-r24"></a>
### M11-R24 — the guard-vs-product tally does not reconcile with its own ledger: 16 + 3 = 19 against 21 rows — *docs, low*

`CLAUDE.md` asks for exactly one number at a milestone boundary: *"count the findings **on the guards**
and those **on the product**, and report both numbers: it is the ratio that says whether the loop is
working or feeding itself."* M11 is at that boundary and publishes the ratio twice, differently, and
neither version adds up to the ledger.

| where | guards + docs | product | total | ledger rows at that point |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| this file, round 5 narrative | 13 | 5 | 18 | 18 (`R0`…`R17`) — consistent |
| `docs/DEVLOG.md`, round-5 entry | **16** | **3** | **19** | **21** (`R0`…`R20`) |

Round 5 added three findings to a base of 13 + 5, which gives **16 + 5 = 21** and matches the ledger
exactly. The DEVLOG instead moved the guards/docs count forward by three and the product count
*backwards* by two, on the strength of *"Round 5: 0 in the product"* — but [M11-R18](#m11-r18) is a
hardening finding **on `src/pii/recognizers.rs`**, open, and named in the same entry as the only thing
standing between the milestone and its tag. Classifying the round's own blocker as not-in-the-product
is what produces the discrepancy.

By the ledger's own severity column the split is: **product** = `R2` (fidelity), `R10` (leak), `R13`
(leak), `R18` (hardening), `R19` (precision) = **5**; **guards, docs and build** = the other **16**.

**Why a row rather than a shrug.** The ratio is the milestone's self-assessment, it is the number that
goes into the hand-over, and it currently understates the product half by 40%. Round 6 moves it again
— three of its four are in the product — so whoever writes the hand-over should compute it from the
ledger rather than by incrementing the previous round's sentence.

**Fix.** Restate in `docs/DEVLOG.md` as **16 on guards/docs/build, 5 in the product, 21 total**, note
that `R18` is a product finding, and derive the figure from the ROADMAP ledger's rows. **Test that
would have caught it:** none is warranted — but the same arithmetic `CAT-01` applies to test ids
(*count the set the code is built from, never a list somebody keeps current*) is what the tally needs,
and the ledger table is that set.

**Closed 2026-09-04 — the behaviour was already right; what was missing was anything holding it
there.** `UTF8-01` (`a_non_ascii_digit_inside_a_value_never_panics_a_validator`) pins it, and the
proof it is reached is a real revert: `iban_mod97` restored **byte-for-byte from `831f916^` via `git
show`** rather than retyped turns it red with the round's own message —

    panicked at src\pii\recognizers.rs:1832:46:
    start byte index 4 is not a char boundary; it is inside '٣' (bytes 3..5 of string)

— against a suite that was 250/0/5 with that same revert before the guard existed.

**The corpus is derived from [`CASE_ANSWERS`]**, so a new letter-bearing recognizer is exercised on
this axis the moment its case answer is recorded: one registry, two guards. Four `\p{Nd}` digits of
**2, 3, 3 and 4** bytes are substituted into every character position of every positive — **932
substitutions**, counted off a run rather than reasoned about (M11-R19's lesson, one round later) —
and each asserts both that no validator panics and that the `mask_all` -> `demask` round trip stays
byte-exact, since a span whose bytes and characters disagree is where an index goes wrong twice.

**The residue is promoted, because it is why this lived ten milestones**: every corpus in this repo
is ASCII, `non_ascii_scripts` included — it puts non-ASCII letters *around* an ASCII value, never a
non-ASCII digit *inside* one. That sentence is now in `docs/TESTING.md` beside `UTF8-01`, and the
rule it produced — *`\d` is Unicode on purpose, so a validator takes `&str` and must treat it as
text* — in `ARCHITECTURE.md` beside the ASCII-word-boundary invariant, where this round put it.

**Closed 2026-09-04 — the invariant is now true by construction rather than by argument, which is
the only honest way to close it.** Option 1 (soften the sentence) was rejected: a claim that the
shrink is free is what the next person adding a rejecting validator will read, and weakening it to
*"except sometimes"* leaves them no rule. Option 2 as the round framed it — make the shrink require
full verification — was rejected too, because it drops the structural-uppercase path that M4 decided
on purpose and is genuinely the maintainer's.

**What was done instead is the invariant itself, one line:** `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` requires each
candidate prefix to be a **full match of the recognizer's own pattern** before the validator is
consulted. `AB12` is not one — the pattern demands eleven more characters or two more groups — so the
shrink walks past it and returns `None`. The emitted set goes back to *"the spans this regex
matches"*, which is what the sentence claimed all along.

**It touches the phone tier too, and that was measured rather than assumed:** the full suite is
**252 / 0 / 5**, every `PHONE-NAT` guard included, so no phone shrink produced a span its own pattern
would not match.

`SHRINK-01` checks it **differentially**, against the patterns from `shipped_patterns()`, so no
chosen example can satisfy it. Removing the one-line fix turns it red naming the span:

    Iban emitted "AB12", which no shipped Iban pattern matches at all — the recognizer's
    output set is wider than its own grammar.

**Two limits are stated rather than left implicit, and the first cost a wrong first attempt.** The
guard originally demanded a *full* match and went red on `020 7946 0958 0161 496 0000` — a
legitimate span, because `push_candidates` coalesces a recognizer's overlapping hits into **maximal
runs**. So it asks for a match *at the start*, which is exactly strong enough for this class (a span
the pattern cannot produce **at all**) and does not claim to catch a *truncating* shrink;
`IBAN-05`/`PHONE-NAT-09` cover that from the other side. And it runs on `raw_candidates`, not
`detect`, because the resolver **unions** overlapping spans and a resolved span legitimately matches
no single pattern. Non-vacuity floor **10, measured at 14** — the 20 that felt right before it was
counted was already too high the day it was written.

The three statements of the false invariant are replaced by what the code does, and the
`shrink_on_reject` doc keeps the old sentence **as a quotation** with the reason it failed, because
*an invariant is only as strong as the set it quantifies over* is the durable half.

**Closed 2026-09-04, in both halves — the statement and the guard that could not have contradicted
it.**

**The statement.** `docs/TESTING.md` -> `IBAN-05` and the source doc now separate the two arms
instead of describing one and naming both: the **continuous** arm yields no candidate in either
letter case, while the **grouped** arm — *the rendering banks print* — yields a candidate that stops
at the last complete group and leaves up to **ten consecutive bytes** in clear, with the round's own
example quoted (`… ad87 [PHONE_1] 2345 6789abcd …`) and the observation that this is the output shape
M11-R13 was filed as a **leak** for, differing only in that R13's trailing token is separated. The
numbers travel with it — **236 of 288 grouped rows leave 4+ bytes**, byte-identical against a
`v1.2.1` build in all 576 cells — so the escalation to the maintainer now carries the real exposure
rather than a sentence that reads as *"nothing happens"*.

**The guard.** `IBAN-05`'s window was **eight** bytes while its doc claimed M10-R1's *no byte*
predicate; it is now **four**, which is a group — the smallest unit of the printed rendering — and
the threshold this round measured the residue with, so guard and finding speak the same language.
Measured rather than chosen: four passes on every separated rendering; **one and two cannot**,
because a single character or a pair legitimately occurs in the carrier sentence. That limit is
written on the guard instead of a predicate it does not have.

Mis-scoped twice in two rounds — M11-R20 fixed the case axis and left the rendering axis wrong in the
same edit — which is the argument for stating a limit as a *matrix over both axes* rather than as a
sentence with an example in it.

**Closed 2026-09-04, and the arithmetic was the smaller half.** Both DEVLOG entries are corrected in
place with a note saying what they used to read: round 4 now says **15 + 3 = 18** and round 5 **16 +
5 = 21**, each matching the ledger's length on the day.

**What made them wrong is that nothing said what counted**, so the fix is a stated rule, carried in
both entries: the split is read off the **ledger's severity cell** — *product* = `leak` /
`fidelity` / `hardening` / `precision`, *net or docs* = `build` / `guard` / `docs`. It is
mechanically checkable from `ROADMAP.md` by anyone, which a judgement call was not, and it is what
stops the same slip recurring: M11-R18 was called *not-in-the-product* while being an open
`hardening` row on `recognizers.rs` that blocks the tag.

Under that rule the milestone stands at **17 on the net or the docs, 8 in the product**, 25 rows.

---

<a id="round-7"></a>
## Round 7 — the axis under the case axis: the separator (2026-09-04)

Round 6 varied the **alphabet** and found a panic older than the milestone. Round 7 varied the one
thing that survived even that: **the byte that sits between a value's groups.**

Every pattern in the structured tier spells its intra-value separator as a literal ASCII space —
`(?: [A-Za-z0-9]{4}){2,7}` for IBAN, `[ -]` for the card and the phone shapes. Every validator behind
those patterns is **separator-agnostic**: `iban_mod97` filters `char::is_whitespace()` (which accepts
U+00A0, U+2007, U+2009, U+202F, U+205F, U+3000 and tab), `luhn_valid` filters to `to_digit(10)`, and
`phonenumber::parse` strips punctuation for itself. That is the exact sentence
[M11-R10](#m11-r10) was written about, one axis over — *the validator was written for input the regex
can never deliver* — and it is the third time this milestone has met it (case, then the digit script,
now the separator).

**The consequence is a total miss, not a shorter span.** A German IBAN, a Visa card number and a
London phone number, each written with U+00A0 between groups — which is what a copy from a bank's web
page, a PDF, Word or Outlook produces, because those pages use `&nbsp;` precisely to stop the number
wrapping — reach the provider **byte for byte**, through the real `.exe`, while an email in the same
sentence is masked. [M11-R25](#m11-r25).

**No corpus in this repository can express the axis.** There is not one non-ASCII space character
anywhere under `tests/` or `src/` — checked, both trees, six code points. Round 6's own 400 000-input
fuzz *had* NBSP and tab in its alphabet and still could not see this: it glued PII fragments together
with Unicode noise rather than substituting the separator **inside** one. That is
[M4's retrospective](M4.md#retrospective), lesson 6 — *ask what a guard holds constant, not only what
it varies* — arriving on the guard that was written to answer lesson 2.

The other four findings are on what the last two rounds published. `ARCHITECTURE.md` still says
**"Nothing enforces this yet"** about M11-R21 and links it as **open**, in the round after `UTF8-01`
closed it ([M11-R26](#m11-r26)) — the second time an M11 closure has failed to reach that file
(M11-R17 was the first). The rationale for leaving `\d` Unicode is published in three places as
*"what lets a value written in Arabic-Indic or fullwidth digits be **detected** rather than
forwarded"*, and measured it is false for **every** checksum-validated recognizer
([M11-R27](#m11-r27)). The `[Unreleased]` changelog does not mention that a 30-byte request returned
**500** on every release ever cut and no longer does ([M11-R28](#m11-r28)). And `iban_length_ok`
compares an ISO 13616 **character** length against a sum of `char::len_utf8` — a byte count — three
lines from where `ARCHITECTURE.md` now writes *"a byte length is not a character count"*
([M11-R29](#m11-r29)).

**No fail-open, no over-mask regression, no raw value in a log, and every M11 guard I removed turned
the suite red.**

### The product, driven through the real binary

The `target/review` `.exe` against a recording mock upstream on an OS-assigned free port. Only the PID
this round spawned was ever killed; `target/debug/` was never written and no port was freed.

- `{"content":"Bonifico su IBAN DE89~3704~0044~0532~0130~00, carta 4111~1111~1111~1111, tel
  020~7946~0958, mail a.b@example.com"}` where `~` is **U+00A0** → **200**, and the mock received the
  IBAN, the card and the phone number **unchanged**, with only `[EMAIL_1]` in place. The email is the
  control: it has no separator, so it is masked in the same request that leaks the other three.
- The identical body with ASCII spaces → the mock received `[IBAN_1]`, `[CREDIT_CARD_1]`,
  `[PHONE_1]`, `[EMAIL_1]`. Same binary, same request shape, one byte different per gap.

### Mutations run in round 7

`src/pii/recognizers.rs` only. Each was written as **bytes** (never through a text-mode helper); the
substitution was **refused unless it occurred exactly once**, and refused again if the result was
byte-identical to the input — so an empty substitution cannot be mistaken for a green. Mutation D was
in fact refused on its first form (`3 occurrences`), which is the harness working. Each was restored
from `git cat-file blob HEAD:...` with the sha256 re-checked; `git status --porcelain` was empty after
every one.

| # | mutation | expected | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | the `regex.find(prefix)` full-match check deleted from `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` (M11-R22's fix removed) | red | **red** — `a_shrunk_span_is_still_a_match_of_its_own_pattern` (`SHRINK-01`), 154 / 1 |
| B | `iban_mod97` reverted **byte-for-byte** to its `831f916^` body (M11-R21's accidental fix removed) | red — this is the mutation that was **green** in round 6 | **red** — `a_non_ascii_digit_inside_a_value_never_panics_a_validator` (`UTF8-01`) panics at `recognizers.rs:1841`, 154 / 1. The gap round 6 found is closed. |
| C | `shrink_on_reject: true` → `false` on the IBAN recognizer (M11-R13's fix removed) | red | **red** — `no_window_of_an_iban_survives_masking` (`IBAN-05`) **and** `SHRINK-01`'s non-vacuity floor, 153 / 2 |
| D | the IBAN pattern's `[A-Za-z]{2}` → `[A-Z]{2}`, in the recognizer **and** in both `CASE_ANSWERS` rows that key on it — i.e. removed the way a developer would remove it, updating the registry rather than leaving it stale (M11-R10's fix) | red, and red on the *case* guards rather than only on the staleness check | **red** — `every_letter_bearing_recognizer_answers_the_case_axis` (`CASE-01`), `a_lowercase_iban_is_masked_only_when_it_verifies` (`CASE-02`) and `IBAN-05`, 152 / 3 |
| — | the separator axis | *no mutation exists*: there is no guard to remove. The demonstration is the reverse — the shipped behaviour already fails on a rendering banks print, and the suite is **252 / 0 / 5**. |

> **A note for the next reviewer, because it cost this one twenty minutes and left no diff.**
> `git checkout -- src/pii/recognizers.rs` is **not** a safe restore in this repository.
> `core.autocrlf=true` and the working tree is LF, so a checkout rewrites every line ending and
> `git diff` shows nothing. The safe restore is `git cat-file blob HEAD:<path>` written in **binary**
> mode, followed by `git add <path>` to re-validate git's stat cache — otherwise `git status` keeps
> reporting ` M` on a file whose blob hash is identical to `HEAD`'s. Verified at the end of this
> round: worktree, index and `HEAD` all hash to `6d5bce91...`.

### What this round could not break

- **M11-R22's fix does not relocate M11-R13's leak.** This was the round's first suspicion — a fix
  that *narrows* what the shrink accepts is exactly the shape that turns an over-mask back into a
  dropped span. Walked every family by hand and then by sweep: for the IBAN grouped arm the cut at
  the last separator gives `head + k groups`, a full match whenever `k >= 2`, and `k = 1` can only
  arise for a compact length of 12 — shorter than the shortest IBAN in the ISO table (NO, 15). For
  `Groups` and `LongBlock` the discarded prefix is 2–3 digits, which `phonenumber::is_valid` rejected
  before the change as well. The 1 080-cell sweep found **0 truncations** on the ASCII-space
  renderings.
- **The round trip.** 1 080 cells across country × rendering × separator × case × carrier:
  `mask_all` → `demask` byte-exact in every one, **including every cell where the value leaks** — a
  miss restores trivially, which is precisely why the `PROP-*` family and round 6's fuzz are all
  satisfied by this class.
- **Fail-closed on budget exhaustion.** `try_detect` still returns `budget_exhausted`, and the
  shrink's own exhausted-budget path returns `None` into it. `iban_case_gate` is wrapped in `free()`,
  so the IBAN shrink spends nothing and cannot itself trip the allowance.
- **The published `\p{Nd}` behaviour, in the one place it is true.** The universal `Phone` recognizer
  has no validator, so `415-555-2671` written in Arabic-Indic, fullwidth or mathematical-bold digits
  *is* detected and masked. It is the thirteen **validated** recognizers where the claim fails — see
  [M11-R27](#m11-r27).

### Guard-vs-product tally

Round 7: **2 in the product** ([R25](#m11-r25) a leak, [R29](#m11-r29) a latent byte-vs-character
gate), **3 on the docs** ([R26](#m11-r26), [R27](#m11-r27), [R28](#m11-r28)). Read off the ledger's
severity cell, per M11-R24's rule. The milestone now stands at **20 on the net or the docs, 10 in the
product**, 30 rows.
