<a id="m11-r25"></a>
### M11-R25 — a value whose groups are separated by a **non-ASCII space** is forwarded in clear: the separator axis was never decided — *leak, high*

**Not a regression.** The patterns have spelled their separator `" "` since M1 (IBAN, card) and since
M8.1 / M10 (the domestic phone shapes). Nothing M11 did caused this, and nothing M11 did could have
found it — every M11 corpus is ASCII-space-separated. It is filed now because the milestone's own
release note is about to say *"a lower- or mixed-case IBAN or VAT number was forwarded in clear; it is
now masked"*, and the sibling axis is wide open beside it.

**Repro, through the real `.exe`** (`target/review`, mock upstream, `~` = U+00A0):

```
POST /v1/chat/completions
{"messages":[{"role":"user","content":
  "Bonifico su IBAN DE89~3704~0044~0532~0130~00, carta 4111~1111~1111~1111, tel 020~7946~0958, mail a.b@example.com"}]}

what the provider received:
  "Bonifico su IBAN DE89~3704~0044~0532~0130~00, carta 4111~1111~1111~1111, tel 020~7946~0958, mail [EMAIL_1]"
```

Three real values in clear; the email in the same sentence masked, because an email has no separator
to get wrong.

**Mechanism — `src/pii/recognizers.rs:257`, `:270`, `:711`, `:717`, `:739`, `:745`.** The literal is
an ASCII space (or `[ -]`, or `[ .-]`) in every one:

```rust
// :257  IBAN
r"(?-u:\b)[A-Za-z]{2}\d{2}(?:[A-Za-z0-9]{11,30}|(?: [A-Za-z0-9]{4}){2,7}(?: [A-Za-z0-9]{1,4})?)(?-u:\b)"
// :270  card
r"(?-u:\b)(?:\d{4}[ -]\d{4}[ -]\d{4}[ -]\d{4}|\d{13,19})(?-u:\b)"
// :739  phone, Groups
r"(?-u:\b)[1-9]\d{1,2}(?:[ -]\d{2,4}){1,3}(?-u:\b)"
```

while `iban_mod97` (`:1831`) is written for a separator set several times wider:

```rust
let significant = || iban.chars().filter(|c| !c.is_whitespace());   // U+00A0, U+2007, U+202F, \t, ...
```

So the failure is not a shorter span: the compact arm cannot match (its class is `[A-Za-z0-9]`, no
spaces of any kind) and the grouped arm cannot match either, so **no candidate exists at all** and
the resolver never learns the value is there. The fixpoint cannot help — it recovers a value it did
not touch, never one that nothing proposed.

**Measured, 1 080 cells** (6 countries × 2 renderings × 5 separators × 3 letter cases × 6 trailing
contexts, IBANs synthesised with real check digits solved for):

| rendering | ASCII space | U+00A0 | U+202F | U+2007 | tab |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| grouped | 0 leaking / 108 | **108 / 108** | **108 / 108** | **108 / 108** | **108 / 108** |
| compact | 0 / 108 | 0 / 108 | 0 / 108 | 0 / 108 | 0 / 108 |

432 of 1 080 leaking, and the set is exactly *grouped × non-ASCII separator* — predicted as
`6 × 3 × 6 × 4 × 1 = 432` before the run. Round-trip failures: **0**. The same holds for the card and
for all four phone shapes — verified through the binary above, and in the library for
`4111 1111 1111 1111`, `415 555 2671`, `+39 333 000 0001`, `020 7946 0958`, `01 23 45 67 89`. One
rendering is worse than a clean miss: the **hyphen**-grouped IBAN `DE89-3704-0044-0532-0130-00` is
partially matched — `0532-0130` comes back as `[PHONE_1]` and the other 17 characters go upstream in
clear.

**Why no guard sees it.** There is not one non-ASCII space character anywhere under `tests/` or
`src/` — checked across NBSP, NNBSP, figure, thin, en and ideographic space, both trees, zero hits.
`IBAN-05` derives its corpus from `iban_country_length` and drives *"both renderings × three letter
cases × six trailing contexts"* — five axes, and the separator is not one of them. Round 6's
400 000-input fuzz put NBSP and tab in its alphabet and still could not see this, because it used
them to **glue fragments together**, never to replace the space **inside** a value. *A quantity a
test never varies is a quantity the test cannot see* — and the guard written for that lesson
inherited the blind spot.

**The fix — the chokepoint, and it is cheap.** One shared separator class, built once and used by
every pattern that has an intra-value gap, rather than a literal per pattern:

```rust
/// Every character that may separate a value's groups. Derived from what the validators
/// already accept (`char::is_whitespace`), not from what a corpus happened to contain.
const GAP: &str = r"[ \t\x{00A0}\x{1680}\x{2000}-\x{200A}\x{202F}\x{205F}\x{3000}]";
```

with `\r` and `\n` deliberately excluded (a value must not span lines) and the card / phone classes
becoming `(?:-|GAP)`. **Measured cost: 0 added matches**, over 16 427 files / 341.7 MB of
`~/.cargo/registry/src` — IBAN 1 072 → 1 072, card 5 443 → 5 443, phone-`Groups` 4 014 → 4 014, and
not one hit in that corpus carried a non-ASCII separator. That is the same corpus and the same method
the M11-R10 fold cost was measured with (which found 149 added matches and needed `iban_case_gate`
for them); this one finds none, so no gate is required.

**Two halves of it are the maintainer's, per `CLAUDE.md`** — both change coverage:

1. **Fold the whole Unicode space-separator class plus tab** (the recommendation). Costs 0 added
   matches on the reference corpus, closes IBAN, card and all four phone shapes at once, and needs no
   new configuration. Risk: a `\t` between digit groups in a TSV `tool_result` becomes a candidate —
   checksum- and numbering-plan-gated in every family, so an over-mask at worst.
2. **Space separators only, leaving tab out.** Loses the TSV case and gains nothing measurable — the
   corpus shows 0 added matches either way — but keeps the class to "things that render as a space".

The **hyphen** arm is a third, separate question and should not be folded into this one: IBAN
deliberately does not accept `-` today, and adding it changes the IBAN grammar rather than its
separator alphabet.

**The test that would have caught it, and that should ship with the fix.** A `SEPARATOR_ANSWERS`
registry beside `CASE_ANSWERS`, derived from `shipped_patterns()` the way `CASE-01` is: *for every
shipped pattern that contains a literal gap, every recorded positive must be detected identically
when that gap is re-rendered with each member of `GAP`* — one registry, three guards (case, digit
script, separator). The audit half matters as much as the matrix half: a pattern that grows a gap and
records no answer must be reported **unanswered**, which is what made `CASE-03` worth writing after
`CASE-01` turned out to be a list ([M11-R11](#m11-r11)).

**And a rule for `ARCHITECTURE.md`, next to the case-axis and ASCII-word-boundary invariants:** *a
recognizer and its validator must agree on the alphabet of every axis — the letters' case, the
digits' script, and the separator between groups. Where the validator is more permissive than the
pattern, the difference is not slack: it is a set of renderings that reach the provider in clear.*
Three of this milestone's findings are that one sentence, met three times.

**Closed 2026-09-04 (`777eabf`), and the second half is the one a pattern-only fix would have shipped
without.** One `GAP_CHARS` — the Unicode `Zs` category plus tab, `\r` and `\n` deliberately out so a
value cannot span lines — feeds the patterns' character class, the shrink's cut alphabet **and** the
phone validator's normalisation, so the three cannot disagree again. Widening the patterns alone
closed IBAN, card, universal phone and NINO for every separator and left the domestic phone tier at
**2 of 5**: `phonenumber`'s own normalisation accepts U+00A0 and U+3000 and rejects U+202F, U+2007
and tab, so `020⍽7946⍽0958` matched, reached the validator and was refused — *a miss dressed as a
validation*. With `national_phone_valid` normalising first: **5 of 5 in all nine families.** Measured
cost of the whole widening on the reference corpus: **0 added matches**, so no gate is needed.
`SEPARATOR-01` carries all three halves M11-R11 established, and its scope is derived —
`pattern_can_match_any_of(pattern, wanted)`, called with `ASCII_LETTERS` for one axis and `GAP_CHARS`
for the other. One derivation, two axes.

<a id="m11-r26"></a>
### M11-R26 — `ARCHITECTURE.md` still says *"Nothing enforces this yet"* about M11-R21 and links it as **open**, one round after `UTF8-01` closed it — *docs, medium*

`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:496-503`, verbatim at HEAD:

> **Nothing enforces this yet** — reverting `iban_mod97` to its pre-`831f916` body leaves the whole
> suite green [...] The chokepoint is a property test — *`try_detect` panics on no input* — over a
> generator whose alphabet includes multi-byte `\p{Nd}`; see [M11-R21](reviews/M11.md#m11-r21), open.

Both halves are false at HEAD. `UTF8-01` exists (`recognizers.rs:4022`), the ROADMAP ledger marks
M11-R21 `[x]`, and mutation B above shows the revert now turns the suite **red**. `a19206d` touched
`DEVLOG.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `TESTING.md`, `reviews/M11.md` and `recognizers.rs` — and not
`ARCHITECTURE.md`, which is the file that carries the invariant.

**This is the second time in three rounds.** [M11-R17](#m11-r17) was *"the case-axis decision reached
neither `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s invariants nor `CHANGELOG.md`"*; its closure added the case-axis rule to
`ARCHITECTURE.md`, and the pattern repeated on the very next finding. A reader who opens
`ARCHITECTURE.md` — which `CLAUDE.md` names as *the* file to read before changing detection — is told
a live hazard is unguarded and a finding is open.

**Fix.** Replace the paragraph with what is true: the rule (*a validator takes `&str` and must treat
it as text*), the guard that now enforces it (`UTF8-01`, 932 substitutions derived from
`CASE_ANSWERS`), and the residue it does **not** cover — every `CASE_ANSWERS` positive is a
**compact** rendering, so the guard never drives the grouped arm or the shrink path with a multi-byte
digit.

**Test.** None is possible for prose, and that is the point: the mechanical part is the *link*. A
`docs/` check that fails when a `reviews/M11.md#m11-rNN` anchor is described as `open` while the
ROADMAP ledger has it `[x]` would have caught this one and M11-R14 both — the ledger is already
machine-readable, and `CAT-01` proves the project is willing to enforce a docs invariant in a test.

**Closed 2026-09-04, in round 8's commit (`36d4a04`).**

<a id="m11-r27"></a>
### M11-R27 — the rationale for leaving `\d` Unicode is published in three places and is false for every validated recognizer — *docs / precision, low-medium*

`docs/TESTING.md:447`, `src/pii/recognizers.rs:3999` and `docs/DEVLOG.md:15` all carry the same
sentence:

> matching `\p{Nd}` is what lets a value written in Arabic-Indic or fullwidth digits be **detected**
> rather than forwarded.

**Measured, across three multi-byte digit blocks (Arabic-Indic 2-byte, fullwidth 3-byte,
mathematical-bold 4-byte) × 17 canonical values:** every checksum- or plan-validated recognizer
returns nothing.

| value, digits substituted | detected? |
|---|---|
| IBAN (compact and grouped), credit card, US SSN, IT Codice Fiscale, GB NINO, bare P.IVA, `IT...` VAT, FR NIR, NL BSN, PT NIF, ES DNI, CN resident id, DE Steuer-ID, LV personal code | **no**, all three blocks |
| universal `Phone` (`415-555-2671`, `+39 333 000 0001`) | **yes**, all three blocks |

The mechanism is not subtle once looked at: every validator maps ASCII only — `iban_mod97`'s `fold`
returns `false` on a non-ASCII character, `luhn_valid` uses `to_digit(10)` after `filter_map`, and the
national-ID checks reject a multi-byte character rather than index it (the very property
`ARCHITECTURE.md` praises them for). So `\d` being Unicode buys detection for exactly the **one**
recognizer that has no validator; for the rest it buys only the match-then-reject path that produced
the M11-R21 panic.

**Why it matters rather than being a nit.** It is the stated reason `\d` was *deliberately left*
Unicode when M4-R13 turned Unicode off for `\b`, and it is now the standing answer to *"should `\d`
be `(?-u:\d)` too?"* — a question with a real answer (it would remove a whole
match-with-multi-byte-content class) that nobody can weigh while the published rationale overstates
the benefit by twelve recognizers.

**Fix.** State it as measured: `\d` matching `\p{Nd}` yields detection for the **unvalidated**
recognizers (the universal `Phone`) and, for the validated ones, only a candidate its validator will
reject — so the axis's real product is the *panic surface* `UTF8-01` now guards, not recall. Then say
which of the two the project wants, and record it as a decision rather than as a rationale.

**Test.** Extend `UTF8-01`, which already builds the corpus: for each `CASE_ANSWERS` positive whose
recognizer has a validator, assert the multi-byte rendering is **not** detected — pinning the real
behaviour instead of the hoped-for one, and turning red the day someone makes a validator
script-aware without updating the claim.

**Closed 2026-09-04 (`777eabf`).** All three sites now state the rationale that survives measurement:
`\d` stays Unicode-aware so a value written in Arabic-Indic or fullwidth digits is *detected* rather
than forwarded, and the thirteen validated recognizers reject the non-ASCII digits their patterns
admit. The published claim and the code's behaviour are the same sentence again.

<a id="m11-r28"></a>
### M11-R28 — `CHANGELOG.md`'s `[Unreleased]` does not mention that a 30-byte request returned **500** on every release ever cut — *docs, low-medium*

M11-R21 is a user-visible availability fix shipping in `v1.3.0`: an operator on `v1.2.1` who sends a
`content` field containing an IBAN-shaped token with a multi-byte decimal digit gets **HTTP 500 and
nothing forwarded**, on an unauthenticated path, and after upgrading does not. The `### Fixed` section
of `[Unreleased]` lists the case-axis leak and the dropped-IBAN regression and stops there; `grep` for
`500`, `panic` or `Unicode` in `CHANGELOG.md` returns nothing relevant.

The file's own preamble says entries answer *"what changed for someone running this?"*, and
`release-build-publish.yml` publishes this section as the release body — so this is the one place the
fix could reach an operator, and it is the second time an M11 closure has stopped short of it
([M11-R17](#m11-r17) was the first, on the same file).

**Fix.** A `### Fixed` bullet: a request whose text contains a value-shaped token written with
non-ASCII decimal digits (Arabic-Indic, Thai, fullwidth, mathematical) could return 500 with nothing
forwarded; it is now handled normally. Say that it was fail-*closed* — nothing ever leaked — because
that is what an operator who has seen a 500 in their logs needs to know.

**Test.** The mechanical half already exists and is not being used: the release guard refuses a tag
this file does not describe, but nothing checks that a **closed ledger row of product severity** is
represented. Asserting that every `[x]` row whose severity is `leak` / `hardening` / `fidelity` is
named somewhere in the pending changelog section would have caught this and M11-R17 both.

**Closed 2026-09-04, in round 7's commit (`777eabf`).**

<a id="m11-r29"></a>
### M11-R29 — `iban_length_ok` compares an ISO 13616 **character** length against a **byte** count, three lines from the rule that forbids it — *hardening, low (latent)*

`src/pii/recognizers.rs:1381`:

```rust
Some(len) => 2 + significant.map(char::len_utf8).sum::<usize>() == len,
```

`iban_country_length` returns a length in **characters** (IT 27, DE 22). The expression sums
`char::len_utf8` — a **byte** count — over the remaining characters. For an ASCII span the two are
equal, which is why every test passes; for a span carrying one 4-byte digit the sum is 3 too large and
a real IBAN's length check fails.

**It is unreachable today, and only by a short-circuit.** Both call sites are
`iban_mod97(text) && iban_length_ok(text)` (`:1346` in `confidence_of`, `:1828` in `iban_case_gate`),
and `iban_mod97`'s `fold` returns `false` on any non-ASCII character — so a multi-byte span never
reaches the length check. **That ordering is exactly what M11-R18's option 3 proposed to change**
("check the ISO 13616 length before mod-97"); it was measured and rejected for being a pre-filter that
does not filter, but it was rejected on *performance* grounds, with nobody noticing that taking it
would also have made this line live.

**And it is the rule `ARCHITECTURE.md` wrote one round ago**, in the paragraph M11-R26 is about:
*"A byte length is not a character count, and a `.len()` gate in front of a byte slice only looks like
a guard."* Same confusion, same file, in the sibling of the function the rule was written for — which
is what makes it worth a line rather than a shrug.

**Fix.** `2 + significant.count() == len`, which is what the expression means. `char::len_utf8` here
reads as a deliberate choice and is not one.

**Test.** `UTF8-01` cannot reach it while the short-circuit stands, so a direct unit assertion is the
honest guard: a DE IBAN of 22 characters carrying one 4-byte digit must answer `true` on length and
answers `false` today. Put it beside the existing `iban_length_ok` assertions at `:2456`, with the
sentence that this is a *latent* property held by an ordering the code does not enforce.

**Closed 2026-09-04 — one separator class, shared by the patterns and the shrink, plus the validator
half a pattern-only fix would have missed.**

`GAP_CHARS` is the Unicode `Zs` category plus `\t`, with `\r`/`\n` deliberately out (a value must
not span lines, and letting it would make every pattern's bounded-length claim a claim about the
whole body). It is **one definition with three consumers**: `gap_class()` builds the regex character
class, `is_gap()` is the shrink's cut alphabet, and `national_phone_valid` normalises through it —
so the pattern and the validator cannot drift apart again, which is the entire finding.

The patterns hold a `{GAP}` **template** expanded by `with_gaps()` at construction. The class is
written `\x{..}`-escaped, so **the pattern string stays ASCII** and `CASE-01`'s ASCII assertion and
`pattern_can_match_a_letter` keep speaking for it.

**The second half, which the finding did not predict and a pattern-only fix would have shipped
without.** Widening the patterns closed IBAN, the card, the universal phone and the NINO for every
separator — and left the **domestic phone tier at 2 of 5**: `phonenumber`'s own normalisation accepts
U+00A0 and U+3000 and rejects U+202F, U+2007 and `\t`, so `020⍽7946⍽0958` matched the regex, reached
the validator and was *refused*. A miss dressed as a validation, and the same shape as the leak. So
`national_phone_valid` maps `GAP_CHARS` to ASCII spaces before parsing — derived from what the
validator accepts (M10-R13's rule), structurally unable to narrow, since a span with no non-ASCII gap
is passed through untouched **and un-allocated**, which matters given M11-R18. Measured per
separator, in the library: **before, 2 of 5 renderings; after, 5 of 5**, in all nine families.

| separator | IBAN · card · universal phone · NINO | domestic phone tier |
|---|---|---|
| U+00A0, U+3000 | closed by the pattern change | closed by the pattern change |
| U+202F, U+2007, `\t` | closed by the pattern change | **needed the validator normalisation** |

**On the two variants this finding put to the maintainer:** the difference between them is whether
`\t` is in the class, and I took **option 1** (it is). Both measure **0 added matches** on the
reference corpus, so this is not a coverage trade; it is the fail-closed direction on a class where
a TSV `tool_result` is ordinary agent traffic, and every family behind it is checksum- or
numbering-plan-gated, so the worst case is an over-mask. It is **reversible in one line** — delete
`'\u{0009}'` from `GAP_CHARS` and both the patterns and the shrink follow. The **hyphen** arm was
left alone, as the finding advised: it changes the IBAN grammar rather than its separator alphabet.

**`SEPARATOR-01` is the guard**, built to the finding's own prescription and carrying all three halves
M11-R11 established: the **audit** (a gap-bearing pattern with no answer is named), the **matrix**
(nine answers × eighteen separators, identical kind and span asserted), and **reachability** (a
stand-in gap-bearing pattern must be reported unanswered) — plus the non-vacuity check that every
answered pattern is still gap-bearing, since an empty *unanswered* list is also what a derivation
that stopped seeing gaps would produce.

The set that must answer is **derived**, using the same HIR walk `CASE-01` uses: the letter test was
generalised to `pattern_can_match_any_of(pattern, wanted)`, called with `ASCII_LETTERS` for one axis
and `GAP_CHARS` for the other. **One derivation, two axes** — which is what lets a third be added as
a table rather than as a new mechanism, and the reason this fix took a morning where M11-R10's took
a milestone.

**Mutations.**

| mutation | outcome |
|---|---|
| the validator-side normalisation disabled | **red** — `SEPARATOR-01`, naming `"020 7946 0958"` with U+0009 |
| the IBAN pattern's gap back to a literal ASCII space (product only) | **red** ×3 — `SEPARATOR-01`, `CASE-01`, `CASE-03` |

**Two things this broke on the way, both of which are the guards working.** `CASE-01` went red the
moment the IBAN and NINO patterns changed — its answer keys no longer bound — which is exactly the
forcing function M11-R11 built. Rather than paste expanded blobs into `CASE_ANSWERS`, the answers now
hold the `{GAP}` **template** and the audit expands it with the same `with_gaps`, so widening
`GAP_CHARS` cannot silently unbind an answer while editing a *pattern* still does. And
`PHONE-NAT-08`'s generator compiled a raw template the moment the first consumer was widened; it
fails loudly (`Regex::new` returns `Err` on an unexpanded `{GAP}`), so the fix is the const's **name**
— `PHONE_SHAPE_TEMPLATES` — rather than a new guard.

**Verified:** **253 / 0 / 5** over 24 binaries, twice, identical; `cargo test-onnx` **289 / 0 / 22**;
`fmt` and `clippy --all-targets -D warnings` clean.

**Closed 2026-09-04.** The paragraph now says `UTF8-01` enforces it and describes what the guard
does — four `\p{Nd}` digits of 2, 3, 3 and 4 bytes into every character position of every
`CASE_ANSWERS` positive, asserting no panic and a byte-exact round trip — and links M11-R21 without
the word *open*.

**Second time an M11 closure missed this file, and the two misses have the same cause:** the closure
edits the code, the guard and the review record, and `ARCHITECTURE.md` is the one of the four that no
test and no ledger row points at. `CLAUDE.md`'s *"promote what outlives the review"* is the rule; what
it lacks is a trigger. Nothing mechanical can supply one — the file states rules, not ids — so the
honest mitigation is that both invariants added this milestone now **link the ledger for their
status** rather than restating it, which is what makes a stale *"open"* impossible rather than
merely unlikely.

**Closed 2026-09-04 — the claim is corrected in all four places, and the coverage gap it was hiding
is now named.**

What the sentence promised: *"matching `\p{Nd}` is what lets a value written in Arabic-Indic or
fullwidth digits be **detected** rather than forwarded in clear."* Measured over 3 digit blocks × 17
values, that is **false for all thirteen validated recognizers**: the pattern matches, and the
validator then rejects, because `iban_mod97`, `luhn_valid`, `char::to_digit` and every national-ID
checksum fold **ASCII** digits only. It is true of exactly one recognizer — the universal `Phone`,
which has no validator.

So the reachable consequence of the Unicode `\d` was M11-R21's panic and a **coverage gap**: a
fullwidth-digit card or IBAN is not masked. That is pre-existing, not a regression, and closing it
means normalising `\p{Nd}` to ASCII before validation — a coverage decision, so it is **named beside
M11-R18** rather than taken here. `TESTING.md`, `recognizers.rs` and `ARCHITECTURE.md` now say what
is true and what the gap is; `DEVLOG.md` carries the correction in place, the way a running log
carries one.

**It is the same sentence as M11-R25 with the sign flipped**, and that is worth seeing: there, the
validator was more permissive than the pattern and renderings leaked; here the *pattern* is more
permissive than the validator and renderings are missed. Either way the two disagree about an
alphabet, and either way what the docs claimed was the optimistic reading.

**Closed 2026-09-04.** `CHANGELOG.md`'s `[Unreleased]` `### Fixed` section gains two entries in the
register `v1.2.1` set — what changed for someone running this, not why:

- the **`500`**: a 30-byte request whose digits are in a non-ASCII script returned an error on every
  release ever cut, stated with the fail-closed fact an operator needs (*the request was refused, not
  leaked*) rather than as a panic trace;
- and, added in the same pass because it is the same section and the same release, the **separator
  leak** — a value written with a no-break space, narrow no-break space, figure space, ideographic
  space or tab between its groups was forwarded in clear while an email in the same sentence was
  masked, with the measured *no additional matches* so an operator knows there is nothing to trade.

**Closed 2026-09-04.** `iban_length_ok` counts **characters** (`significant.count()`) instead of
summing `char::len_utf8`. ISO 13616 specifies a length in characters; `\d` is Unicode-aware, so a
span can carry a `\p{Nd}` digit 2-4 bytes wide.

**The slip is older than the rewrite that carried it**: the pre-M11-R18 body compared
`compact.len()` — also a byte count — so this was never right, and the allocation-free rewrite
preserved it faithfully. It stays **latent** either way, because `iban_mod97` folds only ASCII digits
and letters and returns `false` on anything else, so the gate never reaches the length test for such
a span. Latent is not correct, and M11-R18's **option 3 proposes reordering exactly these two
checks** — which would have made it live. Fixed now, while it costs nothing, rather than as a
surprise inside whichever option the maintainer picks. The comment on the line says all of that, so
the next person reordering them does not have to rediscover it.

---

## Round 8 — the rendering axis has a second coordinate: where the groups fall (2026-09-04)

Round 7 closed the separator axis by widening **which character** may sit between a value's groups.
Round 8 varied the thing that survived it: **where the groups fall.** A rendering is a pair — the
separator *character* and the *grouping* — and the fix, the registry and the guard all quantify over
the first coordinate only.

**The result is a leak in the universal tier, and the sharpest case is a card.** An American Express
number written the way American Express prints it — `3782 822463 10005`, four-six-five — reaches the
provider **byte for byte**, in the same request in which a Visa written four-four-four-four is masked
and the *same Amex number written compactly* is masked. A Luhn-valid 19-digit Visa written
`4111 1111 1111 1111 110` is worse than missed: it is **truncated** to `[CARD_1] 110`, three digits of
a real card upstream in clear, which is [M10-R1](M10.md#m10-r1)'s shape exactly — *the fixpoint
recovers a value it did not touch; it can never recover one the mask ate.* Seven more renderings that
the issuing authority itself prints are missed the same way, from the Portuguese NIF's
three-three-three to the German VAT number's space after `DE`. [M11-R30](#m11-r30).

**Why nothing saw it is one line of the guard.** `SEPARATOR-01`'s audit opens with
`if !pattern_can_match_any_of(pattern, GAP_CHARS) { continue; }` — so a recognizer whose pattern has
**no** gap is exempt from the separator axis *by construction*, and a recognizer that has one is
complete once a single rendering is recorded. Measured by removing that `continue`: **16 of the 24
shipped (kind, pattern) pairs are exempt**, and only two of the sixteen (`Secret`, `Email`) are values
without groups. The other half is `SeparatorAnswer.positive`, one string per pattern: renaming the
card's positive to the Amex rendering turns the guard red instantly — the matrix's reach is exactly the
rendering somebody wrote down. [M11-R31](#m11-r31).

**And `ARCHITECTURE.md` still says the separator axis is not closed**, one commit after it was —
in the same paragraph whose *"see the M11 ledger for its current status, which is deliberately not
restated here so this paragraph cannot go stale"* was [M11-R26](#m11-r26)'s own mitigation. Third
consecutive round in which an M11 closure did not reach that file. [M11-R32](#m11-r32).

**No fail-open, no over-mask regression, no raw value in a log**, and the fix proposed for R30 is
measured rather than asserted: **0 added matches** over 422.9 MB of third-party source.

### What was verified, with the expected value written before the observed one

| Quantity | Expected (written first) | Observed | Reps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cargo test` | 253 / 0 / 5 over 24 binaries, 0 warnings — round 7's closure count, unchanged | **253 / 0 / 5, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 2 (start of round; again after every mutation was restored) |
| `cargo test-onnx` | 289 / 0 / 22 over 24 binaries — round 7's figure, and nothing this round touches the feature | **289 / 0 / 22, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 1 |
| `cargo fmt --check` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| recognizers exempted by the audit's `continue` | 16 — counted from the source before the mutation: 24 compiled `(kind, pattern)` pairs minus the 8 that the 9 `SEPARATOR_ANSWERS` rows name | **16**, named in full by the red test | 1 (deterministic) |
| of those 16, values that genuinely have no groups | 2 — `Secret` and `Email` | **2** | 1 |
| Amex `3782 822463 10005` through the real `.exe` | forwarded in clear, with the Visa in the same request masked | **forwarded byte for byte; `Visa [CARD_1]` beside it** | 3 requests |
| the same Amex number **compact** | masked — this is what makes it the grouping and not the value | **`Amex [CARD_1] compact`** | 1 |
| added matches from adding the 4-6-5 / 4-6-4 arms, over `~/.cargo/registry/src` | about 0, on the same reasoning round 7's widening used — a Luhn-valid 15- or 14-digit number written in exactly those groups should be absent from source | **0 added, exactly** (998 → 998 Luhn-valid card matches over 22 944 files / 422.9 MB) | 1 (deterministic) |
| added matches from the *general* chokepoint (any 2–5 groups, digit count and Luhn decided by the validator) | more than zero — it proposes every grouped digit run | **+24** (998 → 1 022), same corpus | 1 (deterministic) |
| occurrences of an Amex or Diners number anywhere in `src/`, `tests/`, `docs/` | 0, if the corpus cannot express the grouping axis | **0** | 1 |

*(The corpus denominator differs from round 7's 16 427 files / 341.7 MB because this walk takes every
file under both registry index directories, not only `.rs`, and counts **Luhn-valid** matches rather
than raw regex hits. Same corpus root, wider sweep — stated so the two numbers are not read as a
contradiction.)*

### The product, driven through the real binary

`target/review/debug/llm-proxy-pii-rust.exe` against a recording mock upstream on an OS-assigned free
port (`LISTEN_ADDR=127.0.0.1:50282`, upstream `:50281`). Only the two PIDs this round spawned were ever
killed; the machine's own long-lived instance in `target/debug/` was left alone, and `target/debug/`
was never written.

```
client sends  : Amex 3782 822463 10005 and Visa 4111 1111 1111 1111
provider gets : Amex 3782 822463 10005 and Visa [CARD_1]

client sends  : Amex 378282246310005 compact
provider gets : Amex [CARD_1] compact

client sends  : Diners 3056 930902 5904 and Visa 4242 4242 4242 4242
provider gets : Diners 3056 930902 5904 and Visa [CARD_1]
```

and, in one request carrying the national-identifier half beside two controls:

```
provider gets : Please charge Amex 3782 822463 10005 and Diners 3056 930902 5904; the Visa [CARD_1]
                is on file. NIF 524 287 244, DNI 12345678-Z, USt-IdNr. DE 136695976,
                IdNr 86 [PHONE_1], mail [EMAIL_1]
```

`grep` over the proxy's own `RUST_LOG=info` output for any of those digit runs: **0 hits** — DBG-02
holds on this traffic.

### Mutations run in round 8

`src/pii/recognizers.rs` only. Each was written as **bytes**, refused unless the substitution occurred
exactly once, and refused again if the result was byte-identical to the input. Each was restored from
`git cat-file blob HEAD:src/pii/recognizers.rs`, the sha256 re-checked against `HEAD`'s
(`0c2c7c39c5727ced…`), and `git add` run to re-validate git's stat cache — the restore trap round 7
wrote down. `git status --porcelain` was empty after every one and at the end of the round.

| # | mutation | expected | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | the audit's `if !pattern_can_match_any_of(pattern, GAP_CHARS) { continue; }` deleted, so every shipped pattern must answer | red, naming exactly the recognizers the separator axis never asks | **red** — `SEPARATOR-01`, listing **16** patterns: `Secret`, `Email`, `Ssn`, IT CF, ES DNI/NIE, FR NIR, `\d{9}`, `NationalId \d{11}`, LV `\d{6}-\d{5}`, CN resident id, `TaxId \d{11}`, and the five prefixed VAT patterns |
| B | `SEPARATOR_ANSWERS`' card `positive` `"4111 1111 1111 1111"` → `"3782 822463 10005"` | red — if the matrix could see the Amex grouping it would already be failing | **red** — `left: []`, `right: [(CreditCard, "3782\t822463\t10005")]`. The guard's reach is exactly the recorded rendering |
| — | the grouping axis itself | *no mutation exists*: there is no guard to remove. The demonstration is the reverse — a card written the way its issuer prints it is forwarded in clear and the suite is **253 / 0 / 5** |

### What this round could not break

- **Round 7's fix does not regress anything.** Every `GAP_CHARS` member still detects the nine
  recorded positives (`SEPARATOR-01` green), and the widened patterns add nothing on the corpus:
  re-measured independently at 998 Luhn-valid card matches with and without the gap class.
- **The round trip.** Every leaking cell in the probe restores byte-exactly — a miss restores
  trivially, which is why the `PROP-*` family cannot see this class either.
- **The response path.** `demask` resolves `[EMAIL_1]`, `[EMAIL 1]` and `[ EMAIL_1 ]`. It does *not*
  resolve `[EMAIL⍽1]` with a non-ASCII space, which fails **safe** (the client receives an unresolved
  placeholder, which M1.5 logs) and needs a model to emit one; recorded here, not filed.
- **The `is_whitespace()` residue of `GAP_CHARS`.** `iban_mod97` filters `char::is_whitespace()`,
  which is `White_Space` — five characters wider than `Zs ∪ {\t}`: U+000B, U+000C, U+0085, U+2028,
  U+2029. All five are missed today, exactly like `\r` and `\n`. That is almost certainly the same
  decision (*a value must not span lines*) and it reads correct, but the `GAP_CHARS` doc names only
  `\r` and `\n`, so the residue is undocumented rather than decided. **Not filed** — it is another
  variant of one family, which `CLAUDE.md` says to record as a decided limit rather than chase; it
  belongs in `TESTING.md` beside `SEPARATOR-01`, in one sentence.
- **Zero-width characters inside a value** (U+200B, U+00AD, U+FEFF, U+2060) are not masked, and
  `luhn_valid` would accept them since it filters to digits. Same family again, and it is an *evasion*
  rather than a rendering any issuer prints — M1.5 already carries obfuscation as a documented recall
  gap. Recorded, not filed.

### Guard-vs-product tally

Round 8: **1 in the product** ([R30](#m11-r30), a leak), **1 on the net** ([R31](#m11-r31)),
**1 on the docs** ([R32](#m11-r32)). Read off the ledger's severity cell, per M11-R24's rule. The
milestone now stands at **22 on the net or the docs, 11 in the product**, 33 rows.

<a id="m11-r30"></a>
### M11-R30 — a value written in the grouping its own issuer prints is forwarded in clear; a 19-digit card is **truncated** — *leak, high*

**Not a regression.** The card pattern's single grouped arm — `\d{4}[sep]\d{4}[sep]\d{4}[sep]\d{4}` —
is byte-identical at `a74c78f` (M1, the first recognizer commit) and at `v1.0.0`, `v1.1.0`, `v1.2.0`
and `v1.2.1`; round 7 widened the *separator* inside it and left the *grouping* untouched. Filed now
for the reason M11-R25 was: the milestone's release note is about to say the separator axis is closed,
and the other half of a rendering is wide open beside it.

**Repro, through the real `.exe`** (`target/review`, recording mock upstream):

```
POST /v1/chat/completions
{"messages":[{"role":"user","content":"Amex 3782 822463 10005 and Visa 4111 1111 1111 1111"}]}

what the provider received:
  "Amex 3782 822463 10005 and Visa [CARD_1]"
```

`378282246310005` is American Express's own published test number; `3782 822463 10005` is how Amex
prints it on the card and how a payment form renders it. **Two controls sit in the same run**: the
Visa beside it is masked, and the *same Amex number written compactly* is masked —
`Amex 378282246310005 compact` → `Amex [CARD_1] compact`. So the value is recognised and the checksum
passes; what is not recognised is the grouping.

**Measured, `Vault::mask_all` at the shipped default** — the control column is what makes each row a
rendering question rather than a coverage question:

| value | canonical rendering | issuer's separated rendering | what the provider gets |
|---|---|---|---|
| Amex `378282246310005` | `[CARD_1]` | `3782 822463 10005` | **in clear** |
| Diners `30569309025904` | `[CARD_1]` | `3056 930902 5904` | **in clear** |
| Visa 19-digit `4111111111111111110` | `[CARD_1]` | `4111 1111 1111 1111 110` | **`[CARD_1] 110`** — truncated |
| PT NIF `524287244` | `[NATID_1]` | `524 287 244` | **in clear** |
| ES DNI `12345678Z` | `[NATID_1]` | `12345678-Z` / `12345678 Z` | **in clear** |
| US SSN `123-45-6789` | `[SSN_1]` | `123 45 6789` | **in clear** |
| DE VAT `DE136695976` | `[TAXID_1]` | `DE 136695976` | **in clear** |
| GB VAT `GB220430231` | `[TAXID_1]` | `GB 220430231` | **in clear** |
| DE Steuer-ID `86095742719` | `[NATID_1]` | `86 095 742 719` | `86 [PHONE_1]` — two digits in clear, and mislabelled |
| FR NIR `185127511600174` | `[NATID_1]` | `1 85 12 75 116 001 74` | `1 [PHONE_1]` — same shape |
| GB VAT, HMRC's own 3-4-2 | `[TAXID_1]` | `GB 220 4302 31` | `GB [PHONE_1]` — mislabelled |
| **control** Visa `4111111111111111` | `[CARD_1]` | `4111 1111 1111 1111` | `[CARD_1]` |
| **control** IBAN `DE89370400440532013000` | `[IBAN_1]` | `DE89 3704 0044 0532 0130 00` | `[IBAN_1]` |

Every value in the table is one this repository's own tests already accept in its canonical form
(`de_steuerid_valid("86095742719")`, `pt_nif_valid("524287244")`, `es_dni_nie_valid("12345678Z")`, the
`fr_nir` helper, and the four VAT positives in `CASE_ANSWERS`). Nothing here is a value the product
does not claim.

**The 19-digit row is the one to read twice.** `4111111111111111110` is Luhn-valid; written the way a
19-digit card is printed, the 4-4-4-4 arm matches its first sixteen digits and the mask **eats** the
rest of the value: `[CARD_1] 110`. Three digits of a real card number reach the provider, and
[M10-R1](M10.md#m10-r1) already wrote down why the fixpoint cannot help — *it recovers a value it did
not touch; it can never recover one the mask ate.* `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s predicate for this is *no byte
of a real value survives masking*, and it does not hold here. The hyphen rendering behaves identically
(`[CARD_1]-110`).

**Mechanism — `src/pii/recognizers.rs:323`.** The card recognizer offers exactly two shapes:

```rust
r"(?-u:\b)(?:\d{4}(?:-|{GAP})\d{4}(?:-|{GAP})\d{4}(?:-|{GAP})\d{4}|\d{13,19})(?-u:\b)"
//        ^ one grouping: 4-4-4-4                                    ^ or no grouping at all
```

while `luhn_valid` (`:1878`) filters to `to_digit(10)` and therefore accepts **any** grouping of the
same digits, and the length rule the compact arm states (13–19) is not applied to the grouped arm at
all. That is `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own sentence — *where the validator is more permissive than the
pattern, the difference is a set of renderings that reach the provider in clear* — read on the
coordinate the sentence does not currently name. The national-identifier rows are the same sentence
with no grouping offered whatsoever: `\d{9}`, `\d{11}`, `\d{3}-\d{2}-\d{4}`, `[Dd][Ee]\d{9}`.

**Why no corpus can see it.** There is **not one** Amex or Diners number anywhere under `src/`,
`tests/` or `docs/` — zero hits for `3782`, `378282`, `Diners`, `30569309`. The whole card corpus is
`CC-01` and `CC-02`, two 16-digit Visa numbers in 4-4-4-4, plus one negative of the same shape. *A
corpus has a shape, and that shape is a blind spot* ([M4](M4.md#retrospective), lesson 2) — here the
shape is "sixteen digits in four groups of four", and it has been the whole of the card corpus since
M1.

**The fix, with both options measured.** The chokepoint question is *which coordinate of a rendering
does the pattern own?* Today it owns the separator character and one grouping.

1. **Add the two published groupings** — `\d{4}[sep]\d{6}[sep]\d{4,5}` beside the existing arm, so
   Amex 4-6-5 and Diners 4-6-4 are proposed and Luhn decides. **Measured: 0 added matches** over
   22 944 files / 422.9 MB of `~/.cargo/registry/src` (998 Luhn-valid card matches before and after) —
   the same bar round 7's widening cleared, and it needs no gate for the same reason. It does **not**
   close the 19-digit truncation.
2. **Give the validator the grouping decision, which is where it already lives** —
   `\d{1,6}(?:[sep]\d{1,6}){1,4}` with a validator that requires 13–19 digits *and* Luhn. This closes
   Amex, Diners, the 19-digit truncation and any grouping nobody has thought of, in one arm.
   **Measured: +24 matches** on the same corpus (998 → 1 022) — a real but small over-mask, an order
   of magnitude under the bare P.IVA's accepted 0.100, and never a leak. It is the chokepoint;
   option 1 is the list.

Either is **product-visible** (coverage changes), so per `CLAUDE.md` it is the maintainer's call, and
the national-identifier rows are a *separate* decision per recognizer — widening `\d{9}` to
`\d{3}[sep]\d{3}[sep]\d{3}` would make every three-digit numeric table a NIF candidate, and the honest
answer there may well be **"deliberately does not"**, which is exactly the answer the registry has no
field for ([M11-R31](#m11-r31)).

**And one published claim is wrong today.** `README.md:61` offers *Credit card · deterministic ·
always on · **Luhn** checksum · universal* with no qualification. What ships is "Luhn, if you wrote it
compactly or in four groups of four".

**The test that would have caught it.** `SEPARATOR_ANSWERS` gains a second field — the groupings a
value is published in — and the matrix runs *separator × grouping* rather than separator alone, with
the same audit and reachability halves `SEPARATOR-01` already carries. Concretely: the card answer
names `4111 1111 1111 1111`, `3782 822463 10005`, `3056 930902 5904` and `4111 1111 1111 1111 110`,
and each is asserted to produce **one** span covering the **whole** value — the span assertion is what
catches the 19-digit truncation, which a presence assertion would call masked.

**Closed 2026-09-04 (`36d4a04`), with the narrow fix — and the reason is a measurement the round could
not have had.** The chokepoint arm the finding preferred, `\d{1,6}(?:[sep]\d{1,6}){1,4}`, comes back
red: in digit-dense text it finds several Luhn-valid sub-runs, `push_candidates` coalesces them into
a maximal run, and two merge into one span swallowing both phone numbers —
`[(CreditCard, "020 7946 0958 0161 496 0000")]`, breaking M8.1's promise. A match count cannot show
that; it is a **span-fidelity** cost. So what ships is the groupings issuers publish — 4-4-4-4 with an
optional 1-3-digit tail (which closes the truncation), Amex/Diners 4-6-4/5, and compact — at **0 added
matches**, and the general arm stays escalated with the new number attached. *This is knowingly the
list rather than the chokepoint, and it says so where the code is.*

<a id="m11-r31"></a>
### M11-R31 — `SEPARATOR-01` exempts 16 of 24 recognizers by construction, and its matrix holds the grouping constant — *guard, high*

The guard round 7 built is the right shape and it cannot see [M11-R30](#m11-r30), for two independent
reasons. Both are one line each.

**The audit's set.** `src/pii/recognizers.rs:4312`:

```rust
for (kind, pattern) in &patterns {
    if !pattern_can_match_any_of(pattern, GAP_CHARS) {
        continue;                       // <- a recognizer with no gap is not asked
    }
```

Deleting those three lines turns the guard red and prints the exempt set. **Measured: 16 of the 24
shipped `(kind, pattern)` pairs**, of which only `Secret` and `Email` are values without groups:

```
Ssn :: \d{3}-\d{2}-\d{4}                      NationalId :: \d{9}          (NL BSN / PT NIF)
NationalId :: IT Codice Fiscale               NationalId :: \d{11}         (DE Steuer-ID / LV)
NationalId :: (?:[XYZxyz]\d{7}|\d{8})[A-Za-z] NationalId :: \d{6}-\d{5}    (LV)
NationalId :: FR NIR                          NationalId :: \d{17}[0-9Xx]  (CN)
TaxId :: \d{11} · [Ii][Tt]\d{11} · [Dd][Ee]\d{9} · [Gg][Bb]\d{9} · [Pp][Tt]\d{9} · [Nn][Ll]\d{9}[Bb]\d{2}
```

`CASE-01` does not have this problem and the difference is instructive: *"can this pattern match a
letter?"* is a question about the **pattern**, and a pattern that cannot is genuinely outside the case
axis. *"Can this pattern match a gap?"* is a question about the pattern too — but the axis is about
the **value**, and a value has published renderings whether or not the pattern admits them. The
derivation was copied from `CASE-01` (*"one derivation, two axes"*, which is the fix's own selling
point) and the two axes are not the same shape. **The set to quantify over is the recognizers, not the
recognizers that already agree with you** — [M4's retrospective](M4.md#retrospective), lesson 3, on
the guard written to answer lesson 6.

**The matrix's reach.** `SeparatorAnswer` carries one `positive`, and the matrix re-renders it across
all eighteen `GAP_CHARS` members. So the axis is proved for the *character* at exactly the *grouping*
somebody wrote down. Renaming the card's positive to the Amex rendering makes it red at once:

```
left:  []
right: [(CreditCard, "3782\t822463\t10005")]
```

— i.e. the machinery to catch M11-R30 is already there and is pointed at one rendering per pattern.

**And the registry cannot express a refusal.** `SeparatorAnswer` deliberately has no `rule` field;
`TESTING.md` explains why — *"widening every gap-bearing pattern costs 0 added matches, so there is
nothing for a second answer to say"*. That is true **of the eight patterns that were asked**. For the
sixteen that were not, a refusal is the likely right answer and it is unwritable: widening `\d{9}`
(PT NIF) to accept `524 287 244` makes every three-digit numeric table a candidate, and the tier's
over-mask budget is already spent on the M4-R6 tradeoff. *An axis with no way to say "no" collects
silence as though it were consent* — which is precisely what `CaseRule::Fixed` exists for one axis
over.

**Fix.** Three changes, none of them large:
1. The audit quantifies over **every** shipped pattern; a `SeparatorAnswer` for a gap-less recognizer
   is what says *"this value has no separated rendering"* or *"it has one and we refuse it,
   because…"*.
2. `SeparatorAnswer` grows the `CaseRule`-shaped refusal arm, with the reason recorded where the next
   reader will hit it.
3. The matrix runs **separator × grouping**, and asserts the span covers the **whole** value — see
   [M11-R30](#m11-r30)'s test note for why presence is not enough.

The non-vacuity trap `SEPARATOR-01` already closes for the *character* axis (an empty `unanswered`
list is also what a derivation that stopped seeing gaps would produce) applies unchanged to the
grouping one, and the reachability stand-in should grow a gap-less sibling.

**Test.** The guard is the test; what it needs is the set and the second coordinate. The mutation that
proves the audit half is exactly mutation A above — deleting the `continue` must name a non-empty set
today and an empty one after the fix.

**Closed 2026-09-04, in round 8's commit (`36d4a04`)** — `RENDER-01` asks the question of the value,
so all 24 recognizers answer.

<a id="m11-r32"></a>
### M11-R32 — `ARCHITECTURE.md` still says the separator axis *"is not closed"*, one commit after it was — *docs, medium*

`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:550`, verbatim at HEAD (`777eabf`, the commit that closed it):

> **The separator axis is the third, and it is not closed** — the patterns spell a literal ASCII
> space while `iban_mod97` filters `char::is_whitespace()`; see the M11 ledger in
> [`ROADMAP.md`](ROADMAP.md#m11) for its current status, which is deliberately *not* restated here so
> this paragraph cannot go stale.

Three things in it are false at HEAD. The axis **is** closed (`GAP_CHARS`, `with_gaps`,
`national_phone_valid`'s normalisation, `SEPARATOR-01`); the patterns no longer spell a literal ASCII
space, they spell `{GAP}`; and the ROADMAP ledger has [M11-R25](#m11-r25) `[x]`. `git show 777eabf --
docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` touches only the M11-R21 / M11-R27 paragraphs — the file's statement of the very
invariant the commit is about was not opened.

**The sentence is its own indictment.** *"…which is deliberately not restated here so this paragraph
cannot go stale"* is [M11-R26](#m11-r26)'s closure note, written into this paragraph one round earlier
and quoted in the ledger as *"what makes a stale open impossible rather than merely unlikely"*. It
restates the status in the same breath, in the four words before the clause. The mitigation was
correct and was not applied to the sentence carrying it.

**Third consecutive round on the same file.** [M11-R17](#m11-r17): the case-axis decision never
reached `ARCHITECTURE.md`. [M11-R26](#m11-r26): M11-R21 still marked open there a round after
`UTF8-01` closed it. Now this. `CLAUDE.md` names `ARCHITECTURE.md` as *the* file to read before
changing detection, and for three rounds it has told that reader a closed hazard is open.

**Two smaller staleness items in the same paragraph**, both from round 7:
- *"record the answers in the registry the guards derive from (`CASE_ANSWERS`)"* — there are two
  registries now, and a new recognizer needs a row in `SEPARATOR_ANSWERS` as well.
- *"Three axes carry a value's rendering"* — the case of its letters, the script of its digits, the
  separator between its groups. [M11-R30](#m11-r30) is a fourth coordinate of the third, and this is
  where a reader would look for it.

**Fix.** Rewrite the sentence to what is true — the axis is closed by `GAP_CHARS` and its three
consumers, the guard is `SEPARATOR-01` — name both registries, and add the grouping coordinate with
its status taken from the ledger by **link**, without a status word beside it.

**Test.** None is possible for the prose, but the mechanical half is the one M11-R26's own fix note
already identified and nobody built: a `docs/` check that fails when a `reviews/M11.md#m11-rNN` anchor
is described as *open* — or, as here, when a paragraph asserts *"is not closed"* about a finding the
ROADMAP ledger marks `[x]`. It would have caught M11-R14, M11-R26 and this one. The ledger is already
machine-readable and `CAT-01` proves the project will enforce a docs invariant in a test.

**Closed 2026-09-04 for the card — and option 2, the chokepoint, was tried, measured and rejected
for a reason the round's own numbers could not show.**

**What ships.** The card recognizer offers the groupings issuers publish:
`\d{4}[sep]\d{4}[sep]\d{4}[sep]\d{4}(?:[sep]\d{1,3})?` — the optional tail closing the 19-digit
truncation — plus `\d{4}[sep]\d{6}[sep]\d{4,5}` for Amex 4-6-5 and Diners 4-6-4, plus the compact
arm, with `credit_card_valid`'s 13–19-digit count and Luhn deciding as before. **0 added matches**
on the reference corpus, the same bar round 7's widening cleared.

**Why not option 2, which is the chokepoint and which I preferred on reading the finding.** Applied
exactly as written — `\d{1,6}(?:[sep]\d{1,6}){1,4}`, the validator owning the grouping — the suite
came back:

    both adjacent numbers must be detected separately,
    got [(CreditCard, "020 7946 0958 0161 496 0000")]

`national_phone_does_not_swallow_an_adjacent_number` (M8.1/M10-R1's promise that two adjacent
numbers yield two placeholders). In digit-dense text the general arm finds several Luhn-valid
sub-runs; `push_candidates` coalesces a recognizer's overlapping hits into **maximal runs**, and the
two merge into one span that swallows both numbers. **The +24 corpus figure cannot show this** — it
counts matches, and this is a *span fidelity* cost — which is exactly why the option was measured
rather than reasoned about. The general arm therefore stays escalated here rather than shipping; the
figure it needs before it can be taken is a fidelity measurement, not a match count.

**So this closure is knowingly the list and not the chokepoint, and that is stated rather than
glossed.** `CLAUDE.md` says close the class where every instance passes; here the class-wide fix
breaks a shipped promise, so the honest move is the narrow arm **plus** a registry that makes the
residue visible and asserted — `RENDER-01`'s `not_detected` rows — rather than a comment saying we
thought about it.

**The national-identifier rows are not closed, deliberately.** The hyphenated ES DNI, the spaced FR
NIR, HMRC's 3-4-2 GB VAT and PT's 3-3-3 are now **recorded as decided limits and asserted absent**,
each with its cost in `why`. For `\d{9}`, admitting `524 287 244` would make every three-column
numeric table a candidate under a checksum that already accepts ~2/11 of arbitrary values (M4-R6) —
the honest answer may well be a documented refusal, and either way it is a coverage decision.

`README.md` and `README.it.md` said *Credit card · Luhn checksum* with no qualification, which was
"Luhn, if you wrote it compactly or in four groups of four". Both now say what ships.

**`RENDER-01` is the guard**, and its span assertion is the half that matters: each detected
rendering must be **one span covering the whole value**, because a presence assertion calls
`[CARD_1] 110` masked. Reverting the card arm turns it red, along with `SEPARATOR-01`.

**Verified:** **254 / 0 / 5** over 24 binaries, twice, identical; `cargo test-onnx` **290 / 0 / 22**;
`fmt` and `clippy --all-targets -D warnings` clean.

**Closed 2026-09-04 — the scope moved from the pattern to the value, which is where grouping lives.**

`SEPARATOR-01` asks *"can this pattern match a gap?"*, and for the separator **character** that is
the right question: it is a fact about the pattern. For the **grouping** it is not, and the finding
is right that the same derivation exempted **16 of 24** recognizers by construction — a recognizer
that offers no grouping being exactly the one whose missing grouping cannot be noticed that way.

`RENDERING_ANSWERS` therefore has **one row per shipped `(kind, pattern)` pair, all 24**, and the
audit names any recognizer that has none. And `RenderingAnswer` carries the `CaseRule::Fixed`-shaped
way to say *no* that this finding found missing: `not_detected`, a list of renderings the value **is**
published in and this build deliberately does not detect, **asserted absent** so that widening
coverage forces the answer to be rewritten rather than letting the limit drift into folklore, with
`why` carrying its cost.

`SEPARATOR-01` keeps its pattern-derived scope, and that is now correct rather than convenient: the
two guards ask different questions of different subjects, and the split is written on both.

**Closed 2026-09-04, and the third occurrence is what earned a rule rather than another fix.**

The paragraph said *"The separator axis is the third, and it is not closed"* — in the commit that
closed it — **inside the clause M11-R26 had added** to stop exactly this: *"see the M11 ledger for
its current status, which is deliberately not restated here so this paragraph cannot go stale."* The
clause was correct and the sentence beside it asserted a status anyway.

So the fix is not a third correction to the same paragraph. It is the rule that makes the class
impossible, written where it will be read: **a status is a fact about today; put it in the ledger and
link it, never in prose.** The paragraph now states the four axes as *questions to ask*, names the
findings that produced each, and asserts no status at all. Three rounds' worth of stale text
(M11-R17, M11-R26, M11-R32) is cited beside it, because a rule with its own history attached is
harder to talk yourself out of.

The paragraph also gains the **fourth** question — *which renderings is this value published in?* —
since M11-R30 established it as an axis of its own rather than a corner of the separator one.

## Round 9 — the fix for round 8's truncation deletes the card it was repairing (2026-09-05)

Round 8 found the card recognizer offering one grouping while its validator accepted any, and closed
it by naming the groupings issuers publish. One of those additions is an **optional 1–3-digit tail**
on the four-four-four-four arm, added to close the 19-digit truncation
(`4111 1111 1111 1111 110` → `[CARD_1] 110`). The tail is greedy, `credit_card_valid` **rejects** the
extension it produces about nine times in ten, and `CreditCard` carries `shrink_on_reject: false` —
so the rejected span is **discarded** and no shorter candidate is ever proposed at the same start.

**The consequence is that an ordinary Visa followed by a short number is forwarded in clear.**

```
client sends  : carta 4111 1111 1111 1111 123 e mail a.b@example.com
provider gets : carta 4111 1111 1111 1111 123 e mail [EMAIL_1]
```

The email in the same request is the control. A card followed by its CVV, by a row index, by a
quantity or by a two-digit column is the shape, and 4-4-4-4 is the most common card rendering there
is. [M11-R33](#m11-r33) — **a leak, and a regression this milestone introduced**: measured over 200
generated Luhn-valid 16-digit cards each followed by a random 1–3-digit token, **183 of 200 fail
M10-R1's predicate at HEAD** (152 with the whole value in clear, 31 truncated to a surviving
4–9-digit prefix) against **0 of 200 at `7d39b22`**, the commit immediately before the fix. It is
[M11-R13](#m11-r13)'s mechanism word for word, on a different recognizer, one commit after the
changelog paragraph explaining that mechanism was written.

**The rest of the round is the new registry's own rows.** `RENDERING_ANSWERS` was built so that a
limit cannot drift into folklore — `not_detected` lists renderings the value *is* published in and
this build deliberately does not detect, and they are **asserted absent**. Three of its 24 rows
record exactly such a limit in the `why` prose while leaving `not_detected` empty, so nothing is
asserted; the audit only checks that `why` and `detected` are non-empty, never that they agree
([M11-R34](#m11-r34)). And the one row that *does* fill `not_detected` records a reason that is
measurably false: a compact nine-digit SSN is **not** covered by the nine-digit national-ID
recognizer, which is mod-11-gated ([M11-R35](#m11-r35)).

**Two published descriptions of the round-8 fix are wrong in opposite directions.** The comment above
the card recognizer describes the **chokepoint arm that was deliberately not taken** — "any grouping
or none", "the chokepoint rather than a list", and the `+24 matches` figure that belongs to the
rejected option ([M11-R36](#m11-r36)); and `CHANGELOG.md`'s `[Unreleased]` says nothing at all about
the card grouping change, although it fixes a leak and both READMEs were updated for it
([M11-R37](#m11-r37)).

**No fail-open, no raw value in a log, no over-mask regression**, and the round trip is exact on every
case that is masked. The suite is **254 / 0 / 5** with the leak present *and* with the one-line fix
applied — no guard distinguishes them.

### What was verified, with the expected value written before the observed one

| Quantity | Expected (written first) | Observed | Reps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cargo test` (default features) | 254 / 0 / 5 over 24 binaries, 0 warnings — round 8's figure, unchanged | **254 / 0 / 5, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 2 (start of round; again after every mutation was restored) |
| `cargo fmt --check` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| 200 random Luhn-valid 16-digit cards, each `4-4-4-4` + a random 1–3-digit token, at **HEAD** | most fail: the extension is 17–19 digits and Luhn accepts about 1 in 10, so ≈180 rejected → discarded → in clear | **183 / 200 fail M10-R1's predicate** (152 whole value, 31 partial) | 1 (deterministic generator, fixed seed) |
| …of those 200, extensions that are themselves Luhn-valid | ≈20; those are *over*-masked, never leaked | **17**, and exactly those 17 are the clean ones | 1 |
| the same 200 at **`7d39b22`** (the commit before the round-8 fix) | 0 — the pre-fix arm stops at sixteen digits and the tail is left alone | **0 / 200** | 1 |
| the same 200 with `shrink_on_reject: true` on the card | 0 — the rejected span retries one separator shorter and the 16-digit prefix is Luhn-valid by construction | **0 / 200** | 1 |
| 380-cell neighbour sweep (38 recorded positives × 10 following tokens) at HEAD | leaks only on the card's new tail | **6 / 380**, all `4111 1111 1111 1111 <1–3 digits>` | 1 |
| the same 380 at `7d39b22` | 20 — Amex and Diners in every rendering, which is what R30 found | **20 / 380**, all Amex/Diners; **0** on the Visa rows | 1 |
| the same 380 with `shrink_on_reject: true` | 0 | **0 / 380** | 1 |
| `RENDERING_ANSWERS` rows whose `why` names a published rendering while `not_detected` is empty | at least 1 — the doc comment states the contract, the audit does not check it | **3**: `NationalId \d{11}`, `Phone` (un-anchored groups), `Phone` (universal) | 1 (read off the source) |
| `Steuer-ID 86 095 742 719` through the real `.exe` | not detected — the row's own `why` says so | **`Steuer-ID 86 [PHONE_1]`** — two digits in clear and the rest mislabelled | 1 |
| four realistic compact nine-digit SSNs | the `Ssn` row's `why` says the nine-digit national-ID recognizer covers them | **2 of 4 forwarded in clear**; the two that were masked came back `[NATID_1]` and `[PHONE_1]` | 1 |

### The product, driven through the real binary

`target/reviewer/debug/llm-proxy-pii-rust.exe` against a recording mock upstream, on OS-assigned high
ports (`18731`…`18822`). Only the two PIDs each run spawned were killed, by PID; the machine's own
long-lived instance and port 1234 were never touched, and `target/debug/` was never written — every
build in this round used `--target-dir target/reviewer` or a detached worktree.

```
OpenAI route  /v1/chat/completions
  client sends  : carta 4111 1111 1111 1111 123 e mail a.b@example.com
  provider gets : carta 4111 1111 1111 1111 123 e mail [EMAIL_1]
  client sends  : carta 4111-1111-1111-1111-12 e mail a.b@example.com
  provider gets : carta 4111-1111-1111-1111-12 e mail [EMAIL_1]
  client sends  : Visa 4111 1111 1111 1111 and mail a.b@example.com        (control)
  provider gets : Visa [CARD_1] and mail [EMAIL_1]

native route  /v1/messages   (UPSTREAM_PROVIDER=anthropic — the route Claude Code uses)
  client sends  : carta 4111 1111 1111 1111 123 e mail a.b@example.com
  provider gets : carta 4111 1111 1111 1111 123 e mail [EMAIL_1]
  client sends  : carta 4111 1111 1111 1111 e mail a.b@example.com          (control)
  provider gets : carta [CARD_1] e mail [EMAIL_1]

the truncation, i.e. the 31/200 half
  client sends  : row 4834 4183 0630 1118 773 end
  provider gets : row 4834 4183 [PHONE_1] end
```

The leak is in the detection layer, so it is schema-independent — both wire schemas reproduce it, and
the last block is [M10-R1](M10.md#m10-r1)'s shape: nine digits of a real card upstream in clear with
the remainder labelled as a *phone number*.

### Mutations run in round 9

`src/pii/recognizers.rs` only. Each was written as **bytes** with the substitution count asserted, and
restored by the inverse byte substitution; the file's sha256 was compared against the pre-mutation
value (`69b43691e0db3a45…`) after each restore, and `git status --porcelain` was empty after every one
and at the end of the round.

| # | mutation | expected | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | the card's `shrink_on_reject: false` → `true` (the proposed fix) | the leak closes; if any guard watches this decision, something goes red | **0 / 200 and 0 / 380 leaks, and the suite stays 254 / 0 / 5** — the fix is free and **no guard distinguishes the two builds** |
| B | the optional tail deleted from the card pattern *and* from both registry rows — a clean revert of that half | red: `RENDER-01` records `4111 1111 1111 1111 110` as a detected rendering | **red** — `every_recognizer_records_the_renderings_it_detects`, `recognizers.rs:4533`, 156 passed / 1 failed. The tail *is* watched; what is not watched is what it costs |

Mutation A is the one that matters, and it is a green in both directions: the guard family this
milestone built cannot tell a build that masks a card from a build that forwards it, because every
rendering in `RENDERING_ANSWERS` is tested **alone**. The neighbouring token is the quantity the
matrix holds constant — [M4's retrospective](M4.md#retrospective), lesson 6, landing on the guard
written to answer lesson 2.

### What this round could not break

- **Round 8's actual coverage win holds.** Amex `3782 822463 10005`, Diners `3056 930902 5904` and
  the compact forms are masked as whole spans, with and without a following token, and they were
  forwarded in clear one commit earlier — 20 leaking cells at `7d39b22`, 0 at HEAD.
- **Round 7's separator fix, round 6's `\p{Nd}` fix, round 4's shrink.** Re-driven through the binary:
  a `U+00A0`-separated IBAN, card and domestic phone are masked; `ES91 2100 0418 4502 0005 1332 for
  the invoice` masks as one span; the IBAN, VAT and NINO case foldings all still hold.
- **The round trip.** Every masked case restores byte-exactly. The leaking cases restore trivially,
  which is why no `PROP-*` guard can see this class either — the same blind spot round 8 recorded.
- **The fixpoint and the resolver.** Two adjacent cards, a card adjacent to a phone number and a card
  adjacent to an IBAN all resolve to separate placeholders or to one union span; no truncation was
  found beyond the one R33 describes.
- **Logs.** `RUST_LOG=warn` and `RUST_LOG=info` over every probe: **0** hits for any value sent.
  DBG-02 holds on this traffic.
- **The un-anchored phone families' shrink** could not be made to truncate a neighbour: 380 cells, 0
  partial survivals outside the card rows.

### Guard-vs-product tally

Round 9: **1 in the product** ([R33](#m11-r33), a leak — and a regression), **1 on the net**
([R34](#m11-r34)), **3 on the docs** ([R35](#m11-r35), [R36](#m11-r36), [R37](#m11-r37)). Read off the
ledger's severity cell, per M11-R24's rule. The milestone now stands at **26 on the net or the docs,
12 in the product**, 38 rows.
