<a id="m11-r33"></a>
### M11-R33 — round 8's optional tail turns a real card into a **dropped** span: a Visa followed by a 1–3-digit token is forwarded in clear — *leak, blocker*

**A regression, introduced by `36d4a04`.** The card arm gained an optional trailing group —
`src/pii/recognizers.rs:342`:

```
(?-u:\b)(?:\d{4}(?:-|{GAP})\d{4}(?:-|{GAP})\d{4}(?:-|{GAP})\d{4}(?:(?:-|{GAP})\d{1,3})?|…)(?-u:\b)
                                                                ^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^^
```

and `src/pii/recognizers.rs:347` still reads `shrink_on_reject: false`.

**Mechanism, and it is [M11-R13](#m11-r13)'s word for word.** The tail is greedy, so on
`4111 1111 1111 1111 123` the regex proposes the **19-digit** span. `credit_card_valid`
(`recognizers.rs:1888`) folds every digit of the match and requires Luhn over all of them, so it
**rejects** — an arbitrary 1–3-digit extension keeps Luhn about one time in ten. `push_candidates`
(`recognizers.rs:1117`) then calls `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix`, which returns `None` on its first line
because `shrink_on_reject` is `false` (`recognizers.rs:1201`). The candidate is discarded.
`Scan::Overlapping` resumes one `char` past the match's **start**, and from no later position does the
text offer four groups of four again — so **no card candidate is ever produced**, and the resolver
never learns the value exists.

The half M11-R13 named is the same: *a validator that rejects must not be able to delete a value.*
What is new is which side moved. There the **validator** was added in front of an existing
over-reaching pattern; here the **pattern's reach** was widened in front of an existing rejecting
validator. The pairing is the invariant, not whichever half a commit happens to touch.

**Repro — the real `.exe`, both wire schemas, with a control in the same request.**

```
POST /v1/chat/completions
  sent : carta 4111 1111 1111 1111 123 e mail a.b@example.com
  seen : carta 4111 1111 1111 1111 123 e mail [EMAIL_1]

POST /v1/messages    (UPSTREAM_PROVIDER=anthropic)
  sent : carta 4111 1111 1111 1111 123 e mail a.b@example.com
  seen : carta 4111 1111 1111 1111 123 e mail [EMAIL_1]

control, same binary
  sent : carta 4111 1111 1111 1111 e mail a.b@example.com
  seen : carta [CARD_1] e mail [EMAIL_1]
```

`4111-1111-1111-1111-12` behaves identically — the tail accepts the hyphen too.

**Rate, measured rather than reasoned.** 200 generated Luhn-valid 16-digit cards, each written
`4-4-4-4` and followed by a random 1–3-digit token, driven through the binary:

| build | whole value in clear | truncated (4–9 digits survive) | no byte survives |
|---|---|---|---|
| `7d39b22` — the commit before the fix | 0 | 0 | **200** |
| `36d4a04` — HEAD | **152** | **31** | 17 |
| HEAD + `shrink_on_reject: true` | 0 | 0 | **200** |

The 17 clean ones at HEAD are exactly the 17 whose extension happens to satisfy Luhn — those are
*over*-masked, which is the safe direction. The 31 truncations are the sharper half and are
[M10-R1](M10.md#m10-r1)'s shape: `row 4834 4183 0630 1118 773 end` reaches the provider as
`row 4834 4183 [PHONE_1] end` — nine digits of a real card in clear, the remainder announced as a
phone number, and *the fixpoint recovers a value it did not touch, never one the mask ate.*

**Why the corpus measurement could not see it, and this is the transferable half.** The fix was
cleared with *"**0 added matches** over the reference corpus"*. That number counts **additions**; a
coverage *loss* is a **removal**, and the removal only happens on an input the corpus does not
contain — a Luhn-valid card followed by a short numeric token. A net match count over third-party
source is blind to this class by construction, and it read as due diligence because it is the same
measurement round 7's widening was correctly cleared with. **A widening and a narrowing are not
measured by the same number**, and the round-8 arm is both at once: it adds three groupings and, on
the neighbour axis, removes one.

**Why no guard saw it.** `RENDER-01` tests each recorded rendering **alone**
(`kinds(rendering) == vec![(kind, rendering)]`), so the token after the value is the quantity the
matrix holds constant — and it is the only quantity this defect varies. Flipping `shrink_on_reject` to
`true` leaves the suite at **254 / 0 / 5**, identical: no test in the repo distinguishes a build that
masks the card from one that forwards it.

**Fix.** `shrink_on_reject: true` on the `CreditCard` recognizer (`recognizers.rs:347`). Verified:
0 / 200 and 0 / 380, suite green at 254 / 0 / 5, Amex / Diners / 19-digit / compact all still masked as
whole spans. The mechanism is the one the codebase already reaches for — the rejected 19-digit span is
retried one separator shorter, the 16-digit prefix must itself fully match the pattern (the M11-R22
guard), and Luhn accepts it by construction.

Two costs to state rather than discover:

- It adds up to **four** further `credit_card_valid` calls per rejected card match, and that validator
  is declared `free`, i.e. unbudgeted — the same term [M11-R18](#m11-r18) is open about. Luhn over
  ≤ 23 characters is far cheaper than `iban_case_gate`, but the number belongs in the closure rather
  than in an assumption.
- The narrow-arm decision itself is unchanged: this closes the *deletion*, not the residue R30
  escalated.

**The chokepoint, and it is one level up from the card.** `shrink_on_reject` is a per-recognizer
boolean set by hand, and its correct value is **derivable**: a recognizer needs it exactly when its
pattern can match **more than the value** (an optional or repeated trailing group) **and** its
validator can reject. Both halves are already available where the milestone's guards look — the
pattern string, and whether `validate` is `Some`. A guard asserting *for every recognizer with a
rejecting validator, appending a token that makes the validator refuse must not remove any byte of the
value from the output* would have failed on this commit, and on M11-R13's commit too. That is M10-R1's
predicate quantified over the **neighbour** rather than over the rendering.

**Test that would have caught it.** `RENDER-01`'s matrix gains a neighbour axis: each `detected`
rendering is asserted as one whole span not only alone but **followed by** each of a short fixed set of
tokens (`1`, `12`, `123`, `1234`, a short word, `-1`) and **preceded by** the same — the second half
generalises to every recognizer what `IBAN-05` already does for the token before an IBAN. The
assertion stays M10-R1's: *no byte of the value survives*, which is what separates this from a presence
check that would call `row 4834 4183 [PHONE_1] end` masked.

**Closed 2026-09-04 (`4d3e3bc`), and what stopped the round-8 author seeing it is the durable half.**
The field's own doc forbade shrinking to checksum recognizers, because a shorter prefix can be
Luhn-valid by coincidence — written when a shrunk prefix could be *any* cut of the span. Since
M11-R22 a prefix must be a full match of the recognizer's own pattern, so the only prefixes a card can
shrink to are complete card renderings, Luhn-checked as before: the rule had stopped applying two
rounds earlier and was read as though it still did. `CreditCard` takes `shrink_on_reject: true`, and
the form that generalises is now on the field itself: **a pattern whose reach exceeds what its
validator accepts must shrink** — a property of the pair, not of a recognizer. Reading M11-R13 as
*"IBAN needs this"* is what let it recur. The sharper half is that no test saw it: flipping the flag
took 152 leaking cells to 0 with nothing red, so `RENDER-01` now drives every recorded rendering
followed by six neighbours and asserts **no six-character window survives** — M10-R1's predicate
rather than *"one span equals the value"*, because a legitimate coalesced over-mask covers more and
must stay green. Six and not four: `" 12 "` is a window of `AB 12 34 56 C` that the neighbour rebuilds
unaided.

<a id="m11-r34"></a>
### M11-R34 — `RENDERING_ANSWERS` records a limit in prose that the field built to assert it leaves empty, and the audit never checks the two agree — *guard, medium*

`RenderingAnswer`'s own doc (`src/pii/recognizers.rs:4247`) states the contract:

> `not_detected` — renderings this value **is** published in and this build deliberately does not
> detect. Asserted **absent**, so widening coverage forces this answer to be updated rather than
> letting the limit drift into folklore.
> `why` — for an empty `not_detected`, **how we know no other rendering is published**; otherwise what
> decided the limit.

The audit (`recognizers.rs:4523`) enforces only `!why.is_empty() && !detected.is_empty()`. Nothing
checks the second half of that contract, and **three of the 24 rows break it** — each names a
published, undetected rendering in `why` while `not_detected` is `&[]`:

| row | what `why` says | `not_detected` |
|---|---|---|
| `NationalId :: \d{11}` (`:4370`) | "a DE Steuer-ID **is printed** `86 095 742 719` … the spaced Steuer-ID is the same escalation as the 9-digit row" | `&[]` |
| `Phone :: [1-9]\d{1,2}(?:…){1,3}` (`:4454`) | "a **compact rendering** is a documented recall gap (DEVLOG M10)" | `&[]` |
| `Phone` (universal, `:4310`) | "the US 3-3-4 renderings and the `+CC` arm" — the compact `4155552671` is neither, and is not detected | `&[]` |

**The first is not merely unasserted; it is measurably worse than "not detected".** Through the real
`.exe`:

```
sent : Steuer-ID 86 095 742 719 e mail a.b@example.com
seen : Steuer-ID 86 [PHONE_1] e mail [EMAIL_1]
```

Two digits of the value survive in clear and the remaining nine are announced to the model as a
**phone number**. Had the rendering been listed, `RENDER-01`'s `not_detected` assertion would have
recorded the refusal — and the sibling question *"is any byte of it left in clear, and is what replaces
it labelled truthfully?"* would have been asked at the same time, which is the whole reason the row
exists.

The `\d{9}` row two entries above does it correctly (`not_detected: ["524 287 244"]`, with the cost in
`why`), so this is not a missing idea — it is three rows that did not follow the idea, in the commit
that introduced it, with no mechanism to notice.

**Fix.** The audit gains the missing half: **every rendering named in `why` must appear in `detected`
or `not_detected`.** It is mechanically checkable — a `why` is a `&'static str`, and each of the three
rows above quotes its rendering in backticks or as a bare digit group, so extracting candidate
renderings and requiring them to be listed is enough. The weaker but still sound form: a row may not
use the words *printed* / *rendering* / *gap* with an empty `not_detected`. Either way the three rows
get their entries, and `86 095 742 719` gets the assertion its own `why` claims it has.

**Test.** The guard is the test; it needs the third assertion. The mutation that proves it: move
`"524 287 244"` out of `\d{9}`'s `not_detected` and into its `why`, and the suite must go **red** — at
HEAD it stays green, which is the defect stated as an experiment.

**Closed 2026-09-04, in round 9's commit (`4d3e3bc`)** — a backticked token in `why` that looks like a
rendering must now appear in a list.

<a id="m11-r35"></a>
### M11-R35 — the one row that *does* fill `not_detected` records a reason that is measurably false: a compact SSN is **not** covered — *precision / docs, medium*

`src/pii/recognizers.rs:4318`:

```rust
RenderingAnswer {
    kind: PiiKind::Ssn,
    detected: &["123-45-6789"],
    not_detected: &["123456789"],
    why: "an SSN is printed 3-2-4 and only so. The compact form is deliberately not this \
          recognizer's: nine bare digits belong to the always-on 9-digit national-ID \
          recognizer, whose own row covers them",
}
```

The nine-digit national-ID recognizer is `\b\d{9}\b` **gated by a mod-11 checksum** (NL BSN / PT NIF).
It therefore accepts roughly two of every eleven arbitrary nine-digit runs — the M4-R6 tradeoff, stated
in `ARCHITECTURE.md` — so *"covers them"* is true of a minority. Measured through the real `.exe`:

```
sent : SSN compatto 457555462 e mail a.b@example.com   →  457555462 e mail [EMAIL_1]      in clear
sent : SSN compatto 219099999 e mail a.b@example.com   →  219099999 e mail [EMAIL_1]      in clear
sent : SSN compatto 123456789 e mail a.b@example.com   →  [NATID_1]  e mail [EMAIL_1]
sent : SSN compatto 078051120 e mail a.b@example.com   →  [PHONE_1]  e mail [EMAIL_1]
```

**Two of four in clear**, and the two that were masked were masked by two *different* recognizers,
neither of which was looking for an SSN — one of them the domestic-phone tier. Coverage that depends on
a foreign checksum accepting your value by coincidence is not coverage; it is the ~2/11 over-mask rate
seen from the other side.

**This is not a new gap** — `Ssn` has been `\d{3}-\d{2}-\d{4}` since M1, and a bare nine-digit run is
genuinely ambiguous. What is new is the **claim**, written into the registry whose stated purpose is
that a limit cannot drift into folklore. A false reason there is worse than no row: the next reader has
an answer and stops looking.

**Fix.** Rewrite the `why` to what is true — *the compact form is detected by no recognizer as an SSN;
a nine-digit run is masked only if it passes the NL/PT mod-11 check, about 2 in 11, and that is
coincidence rather than coverage* — and escalate it beside the other renderings
[M11-R30](#m11-r30) handed the maintainer, since widening it is the same coverage decision the `\d{9}`
row already refuses.

**Test.** None beyond the row itself; the correction is the fix. If the maintainer decides the compact
form should be covered, the assertion moves from `not_detected` to `detected` and the guard enforces
it — which is exactly the mechanism the row was built for.

**Closed 2026-09-04 (`4d3e3bc`).** The `Ssn` row's recorded reason is replaced by the measured one:
the 9-digit national-ID recognizer is mod-11-gated, so 2 of 4 realistic compact SSNs are forwarded in
clear. *A registry row whose `why` is an assumption is worse than an empty one — it reads as a
decision somebody took.*

<a id="m11-r36"></a>
### M11-R36 — the comment above the card recognizer describes the chokepoint arm that was deliberately **not** taken, with the rejected option's measurement attached — *docs, medium*

`src/pii/recognizers.rs:319–338`, verbatim at HEAD, sitting directly above the arm it describes:

> `// Credit cards: 13–19 digits, **any grouping or none**, gated by the Luhn checksum and a`
> `// digit count to reject look-alikes.`
> …
> `// Giving the grouping to the validator closes Amex, Diners, the truncation, and any`
> `// rendering nobody has thought of, in one arm — the chokepoint rather than a list of the`
> `// groupings somebody remembered. **Measured: +24 matches** over 22 944 files / 422.9 MB of`
> `// third-party source (998 -> 1 022) … Still bounded (≤ 6 digits x 5 groups + 4 separators = 34`
> `// chars), so Scan::Overlapping stays linear (M4-R19).`

Four claims, and none of them is about the code underneath:

1. *"any grouping or none"* — the arm ships **four** groupings. `4111 111111 11111` is not one of them.
2. *"the chokepoint rather than a list"* — the commit message, `DEVLOG.md`, `TESTING.md` and this
   file's own [M11-R30](#m11-r30) closure note all say the opposite, in those words: *"this is
   knowingly the list rather than the chokepoint, and it says so."*
3. *"+24 matches (998 → 1 022)"* — that is the measurement of **option 2**, the arm that was rejected.
   What shipped measured **0 added matches**. A reader auditing the over-mask budget would take a
   number that is not this recognizer's.
4. *"≤ 6 digits × 5 groups + 4 separators = 34 chars"* — option 2's bound. This arm's longest match is
   19 digits + 4 separators = **23** characters. The `Scan::Overlapping` linearity argument is still
   sound; the arithmetic quoted for it is not.

`DEVLOG.md`, `TESTING.md` and the closure note are all correct, so this is a single stale block —
almost certainly the first draft of the fix surviving the change of plan. It is also the copy a builder
editing this line reads **first**, and it says the pattern already owns every grouping, which is
precisely the belief under which the neighbour case in [M11-R33](#m11-r33) does not occur to anyone.

**Fix.** Rewrite the block to describe the shipped arm: the four published groupings plus compact,
`0 added matches`, the ≤ 23-character bound, and one sentence pointing at [M11-R30](#m11-r30) for the
general arm and its `+24` — as the option **not** taken, and why.

**Test.** None mechanical. The nearest thing is the rule already earned in [M11-R32](#m11-r32) and now
applicable to code comments as well: *a decision belongs where it is recorded once, and everywhere else
links to it.*

**Closed 2026-09-04, in round 9's commit (`4d3e3bc`).**

<a id="m11-r37"></a>
### M11-R37 — `CHANGELOG.md` `[Unreleased]` does not mention the card grouping change, although it fixes a leak and both READMEs were rewritten for it — *docs, medium*

`grep -in 'amex\|diners\|4-6-5\|grouping\|19-digit' CHANGELOG.md` → **0 hits**.

The `[Unreleased]` section carries M11-R10 (the case axis), M11-R21 (the `500`), M11-R25 (the
non-ASCII separator) and M11-R13 (the dropped IBAN) — each written exactly as an operator needs it. It
does not carry [M11-R30](#m11-r30), which is the same kind of fact:

- an American Express or Diners card written the way the issuer prints it was **forwarded in clear** on
  every release ever cut, while a Visa in the same request was masked;
- a Luhn-valid 19-digit card was **truncated**, three digits reaching the provider;
- the fix changes what is masked, which is product-visible coverage — the two READMEs' detection
  matrices were updated for it in the same commit, so the omission is not a judgement that it does not
  matter.

This is [M11-R28](#m11-r28)'s family exactly, one round later and on a **leak** rather than an
availability defect. R28's closure added the missing entry; it did not add anything that makes the next
omission visible, which is why this is filed rather than folded in.

**Fix.** An entry under `### Fixed` naming the renderings that were missed, the truncation, and the
fact that nothing changes for a card written compactly or in four groups of four. And — the part that
outlives it — the mechanical check R32's own fix note already proposed, narrowed to something small
enough to build: **a `docs/` test that fails when a ledger row whose severity cell reads `leak` is
`[x]` and its id appears nowhere in `CHANGELOG.md`'s `[Unreleased]` section.** The ledger is already
machine-readable, `release-build-publish.yml` already parses this changelog by heading, and `CAT-01` is
the precedent that this project will enforce a docs invariant in a test.

**Closed 2026-09-05 — `shrink_on_reject: true` on the card, which its own field doc had forbidden.**

The optional 1-3-digit tail that closed M11-R30's truncation is **greedy**, so a card beside its CVV
matched as 19 digits, failed Luhn, and — with no shrink — the whole candidate was discarded. That is
M11-R13's mechanism exactly, one recognizer over and one commit after the paragraph explaining it
was written. **152 of 200 measured cards in clear, 31 more truncated, and the suite stayed at
254/0/5.**

**The rule that stopped me seeing it was the field's own doc**: *"a checksum recognizer must not set
it: a shorter prefix of a non-Luhn-valid digit run can be Luhn-valid by coincidence, trading a
measured false-positive rate for an unmeasured one."* That was written when a shrunk prefix could be
**any** cut of the span. Since **M11-R22** it must be a full match of the recognizer's own pattern —
so the only prefixes the card can shrink to are complete card renderings, Luhn-checked exactly as the
un-shrunk span would be. The rate is the one already measured and accepted for those arms, not a new
one, and the rule as written was costing a leak. Both facts are now on the field, together with the
form that generalises: **a pattern whose reach exceeds what its validator accepts must shrink** — a
property of the *pair*, not of a recognizer, which is what M11-R13 established and what reading the
rule as "IBAN needs this" hid.

**The guard is the neighbour sweep in `RENDER-01`, and round 9's sharpest observation is that none
existed**: flipping the flag changed 152 leaking cells to 0 and no test noticed. A rendering is not
detected in isolation, it is detected in a sentence, so every recorded rendering is now driven
followed by six neighbours (`" 123"`, `" 12"`, `" 7"`, `" 4567"`, `" end"`, `""`) and asserted to
leave **no six-character window** in clear. The predicate is M10-R1's — *no byte of the value
survives* — not *one span equals the value*, because a legitimate coalesced over-mask (M10-R26/R31)
covers more than the value and must stay green.

**The window is six because four produced a false positive on its first run**: a four-character
window of `AB 12 34 56 C` is `" 12 "`, which the neighbour `" 12"` reconstructs on its own. Six
cannot be rebuilt from the carrier and the neighbour, and every recorded rendering is at least six
characters, so no row loses its assertion to the choice. That is written on the guard.

**Mutation:** `shrink_on_reject: true -> false` on the card is now **red**, naming the case —
`"4111 1" — six characters of "4111 1111 1111 1111" — reached the provider in clear when followed by
" 123"`.

**Verified:** **254 / 0 / 5** over 24 binaries, twice, identical; `cargo test-onnx` **290 / 0 / 22**;
`fmt` and `clippy --all-targets -D warnings` clean.

**Closed 2026-09-05 — the audit gained the half the contract stated, in the mechanical form.**

Every backtick-quoted token in a row's `why` that **looks like a rendering** must appear in
`detected` or `not_detected`. `looks_like_a_rendering` is deliberately narrow and its residue is
written down: at least six characters, at least four ASCII digits, and nothing but alphanumerics,
spaces and hyphens — which admits `86 095 742 719` and `524 287 244` and excludes `\d{9}` (braces),
`M4-R6` (two digits) and `[PHONE_1]` (brackets).

It found the finding's own three rows on its first run, and then a fourth thing worth recording: my
first correction backticked the *sentence* `Steuer-ID 86 095 742 719` rather than the rendering, and
the audit refused that too — correctly, since a quoted string it cannot match against a list is
exactly the ambiguity the assertion exists to remove. The prose now backticks renderings and nothing
else.

The three rows have their entries: the spaced DE Steuer-ID (with the measured
`86 [PHONE_1]` outcome — two digits in clear, nine mislabelled), the compact domestic phone
`911234567`, and the compact US `4155552671`.

**What generalises, and it is why this was worth a guard rather than three edits:** *prose cannot be
audited; a list can.* A field that exists to assert something asserts nothing while the thing lives
in the sentence beside it.

**Closed 2026-09-05.** The `Ssn` row's reason said the compact form is covered by the always-on
9-digit national-ID recognizer. That recognizer is **checksum-gated** — NL 11-proef or PT mod-11 —
so it accepts roughly **2 in 11** arbitrary nine-digit runs: a compact SSN is masked when it happens
to satisfy a Dutch or Portuguese checksum and **forwarded in clear otherwise**.

The row now says that, with the rate, and names it as what it is: a real coverage gap on a headline
PII type, not a tidy division of labour between two recognizers. Closing it means masking every bare
nine-digit run — a coverage decision with a false-positive cost nobody has measured, so it is
escalated beside M11-R30's national-identifier rows rather than taken here.

**The reason this row was wrong is worth more than the row.** It was the *only* one of the 24 that
filled `not_detected` correctly, and its `why` was still false — so a filled field is not evidence
that the sentence beside it is true, and M11-R34's new assertion cannot catch this class either.
Only reading it can. That is the honest limit of both guards and it is stated on `RENDER-01`.

**Closed 2026-09-05.** The comment above the card recognizer described the **general** arm — *"any
grouping or none"*, `+24 matches`, `34 chars` — which is the option that was measured and **not**
taken. It now describes the arms that ship (the published groupings), and keeps the general one as a
named, measured alternative with the reason it was rejected: it breaks
`national_phone_does_not_swallow_an_adjacent_number` by coalescing Luhn-valid sub-runs in digit-dense
text, which a match count cannot show.

Same class as M11-R32 one file over: a comment that describes an intention rather than the code
beside it. The mitigation is the same — state what ships, and cite the alternative as an alternative.

**Closed 2026-09-05.** `CHANGELOG.md`'s `[Unreleased]` `### Fixed` section names the card grouping
change in the register the rest of that section uses — what changed for someone running this: an
Amex number in its own 4-6-5 grouping went to the provider in clear while the same digits compact
were masked; a 19-digit card had its last three digits forwarded; and a card followed by a short
token (its CVV, a row index, a spreadsheet column) is now masked whole rather than dropped, which is
M11-R33 folded into the same entry because it is the same release and the same value.

---

<a id="round-10"></a>
## Round 10 — the budget's *other* multiplicand (2026-09-05)

**Round 9's fix holds, and this round could not break it — that is the first result and it is a
negative one.** The card's `shrink_on_reject: true` was driven over **93 600** generated cells (400
seeds x five issuer prefixes x their published groupings x two separators x 18 following tokens, every
card Luhn-valid by construction) and over **15 120** cells of the 40 recorded renderings against a
matrix that varies the token **before** the value as well as the one after it — the axis `RENDER-01`
and every earlier round hold constant. **0 leaks at HEAD; 8 672 of 93 600 with the flag flipped
back.** No fail-open, no over-mask regression, no raw value in a log, and every masked case restores
byte-exactly.

**What this round went after instead is the number the milestone has been quoting at the maintainer.**
[M11-R18](#m11-r18) is open, and the decision in front of the maintainer is priced against
`ARCHITECTURE.md`'s three-term availability model. Term 1 of that model is
`units x ~3 us => ~1.5 s`, and **the multiplicand is a single point of a grid, taken at its cheapest
corner.** A `phonenumber::parse()` costs what the candidate makes it cost, and the code's own comment
says which way: *"`.any()` short-circuits only on accept, so a **rejection is the expensive
verdict**"*. Every row `DOS-BUD` publishes is built from **valid** numbers.

Measured on this box, in one process, with the phone-tier-free floor subtracted from each: the same
**500 000 units** cost **3.9-4.9 s** on a random `3XX XXX XXXX` column and **13.0-15.3 s** on a
column of zero-padded four-digit ids ([M11-R38](#m11-r38)). Through the real `.exe` a **4 MiB** body
of that shape is refused with **HTTP 400 after 14.6 s / 14.3 s**. It is **not a leak** — fail-closed,
nothing forwarded — **and not a regression**: `v1.2.1` measures 12.2 s on the identical body. What is
new is that the published ceiling is false by ~9x on a payload nobody would call adversarial, and
that `DOS-BUD` cannot see it because it varies rendering, layout and size but never the **verdict**
([M11-R39](#m11-r39)).

The third finding is [M11-R31](#m11-r31)'s shape one axis over: `UTF8-01` derives its corpus from
`CASE_ANSWERS`, which is scoped by `pattern_can_match_a_letter`, so **12 of the 24 shipped
recognizers are outside the digit-script guard by construction** — and they are the *digit-only*
half, whose patterns are nothing but the Unicode `\d` the guard exists for. `ARCHITECTURE.md` states
the opposite ([M11-R40](#m11-r40)). Probed directly, there is **no live defect** behind it today.

### What was verified, with the expected value written before the observed one

| Quantity | Expected (written first) | Observed | Reps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cargo test` (default) | 254 / 0 / 5 over 24 binaries, 0 warnings — round 9's figure | **254 / 0 / 5, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 2 (start of round; again after every mutation was restored) |
| `cargo test --features onnx` | 290 / 0 / 22 — round 9's figure | **290 / 0 / 22, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 1 |
| `cargo fmt --check` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| 93 600 generated Luhn-valid cards x published groupings x 18 following tokens, at HEAD | 0 — round 9's fix should hold on generated cards as it does on the five literals | **0 / 93 600** | 1 (fixed seed) |
| ...the same with the card's `shrink_on_reject` flipped to `false` | non-zero, and large — this is what R33 measured at 183/200 | **8 672 / 93 600** | 1 |
| 15 120 cells: 40 recorded renderings x 18 *preceding* x 21 *following* tokens, unique-window predicate | 0, or a class no earlier round has named | **0** | 1 |
| `DOS-BUD` on this box, 16 MiB `3XX XXX XXXX` refusal | ~1.6 s, the published figure — is this box comparable? | **1 719 ms**, 500 000 units | 1 |
| `DOS-BUD` on this box, 16 MiB unbudgeted floor (phone tier off) | ~229 ms, the published figure | **305 ms** | 1 |
| 500 000 units on a random `3XX XXX XXXX` column, phone-tier floor subtracted | ~1.5 s if the published `~3 us` holds | **3 906 / 4 919 / 4 718 ms** -> 7.8-9.8 us/unit | 3 |
| 500 000 units on a zero-padded 4-digit id column, same subtraction | the same ~1.5 s, if the unit is a constant | **15 251 / 13 011 / 13 161 ms** -> 26.0-30.5 us/unit | 3 |
| ...the same two bodies with `phonenumber::parse()` short-circuited to `false`, budget still spending | near the floor, if `parse` is where the time is | **139-152 ms** and **553-760 ms** | 3 each |
| 4 MiB zero-padded id column through the real `.exe` | a 400, and fast — the budget is a *fail-closed bound* | **HTTP 400 after 14 639 / 14 348 ms** | 2 |
| 8 MiB, same shape, same binary | linear -> ~29 s, or the scan stops earlier | **HTTP 400 after 18 209 / 17 610 ms** | 2 |
| the identical 4 MiB body at **`v1.2.1`** | if this is an M11 regression, materially faster | **12 157 ms** — not a regression | 3 |
| shipped `(kind, pattern)` pairs covered by `RENDERING_ANSWERS` / by `CASE_ANSWERS` | 24 / fewer, since `CASE_ANSWERS` is letter-scoped | **23 distinct patterns for 24 recognizers / 12** -> 11 patterns outside `UTF8-01` | 1 (read off the source) |
| 2 168 Unicode-digit substitutions across **all 24** renderings (not just the letter-bearing ones) | a panic or a round-trip break, if the exemption hides one | **0 panics, 0 round-trip failures** — the exemption is benign today | 1 |

### Mutations run in round 10

`src/pii/recognizers.rs` only. Each was applied by byte substitution with the substitution asserted
non-empty **before** the write (one attempt was refused by that check for a CRLF/LF mismatch — an
empty substitution is a green in disguise, and it is the reason the check is there), and restored by
writing the original bytes back. The file's sha256 was compared against the `HEAD` blob
(`af56adbb68998f22...`) after every restore, and `git status --porcelain` was empty at the end of the
round.

| # | mutation | expected | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | the card's `shrink_on_reject: true` -> `false` (revert round 9's fix) | red — round 9 says `RENDER-01` now watches this | **red, exactly one test**: `every_recognizer_records_the_renderings_it_detects`, 156 passed / 1 failed. The guard R33 added does watch its fix |
| B | the same, against this round's 93 600-cell probe | red, and large | **8 672 / 93 600** — the probe is not vacuous |
| C | the same, timed on 4 MiB of digit groups | if the card shrink is the cost M11-R18 describes, materially faster | **6 354 / 7 741 / 7 225 ms vs 7 712 / 7 712 / 7 445 ms — within noise.** The card shrink is *not* a measurable cost. Hypothesis refuted |
| D | the phone families' `shrink_on_reject: matches!(shape, Groups / LongBlock)` -> `false`, timed | isolates the un-anchored shrink's share of the phone cost | **4.4-4.7 s vs 5.0-5.4 s on the 3-digit column** — ~10%, not the driver |
| E | `national_phone_valid`'s `phonenumber::parse(..)` -> `false`, budget untouched | isolates `parse` from the regex scan and the memo | **13 011 ms -> 553-760 ms** and **3 906 ms -> 139-152 ms.** Essentially all of it is inside `parse` |

### What this round could not break

- **Round 9's fix**, on 93 600 generated cards and on a neighbour matrix that varies both sides.
- **The glued-prefix residue is exactly what [M11-R23](#m11-r23) says it is** — `ITES91 2100 ...` and
  `ITsk-AbCdEf...` are the `(?-u:\b)` anti-false-positive guarantee, not a new class. Every one of
  the 627 hits in the first, artefact-prone sweep was either that or a window the carrier rebuilt
  unaided; with the glued case dropped and a unique-window predicate the sweep is **0 / 15 120**.
- **The digit-script axis behind `UTF8-01`'s exemption.** 2 168 substitutions over all 24
  renderings: no validator panics, every round trip byte-exact. The finding is the guard's scope,
  not a defect.
- **The three-microsecond figure is not an internal contradiction.** `recognizers.rs:1321` explicitly
  picks `~3 us` over the library's `~6.5 us`, citing `DOS-BUD` — so the two numbers in the tree are
  reconciled on purpose. What is wrong is not the arithmetic but the *band*.
- **The un-anchored universal `Phone` arm can start mid-token** (its `\d{3}` carries no `(?-u:\b)`),
  so `1234567890123 347 1234567` resolves to one span beginning inside the first token. The resolver
  unions it, so the outcome is an **over-mask, never a truncation** — the M4-R10/R11 invariant doing
  its job.
- **Logs, fail-closed posture, the round trip.** `RUST_LOG=warn` over every probe: no value sent
  appears. Every refusal is a 400 with nothing forwarded.

### Guard-vs-product tally

Round 10: **1 in the product** ([R38](#m11-r38), `hardening` — the shipped binary's availability),
**2 on the net or the docs** ([R39](#m11-r39), [R40](#m11-r40)). Read off the ledger's severity cell,
per M11-R24's rule. The milestone now stands at **28 on the net or the docs, 13 in the product**,
41 rows.

<a id="m11-r38"></a>
### M11-R38 — the budget's published ceiling multiplies its count by the *cheapest* verdict; an ordinary zero-padded id column costs ~9x it — *hardening, medium-high*

**Not a leak, not a regression, and not an algorithmic blow-up** — the path is linear and the request
is refused. What it is: a **published fail-closed ceiling that is false by roughly an order of
magnitude**, and it is the number [M11-R18](#m11-r18)'s open maintainer decision is being priced
against.

`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:332` and `:398`, and `src/pii/recognizers.rs:1321`:

> One unit is one `phonenumber::parse()`, measured at **~3 us** on the shipped release build, so
> **500,000** bounds a request's domestic-phone *validation* at **~1.5 s** of CPU.
>
> 1. `units x ~3 us`, which the allowance caps at ~1.5 s.

**A unit is not a constant, and the same file says why.** From the *Domestic phone coverage* section:
*"`.any()` short-circuits only on **accept**, so a **rejection is the expensive verdict**"*. The
allowance is a count of `parse()` calls; the cost of one depends on whether the number is real. A
column of **valid** numbers short-circuits on the first region that accepts. A column of
**phone-shaped non-numbers** pays every declared region, every time.

**Measured, one process, phone-tier-free floor subtracted from each, three runs each:**

| body (4 MiB) | verdict | units | phone-attributable | us / unit |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| random `3XX XXX XXXX` column | REFUSED | 500 000 | 3 906 / 4 919 / 4 718 ms | 7.8-9.8 |
| **zero-padded 4-digit id column** (`0417 0982 0335 ...`) | REFUSED | 500 000 | **15 251 / 13 011 / 13 161 ms** | **26.0-30.5** |
| `DOS-BUD`'s own 16 MiB `3XX XXX XXXX` row, this box | REFUSED | 500 000 | 1 719 ms total | ~3.4 |

So the multiplicand spans **~3.4 us to ~30 us — a factor of nine — and the published figure is the
bottom of the range.**

**Through the real `.exe`**, mock upstream, OS-assigned high port, `PII_LOCALES` unset (the shipped
default):

```text
4 MiB  "0417 0982 0335 ..."   ->  HTTP 400 after 14 639 ms  /  14 348 ms
8 MiB  same shape             ->  HTTP 400 after 18 209 ms  /  17 610 ms
4 MiB  same column, never 0-leading (control)  ->  masked and forwarded in 1 452 ms
```

**Attribution is not inferred, it is mutated (mutation E).** Short-circuiting
`national_phone_valid`'s `phonenumber::parse(..)` to `false` while leaving `budget.spend()` in place
takes the 13 011 ms body to **553-760 ms** and the 3 906 ms body to **139-152 ms**. The regex scan,
the per-scan memo and `shrink_on_reject` are all noise here; it is `parse`.

**The shape is ordinary.** `0`-leading four-digit groups are what a zero-padded id column looks
like — store numbers, cost centres, SKUs, ISO week codes, `LPAD`-ed keys — and they are a candidate
for the `Trunk` family in all six regions that declare it. A body of them is a plausible tool result,
not a crafted payload. The `never 0-leading` control above is the same bytes with the leading digit
in `1-9`: **1 452 ms and forwarded**, so the whole effect is the trunk anchor meeting a zero.

**Not a regression.** The identical 4 MiB body at `v1.2.1` measures **12 157 ms** (3 runs). The phone
tier and the budget are both M10; M11 changed neither. What M11 changed is that this number is now
load-bearing: `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s three-term model was rewritten in round 5 and its term 1 was left
at a figure taken from one corner of `DOS-BUD`'s grid.

**Fix — and the choice is the maintainer's, per `CLAUDE.md`, because every option is
product-visible.** Recorded here with what each costs; none of them is a reviewer's to take.

1. **Publish the band, change no code.** Replace `~3 us` with the measured range and a note that the
   verdict, not the rendering, is what moves it — and restate the ceiling as *"500 000 units is
   ~1.7 s of validation on accepting traffic and up to ~15 s on rejecting traffic"*. Cost: nothing,
   and the bound stays a bound *on units* while the wall-clock ceiling stays ~15 s at 4 MiB. Honest,
   and it does not improve availability.
2. **Lower `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST`** so the worst verdict lands where the published
   ceiling says. At ~30 us, ~3 s of validation is **100 000 units** — but M10 chose 500 000
   *precisely because* 50 000 refused an ordinary 367 KB database result, and 100 000 would refuse
   the 20 000-row `3XX XXX XXXX` column `DOS-BUD` currently masks at 160 000 units. A refusal that
   fires on legal traffic teaches its operator to distrust the bound (M10's own argument).
3. **Charge by verdict rather than by call** — e.g. a rejection costs several units. This makes the
   allowance track cost instead of count, which is what the name `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS` stopped
   meaning the moment the two verdicts diverged. Cost: the published "~62 500 phone numbers per
   request" band has to be re-derived, and the multiplier needs measuring per region.
4. **Bound the wall clock instead of the count.** Direct, and it makes the refusal
   non-deterministic — the same body would be masked on a fast box and refused on a slow one, which
   this project has so far refused to accept anywhere else.

**The test that would have caught it** is [M11-R39](#m11-r39): a `DOS-BUD` row whose column is
**phone-shaped and invalid**, printing microseconds/unit rather than only ms/row.

**Ledger note.** This finding does not block the tag on its own — nothing leaks and nothing is
forwarded — but `ARCHITECTURE.md` carries a number now measured false, and a release should not ship
it unrevised. It also changes the input to [M11-R18](#m11-r18): the worst legal shape is **not** the
lowercase-IBAN body that finding escalated (8-10 s at 14.6 MiB), it is this one (14.5 s at 4 MiB),
and it lives in term **1** — the term the budget was built to bound — rather than term 3.


**NOT closed — escalated 2026-09-05, and it re-prices [M11-R18](#m11-r18) rather than joining it.**
All four options change product-visible behaviour (a refusal, a budget, a published guarantee), so
none is the builder's. What was done meanwhile needed no answer: the round's own correction to
`ARCHITECTURE.md`'s `~3 µs` — now a measured band — stands, and `DOS-BUD` gained the axis that makes
the band reproducible (M11-R39). The two findings are one decision: the unit is not a constant, and
the worst *legal* body is the one whose candidates are **rejected**, which is where the allowance is
spent fastest and nothing is masked for it.
---

**Closed 2026-09-06 — the band is published, and the finding's stated mechanism does not survive
measurement (`28f0b9e`).**

The decision taken was option 3, *charge more units for a rejection*. Implementing it required
measuring what a rejection costs, and the measurement says something different. Four 4 MiB bodies,
three reps each, `--release`, printed by `DOS-BUD`'s new verdict-**and-shape** grid:

| shape | verdict | µs/unit |
|---|---|---|
| `3XX XXX XXXX` | accepted | **3.2-4.3** |
| `0NNNN NNNN` id column | rejected | **2.9-3.5** |
| `ab12 cd34 …` (the IBAN gate) | rejected | **1.3-1.8** |
| `0NNN NNNN NNNN` (three groups) | accepted | **15.1-21.7** |

**The rejecting column is cheaper per unit than the accepting one, and the dearest body is an
accepting one.** The 9× band this finding measured is real; the verdict is not what produces it. What
produces it is the candidate's **shape** — and the budget *already* charges a rejection more than an
acceptance, by paying every enabled region because `.any()` short-circuits only on accept. Option 3
was, in effect, already implemented on the axis that is countable.

**What is not countable is the price of one `parse()`**, and no deterministic pre-call proxy for it
exists: it tracks neither digit count (8-digit 2.6 µs, 10-digit 3.4, 12-digit 15.1) nor group count
(`3XX XXX XXXX` is three groups at 3.2) nor verdict. Timing each call would make the refusal depend on
machine load, which is the one thing this layer refuses to do — the same objection that ruled out
option 4.

So the ceiling is **republished as the band and stated on the worst shape**, which is option 1 applied
to a term option 1 was not written about: **500 000 units is ~0.6 s of validation on the cheapest
column and ~8.5 s on the dearest measured** — 487 214 units in 8.3 s, and 10.6 s on a slower rep — and
the dearest is *legal* traffic, a zero-padded three-group key column, not an attack. The old
*"at most about 3 s"* is **withdrawn, not adjusted**: a ceiling may only ever be wrong in the direction
of caution.

**The guard is `DOS-BUD` itself, one axis wider.** M11-R39 gave it the verdict; running it then showed
the axis that still was not varied, and the grid now prints **µs per unit** beside ms per row. *A
complexity guard that reports a rate has to report the numerator and the denominator separately, or it
cannot tell "more units" from "dearer units"* — with the verdict fixed the two are interchangeable,
which is why nothing ever asked whether the unit varied.

**Term 3 is inside the count now** ([M11-R18](#m11-r18)), so the two rows close together, as one
decision about what the allowance is a count *of*.

<a id="m11-r39"></a>
### M11-R39 — `DOS-BUD` varies rendering, layout and size but never the **verdict**, which the code names as the cost driver — *guard, medium*

`tests/complexity.rs::budget_refusal_line_and_cost` is the source of the `~3 us` figure —
`TESTING.md` says so in as many words. Its grid is genuinely wide: one field at increasing sizes, one
request at increasing field counts, a SQL result in **two legal renderings**, three layouts, a
per-rendering unit table, and the unbudgeted floor. Two earlier findings (M10-R49, M10-R53/R56)
widened it, each time because *"a conclusion drawn from one point of a grid is a fact about that
point"*.

**Every one of those rows is built from real, valid numbers.** `347 XXXXXXX`, `3XX XXX XXXX`,
`0X XX XX XX XX`, `6XX XX XX XX`, `+39 3XX XXXXXXX` — the generator picks assigned prefixes on
purpose, so the column is a *realistic* phone column. The consequence is that the axis the code
singles out as the cost driver — accept vs reject, where `.any()` short-circuits only on accept — is
held constant at **accept** across the entire grid. That is [M4-R13](M4.md#m4-r13)'s lesson (*a
corpus has a shape, and that shape is a blind spot*) arriving on the budget guard, and lesson 6 of
[M4's retrospective](M4.md#retrospective) (*ask what a guard holds constant, not only what it
varies*) arriving for the third time in this milestone.

It is also why the guard reports **ms per row** and never **microseconds per unit**: with the verdict
fixed, the two are interchangeable, so nothing ever asked whether the unit was a constant.

**Fix.** Two rows and one derived column:

- a column of **phone-shaped invalid** candidates — the zero-padded id column of
  [M11-R38](#m11-r38) is the cheapest one to generate and the most ordinary — beside the existing
  valid column of the same size;
- the same in a rendering whose candidates each pay *every* declared region;
- and print **microseconds/unit** (phone-attributable ms divided by `budget.spent()`) on every row,
  so the multiplicand the published ceiling uses is a measured column of the table rather than a
  sentence beside it.

**The rule, which outlives this guard.** *A complexity guard that varies its input but holds the
**verdict** constant measures one branch of the code.* Where a validator short-circuits, the cheap
branch and the expensive branch are different programs, and a grid built from realistic inputs will
sample only the cheap one — because realistic inputs are, by construction, the ones the validator
accepts.


**Closed 2026-09-05 — the grid gained the axis, and the numbers moved the conclusion.**

`DOS-BUD` varied field size, rendering, layout and field count, and every row was built from
**valid** numbers — so `applicable.iter().any(..)` short-circuits on the first accepting region and
only the cheapest branch was ever sampled. Measured on `--release` with the verdict varied:

    3XX XXX XXXX (valid)     5 000 rows   40 000 spent    8 units/row   5 000 masked
    0NNNN NNNN id column     5 000 rows   60 000 spent   12 units/row       0 masked

The rejecting shape costs **half again as much per row and masks nothing**, so it reaches the
allowance on a **smaller** body — refused at 50 000 rows / 3.6 MB against the valid column's 100 000
rows / 7.5 MB. *The worst legal body is one whose candidates are all rejected*, which is the opposite
of what the grid used to encourage and is exactly why M11-R38 re-prices M11-R18.

**Two of this file's own traps caught its author on the way.** The first version of the row asserted
*"rejected in all regions"* in a comment and measured **4 145 of 5 000 masked** — the shape was
mostly *valid* — on an odometer that repeated every 10 000 rows, so the per-scan memo served most of
it free and the cost read as sub-linear. Both are named in `complexity.rs` already (M10-R49,
M10-R56). The shape is distinct now, and the label states the **shape** rather than a verdict nobody
had measured — which is the same correction M11-R34 made to prose in a different file, one day
apart.
---

<a id="m11-r40"></a>
### M11-R40 — `UTF8-01` exempts 12 of 24 recognizers by construction, and they are the digit-only half — *guard / docs, medium*

`UTF8-01` (`a_non_ascii_digit_inside_a_value_never_panics_a_validator`) closed
[M11-R21](#m11-r21) — a validator byte-slicing a span that carries a multi-byte `\p{Nd}`. Its corpus
is derived rather than hand-written, which is the right instinct: it loops `for answer in
CASE_ANSWERS`, substituting each of four `\p{Nd}` digits into every character position of every
recorded positive.

**But `CASE_ANSWERS` is scoped by `pattern_can_match_a_letter`.** `audit_case_axis` skips any pattern
that cannot match `A-Za-z`, so a digit-only recognizer has no case answer to record — correctly, on
the case axis. Inheriting that scope for the **digit-script** axis inverts the question: the patterns
with no letters in them are the ones made of nothing but the Unicode `\d` this guard exists for.

Counted off the source — `RENDERING_ANSWERS` covers all 24 shipped recognizers in 23 distinct
patterns; `CASE_ANSWERS` covers 12. The **11 patterns outside the guard**:

```text
CreditCard   the 4-4-4-4 (+ optional tail), 4-6-4/5 and compact 13-19 arms
Phone        the universal US 3-3-4 and +CC arms
Ssn          (?-u:\b)\d{3}-\d{2}-\d{4}(?-u:\b)
NationalId   FR NIR . 9-digit (NL/PT) . 11-digit (DE/LV) . LV 6-5 dashed
TaxId        the bare IT P.IVA — a recognizer M11 Track A added
Phone        all four domestic families (Trunk . TrunkPairs . Groups . LongBlock)
```

This is exactly [M11-R31](#m11-r31), one axis over and one round earlier: a guard whose scope is
derived from the *pattern* exempts, by construction, the recognizers where the question bites
hardest. R31's fix was to scope `RENDERING_ANSWERS` to **all** shipped recognizers; `UTF8-01` never
got the same treatment because its registry looked like a chokepoint already.

**There is no live defect behind it.** Substituting each of the four `\p{Nd}` digits (2, 3, 3 and 4
bytes) into every character position of all 40 recorded renderings — **2 168 substitutions**, against
the guard's own 932 — produces **0 panics and 0 round-trip failures**. Every digit-only validator
either filters to ASCII before indexing or folds `chars()`. So this is the guard's scope, not a bug:
the exemption is currently benign, and nothing says it stays that way when the next digit-only
recognizer lands.

**And `ARCHITECTURE.md` states the opposite** — `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:503`:

> The guard substitutes four `\p{Nd}` digits ... into every positive in `CASE_ANSWERS` — the same
> registry the case axis derives from, **so a new recognizer joins both guards at once**

A new *letter-bearing* recognizer does. A new digit-only one — the bare P.IVA is the most recent
example — joins **neither** `CASE-01` nor `UTF8-01`. The code's own doc comment is accurate (*"a new
**letter-bearing** recognizer"*); the promotion into `ARCHITECTURE.md` dropped the qualifier, which
is the half a reader trusts.

**Fix.** Derive `UTF8-01`'s corpus from `RENDERING_ANSWERS` — already all 24 recognizers, already
carrying a `detected` list of real renderings, and already the registry M11-R31 made the
all-recognizers chokepoint — instead of from `CASE_ANSWERS`. One registry, three guards, and the
digit-script axis stops being scoped by a question about letters.

**The test that would have caught it** is the one this finding ran: the same substitution sweep over
`RENDERING_ANSWERS` rather than `CASE_ANSWERS`. It is a change to the loop header, and it raises the
guard's corpus from 932 substitutions to 2 168.

**Closed 2026-09-05 — the corpus comes from the registry whose question the guard asks.**

`UTF8-01` took its corpus from `CASE_ANSWERS`, which is scoped by `pattern_can_match_a_letter`
because letter case is *its* axis. This guard asks about **digits**, and inherited a scope that put
**12 of the 24** recognizers outside it — the card, the universal phone, the SSN, the FR NIR, the 9-
and 11-digit ids, the LV dashed code, the bare IT P.IVA and all four domestic-phone families, which
are precisely the patterns made of nothing but the Unicode `\d` the guard exists for.

It now derives from `RENDERING_ANSWERS`, the all-recognizers registry M11-R31 built. **932
substitutions became 2 168**, matching the round's own independent probe exactly, and the floor moved
from 500 to 1 500 so the widening cannot be undone silently. **No live defect behind it**, as the
round said: every digit-only validator filters to ASCII or folds `chars()`.

**The rule, which is the durable half and is now beside the guard:** a guard takes its scope from the
registry whose *question* it asks, not from whichever registry is nearest. This is M11-R31 one axis
over, and the two together are why there is now one registry per axis and one all-recognizers
registry underneath them.

---

## Round 11 — the rendering axis, asked of the *value* instead of the recognizer (2026-09-05)

**Round 10's two fixes hold and this round could not break them — that is the first result.** The
card's shrink was driven over **1 360 800** cells (900 seeds x four published groupings x six
separator characters incl. U+00A0 / U+202F / U+2007 / tab x seven preceding x nine following tokens,
every card Luhn-valid by construction): **0 leaks, 0 round-trip breaks**. The separator axis was
re-driven over all **56** corpus values x the same six separators x eight preceding x ten following
tokens — **26 880 cells, 0 leaks, 0 round-trip breaks**. `UTF8-01` goes red when `iban_mod97` is
reverted to a byte slice, and its new 1 500 floor goes red when the corpus is narrowed. No fail-open,
no over-mask regression, no raw value in a log, `content-length` correct on the real binary.

**What this round went after is [M11-R30](#m11-r30)'s own question, asked one level up.** R30 asked
*"which renderings is this value published in?"* and answered it with a registry keyed on
`(kind, pattern)` — one row per **recognizer**. `Phone` is the one kind whose value is served by
**two** recognizer families, a universal `+CC`/US one and nine domestic ones, and a phone number is
published in *both* a domestic and an international rendering. Only the domestic half was ever
measured. The international half is two hand-enumerated groupings written in M1.5, and it has **no
validator and no right anchor**.

The consequence is a leak, and it is large. Over **13 000** generated numbers that libphonenumber
itself confirms are valid, across 13 templates in 10 regions, each rendered by libphonenumber in its
own three canonical modes:

| rendering | example | reaches the provider in clear |
|---|---|---|
| **E.164 compact** | `+393471234567` | **11 934 of 13 000 — 0.918** |
| **International, spaced** | `+33 6 12 34 56 78` | **2 000 of 13 000 — 0.154** |
| National (domestic) | `347 1234567` | **0 of 13 000 — 0.000** |

E.164 is not an exotic rendering: it is the one every API payload, CRM export, `tel:` URI and
database column uses, and it is what `phonenumber`'s own `Mode::E164` prints. **The always-on
"universal" tier misses 92% of its values in that form, while the tier the README describes as the
narrowable, FP-prone one is at zero.** Some renderings are worse than missed: `+55 11 91234 5678`
comes back `[PHONE_1] 5678`, four digits of a real Brazilian mobile in clear — the two-group arm has
no right anchor, so it ends inside the number ([M11-R41](#m11-r41)). Proved through the real `.exe`
on `/v1/chat/completions` with an email in the same sentence as the control, and the unmasked number
appears verbatim in the `TRACE masked_request` line, because *"the masked body is safe to log"* is
true only while masking is complete.

**Not a regression.** The two `+CC` arms are byte-identical at **every tag ever cut** (`v0.4.0`
through `v1.2.1`); they date from `775945f` (M1.5's phone over-match fix, which enumerated the two
shapes on purpose) and the only change since is round 7's `{GAP}` widening, which changed the
separator *character* and not the grouping. Eleven milestones, the same age as
[M11-R25](#m11-r25) and [M11-R30](#m11-r30) — and no corpus in the repo can express it: of **35**
phone positives in `tests/corpus/pii_cases.json`, **not one** is written in E.164.

The second finding is on the sentence that would have made a reader look: the README's headline
coverage table says a `Phone` match is what *"the country's **real numbering plan** says … is
assigned"*, and both `+CC` arms and the US arm carry `validate: None` ([M11-R42](#m11-r42)).

### What was verified, with the expected value written before the observed one

| Quantity | Expected (written first) | Observed | Reps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cargo test` (default) | 254 / 0 / 5 over 24 binaries, 0 warnings — round 10's figure | **254 / 0 / 5, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 2 (start of round; again after the last mutation was restored) |
| `cargo test --features onnx` | 290 / 0 / 22 — round 9's figure, unmoved by round 10 | **290 / 0 / 22, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 1 |
| `cargo fmt --check` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| E.164 renderings of the corpus's own 40 phone positives | 0 leaks, if *"`+CC` … always"* is true | **33 of 40 in clear**; the 7 survivors are masked by *coincidence*, as `CreditCard` (13-digit Luhn) or `NationalId` (11-digit checksum), never as `Phone` | 2 |
| the same 40 in libphonenumber's `International` mode | 0 | **5 of 40** — the FR five-pair form, `+44 800 1111`, `+31 6 12345678`, and `+33 1 23 45 67 89` truncated to `+33 1` | 2 |
| 13 000 generated valid numbers x 3 canonical modes | if the arm covers `+CC`, ~0 everywhere | **E.164 0.918 · International 0.154 · National 0.000** | 2 |
| the leak through the real `.exe`, 5 international renderings + 1 control | the control masked, and if the finding is real, the others forwarded | **`+393471234567` · `+442079460958` · `+14155552671` · `+33 6 12 34 56 78` · `+31 6 12345678` all byte-identical upstream; `+39 347 1234567` → `[PHONE_1]`; `[EMAIL_1]` in every one** | 1 |
| the same value in the `RUST_LOG=trace` `masked_request` line | present, since the trace prints what is forwarded | **present** — and the *masked* control run carries **0** occurrences of the email, IBAN, card or phone it sent | 1 each |
| `+55 11 91234 5678` (published Brazilian mobile rendering) | masked whole, or missed | **truncated: `[PHONE_1] 5678`** — M10-R1's predicate | 1 |
| `+99 123 456 7890` / `+99 999 999 9999` (country code 99 does not exist) | rejected, if the plan check is what the README says | **masked** — `phonenumber::parse` answers `InvalidCountryCode` | 1 |
| `555-867-5309` (the repo's own `PHONE-01`) under `is_valid` | valid, if the README's sentence covers the US arm | **`is_valid` = false**, and it is masked anyway | 1 |
| cost of the fix: a general `+CC` arm gated by `parse(None, ..).is_valid()`, over `~/.cargo/registry/src` | more than today's 5, and the added ones should be real numbers | **17 413 files / 358.4 MB: shipped arms 5, validated general arm 83, and all 83 are inside the `phonenumber` crate's own corpus — 0 non-phone false positives** | 2 |
| the same arm **without** the validator | strictly worse — but by how much? | **273 spans**; the 190 rejected are expanded-year ISO 8601 dates (`+10000-12-31`, `+262142-12-31`, `+002016-07-08`) and floats (`+1441497364.649`). The validator is load-bearing | 1 |
| 1 360 800 card cells (900 seeds x 4 groupings x 6 separators x 7 before x 9 after) | 0 — round 9/10's fix should hold on non-ASCII separators too | **0 leaks, 0 round-trip breaks** | 1 |
| 26 880 corpus cells (56 values x 6 separators x 8 before x 10 after) | 0 — round 7's fix | **0 leaks, 0 round-trip breaks** | 1 |
| `content-length` on the real binary's restored (longer) response | header equals the actual body length | **264 = 264**, and **201 = 201** on the second run | 2 |

### Mutations run in round 11

`src/pii/recognizers.rs` only. Each was applied by byte substitution with the anchor asserted to
match **exactly once** before the write — an empty substitution is a green in disguise — and restored
by writing the original bytes back. The file's sha256 was compared against the `HEAD` blob
(`03b4a01606ff5907...`) after every restore, and `git status --porcelain` was empty at the end of the
round.

> **One run of mutation A was a *red in disguise*, and it is worth recording.** Launched from the Bash
> tool it failed at **link** time (`link: missing operand`) rather than at test time — Git-for-Windows'
> own `link` shadowing MSVC's. A non-zero exit is not a red: the harness has to show the failing
> **test name**. Re-run from PowerShell, where the MSVC env applies, it produced the assertion below.

| # | mutation | expected | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | add `"+393330000001"` and `"+33 6 12 34 56 78"` to the universal `Phone` row's `detected` in `RENDERING_ANSWERS` | red, if `RENDER-01` watches the rendering axis for this recognizer | **red, exactly one test**: `every_recognizer_records_the_renderings_it_detects`, `left: []` against `right: [(Phone, "+393330000001")]`. The guard machinery works; what was missing is the **answer** |
| B | revert `iban_mod97` to the pre-`831f916` byte slice (`&compact[..4]`) | red — [M11-R21](#m11-r21) says `UTF8-01` now closes this | **red**: *end byte index 4 is not a char boundary* |
| C | narrow `UTF8-01`'s corpus to `RENDERING_ANSWERS.iter().take(3)` | red — round 10 raised the floor to 1 500 so the widening cannot be undone silently | **red**: *"only 700 substitutions were built"* |

### What this round could not break

- **The card grouping fix (R30/R33)** over 1 360 800 cells, including the four non-ASCII separators
  and a neighbouring 11-digit P.IVA.
- **The separator fix (R25)** over all 56 corpus values in six separator characters, both neighbours
  varied; every masked case restores byte-exactly.
- **The domestic phone tier.** 0 of 13 000 generated valid numbers leak in their national rendering —
  the tier that carries a measured FP cost is the one with no recall gap.
- **Logging.** `RUST_LOG=trace` through the real `.exe` on a request carrying an email, an IBAN, a
  card and a phone number: **0** occurrences of any of the four raw values in 21 log lines, and the
  `masked_request` trace holds placeholders only. The one exception is the direct consequence of
  R41 — an unmasked value is forwarded *and* logged, which is why that invariant is downstream of
  masking being complete rather than independent of it.
- **Header correctness and the fail-closed posture.** `content-length` matches the restored body on
  every run; nothing is forwarded on a refusal.

### Guard-vs-product tally

Round 11: **2 in the product** ([R41](#m11-r41), a leak; [R42](#m11-r42), `precision/docs` on the
shipped coverage table), **0 on the net**. The milestone now stands at **28 on the net or the docs,
15 in the product**, 43 rows.

<a id="m11-r41"></a>
### M11-R41 — a phone number in its **international** rendering is forwarded in clear (E.164 0.918, spaced 0.154) or **truncated** — *leak, high*

**The mechanism, in one line:** the universal `Phone` recognizer's `+CC` family is two hand-written
groupings with **no validator and no right anchor**, so every other rendering of an international
number — including E.164, the canonical one — proposes no candidate at all, and the shapes it does
match can end inside the value.

```text
src/pii/recognizers.rs  (universal_recognizers, PiiKind::Phone)
  \+\d{1,3}{GAP}\d{2,4}{GAP}\d{2,4}{GAP}\d{3,4}     +CC + exactly three groups
| \+\d{1,3}{GAP}\d{2,4}{GAP}\d{5,8}                 +CC + exactly two groups
```

Both arms require **at least two separators**, so a compact `+CC` number is not a candidate; neither
ends on `(?-u:\b)`, so the two-group arm can stop mid-number.

**Repro — through the real `.exe`**, mock upstream, default provider preset, one email in each request
as the control:

```text
client sends : Chiamami al +393471234567 oppure scrivi a mario.rossi@example.com
upstream sees: Chiamami al +393471234567 oppure scrivi a [EMAIL_1]

client sends : Ring me on +442079460958 or email mario.rossi@example.com
upstream sees: Ring me on +442079460958 or email [EMAIL_1]

client sends : Call +14155552671 or email mario.rossi@example.com
upstream sees: Call +14155552671 or email [EMAIL_1]

client sends : Appelle le +33 6 12 34 56 78 ou ecris a mario.rossi@example.com
upstream sees: Appelle le +33 6 12 34 56 78 ou ecris a [EMAIL_1]

client sends : Bel +31 6 12345678 of mail mario.rossi@example.com
upstream sees: Bel +31 6 12345678 of mail [EMAIL_1]

CONTROL      : Chiamami al +39 347 1234567 oppure scrivi a mario.rossi@example.com
upstream sees: Chiamami al [PHONE_1] oppure scrivi a [EMAIL_1]
```

And the truncating shape, which is [M10-R1](M10.md#m10-r1)'s predicate rather than a miss:

```text
br +55 11 91234 5678 ok  ->  br [PHONE_1] 5678 ok    four digits of a real mobile, in clear
+33 1 23 45 67 89        ->  +33 1 [PHONE_1]         `23 45 67 89` is masked by a *domestic* family
```

**Measured.** 13 000 numbers generated across 13 templates in 10 regions, every one confirmed by
`phonenumber::is_valid`, each rendered by libphonenumber in its own three canonical modes and run
through `Vault::mask_all` against M10-R1's predicate (*no six consecutive digits of the value
survive*):

| rendering | leaks |
|---|---|
| `Mode::E164` | **11 934 / 13 000 — 0.918** |
| `Mode::International` | **2 000 / 13 000 — 0.154** (the FR five-pair family and NL `+31 6 NNNNNNNN`) |
| `Mode::National` | **0 / 13 000** |

Independently, over the repo's **own** corpus: **33 of 40** E.164 renderings of its phone positives
reach the provider in clear. The 7 that do not are masked by *coincidence* — as `CreditCard`
(`+4915112345678` is a Luhn-valid 13-digit run) or as `NationalId` (`+32475123456` satisfies an
11-digit checksum) — never as `Phone`, so they are an over-mask under the wrong kind rather than
coverage.

**Not a regression, and not new.** The two arms are byte-identical at `v0.4.0`, `v1.0.0`, `v1.1.0`,
`v1.2.0` and `v1.2.1`; they were written in `775945f` (M1.5, *"enumerating the shapes stops the match
from swallowing an unrelated trailing number"* — the same enumeration [M11-R30](#m11-r30) found on the
card), and round 7's `{GAP}` widening changed the separator character, not the grouping.

**Why ten milestones of guards could not see it.** Three reasons, and they are the same one:

1. **The corpus has a shape.** `tests/corpus/pii_cases.json` holds **35** phone positives; **not one**
   is written in E.164, and only two are `+CC` at all — both in a grouping the arm happens to
   enumerate. ([M4-R13](M4.md#m4-r13)'s lesson, landing for the fourth time this milestone.)
2. **`RENDERING_ANSWERS` is keyed on `(kind, pattern)` — one row per *recognizer*.** `Phone` is the
   only kind served by **two** families, and a phone number is published *both* domestically and
   internationally. The universal row records four renderings, one of them `+CC`, and its `why` says
   *"the US 3-3-4 renderings and the `+CC` arm"* — a sentence that reads as an answer about the `+CC`
   **value** while being an answer about the **arm**. The domestic rows answer for the domestic
   renderings. **No row is responsible for the value**, so the missing half has no home to be missing
   from.
3. **`looks_like_a_rendering` ([M11-R34](#m11-r34)) cannot help**, because the row quotes no
   international rendering to be checked — the gap is a rendering nobody wrote down, not a rendering
   written down in prose.

**Fix — the chokepoint, not a third and fourth grouping.** `phonenumber` is already a dependency and
already this tier's authority, and **the international form is the easiest case it has**: it carries
its own country code, so `phonenumber::parse(None, candidate)` needs no region hint — unlike the nine
domestic families, which do. So propose `+` followed by 7–15 digits in **any** grouping of the
existing gap class (plus `-` and `.`, which libphonenumber normalises away) and let `is_valid()`
decide, exactly as `national_phone_valid` already does one tier down.

Measured cost, on this project's own methodology — `~/.cargo/registry/src`, **17 413 files /
358.4 MB**:

| arm | spans masked | what they are |
|---|---|---|
| the two shipped `+CC` arms | **5** | — |
| a general `+CC` arm, `is_valid`-gated | **83** | **all 83 inside the `phonenumber` crate's own test and doc corpus — every one a real phone number. 0 non-phone false positives** |
| the same arm with **no** validator | 273 | the 190 extra are expanded-year ISO 8601 dates (`+10000-12-31`, `+262142-12-31`, `+002016-07-08`) and floats (`+1441497364.649`) |

So the validator is load-bearing, and this is *not* the unvalidated widening [M4-R6](M4.md#m4-r6)
would have to argue for; it is the trade M10 already took, on the easier half of the same problem.

**Three things the fix must carry, or it becomes the next round's finding:**

- **It gives the universal `Phone` recognizer its first *rejecting* validator**, and a `+CC` pattern
  that can reach past the value (an any-grouping arm does) in front of a validator that can reject is
  precisely the pair [M11-R13](#m11-r13) established and [M11-R33](#m11-r33) met again. It needs
  `shrink_on_reject`, or its own recognizer beside the US arm — which is the cleaner shape, because
  of the next bullet.
- **The US arm must keep `validate: None`.** `is_valid("+15558675309")` is **false**, and
  `555-867-5309` is the repo's own `PHONE-01` positive: gating the US arm would delete a shipped,
  measured capability and turn the corpus red. That asymmetry is a decision, and it belongs in the
  row rather than in a comment.
- **It is product-visible, so gate-vs-confidence is the maintainer's call.** `is_valid()` rejects
  things masked today — `+99 999 999 9999` (`InvalidCountryCode`) and `+391234567890` (well-formed,
  unassigned). Both are over-masks, so gating loses nothing a privacy tool needs; the alternative,
  `is_valid` setting `Confidence` rather than admission, keeps every current mask and adds the
  ISO-date over-masks above. The measurement says gate.

**The test that would have caught it, verified.** Adding `"+393330000001"` and `"+33 6 12 34 56 78"`
to the universal `Phone` row's `detected` in `RENDERING_ANSWERS` turns `RENDER-01` red immediately:

```text
assertion `left == right` failed: "+393330000001" must be detected as a single span
  left: []
 right: [(Phone, "+393330000001")]
```

The guard is sound; it was never asked the question. So the fix's test half is: **record the
international renderings in the row** — `Mode::E164` and `Mode::International` of at least one number
per family the arm claims — and, because those are libphonenumber's own output, generate them rather
than hand-write them, so the guard's corpus cannot drift from what the library prints.

**The rule, which is the durable half.** [M11-R30](#m11-r30) put the rendering question on the
*recognizer*. It belongs on the **kind**: *a value's published renderings are a property of the value,
so where one kind is served by more than one recognizer, every rendering must be owned by exactly one
row, and no row may answer for a rendering it does not carry.* `Phone` is the case that shows it,
because its two families split the rendering space between them and the split was never written
down — each row was locally true and their union had a hole. Promoted to
[`ARCHITECTURE.md`](../ARCHITECTURE.md) beside the four-questions paragraph and to
[`TESTING.md`](../TESTING.md) beside `RENDER-01`.


**Closed 2026-09-05 — a separate, validated recognizer for the compact `+CC` form.**

`\+\d{8,15}` gated by `phonenumber::parse(None, ..)`, which needs no region hint because the country
code is **in the value** — so this is the easiest case for the check the nine domestic families
already run, and it was the only phone family with no validator at all. Measured on the round's own
methodology over 358.4 MB: **5 masked spans → 83, all 83 real phone numbers.** Unvalidated it is 273,
the extra 190 expanded-year ISO dates and floats, so the arm is **validated rather than merely
widened** — that difference is the finding's own measurement and it decided the shape of the fix.

**A separate recognizer, not a third alternation**, deliberately: the existing `+CC` recognizer has
`validate: None`, and giving it a validator would also gate the US 3-3-4 arm — which masks
`555-867-5309`, `is_valid`-false and masked on purpose. Narrowing that is a coverage decision and not
this fix's to take (it is [M11-R42](#m11-r42)'s subject).

`shrink_on_reject: false`, and the reason is stated rather than inherited: the span is one unbroken
digit run, so there is no separator for `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` to cut at, and the trailing
`(?-u:)` prevents the over-reach that obliges a shrink elsewhere (M11-R13 / M11-R33). One budget
unit per `parse()`, per M10-R29 — one call per candidate here rather than up to nine.

**`RENDER-01` demanded the answer before the test suite would go green**, which is the registry doing
its job: adding a recognizer without recording what renderings it detects is a hard error now.
Mutation: neutering the arm turns `RENDER-01` red.

**The structural limit this exposes is worth more than the fix.** `RENDER-01` had a row for the `+CC`
recognizer and it passed, because a row lists the renderings *somebody wrote down* — the compact form
was in neither list, so nothing asked about it. **An unasked question looks exactly like an answered
one.** That is now stated on the guard and in `TESTING.md`; it is the honest boundary of every
registry in this file, and no further guard can close it, because the missing entry is by definition
the one nobody thought of.

**Verified:** 254/0/5 over 24 binaries, twice, identical; `cargo test-onnx` 290/0/22; `fmt` and
`clippy --all-targets -D warnings` clean.

> **Closed on the compact rendering only — see [M11-R43](#m11-r43).** This entry measured three
> renderings (E.164 0.918 · International 0.154 · National 0.000) and the shipped arm is one unbroken
> digit run, so it closes the first. Round 12 re-measured the other two at HEAD: **0 of 3 250** leak in
> `Mode::E164`, **973 of 3 250** still carry digits in `Mode::International`, and
> `+55 11 91234 5678 -> [PHONE_1] 5678` — the block quoted above — reproduces through the real `.exe`.
> The box stays flipped because the rendering this fix names is genuinely closed; the remainder is
> [M11-R43](#m11-r43)'s, open.

<a id="m11-r42"></a>
### M11-R42 — the README's coverage table credits the `+CC` and US phone arms with a numbering-plan check they do not run — *precision / docs, medium*

`README.md`'s detection matrix — the first table a reader meets, and the one an operator decides on —
says of `Phone`: *what makes it a match* = **"the country's real numbering plan says the number is
*assigned*"**, *coverage* = **"`+CC` and US always · 9 domestic plans"**.

Both halves are false for the universal recognizer, which carries `validate: None`
(`src/pii/recognizers.rs`, `universal_recognizers`):

- `+99 123 456 7890` and `+99 999 999 9999` are **masked**, though `phonenumber::parse` answers
  `InvalidCountryCode` — country code 99 is assigned to nobody;
- `555-867-5309` — the repo's own `PHONE-01` corpus positive — is **masked**, and
  `is_valid("+15558675309")` is **false**;
- and "`+CC` … always" overstates the coverage by the whole of [M11-R41](#m11-r41).

`ARCHITECTURE.md`'s *Locale coverage* bullet has the same shape (*"**Universal** — … and phone (US +
`+CC`). Always on."*), and `README.it.md` mirrors the table.

**This is the over-mask direction, so it is not a leak** — and the US arm's behaviour is *right*:
masking a fictional `555` number is privacy-first, and the `555` block is the test-corpus convention
precisely because it is unassigned. What is wrong is the **sentence**, in the place where a reader
forms their model of why the tier can be trusted. It also outlives R41's fix: even once `+CC` is
routed through `is_valid()`, the US arm still will not run a plan check, and it must not.

**Fix.** Split the cell by family rather than blending three tiers into one claim — the plan check is
what makes the **nine domestic regions** safe (it is the whole M10 argument), while the `+CC` and US
arms are **shape-anchored and deliberately permissive**, erring toward the over-mask that a `+` or a
US 3-3-4 grouping makes cheap. Three cells, three honest answers, and the coverage cell stops
claiming a rendering space R41 shows is not covered.

**Test.** None is possible on a README sentence — which is the argument for deriving it from the row
that *is* checkable: `RENDERING_ANSWERS`' universal `Phone` row already has a `why`, and `RENDER-01`
already asserts every rendering quoted in it ([M11-R34](#m11-r34)). Whatever the table says about
`+CC` should be that row's answer restated, not a second, uncheckable copy of it.

**Closed 2026-09-05 as a documentation fix, which is what it is.** `README.md` and `README.it.md`
credited the whole `Phone` row with *"the country's real numbering plan says the number is
assigned"*. True of the nine domestic families and now of the compact `+CC` form; **not** true of the
spaced `+CC` groupings or the US 3-3-4 arm, which have no validator and mask on shape alone — so
`+99 999 999 9999` is masked though country code 99 belongs to nobody, and `555-867-5309` (this
repo's own `PHONE-01`) is `is_valid`-false and masked anyway.

Both tables now split the claim: plan-checked for the domestic families and E.164, **shape-only** for
the other two, with the direction named — an over-mask, never a miss. Narrowing those two arms to the
plan check is a coverage decision and is *not* taken here; the row says what ships, which is the
whole of this finding.
