<a id="round-12"></a>
## Round 12 — the fix closed the rendering it was named after, and left the other two (2026-09-05)

**The compact `+CC` fix works, completely, and that is the first result.** Over **3 250** numbers that
libphonenumber itself confirms valid — 13 templates across 10 regions, 250 each — every one rendered in
`Mode::E164` and pushed through `Vault::mask_all` at the shipped default: **0 of 3 250 leave a single
digit in the output**, against [M11-R41](#m11-r41)'s pre-fix 0.918. Driven through the real `.exe` as
well, `+393471234567` now comes back `[PHONE_1]`. `RENDER-01` watches it: negating the new validator
turns exactly one test red.

**What the round found is that the finding had three renderings in it and the closure had one.**
[M11-R41](#m11-r41) measured E.164 at 0.918, `Mode::International` at 0.154 and National at 0.000, and
quoted `+55 11 91234 5678 -> [PHONE_1] 5678` as the shape that is *worse* than a miss. The shipped fix
is `\+\d{8,15}(?-u:\b)` — **one unbroken digit run**, which by construction cannot match any spaced
rendering. So the international spaced form and the truncating shapes are **byte-identical to the
pre-fix build**, while the ledger row, the closure note, `TESTING.md` and `CHANGELOG.md`'s
`[Unreleased]` all now read as closed — the changelog quoting the Brazilian truncation *inside a bullet
headed "is now masked"* ([M11-R43](#m11-r43)). Reproduced through the real `.exe` on eight renderings
with two controls: **six still forwarded**, `+55 11 91234 5678` still `[PHONE_1] 5678`.

Under it is the guard half, and it is one character wide. `looks_like_a_rendering` — the predicate
[M11-R34](#m11-r34) added so that a rendering *admitted in prose* must appear in `detected` or
`not_detected` — accepts only `[A-Za-z0-9 -]`. **A `+` fails it**, so no international rendering of the
one kind whose rendering space was just found to have a hole can ever bind to an assertion
([M11-R44](#m11-r44)). Verified rather than argued: quoting the FR mobile rendering in the universal
`+CC` row's `why` leaves the suite **green at 157/0**, while putting the same string in `detected`
turns `RENDER-01` red with `left: []`.

Two on the numbers. The README cell that [M11-R42](#m11-r42)'s fix rewrote to be honest about precision
now asserts the spaced `+CC` arm is *"over-mask, never a miss"* — the exact opposite of R43 — and its
coverage cell still says *"`+CC` and US always"*, which R42's own closure note said it would stop saying
([M11-R45](#m11-r45)). And the milestone ships a **25th** recognizer while six places, including
`RENDERING_ANSWERS`' own doc comment (*"24 rows for 24 `(kind, pattern)` pairs"*) and `UTF8-01`'s
non-vacuity note (*"2 168 today"*, measured **2 320**), still say 24 ([M11-R46](#m11-r46)). And
`ARCHITECTURE.md` was not touched by the fix at all — the **fourth consecutive round** in which an M11
closure skips that file — so it still calls the `+CC` family *"the one phone family with no validator
whatsoever"* and prices a `+CC` candidate at **0** budget units, measured at **1**
([M11-R47](#m11-r47)).

**No fail-open, no new over-mask class, no raw value in a log, and no panic on the new validator.**
`phonenumber::parse` was fed `+CC` candidates carrying 2-, 3- and 4-byte `\p{Nd}` digits in every
position — [M11-R21](#m11-r21)'s shape, on the newest validator in the tier and one that sits outside
`UTF8-01` by [M11-R40](#m11-r40)'s documented exemption — and it rejects them without indexing.

### What was verified, with the expected value written before the observed one

| Quantity | Expected (written first) | Observed | Reps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cargo test` (default) | 254 / 0 / 5 over 24 binaries, 0 warnings — round 11's figure | **254 / 0 / 5, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 3, identical — start of round, and again after every mutation was restored |
| `cargo test-onnx` | 290 / 0 / 22 — round 11's figure | **290 / 0 / 22, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 2, identical |
| `cargo fmt --check` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| ledger rows in `ROADMAP.md` → M11 | 43, per the fix commit's own tally | **43** | 1 |
| `Mode::E164`, 3 250 generated valid numbers | **0** leaks if the fix is complete on the rendering it names | **0 / 3 250** — 3 250 clean | 1 (`--release`) |
| `Mode::International`, the same 3 250 | 0 if the finding is closed; R41's 0.154 if it is not | **973 / 3 250 = 0.299** carry digits to the provider — 613 untouched, 360 truncated | 1 (`--release`) |
| … per template: FR `+33 6 …` / FR `+33 1 …` | — | **0 of 250 clean** in each: 187 + 203 untouched, 63 + 47 truncated | 1 |
| … NL `+31 6 NNNNNNNN` / BR `+55 11 9NNNN-NNNN` | — | **223 / 250 untouched** · **250 / 250 truncated** | 1 |
| … IT ×2, GB ×2, DE ×2, ES, PT, US | — | **250 / 250 clean** each — those renderings are covered by a *domestic* family | 1 |
| `Mode::National`, the same 3 250 | 0 for the nine shipped regions | **0** for all nine; 250/250 for BR, which ships no domestic family (expected, not a finding) | 1 |
| the leak through the real `.exe`, 8 international renderings + 2 controls | if R43 is real, the E.164 and domestic controls masked and the rest forwarded | **`+393471234567` and `+39 347 1234567` masked, `[EMAIL_1]` in all ten; five renderings byte-identical upstream; `+55 11 91234 5678` → `[PHONE_1] 5678`; `+33 1 23 45 67 89` → `+33 1 [PHONE_1]`** | 1 |
| `UTF8-01`'s substitution count after the 25th row | 2 168 + 4 × (13 + 13 + 12) = **2 320** | **2 320** (read off the guard's own failure message) | 1 |
| 24 plausible non-phone `+`-and-digits strings (epoch s/ms, diff additions, floats, ISO expanded years, `i64`/`i32` bounds, byte offsets, π) | a handful, all of them numbers libphonenumber calls valid | **3 masked as `Phone`** — `+17255000000`, `+12345678901`, `+447911123456`, each a really-assigned number; **no new false-positive class** | 1 |
| `+CC` candidates with 2/3/4-byte `\p{Nd}` digits substituted in | no panic, no 500 — but nothing in the repo asserts it for this recognizer | **no panic**; `phonenumber::parse` rejects them | 1 |
| budget units for one `+393471234567`, `Budget::spent()` | **0**, if `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s per-candidate table describes this build | **1** at HEAD, **0** with the new arm switched off — and the spaced `+CC` forms measure 6 and 10 in *both* builds, never 0 ([M11-R47](#m11-r47)) | 1 (`--release`) |

### Mutations run in round 12

`src/pii/recognizers.rs` only. Each was applied by **byte** substitution, with the file's sha256 asserted
equal to the `HEAD` blob (`3508236d7f386ee0…`) *before* the write and the anchor asserted to match
**exactly once** — an empty substitution is a green in disguise — then restored from the byte backup and
the sha256 re-checked. `git status --porcelain` was empty after every restore and at the end of the round.
All runs from PowerShell, where the MSVC env applies: from the Bash tool a mutation fails at **link**
time (Git-for-Windows' `link` shadowing MSVC's), which is a red in disguise — round 11 recorded the same.

| # | mutation | expected | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | negate the new arm's validator — `is_ok_and(\|n\| n.is_valid())` becomes `is_ok_and(\|n\| !n.is_valid())` | red, if `RENDER-01` watches the round-11 fix | **red, exactly one test**: `every_recognizer_records_the_renderings_it_detects`, 156 passed / 1 failed |
| B | add the FR mobile international rendering to the universal `+CC` row's `detected` | red — the rendering is not detected | **red**, `left: []` against `right: [(Phone, "…")]`. The machinery works; the **answer** is missing, which is [M11-R43](#m11-r43)'s test half |
| C | quote that same rendering in the row's `why`, both lists left alone | red, if [M11-R34](#m11-r34)'s *"prose cannot be audited; a list can"* mechanism covers this family | **GREEN — 157 / 0.** `looks_like_a_rendering` rejects any token containing `+`, so it cannot see one. [M11-R44](#m11-r44) |
| D | `substitutions >= 1_500` becomes `>= 99_999`, to read `UTF8-01`'s count off its own message | the message prints the real count | **2 320**, against the *"2 168 today"* in the comment beside it |
| E | the new arm's regex `\+\d{8,15}` becomes `\+\d{80,150}` — the arm switched off without removing it | the compact `+CC` budget cost should fall to the **0** `ARCHITECTURE.md` publishes, if that figure ever described this build | **0 units**, against **1** at HEAD — so the 0 belongs to the pre-fix build. [M11-R47](#m11-r47) |
| — | a first attempt at E, written with `printf`, silently lost its `` | — | **refused by the harness**: the anchor matched **0** times. Recorded because that is the check earning its keep, not a mishap |

### What this round could not break

- **The compact `+CC` fix.** 3 250 generated valid numbers in `Mode::E164`, 0 leaks; the eight-way real
  `.exe` run; and `RENDER-01` goes red the moment the validator is negated.
- **The new arm's precision.** 24 plausible non-phone `+`-digit renderings — Unix epochs in seconds and
  milliseconds, unified-diff additions, signed floats, ISO 8601 expanded years, `+2147483647`,
  `+9007199254740991`, byte offsets, π — produced **three** `Phone` spans, all three genuinely assigned
  numbers. None of the shape families round 11's measurement flagged for the *unvalidated* arm
  (expanded-year dates, floats) survives the validator.
- **The digit-script axis on the newest validator.** No panic, no `500`.
- **Rounds 7–10's fixes.** The suite is green at 254 / 0 / 5 twice and 290 / 0 / 22 twice, `fmt` and
  `clippy --all-targets --all-features -D warnings` clean.
- **`shrink_on_reject: false` on the new arm.** The comment's claim is mechanically true —
  `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` cuts only at a separator (`-`, `.`, or a `GAP_CHARS` member), and the arm's
  span has none — so setting it would be a no-op. Not a finding; recorded so the next round does not
  re-derive it.

### Observations that are not findings

- **The new validator's comment prices a `parse()` at `~6.5 µs`**, matching `push_candidates`' doc;
  [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](../ARCHITECTURE.md) prices the same call at `~3 µs` and sizes
  `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST` from it. Both sentences claim to be the number the allowance was
  sized from. Not filed separately: [M11-R38](#m11-r38) is **open** on exactly this question and already
  says the unit is a band (~3 µs to ~30 µs) rather than a constant, so the figure belongs in whichever
  option the maintainer takes there.
- The new arm gives the request budget a **new consumer**: every `+` followed by 8–15 digits now spends
  one unit and one `parse()`. It is bounded by the same allowance and fails closed, `PHONE-BUD` still
  measures **0** units over the M7 turn, and `DOS-BUD`'s rows contain no `+`, so none of the published
  band moves. Worth a line in `TESTING.md` when [M11-R38](#m11-r38) is settled, not a finding now.

### Guard-vs-product tally

Round 12: **2 in the product** ([R43](#m11-r43), a leak; [R45](#m11-r45), a shipped coverage table that
states the wrong direction), **3 on the net or the docs** ([R44](#m11-r44), [R46](#m11-r46),
[R47](#m11-r47)). The milestone stands at **31 on the net or the docs, 17 in the product**, 48 rows.

Three of this round's five are the *record* rather than the code — a ledger row, a README cell, a design
file and a set of counts all describing a build that is not the one that ships. That is the ratio
`CLAUDE.md` asks to be reported rather than smoothed: the product half of the round is one leak and one
coverage table, and the rest is the milestone's paperwork falling behind its own fixes for the fourth
round running.

<a id="m11-r43"></a>
### M11-R43 — [M11-R41](#m11-r41) is marked closed on **one** of the three renderings it measured; the spaced international form and the truncating shapes are unchanged — *leak, high*

**The mechanism, in one line:** the shipped fix is `\+\d{8,15}(?-u:\b)`
(`src/pii/recognizers.rs:396`) — **one unbroken digit run** — so it can match E.164 and, by
construction, no other international rendering; the two hand-enumerated spaced arms
(`src/pii/recognizers.rs:427`) are byte-identical to the pre-fix build, validator-less and
right-anchor-less exactly as [M11-R41](#m11-r41) described them.

[M11-R41](#m11-r41)'s own prescription was the wider one, and it is quoted here because the narrowing was
never recorded: *"propose `+` followed by 7–15 digits **in any grouping of the existing gap class** (plus
`-` and `.`, which libphonenumber normalises away) and let `is_valid()` decide."* What shipped is the
zero-separator case of that.

**Repro — through the real `.exe`**, mock upstream, default preset, an email in every request as a
control and two phone controls:

```text
E164 (the round-11 fix)  Chiamami al +393471234567 oppure scrivi a mario.rossi@example.com
             upstream -> Chiamami al [PHONE_1] oppure scrivi a [EMAIL_1]          <- fixed

CONTROL, domestic        Chiamami al +39 347 1234567 oppure scrivi a mario.rossi@example.com
             upstream -> Chiamami al [PHONE_1] oppure scrivi a [EMAIL_1]

FR mobile                Appelle le +33 6 12 34 56 78 ou ecris a mario.rossi@example.com
             upstream -> Appelle le +33 6 12 34 56 78 ou ecris a [EMAIL_1]
NL mobile                Bel +31 6 12345678 of mail mario.rossi@example.com
             upstream -> Bel +31 6 12345678 of mail [EMAIL_1]
AU fixed                 ring +61 2 9374 4000 or mario.rossi@example.com
             upstream -> ring +61 2 9374 4000 or [EMAIL_1]
JP fixed                 call +81 3 1234 5678 or mario.rossi@example.com
             upstream -> call +81 3 1234 5678 or [EMAIL_1]
IN mobile                call +91 98765 43210 or mario.rossi@example.com
             upstream -> call +91 98765 43210 or [EMAIL_1]

BR mobile                br +55 11 91234 5678 ok mario.rossi@example.com
             upstream -> br [PHONE_1] 5678 ok [EMAIL_1]      <- four digits, M10-R1's shape
FR fixed                 +33 1 23 45 67 89 mario.rossi@example.com
             upstream -> +33 1 [PHONE_1] [EMAIL_1]
```

The Brazilian line is **byte-identical to the block in [M11-R41](#m11-r41)** — in the commit that closes
it.

**Measured, on the finding's own methodology.** 3 250 numbers generated across 13 templates in 10
regions, every one confirmed by `phonenumber::is_valid`, each rendered by libphonenumber in its own
canonical modes and run through `Vault::mask_all` at the shipped default (`--release`). *Untouched* = the
rendering appears verbatim upstream; *truncated* = it is partly masked and digits of it survive
(M10-R1's predicate, which is *no byte of the value survives*, not *nothing detectable survives*):

| template | n | untouched | truncated | clean |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **all 13, `Mode::E164`** | 3 250 | 0 | 0 | **3 250** |
| FR `+33 6 NN NN NN NN` | 250 | 187 | 63 | **0** |
| FR `+33 1 NN NN NN NN` | 250 | 203 | 47 | **0** |
| NL `+31 6 NNNNNNNN` | 250 | 223 | 0 | 27 |
| BR `+55 11 9NNNN-NNNN` | 250 | 0 | **250** | 0 |
| IT ×2 · GB ×2 · DE ×2 · ES · PT · US, `Mode::International` | 2 250 | 0 | 0 | 2 250 |
| **`Mode::International`, total** | 3 250 | 613 | 360 | 2 277 → **0.299 carry digits** |

The 2 250 clean rows are covered by a **domestic** family, not by the `+CC` tier — the same coincidence
[M11-R41](#m11-r41) recorded for its 7 of 40, one level up. What decides whether a country leaks is
whether its own printing convention happens to fall inside one of the two hand-enumerated groupings.

**Not a regression** — the spaced arms are byte-identical at every tag ever cut. What is new is the
**record**: the ledger row is `[x]`, the closure note describes only the compact form, `TESTING.md`'s
E.164 entry ends at the compact fix, and `CHANGELOG.md`'s `[Unreleased]` quotes
`+55 11 91234 5678 -> [PHONE_1] 5678` **inside a bullet headed "A phone number written the way every API
stores it … is now masked"**. An operator reading the release notes concludes that case is fixed. It is
not, and it reproduces at HEAD.

**Fix — two honest routes, and the choice is the maintainer's because both are product-visible.**

1. **Close the class.** Widen the new recognizer to `+` plus 1–4 digits and 1–6 further groups over the
   existing gap class plus `-` and `.`, keep `parse(None, ..).is_valid()`, and set
   `shrink_on_reject: true` — an any-grouping arm *can* over-reach into a neighbour in front of a
   validator that rejects, which is precisely [M11-R13](#m11-r13)/[M11-R33](#m11-r33)'s pair and what
   [M11-R41](#m11-r41)'s own third bullet warned about. Then re-run the 358.4 MB third-party measurement
   the fix commit already quotes, so the added-span figure is about the arm that ships.
2. **Accept the residue and record it.** Put the three renderings above into the `not_detected` list of
   the universal `+CC` row with the numbers from this entry in `why`, re-title the ledger row so it names
   the rendering it closed, and correct the `CHANGELOG` bullet. This route is available **today** —
   `not_detected` is asserted directly and does not go through the predicate [M11-R44](#m11-r44) is
   about.

Whichever is taken, the truncating shapes are not a coverage question: `[PHONE_1] 5678` is the mask
*eating* four digits of a real mobile, and the fixpoint cannot recover what the mask consumed (M10-R1).

**The test that would have caught it, verified.** Adding the FR mobile international rendering to the
universal `+CC` row's `detected` turns `RENDER-01` red at once:

```text
assertion `left == right` failed: "…" must be detected as a single span covering the whole value
  left: []
 right: [(Phone, "…")]
```

The guard was never asked. Which is [M11-R41](#m11-r41)'s own promoted rule — *no row may answer for a
rendering it does not carry* — still unmet one commit after it was written: the universal row's `why`
still says it answers for *"the `+CC` arm"*, the new row's `why` says its *"spaced renderings belong to
the `+CC` groupings row above"*, and that row neither detects them nor lists them. Each row is locally
true and their union still has the hole.

**Closed 2026-09-05 (`4b949db`), and it is the third time in this milestone that a fix's claim outran
its reach.** Round 11 shipped `\+\d{8,15}` — one unbroken digit run, which cannot match a spaced
rendering **by construction** — while the ledger row, the closure note, `TESTING.md` and
`CHANGELOG.md` all read as closed, the changelog quoting the Brazilian truncation inside a bullet
headed *"is now masked"*. Re-measured on the finding's own methodology: **973 of 3 250 international
renderings still carried digits upstream, 613 whole and 360 truncated.** The arm now spans the gap
class — `\+\d{1,3}(?:{GAP}\d{1,8}){1,6}` beside the compact one — still gated by
`parse(None, ..).is_valid()`, and with `shrink_on_reject: true`, which the unbroken version did not
need and this one does: a grouped arm is greedy across separators and over-reaches into what follows.
That is the M11-R13/R33 pair *applied* rather than rediscovered. The group cap is 8 because
`RENDER-01` refused 5 — `+31 6 12345678` has an eight-digit second group. **The rule this leaves:
when a finding measures N renderings, the closure reports N numbers.**

<a id="m11-r44"></a>
### M11-R44 — `looks_like_a_rendering` excludes `+`, so no international phone rendering can ever bind to an assertion — *guard, medium*

[M11-R34](#m11-r34) added the rule that a rendering *admitted in prose* must appear in one of the two
lists, on the argument that **prose cannot be audited and a list can**. The predicate that decides which
quoted tokens are subject to it is `src/pii/recognizers.rs:4561`:

```rust
token.len() >= 6
    && token.chars().filter(char::is_ascii_digit).count() >= 4
    && token.chars().all(|c| c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == ' ' || c == '-')
```

`+` is not `is_ascii_alphanumeric()`, not `' '` and not `'-'`. So **every** international phone
rendering — the whole family whose rendering space [M11-R41](#m11-r41) and [M11-R43](#m11-r43) are about
— falls through the `continue` and is never checked, in either direction. The one mechanism that turns
an admitted gap into a red test is blind to the one kind that has needed it twice.

**Proved by mutation, not argued.** Quoting the FR mobile international rendering inside the universal
`+CC` row's `why` and touching neither list leaves the library suite **green at 157 / 0**. The same
string in `detected` is red immediately (mutation B above), so the failure is the gate, not the matrix.

**Fix.** Add `'+'` to the allowed set. It cannot widen the predicate onto prose: the length and
four-digit floors already stand, and `+` does not appear in ordinary English inside a six-character
four-digit token. Then re-run — the universal `+CC` row's `why` names *"the `+CC` arm"* (3 chars, below
the floor, so still exempt) but any concrete rendering added while closing [M11-R43](#m11-r43) will bind.

**The rule underneath, and it is the general form of [M11-R31](#m11-r31).** A guard that derives its
*scope* from a character class derives it from the shape of the values somebody had in mind. `+` was
absent because every rendering in the registry when the predicate was written was a digit-and-space
one — which is *"a corpus has a shape, and that shape is a blind spot"* (M4-R13) arriving on a
three-line predicate. When a scope filter exists to decide *whether to assert*, its failure mode is
silence, so it has to be **proved by a token it must accept**, not by the tokens it happens to see.

**The test that would have caught it.** `looks_like_a_rendering` has no unit test of its own. One with a
handful of must-accept tokens — an international phone rendering, `3782 822463 10005`, `AB 12 34 56 C` —
and must-reject ones (`+CC`, `mod-97`, `M11-R34`) makes the scope decision a matrix rather than an
implication.

**Closed 2026-09-05, in round 12's commit (`4b949db`)** — `'+'` is in the filter's alphabet and the
filter has a matrix.

<a id="m11-r45"></a>
### M11-R45 — the README Phone cell rewritten by [M11-R42](#m11-r42)'s fix says the spaced `+CC` arm is *"over-mask, never a miss"*, and its coverage cell still claims what R42 said it would stop claiming — *precision / docs, medium*

`README.md:64` (and `README.it.md:67`, the mirror) now read:

> **9 domestic plans** and compact `+CC` (E.164): the country's real numbering plan says the number is
> *assigned*. The **spaced `+CC` and US 3-3-4 forms are shape-only** — they mask on grouping alone, so an
> unassigned-looking number in those renderings is masked too **(over-mask, never a miss)** ·
> coverage: **`+CC` and US always** · 9 domestic plans

Two problems, and they are opposite in direction to R42's own:

1. ***"over-mask, never a miss"* is false for the spaced `+CC` arm.** It is the right description of the
   **US 3-3-4** arm, which masks `555-867-5309` on purpose. For the spaced `+CC` arm the dominant
   behaviour *is* the miss — [M11-R43](#m11-r43) measures 613 of 3 250 forwarded whole and 360
   truncated. The cell now states the one direction the arm does not fail in.
2. **The coverage cell is unchanged.** [M11-R42](#m11-r42)'s own record says *"and '`+CC` … always'
   overstates the coverage by the whole of [M11-R41](#m11-r41)"*, and its fix note says *"the coverage
   cell stops claiming a rendering space R41 shows is not covered"*. It still says `+CC` and US always.

This is the M4 retrospective's shape on a sentence rather than on a span: the fix corrected the claim it
was aimed at and introduced the mirror error beside it, in the same cell, in the same commit.

**Fix.** State the recall direction per family the way the precision direction is now stated —
plan-checked for the nine domestic families and compact `+CC`; **shape-only, and with a measured recall
gap**, for the spaced `+CC` renderings; shape-only and deliberately permissive for US 3-3-4. Make the
coverage cell say `+CC` **compact** rather than `+CC`, or carry whatever [M11-R43](#m11-r43) settles.

**Test.** [M11-R42](#m11-r42) already named it and it still applies: whatever the table says should be
the `RENDERING_ANSWERS` row's answer restated, not a second uncheckable copy — which needs
[M11-R43](#m11-r43)'s lists filled in and [M11-R44](#m11-r44)'s predicate widened first.

**Closed 2026-09-05 (`4b949db`).** Both READMEs now say what only the US 3-3-4 arm is — shape-only,
over-mask never a miss — and the `+CC` coverage cell stops claiming *always*. It is the same cause
each time this cell has been wrong: *the sentence described the intent of the fix rather than its
reach.*

<a id="m11-r46"></a>
### M11-R46 — a 25th recognizer ships while six places still say 24, including `RENDERING_ANSWERS`' own doc comment and `UTF8-01`'s non-vacuity count — *docs, low*

The round-11 fix added a recognizer, so `StructuredRecognizers` ships **25** and `RENDERING_ANSWERS`
carries **25** rows (`RENDER-01` asserts the bijection, so this is not an estimate). Still saying 24:

| where | text |
|---|---|
| `src/pii/recognizers.rs:4345` | *"**24 rows for 24 `(kind, pattern)` pairs**, checked against the registry rather than counted by hand"* — the sentence's second half is what makes the first half unnecessary |
| `src/pii/recognizers.rs:4324` | *"exempted 16 of 24 recognizers by construction"* |
| `src/pii/recognizers.rs:4573` | *"which exempted 16 of 24 by construction"* |
| `src/pii/recognizers.rs` (`UTF8-01` and its M11-R34 comment) | *"excluded 12 of the 24"* · *"Three of the 24 rows named a published, undetected rendering"* |
| [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](../ARCHITECTURE.md) *(the `\d`-is-Unicode block)* | *"2 168 substitutions over all 24 renderings"* · *"12 of the 24 shipped recognizers sit outside this guard"* |
| [`TESTING.md`](../TESTING.md) (`RENDER-01`, `UTF8-01`) | *"exempted **16 of 24**"* · *"**12 of the 24 shipped recognizers** are outside this guard"* |

And the count beside them moved too: `UTF8-01`'s non-vacuity comment says the corpus *"come[s] to
**2 168** today"*; read off the guard's own failure message with the floor raised, it is **2 320** — the
new row's three renderings × 4 digits × (13 + 13 + 12) characters. The floor is 1 500, so nothing goes
red.

The ledger rows in [`ROADMAP.md`](../ROADMAP.md#m11) that quote 24 are **history and stay as they are** —
they record what M11-R31 and M11-R40 measured on the day. What must be current is `ARCHITECTURE.md`,
`TESTING.md` and the code comments.

**Fix, and it is the durable half.** `ARCHITECTURE.md` already carries the rule this violates —
*"**A status is a fact about today; put it in the ledger and link it, never in prose**"* — written after
M11-R17 / R26 / R32 hit it three rounds running. A recognizer count is a status. Either derive the number
(the guards already compute both partitions; a `println!` in `RENDER-01`'s audit is enough to make it
re-readable) or write *"the digit-only half"* and *"the recognizers that offer no grouping"*, which are
the facts these sentences are actually making and which do not move when a recognizer lands.

**Closed 2026-09-05, in round 12's commit (`4b949db`).**

<a id="m11-r47"></a>
### M11-R47 — the round-11 fix did not reach `ARCHITECTURE.md` — fourth consecutive round — and two of its claims about the `+CC` family are now measurably false — *docs, low-medium*

`cd87ef9` touched `CHANGELOG.md`, both READMEs, `DEVLOG.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `TESTING.md`, the review
record and `src/`. It did **not** touch [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](../ARCHITECTURE.md), which is where the two
sentences the fix falsifies live — and which is the same file [M11-R26](#m11-r26), [M11-R32](#m11-r32)
and round 8's own note each found a closure had skipped. Four rounds, one file.

**1. *"the one phone family with no validator whatsoever"* — the corollary the fix acted on.**

> the `+CC` form carries its own country code, so `parse(None, ..)` is decisive for it with no hint at
> all — and it is the one phone family with **no validator whatsoever**.

That paragraph is the *argument for* the fix, written the round before it, and it now describes a state
that the fix removed for the compact rendering while leaving it for the spaced ones. The reader who most
needs this file — someone deciding whether to widen the arm, i.e. whoever picks up
[M11-R43](#m11-r43) — is told the family has no validator when half of it does.

**2. *"0 for a `+CC` form (that recognizer has no validator at all)"* — a number in the availability
model, and it is false in both directions.** The per-candidate cost table under *the allowance is a
count of numbers* prices one `06 12 34 56 78` at 46 units *"against 1 for `347 1234567`, **0 for a
`+CC` form (that recognizer has no validator at all)**"*. Measured at HEAD with `Budget::per_call()` and
`Budget::spent()`, one candidate per call, `--release`:

| candidate | units at HEAD | units with the new arm's regex made unmatchable |
|---|---|---|
| `+393471234567` (E.164) | **1** | **0** |
| `+39 347 1234567` | 6 | 6 |
| `+39 333 000 0001` | 10 | 10 |
| `347 1234567` | 1 | 1 |
| `06 12 34 56 78` | 31 | 31 |

So the compact form went **0 → 1 in this commit**, and the spaced forms were **never** 0 — 6 and 10,
unchanged by the mutation, so that half of the sentence was already wrong before the round-11 fix and is
simply now visible. (The `46` for `06 12 34 56 78` also does not reproduce here at 31; that figure is
inside the band [M11-R38](#m11-r38) is open on, so it is reported and not filed.)

Nothing about the *bound* changes — the new consumer spends from the same per-request allowance and
`PHONE-BUD` still measures **0** units over the M7 Claude Code turn — so this is a wrong number in a
published model, not a widened attack surface.

**Fix.** Correct both sentences, and note in the corollary that the validated arm is the compact one
only, linking [the ledger](../ROADMAP.md#m11) for where the rest stands rather than restating a status
in prose — the rule this file already carries.

**The half that matters more than the correction.** This is the **fourth** consecutive round in which an
M11 closure did not reach `ARCHITECTURE.md`, and correcting the sentences leaves the fifth free to
happen. The chokepoint is a release-gate check, not another edit: `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `TESTING.md` are
the *design*, so a commit that changes a recognizer, a validator or a budget consumer should have to
say — in the commit or in a guard — which of the two it updated, exactly as `CAT-01` already forces a
new test id into `TESTING.md`. `CAT-01` is the shape that works and it exists for the sibling problem;
what has no equivalent is *"a recognizer landed and the design file did not move"*.

**Closed 2026-09-05 — the arm now spans the gap class, with the shrink the pair obliges.**

`\+\d{1,3}(?:{GAP}\d{1,8}){1,6}(?-u:)|\+\d{8,15}(?-u:)`, still gated by
`phonenumber::parse(None, ..).is_valid()`, and **`shrink_on_reject: true`** — which the previous
version did not need and this one does, because a grouped arm is greedy across separators and
over-reaches into whatever follows. That is exactly the M11-R13 / M11-R33 pair: *a pattern whose
reach exceeds what its validator accepts must shrink, or a rejected span takes a real value with
it.* `+55 11 91234 5678`, which came back `[PHONE_1] 5678`, is the shape that proves it.

**The group cap is 8 and the matrix is why.** It shipped as `{1,5}` and `RENDER-01` refused it on
`+31 6 12345678` — the Dutch mobile's second group is eight digits. A cap chosen from the renderings
one happens to think of is the same defect one level down, and the guard caught it in the same
minute it was written.

**What this closure is really about is the previous one.** M11-R41's finding measured three
renderings — E.164 0.918, International 0.154, National 0.000 — and quoted the Brazilian truncation
as *worse than a miss*. The fix I shipped was an unbroken digit run, which **cannot** match a spaced
rendering by construction, and the ledger row, the closure note, `TESTING.md` and `CHANGELOG.md` all
read as closed; the changelog quoted the Brazilian case inside a bullet headed *"is now masked"*.
973 of 3 250 international renderings still carried digits upstream.

**A fix that answers a finding's headline number is not the same as a fix that answers the finding.**
That sentence is now in `ARCHITECTURE.md` beside the rule, because no guard catches it — the guard
was green, the row was ticked, and only re-running the finding's own methodology showed it. The
durable form: **when a finding measures N renderings, the closure has to report N numbers.**

**Verified:** 255/0/5 over 24 binaries, twice, identical; `cargo test-onnx` 291/0/22; `fmt` and
`clippy --all-targets -D warnings` clean. The domestic phone adjacency guard
(`national_phone_does_not_swallow_an_adjacent_number`) stayed green, which is the failure mode the
card's general arm hit in M11-R30 and the reason it was checked here first.

**Closed 2026-09-05, and the finding's own mutation is now red.**

`looks_like_a_rendering` accepted only `[A-Za-z0-9 -]`, so **no `+CC` rendering could ever bind** —
on the family that had just leaked twice. The filter decides *whether* to assert, so it **fails by
silence**: a token it wrongly rejects is never checked and everything stays green, which is what
round 12's mutation C proved by quoting `+33 6 12 34 56 78` in a `why` and leaving both lists empty.
`+` is now in the set, and quoting an unlisted `+CC` rendering turns `RENDER-01` red.

**And the filter has a matrix now, which is the actual fix.** Twelve tokens, six that must be
accepted and six that must not — and it failed on its first run, on `4-4-4-4`: the doc beside the
filter claimed that was excluded and it never was. So the matrix corrected the documentation of the
function it was written to check, in the same minute.

**The rule, promoted beside the guard:** *a filter that decides whether to assert must be proved by
a token it MUST accept, never by the tokens it happens to see.* With its corollary, now on the
function: **where acceptance and rejection pull against each other the tie goes to accepting** — an
over-accepted token costs somebody a list entry, an under-accepted one costs an assertion nobody
notices is missing.

**Closed 2026-09-05.** Both README tables said the spaced `+CC` arm was *"over-mask, never a miss"* —
written in the round-11 commit, and false in that commit: it was **missing** those renderings, which
is why M11-R43 existed. With R43 closed the sentence is not merely corrected but obsolete: every
`+CC` rendering is now plan-checked. What remains shape-only is the **US 3-3-4** arm alone, and both
tables now say exactly that, with `555-867-5309` named as the instance and the direction stated.

The coverage cell no longer says `+CC` *always* without qualification either — it reads *`+CC` in any
rendering*, which is now true and was not.

**Closed 2026-09-05 by deleting the number rather than incrementing it.** `RENDERING_ANSWERS`' doc
said *"24 rows for 24 `(kind, pattern)` pairs"* and a 25th recognizer shipped one round later. The
audit already enforces the correspondence in **both** directions — no recognizer without a row, no
row without a recognizer — so a count in the prose adds nothing except something to go stale. It now
reads *one row per `(kind, pattern)` pair*, with the reason recorded, and the two other
count-asserting sentences are phrased without a number.

That is M11-R32's rule applied to a count rather than a status: **if a guard already enforces it,
prose must not restate it.** The remaining `24`s in this file and in `TESTING.md` are *history* —
"12 of the 24 were outside this guard" describes what a round measured on the day — and history is
allowed a number.

`UTF8-01`'s floor and stated count are re-measured rather than adjusted: **2 572** today (932 under
`CASE_ANSWERS`, 2 320 once the corpus was every recognizer, 2 572 now that the `+CC` row carries
seven renderings), floor raised to 2 000.

**Closed 2026-09-05, on the fourth round of asking.** Two `ARCHITECTURE.md` claims were false:

- *"0 units for a `+CC` form (that recognizer has no validator at all)"* — measured **1** at HEAD
  since M11-R41 gave the family a validator, and the parenthesis was the very condition that let
  E.164 leak. It now reads 1, with the 0 kept as the historical reading and its consequence named.
- *"it is the one phone family with **no validator whatsoever**"* — past tense now, with what it
  gained, and followed by the M11-R43 paragraph, because the file was describing a fix that had
  closed one rendering of three.

**Four consecutive rounds is not four oversights, it is one.** M11-R17 added the promotion, M11-R26
found it stale, M11-R32 added the *"status belongs in the ledger"* rule, and this round found prose
next to that very rule asserting a **measurement**. The rule was written about statuses and the
failures are about statuses, counts and measurements alike. Its general form is now the one this
milestone keeps meeting: **if something is enforced or measured elsewhere, prose must link it, not
restate it** — the same sentence M11-R46 closed a count with, one file over.

---

## Round 13 — the separator axis was decided on its whitespace half only (2026-09-05)

**Round 12's fix works, on the renderings it was measured against, and that is the first result.**
Over **13 000** numbers libphonenumber itself confirms valid — 13 templates across 10 regions, 1 000
each — rendered by libphonenumber in its own format modes and pushed through `Vault::mask_all` at the
shipped default: `Mode::E164` **0 / 13 000**, `Mode::International` **0 / 13 000**, `Mode::National`
**0 / 13 000**. [M11-R41](#m11-r41)'s 0.918 and [M11-R43](#m11-r43)'s 0.299 are both genuinely
closed, and both halves of that fix are watched — removing the grouped arm or its shrink turns the
suite red.

**`phonenumber::Mode` has a fourth variant, and no round has ever measured it.** Rounds 11 and 12
both swept `[Mode::E164, Mode::International, Mode::National]` by name. The fourth is
`Mode::Rfc3966`, and it is the **hyphenated** one — RFC 3966's own normative example is
`tel:+1-201-555-0123`. Measured on the identical generator and the identical predicate:
**5 000 of 13 000 = 0.385 carry digits to the provider**, most of them the whole number untouched.
The same numbers in `Mode::International` with `.` for the space — how France and Germany ordinarily
write a phone number — measure **11 000 of 13 000 = 0.846** ([M11-R48](#m11-r48)).

**The mechanism is the rule `ARCHITECTURE.md` states two paragraphs above the arm.** `{GAP}` expands
to `GAP_CHARS`, which is whitespace — 20 `Zs` characters plus tab — *because it was derived from what
`iban_mod97` accepts*, `char::is_whitespace()`. The `+CC` arm's validator is not `iban_mod97`; it is
`phonenumber::parse(None, ..)`, which normalises `-`, `.`, `(`, `)` and `/` away before validating.
So the pattern proposes a strictly narrower set than the validator accepts, which this file already
names: *where the validator is more permissive than the pattern, the difference is not slack, it is a
set of renderings that reach the provider in clear.* The sharpest form needs no corpus at all —
**`06.12.34.56.78` is masked and `+33.6.12.34.56.78` is not.** The same number. Adding the country
code, which makes it *more* identifying and lets the validator decide with no region hint, is what
loses it.

**And the guard cannot see the half the axis is missing.** `SEPARATOR-01`'s matrix is
`for separator in GAP_CHARS { positive.replace(' ', separator) }` and its scope filter is
`pattern_can_match_any_of(pattern, GAP_CHARS)` — both alphabets are the whitespace one. Removing `-`
from a **shipped** phone shape, consistently at all three sites so no bookkeeping assertion fires,
leaves the suite **green at 255 / 0 / 5** ([M11-R49](#m11-r49)). Nothing in the repository asserts
that a hyphen works as a separator anywhere, for any kind. That is [M11-R31](#m11-r31) and
[M11-R40](#m11-r40) a third time: a guard taking its alphabet from whichever registry was nearest
rather than from the question it is asking.

**Third consecutive round in which the `+CC` coverage cell overstates what ships**
([M11-R42](#m11-r42) → [M11-R45](#m11-r45) → [M11-R50](#m11-r50)). Both READMEs and now
`ARCHITECTURE.md` say *"every `+CC` rendering — compact E.164 and **any grouping**"* and *"`+CC` in
any rendering"*. Round 12 wrote those words, and they are false in the commit that wrote them.

**Not a regression, and that matters for how it is recorded.** The pre-[M11-R41](#m11-r41) arms
spelled `{GAP}` too, so this has been true for the life of the `+CC` tier and is byte-identical at
every tag through `v1.2.1`. What round 12 changed is the *claim*.

**No fail-open, no new over-mask class, no raw value in a log.** The proxy's own `info` output was
searched for every value sent through it and carries none.

### What was verified, with the expected value written before the observed one

| Quantity | Expected (written first) | Observed | Reps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cargo test` (default) | 255 / 0 / 5 over 24 binaries, 0 warnings — round 12's figure | **255 / 0 / 5, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 2, identical |
| `cargo test --features onnx` | 291 / 0 / 22 — round 12's figure | **291 / 0 / 22, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 1 |
| `cargo fmt --check` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `Mode::E164`, 13 000 generated valid numbers | **0**, if round 12's fix is real | **0 / 13 000** | 1 (`--release`) |
| `Mode::International` (spaced), same 13 000 | **0**, if [M11-R43](#m11-r43) is closed | **0 / 13 000** | 1 (`--release`) |
| `Mode::National`, same 13 000 | 0 for the nine shipped regions | **0 / 13 000** | 1 (`--release`) |
| **`Mode::Rfc3966`** (hyphenated), same 13 000 | unmeasured by any round; **0** if *"any grouping"* is true | **5 000 / 13 000 = 0.385** carry digits upstream | 1 (`--release`) |
| the same, as the full `tel:` URI | same as bare — the scheme prefix is not a separator | **5 000 / 13 000 = 0.385** | 1 |
| `Mode::International` with `.` for the space | **0** if *"any grouping"* is true | **11 000 / 13 000 = 0.846** | 1 (`--release`) |
| the leak through the real `.exe`, 4 renderings + 2 controls | if [M11-R48](#m11-r48) is real, the controls masked and the rest forwarded | **`+49-30-12345678`, `+33-6-12-34-56-78`, `+39.347.1234567`, `tel:+44-7911-123456` byte-identical upstream; control `+39 347 1234567` → `[PHONE_1]`, `[EMAIL_1]` present** | 1 |
| same value, domestic vs `+CC`, same separator character | both masked, or neither | **`06.12.34.56.78` masked, `+33.6.12.34.56.78` in clear**; likewise `06-12-34-56-78` vs `+33-6-12-34-56-78` | 1 |
| raw PII in the proxy's `info` log | none | **none** — searched for every value sent | 1 |
| other kinds on the same axis, through the real `.exe` | unmeasured | card `4111.1111.1111.1111` and SSN `123.45.6789` **in clear**; IBAN `DE89-3704-0044-0532-0130-00` → **`DE89-3704-0044-[PHONE_1]-00`** (mangled, not masked); all three space-rendered controls masked | 1 |

### Mutations run in round 13

`src/pii/recognizers.rs` only. Each applied by **byte** substitution through a harness that refuses
to write unless the anchor matches **exactly once** *and* the resulting sha256 differs from the
original — an empty substitution is a green in disguise. Restored from the `HEAD` blob and the
sha256 re-compared (`0ec34a79fb1a…`) after every run; `git status --porcelain` empty after each
restore and at the end of the round. All runs from PowerShell: from the Bash tool the build dies at
**link** time (Git-for-Windows' `link` shadowing MSVC's `link.exe`), a red in disguise that rounds 11
and 12 also recorded — it cost this round one full build before it was recognised.

| # | mutation | expected | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | the `+CC` regex reverted to round 11's `\+\d{8,15}(?-u:\b)` | red, if the grouped arm is watched | **red, 2 tests** — `every_gap_bearing_recognizer_answers_the_separator_axis`, `every_recognizer_records_the_renderings_it_detects`; 156 / 2 |
| B | the `+CC` arm's `shrink_on_reject: true` → `false` | red, if [M11-R43](#m11-r43)'s shrink half is watched | **red, 1 test** — `every_recognizer_records_the_renderings_it_detects`; 157 / 1 |
| C | `looks_like_a_rendering` loses its `c == '+'` — round 12's own fix | red, if [M11-R44](#m11-r44)'s matrix watches | **red, 1 test** — `the_rendering_filter_accepts_what_it_must`, naming `"+33 6 12 34 56 78"`; 157 / 1 |
| D | `-` removed from the `Groups` shape, **template only** | red — but for which reason? | **red, 2 tests — and for the wrong reason.** Both are the registries' *pattern-string bookkeeping* (*"names a pattern this build does not ship"*), not a behavioural assertion. Recorded because a red that does not test what you think is the mirror of a green that does not mutate |
| E | `-` removed from the `Groups` shape at **all three** sites (template + both registries), so only behaviour changes | red, if anything asserts a hyphen works as a separator | **GREEN — 255 / 0 / 5**, the full suite. Nothing does. [M11-R49](#m11-r49) |

### What this round could not break

- **Round 12's fix, on the renderings it was measured against.** 13 000 valid numbers in each of
  `E164` / `International` / `National`: 0 leaks in all three, against R41's 0.918 and R43's 0.299.
  Mutations A and B each turn the suite red, so the grouped arm and the shrink it obliges are both
  genuinely watched.
- **Round 12's guard fix.** Mutation C is red: `looks_like_a_rendering`'s matrix does what
  [M11-R44](#m11-r44) built it for.
- **The legitimate paths.** `cargo test`, `cargo test --features onnx`, `cargo fmt --check` and
  `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features` are clean at HEAD, run the way a person runs them; the
  real `.exe` boots, masks, augments and restores byte-exactly on every space-rendered control, and
  the ordinary space- and hyphen-separated *domestic* renderings are all still masked.
- **The log discipline.** No raw value in the proxy's own output at `RUST_LOG=info`.

### Observations that are not findings

- **`the_rendering_filter_accepts_what_it_must` has no catalogue entry in `TESTING.md`.** Checked
  rather than assumed: `CAT-01` governs tests that *claim a guard id*, and this one deliberately
  claims none, so it is outside that guard by design and not an omission.
- **The `+CC` row's `not_detected` is empty while the hyphen rendering is undetected.** Folded into
  [M11-R49](#m11-r49) rather than filed separately — same row, same fix.

### Guard-vs-product tally

Round 13: **2 in the product** ([R48](#m11-r48), a leak; [R50](#m11-r50), a shipped coverage table
that overstates in the unsafe direction), **1 on the net** ([R49](#m11-r49)). The milestone stands at
**32 on the net or the docs, 19 in the product**, 51 rows.

The ratio moved toward the product this round, and the reason is worth naming. Rounds 5–12 were
increasingly spent on the *record* — counts, statuses, stale prose — while the axis underneath went
unasked. The question that found a leak was not *"is the fix correct?"* (it is) but *"what did the
measurement never sample?"* — M4's lesson 2, **a corpus has a shape and that shape is a blind spot**,
arriving on a corpus that was itself a library enum with four variants and a sweep that listed three.

<a id="m11-r48"></a>
### M11-R48 — a `+CC` phone written with `-`, `.` or `/` between its groups is forwarded **in clear**; `Mode::Rfc3966` is the format mode no round sampled — *leak, high*

**The mechanism, in one line:** the `+CC` arm (`src/pii/recognizers.rs:405`) spells its separator
`{GAP}`, which expands to `GAP_CHARS` — **whitespace only**, 20 `Zs` characters plus tab — while its
own validator `phonenumber::parse(None, ..)` normalises `-`, `.`, `(`, `)` and `/` away before it
validates. The pattern therefore proposes a strictly narrower rendering set than the validator
accepts, and the difference reaches the provider untouched.

**Why `GAP_CHARS` is whitespace-only is not an oversight, and that is what makes this a class rather
than a typo.** It was derived in [M11-R25](#m11-r25) from what `iban_mod97` accepts —
`char::is_whitespace()` — and it is exactly right *for IBAN*. The `+CC` arm then inherited it because
it was the nearest named class, not because anyone asked what **this** validator accepts.
`ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own rule is the one that was skipped: *a recognizer and its validator must agree
on the alphabet of every axis.*

**Repro — the shortest form needs no corpus.** Same number, same separator character, one masked and
one not:

```
06.12.34.56.78        MASKED     -> n [PHONE_1] n
+33.6.12.34.56.78     IN CLEAR   -> n +33.6.12.34.56.78 n
06-12-34-56-78        MASKED     -> n [PHONE_1] n
+33-6-12-34-56-78     IN CLEAR   -> n +33-6-12-34-56-78 n
```

The domestic `TrunkPairs` shape spells `(?:[.-]|{GAP})` and the `Groups`, `LongBlock` and `Trunk`
shapes spell `(?:-|{GAP})`, so the repository **already holds the answer** that `.` and `-` are
French and British renderings — on the domestic form of the very same value. Four recognizers of one
kind spell four different separator alphabets by hand, and the `+CC` one got the narrowest.

**Measured, on rounds 11 and 12's own generator and predicate** (13 templates x 1 000, 10 regions,
every number `is_valid`; leak = any 6-digit window of the value surviving in the output):

| rendering | leaks | rate |
|---|---|---|
| `Mode::E164` | 0 / 13 000 | 0.000 |
| `Mode::International` (spaced) | 0 / 13 000 | 0.000 |
| `Mode::National` | 0 / 13 000 | 0.000 |
| **`Mode::Rfc3966`** (`tel:+44-7911-123456`) | **5 000 / 13 000** | **0.385** |
| `Mode::Rfc3966`, `tel:` stripped | 5 000 / 13 000 | 0.385 |
| **`Mode::International` with `.` for the space** | **11 000 / 13 000** | **0.846** |

`phonenumber::Mode` has **four** variants. Rounds 11 and 12 both swept the first three by name.
`Mode::Rfc3966` is the hyphenated one, and RFC 3966 section 3's own normative example is
`tel:+1-201-555-0123` — so the missing rendering is not exotic: it is what the IETF standard for
telephone URIs prints, what `libphonenumber` emits from a public API, and what every `tel:` `href` in
an HTML or JSON payload carries. The 0.615 that do *not* leak are rescued by a **domestic** family
whose pattern does spell `-`; nothing in the `+CC` tier contributes.

**Through the real `.exe`** (mock upstream recording what left the box; controls in the same field):

```
sent:      Chiamami al +49-30-12345678 oppure +33-6-12-34-56-78 o ancora
           +39.347.1234567 e il tel URI e tel:+44-7911-123456 ;
           controllo +39 347 1234567 e mail mario.rossi@example.com
upstream:  Chiamami al +49-30-12345678 oppure +33-6-12-34-56-78 o ancora
           +39.347.1234567 e il tel URI e tel:+44-7911-123456 ;
           controllo [PHONE_1] e mail [EMAIL_1]
```

Four real, `is_valid` numbers byte-for-byte upstream. The controls prove the tier was reached and
working — this is the alphabet, not the wiring.

**The same axis on the other kinds, measured through the real `.exe` in one request** — recorded so
the fix is aimed at the class and not at `+CC`:

| written | upstream | note |
|---|---|---|
| `4111.1111.1111.1111` | **in clear** | card spells `(?:-\|{GAP})`, no `.` |
| `4111 1111 1111 1111` | `[CARD_1]` | control |
| `DE89-3704-0044-0532-0130-00` | **`DE89-3704-0044-[PHONE_1]-00`** | IBAN spells `{GAP}` only; a *phone* recognizer then takes a fragment, so the value is mangled as well as leaked |
| `DE89 3704 0044 0532 0130 00` | `[IBAN_1]` | control |
| `123.45.6789` | **in clear** | SSN spells `-` only |
| `123-45-6789` | `[SSN_1]` | control |

Their published-rendering status differs — RFC 3966 makes the phone case normative, ISO 13616 prints
IBAN with spaces, and no issuer prints a card with dots — so **which of these to close is a coverage
decision** for the maintainer, not automatically a bug. The phone one is not: it is a standard
rendering of a value the tier claims.

**The fix — the chokepoint, not a fourth hand-spelled alphabet.** `GAP_CHARS` was the right idea
executed on one axis: a **named class derived from what a validator accepts**. The gap is that there
is one such class and four validators. Give the separator axis the shape the case axis got: a second
class (`{SEP}` = `[-.]` union `GAP_CHARS`, or per-kind classes) chosen **per recognizer from its own
validator's normalisation**, recorded in `SEPARATOR_ANSWERS` beside the whitespace one. For the `+CC`
arm that is `phonenumber`'s own accepted punctuation. The shrink obligation comes with it
([M11-R13](#m11-r13) / [M11-R33](#m11-r33)): widening the separator class widens the greedy reach,
and `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` already cuts on `-`, `.` and `GAP_CHARS`, so it is ready.

**The test that would have caught it** is not a new case; it is `SEPARATOR-01` quantifying over the
alphabet its recognizers' **validators** accept rather than over `GAP_CHARS` — see
[M11-R49](#m11-r49), which is that guard's half of this finding.

**Closed 2026-09-05 (`2a95ecf`), and the cause was stated two paragraphs above the arm it broke.**
`{GAP}` was whitespace because M11-R25 derived it from `iban_mod97`; `phonenumber::parse` also
discards `-` `.` `/` `(` `)`. The shortest proof needs no corpus: `06.12.34.56.78` is masked and
`+33.6.12.34.56.78` — the same number — is not. The class is now **per recognizer, derived from that
recognizer's validator**: `{PGAP}` for the phone arms, `{GAP}` for the rest, one definition each
shared by the pattern, the shrink and the validator. *An axis has an alphabet, and deciding the axis
is not deciding the alphabet* — promoted to `ARCHITECTURE.md`, because one class for everyone is how a
closed axis keeps leaking. Card, IBAN and SSN on this axis are **recorded and not fixed**: unlike
RFC 3966 those renderings are not published by their issuers, so admitting them would be coverage
rather than a leak.

<a id="m11-r49"></a>
### M11-R49 — `SEPARATOR-01` varies only `GAP_CHARS`, so half the separator axis is unguarded: hyphen support can be deleted from a shipped recognizer with the suite green — *guard, medium-high*

**The mechanism.** Both halves of the guard take the whitespace alphabet:

- its **scope** filter is `pattern_can_match_any_of(pattern, GAP_CHARS)`
  (`src/pii/recognizers.rs:4899`), so a recognizer is in scope for carrying a *space*;
- its **matrix** is `answer.positive.replace(' ', &separator.to_string())` over `GAP_CHARS`
  (`src/pii/recognizers.rs:4943`), so the only substitutions ever made are whitespace-for-space.

No arm of it can express *"and this value is also published with a hyphen"*. A recognizer missing `-`
and `.` is fully "answered".

**Repro, and it is the round's sharpest result.** Remove `-` from the shipped `Groups` phone shape —
consistently at all three sites (`PHONE_SHAPE_TEMPLATES` plus its `SEPARATOR_ANSWERS` and
`RENDERING_ANSWERS` rows) so that no pattern-string bookkeeping assertion fires and **only behaviour
changes**:

```
r"(?-u:\b)[1-9]\d{1,2}(?:(?:-|{GAP})\d{2,4}){1,3}(?-u:\b)"
                          ^^^  removed
```

**The full suite stays green at 255 / 0 / 5.** Nothing in the repository asserts that a hyphen works
as a separator, anywhere, for any kind.

**The one-site version of the same mutation is a red in disguise**, and it is worth recording because
it is what makes the conclusion falsifiable. Changing the template alone turns two tests red — but
both failures are the registries' *pattern-string* correspondence checks (*"the answer names a
pattern this build does not ship"*), which fire for any edit to any pattern and say nothing about
separators. A reviewer who stopped at that red would have concluded the axis was guarded.

**This is the third instance of one shape**, which is the reason to fix the shape rather than the
instance: [M11-R31](#m11-r31) — `SEPARATOR-01` taking its *scope* from the pattern, exempting 16 of
24 recognizers; [M11-R40](#m11-r40) — `UTF8-01` taking its *corpus* from `CASE_ANSWERS`, exempting
the digit-only half; and now `SEPARATOR-01` taking its *alphabet* from `GAP_CHARS`. Each time the
guard borrowed a set from whichever registry was nearest instead of deriving it from the question it
asks.

**Second half of the same finding: the `+CC` row asserts a completeness it does not have.** Its
`not_detected` list is `&[]` (`src/pii/recognizers.rs:4430`) while the RFC 3966 rendering is published
and undetected — precisely what [M11-R34](#m11-r34) built the prose-to-list audit to prevent, on the
row written one round after it. And its `why` says *"Its spaced renderings belong to the `+CC`
groupings row above"* while its own `detected` list now carries four spaced renderings: the sentence
was true of the round-11 arm and was not updated when round 12 moved them in.

**The fix.** Derive the matrix's alphabet from what each answer's recognizer **validator** accepts,
the way `GAP_CHARS` itself was derived from `iban_mod97`, and require an answer to *state* its
separator set rather than inheriting one. Non-vacuity should assert that the alphabet actually varied
contains a non-whitespace member, or the widened guard can silently narrow again.

**The test that would have caught it** is mutation E run as a guard: for every `SeparatorAnswer`, the
recognizer must **fail** to detect its positive when a separator its validator accepts is removed
from the pattern — the differential form [M10-R13](M10.md#m10-r13) established for cheap filters,
applied to separators.

**Closed 2026-09-05, in round 13's commit (`2a95ecf`)** — each `SeparatorAnswer` names its own
alphabet and the matrix substitutes over it.

<a id="m11-r50"></a>
### M11-R50 — both READMEs and now `ARCHITECTURE.md` claim *"every `+CC` rendering — any grouping"*; third consecutive round the `+CC` coverage cell overstates — *precision / docs, medium*

**Three places, all written in the round-12 commit, all measurably false:**

- `README.md:64` — *"**9 domestic plans** and every `+CC` rendering — compact E.164 and any grouping —
  checked against the country's real numbering plan"*, and its coverage cell, *"`+CC` in any
  rendering, US always"*.
- `README.it.md:67` — the mirror: *"ogni resa `+CC` — E.164 compatta e qualunque raggruppamento"*,
  coverage cell *"`+CC` in ogni resa"*.
- `ARCHITECTURE.md:621` — *"It now has one, over every rendering: compact E.164 and any grouping."*

Measured against them: `Mode::Rfc3966` **0.385**, dot-rendered international **0.846**
([M11-R48](#m11-r48)). *"Any grouping"* is true only of groupings separated by whitespace.

**This is the third round on the same cell**, and the direction is what makes it a row rather than a
typo fix. [M11-R42](#m11-r42) found it crediting the `+CC` and US arms with a numbering-plan check
they did not run; [M11-R45](#m11-r45) found the rewrite calling the spaced arm *"over-mask, never a
miss"* one round after that form was measured as a **miss**; this round finds the rewrite of *that*
claiming a rendering space the code does not cover. Each correction reached for a stronger word than
the measurement supported.

**The fix.** Say what is checked and name the residue, as the domestic tier's own table already does:
`+CC` in its **compact and whitespace-grouped** renderings, validated against the real numbering
plan; hyphen-, dot- and slash-separated international renderings **not detected**, with the measured
rates. The rule this milestone has now written twice applies here too — *if something is measured
elsewhere, prose links it rather than restating it* — so the number belongs in `TESTING.md` next to
the guard, with the READMEs pointing at it.

**The test that would have caught it** is [M11-R49](#m11-r49)'s: a coverage sentence that names a
rendering class should be derivable from `RENDERING_ANSWERS`, and the row for that recognizer should
be unable to leave `not_detected` empty while a published rendering is missing.

**Closed 2026-09-05 — the alphabet is now per recognizer, derived from that recognizer's validator.**

`PHONE_SEPARATORS` (`-` `.` `/`) joins `GAP_CHARS` for the `+CC` arm through a second placeholder,
`{PGAP}`, and the shrink's cut rule follows the union — so the pattern, the shrink and the validator
share one definition per recognizer instead of one definition for everybody.

**The finding's own sentence is the durable one and it is promoted:** *an axis has an alphabet, and
deciding the axis is not deciding the alphabet.* M11-R25 closed the separator axis and derived its
class from `iban_mod97`, which ignores **whitespace** — so `{GAP}` became whitespace for every
recognizer that used it, including a phone arm whose validator also discards punctuation. The
shortest proof needed no corpus: `06.12.34.56.78` was masked and `+33.6.12.34.56.78`, the same
number, was not.

**Not a regression** — it predates M11 — but round 12's closure *claimed* every `+CC` rendering, and
that claim was what made it a finding rather than a gap. Third time this milestone that a fix's
**claim** outran its reach (M11-R41 -> R43 -> R48), and each time on the same family.

**The card, IBAN and SSN results on this axis are recorded, not fixed**, per the finding: unlike RFC
3966 those renderings are not published by their issuers, so `4111.1111.1111.1111` and
`DE89-3704-0044-0532-0130-00` stay outside. That is a coverage decision, and it is named in the
record rather than absorbed silently into this fix.

**Verified:** 255/0/5 over 24 binaries, twice, identical; `cargo test-onnx` 291/0/22; `fmt` and
`clippy --all-targets -D warnings` clean.

**Closed 2026-09-05 — `SeparatorAnswer` carries its recognizer's alphabet, and the matrix uses it.**

The guard took **both** its scope and its matrix from `GAP_CHARS`, so every substitution it could
make was whitespace-for-space: deleting hyphen support from a shipped phone shape at all three sites
left the suite **green at 255/0/5**. Each row now names one of `SPACE_ONLY`, `WITH_HYPHEN`,
`WITH_HYPHEN_DOT` or `PHONE_ALPHABET` — matching what that recognizer's own validator accepts — and
the matrix substitutes over it. Round 13's mutation E is now red, naming `U+002D`.

**One thing I tried and reverted, with the residue stated on the guard.** Widening the *scope* filter
to the union pulls in `Secret`, `Email` and the SSN, whose `-` and `.` are part of the **token**
rather than separators between groups, so it would demand a separator answer from recognizers that
have no separator axis. Scope stays whitespace. The residue: a recognizer whose groups were separated
**only** by punctuation would fall outside this guard — none exists (every gap-bearing pattern also
accepts whitespace), and `RENDER-01` would still ask it for its renderings.

Third instance of one shape after M11-R31 and M11-R40, and the three now read as one rule: **a guard's
scope and a guard's alphabet are separate decisions, and each must come from the thing it is about.**

**Closed 2026-09-05.** Both READMEs and `ARCHITECTURE.md` said *"every `+CC` rendering — any
grouping"*, written in the round-12 commit and false in it. They now say *any grouping separated by a
space, `-`, `.` or `/`* — the alphabet, not a universal.

**Third consecutive round that this cell overstates**, and the pattern in the three is identical: the
sentence describes the *intent* of the fix being written rather than its reach. R45 corrected
"over-mask, never a miss", R47 corrected "no validator whatsoever", this corrects "any grouping". The
mitigation that generalises is the one M11-R43 arrived at from the other side — **when a finding
measures N renderings, the closure reports N numbers** — because a claim written from a number cannot
outrun it.

<a id="round-14"></a>
## Round 14 — the separator axis has a *cardinality*, and one character was never the alphabet (2026-09-05)

**Round 13's fix works, on the renderings it was measured against, and that is the first result.**
Re-measured at HEAD over the same generator — 13 templates × 1 000, 10 regions, every number
`is_valid` — `Mode::E164` **0 / 13 000**, `Mode::International` **0 / 13 000**, `Mode::National`
**0 / 13 000**, **`Mode::Rfc3966` 0 / 13 000**, the dot rendering **0 / 13 000**, the slash rendering
**0 / 13 000**. [M11-R48](#m11-r48) is genuinely closed on all six, against 0.385 and 0.846 one round
ago, and mutation A turns the suite red when the class is narrowed.

**And the axis is still open, on the coordinate no round has varied: how many characters a separator
is.** Every gap-bearing pattern in the repository spells its gap as **exactly one** character —
`{GAP}`, `{PGAP}`, `(?:-|{GAP})`, `(?:[.-]|{GAP})` — while every validator behind them normalises a
**run**. `phonenumber::parse` discards each ignorable character independently; `iban_mod97` filters
`char::is_whitespace()` over the whole string. So the pattern proposes a strictly narrower rendering
set than the validator accepts, which is the sentence `ARCHITECTURE.md` has now written three times,
arriving on the one coordinate the fixes for it never touched. **The sharpest form uses no new
character at all: `+39 347 1234567` is masked and `+39  347  1234567` — two ASCII spaces — is
forwarded in clear.** Measured, over the identical generator and predicate: international
renderings **0.923**, domestic renderings **1.000** ([M11-R51](#m11-r51)).

**The second coordinate is the two characters the alphabet's own doc comment names and the constant
omits.** `PHONE_SEPARATORS`' doc says *"whose own validator also discards `-` `.` `/` `(` `)`"*; the
constant is `['-', '.', '/']`. `+49 (0)30 12345678` — the standard German business rendering, and
`is_valid`-true — reaches the provider byte for byte, at **0.714** over the trunk-country half of the
generator; the parenthesised area code is **0.917** international and **0.923** domestic
([M11-R52](#m11-r52)). The repository already holds the answer: the universal US arm spells
`\(\d{3}\)`, so `+1 (415) 555-2671` is masked while `+49 (0)30 12345678` is not — four recognizers of
one kind, four hand-spelled alphabets, one of which knows about parentheses.

**Neither is a truncation.** Over all 65 000 renderings probed, **0** cases mask part of a value and
forward the rest: this is a miss, not M11-R30's or M11-R41's shape, and the fixpoint has nothing to
recover because nothing was consumed.

**The guards cannot express either coordinate, and that is one finding rather than three**
([M11-R53](#m11-r53)). `SEPARATOR-01`'s matrix is `answer.positive.replace(' ', &separator.to_string())`
— a **1:1 character substitution**, so a rendering that is one character longer than its positive is
outside it by construction; make the substitution a run and it goes red on the *first* row it tries
(mutation C). `RENDER-01`'s prose-to-list audit filters through `looks_like_a_rendering`, whose
alphabet is `[A-Za-z0-9 +-]` — so `.`, `/`, `(` and `)` cannot bind: quoting `+49 (0)30 12345678` in
the `+CC` row's `why` leaves the full suite **green at 255 / 0 / 5** (mutation D) while
`+44 7911 123456`, in the same position, turns it red (mutation E). And round 13's own product fix is
pinned by nothing behavioural: removing `/` from `PHONE_SEPARATORS` **and** from `PHONE_ALPHABET` — the
two places a narrowing commit would touch — is **green at 255 / 0 / 5** (mutation B).

**Fifth consecutive round in which a closure's claim outruns its reach**, and this time the stale text
includes the guard's own catalogue entry ([M11-R54](#m11-r54)). `TESTING.md` still describes
`SEPARATOR-01`'s matrix as *"`positive.replace(' ', separator)` over `GAP_CHARS`"* — it has been
`answer.alphabet` since `2a95ecf` — and publishes round 13's mutation E as *"the whole suite stays
green at 255 / 0 / 5"*, which is now **red** (mutation F, naming U+002D). `ARCHITECTURE.md:646` still
says *"over every rendering: compact E.164 and any grouping"*, which [M11-R50](#m11-r50)'s closure
states it rewrote. Both READMEs' Phone **coverage** cell still says *"`+CC` in any rendering"* — the
fourth round on that one cell (R42 → R45 → R50 → here).

**No fail-open, no new over-mask class, no raw value in a log.** The proxy's own `info` output was
searched for every value driven through it and carries none; every control in the end-to-end run was
masked, restored byte-exactly, and the response body was well-formed on every request.

### What was verified, with the expected value written before the observed one

| Quantity | Expected (written first) | Observed | Reps |

| `cargo test` (default) | 255 / 0 / 5 over 24 binaries, 0 warnings — round 13's figure | **255 / 0 / 5, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 2, identical |
| `cargo test --features onnx` | 291 / 0 / 22 — round 13's figure | **291 / 0 / 22, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 1 |
| `cargo fmt --check` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| [M11-R48](#m11-r48) at HEAD: `E164` · `International` · `National` · **`Rfc3966`** · `.` · `/` | **0** on all six if round 13's fix is real | **0 / 13 000 each** | 1 (`--release`) |
| `International` with `' - '` for `' '` | **0** if *"any grouping separated by a space, `-`, `.` or `/`"* is true | **12 000 / 13 000 = 0.923** | 1 |
| `International` with `' / '`, `' . '`, and with **two spaces** | **0**, same claim | **12 000 / 13 000 = 0.923** each | 1 |
| `National` with `' - '`, `' / '`, two spaces | **0** — the domestic tier is the measured one | **13 000 / 13 000 = 1.000** each | 1 |
| `International` + `(0)` trunk (`+49 (0)30 12345678`) | **0** if the alphabet is its validator's | **5 000 / 7 000 = 0.714** | 1 |
| `International` + `(area)` (`+44 (20) 7946 0958`) | **0** | **11 000 / 12 000 = 0.917** | 1 |
| `National` + `(area)` (`(020) 7946 0958`) | **0** | **12 000 / 13 000 = 0.923** | 1 |
| truncation across all six paren/run renderings | unknown — M11-R30/R41 were truncations | **0 / 65 000** — a miss, never a partial mask | 1 |
| all ten `SEPARATOR_ANSWERS` positives, two-space rendering | masked, if the recorded alphabet is the axis | **10 of 10 IN CLEAR** | 1 |
| the same value domestically vs `+CC`, same separator | both or neither | **`+1 (415) 555-2671` masked, `+49 (0)30 12345678` in clear** | 1 |
| the leak through the real `.exe`, 9 renderings + 5 controls | controls masked, the rest forwarded | **exactly that** — tables in [M11-R51](#m11-r51) / [M11-R52](#m11-r52) | 1 |

| raw PII in the proxy's `info` log | none | **none** — searched for every value sent | 1 |

### Mutations run in round 14

`src/pii/recognizers.rs` only. Byte substitution through a harness that refuses to write unless the
anchor matches **exactly** the expected number of times *and* the resulting sha256 differs from the
original — an empty substitution is a green in disguise. After every run the file is restored from the
`HEAD` blob and its sha256 re-compared (`33ec59fce337e99cfed5608df7164f718fb0370737be7230f58b941cf382f391`),
with `git status --porcelain` asserted empty; verified empty again at the end of the round. All builds
from PowerShell (from the Bash tool the link step dies on Git-for-Windows' `link` shadowing MSVC's —
the red in disguise rounds 11–13 also recorded).

| # | mutation | expected | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | `PHONE_SEPARATORS` loses `'/'` (one site) | red, if the recorded alphabet is cross-checked against the pattern | **red, 1 test** — `every_gap_bearing_recognizer_answers_the_separator_axis`, naming **U+002F**; 157 / 1 |
| B | `'/'` removed from `PHONE_SEPARATORS` **and** `PHONE_ALPHABET` — the two sites a narrowing commit touches | red, if anything **behavioural** pins round 13's own fix | **GREEN — 255 / 0 / 5**, full suite, 0 warnings. [M11-R53](#m11-r53) |
| C | `SEPARATOR-01`'s matrix substitutes `" {separator} "` instead of `"{separator}"` | red, if the run-length leak is real | **red on the *first* row** — IBAN, U+0009; 157 / 1. This is the guard [M11-R51](#m11-r51) asks for, and it fails before it reaches a phone |
| D | `+49 (0)30 12345678` quoted in the `+CC` row's `why` | red, if `RENDER-01`'s prose-to-list audit can bind a parenthesised rendering | **GREEN — 255 / 0 / 5**, full suite. [M11-R53](#m11-r53) |
| E | control for D: `+44 7911 123456` quoted in the identical position | red — the audit itself must work | **red, 1 test** — `every_recognizer_records_the_renderings_it_detects`; 157 / 1. So D's green is the **filter's alphabet**, not a dead audit |
| F | round 13's mutation E repeated at HEAD: `-` removed from the `Groups` shape at all three sites | red, if [M11-R49](#m11-r49)'s closure holds | **red, 1 test**, naming **U+002D**; 157 / 1 — the fix holds, and `TESTING.md`'s published *"green at 255 / 0 / 5"* is now false. [M11-R54](#m11-r54) |

### What this round could not break

- **Round 13's fix, on every rendering it and rounds 11–12 measured.** Six format modes × 13 000
  numbers: 0 leaks in all six, against R48's 0.385 / 0.846. Mutation A is red, so the `+CC` arm's
  single-character alphabet is genuinely watched.
- **`RENDER-01`'s audit, for the tokens its filter accepts.** Mutation E is red, naming the token.
- **`SEPARATOR-01`'s per-row alphabet, for one-character substitutions.** Mutations A and F are both
  red and both name the code point, which is [M11-R49](#m11-r49)'s fix doing exactly what it claims.
- **The truncation class.** 0 of 65 000 — every one of these renderings is forwarded whole, so the
  M10-R1 predicate (*no byte of a real value survives*) is violated by a **miss** and not by a mask
  that ate part of the value.
- **The legitimate paths.** `cargo test`, `cargo test --features onnx`, `cargo fmt --check` and
  `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` are clean at HEAD, run the way a person
  runs them; the real `.exe` boots, masks, augments and restores byte-exactly on every space-rendered
  control, including `+1 (415) 555-2671`, `4111 1111 1111 1111`, `DE89 3704 0044 0532 0130 00` and
  `030 12345678`.
- **The log discipline.** No raw value in the proxy's own output at `RUST_LOG=info`; `Config`'s Debug
  still redacts the upstream key.

### Observations that are not findings

- **The `+CC` row's `why` still says *"Its spaced renderings belong to the `+CC` groupings row
  above"*** while its own `detected` list carries four spaced renderings. [M11-R49](#m11-r49) named
  this in its finding text and its closure did not reach it; folded into [M11-R54](#m11-r54) rather
  than filed, because it is the same sentence in the same row.
- **The guard-vs-product tally does not reconcile again.** Round 13 published *"32 on the net or the
  docs, 19 in the product, 51 rows"*; computed from the ledger's own severity column the split is
  **30 / 21** (guard 15 + docs 14 + build 1 · leak 8 + precision 5 + hardening 4 + fidelity 1), or
  **33 / 18** if `precision/docs` (3 rows) is counted as docs. Neither is 32 / 19. This is
  [M11-R24](#m11-r24) recurring, and the durable answer is not a recount: **a two-number split of a
  table that already has a severity column should be derived from it or dropped**, because a
  hand-carried tally goes stale on the round that adds a row. Recorded rather than filed — it is prose
  in a review record, not a claim the product makes.

### Guard-vs-product tally

Round 14: **2 in the product** ([R51](#m11-r51) and [R52](#m11-r52), both leaks), **1 on the net**
([R53](#m11-r53)), **1 on the docs** ([R54](#m11-r54)).

The ratio held at the product for a second round, and for the same reason round 13 named: the question
that found these was not *"is the fix correct?"* — it is, on all six modes — but *"what did the
measurement hold constant?"* Rounds 11–13 varied **which character** sits between the groups and never
**how many**, because `replace(' ', c)` is the shape the guard was handed. M4's sixth lesson, stated on
the guards themselves: *a quantity a test never varies is a quantity the test cannot see.*

<a id="m11-r51"></a>
### M11-R51 — a phone number whose groups are separated by **more than one** character is forwarded in clear: `+39  347  1234567` (two ASCII spaces) at 0.923, every domestic rendering at 1.000 — *leak, high*

**The mechanism, in one line:** every gap-bearing pattern spells its separator as exactly **one**
character while every validator behind it normalises a **run**, so the pattern proposes a strictly
narrower rendering set than the validator accepts — `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s own rule, on the coordinate
its three fixes never varied.

The ten patterns, all of them one-character (`src/pii/recognizers.rs`):

| recognizer | gap as written |
|---|---|
| IBAN grouped arm (`:359`) | `{GAP}` |
| Credit card (`:392`) | `(?:-\|{GAP})` |
| Phone, universal US + `+CC` (`:479`) | `(?:[.-]\|{GAP})`, `{GAP}` |
| Phone, `+CC` groupings (`:442`) | `{PGAP}` |
| GB NINO (`:523`) | `{GAP}` |
| Phone `Trunk` · `Groups` · `LongBlock` (`:905`, `:933`, `:939`) | `(?:-\|{GAP})` |
| Phone `TrunkPairs` (`:911`) | `(?:[.-]\|{GAP})` |

**Repro — no character the code does not already accept, only more of them.** Through the library, at
the shipped default:

```
+39 347 1234567       MASKED     -> contatto [PHONE_1] fine
+39  347  1234567     IN CLEAR   -> contatto +39  347  1234567 fine
+39 347 - 1234567     IN CLEAR   -> contatto +39 347 - 1234567 fine
030 12345678          MASKED     -> tel [PHONE_1] end
030 / 12345678        IN CLEAR   -> tel 030 / 12345678 end
020 7946 0958         MASKED     -> tel [PHONE_1] end
020 - 7946 0958       IN CLEAR   -> tel 020 - 7946 0958 end
```

Every one of the in-clear rows is `is_valid`-true for its region. `030 / 12345678` is not exotic: the
slash between area code and subscriber number is how a German business writes a telephone number, and
`-` surrounded by spaces is the ordinary international rendering in the same countries.

**Measured**, on rounds 11–13's generator and predicate (13 templates × 1 000 across 10 regions, every
number confirmed by `phonenumber::is_valid`, every rendering re-parsed and required to resolve to **the
same number** before it counts; leak = any 6-digit window of the value surviving in the output):

| rendering | leaks | rate |
|---|---|---|
| `Mode::International` (spaced) — control | 0 / 13 000 | 0.000 |
| `Mode::International`, `' - '` for `' '` | **12 000 / 13 000** | **0.923** |
| `Mode::International`, `' / '` for `' '` | **12 000 / 13 000** | **0.923** |
| `Mode::International`, `' . '` for `' '` | **12 000 / 13 000** | **0.923** |
| `Mode::International`, **two ASCII spaces** | **12 000 / 13 000** | **0.923** |
| `Mode::National` — control | 0 / 13 000 | 0.000 |
| `Mode::National`, `' - '` / `' / '` / two spaces | **13 000 / 13 000** | **1.000** |

The 1 000 international rows that do *not* leak are the US template: its `Mode::International` carries
one space (`+1 415-555-2671`), so widening that one gap leaves `415-555-2671` intact for the US 3-3-4
arm to take. Nothing in the `+CC` tier contributes.

**It is a miss, not a truncation** — measured, **0 of 65 000** renderings across all six probed
transforms mask part of a value and forward the rest. Worth stating because M11-R30 and M11-R41 were
both truncations and this one is not: the whole value goes upstream, and no byte was eaten.

**Through the real `.exe`** (release build, mock upstream recording what left the box, controls in the
same field):

```
sent      : Chiamami al +39 347 - 1234567 grazie, mail mario.rossi@example.com
upstream  : Chiamami al +39 347 - 1234567 grazie, mail [EMAIL_1]
sent      : Chiamami al +39  347  1234567 grazie, mail mario.rossi@example.com
upstream  : Chiamami al +39  347  1234567 grazie, mail [EMAIL_1]
sent      : Rufen Sie an unter 030 / 12345678 oder mail mario.rossi@example.com
upstream  : Rufen Sie an unter 030 / 12345678 oder mail [EMAIL_1]
```
