```
sent      : Ring 020 - 7946 0958 or mail mario.rossi@example.com
upstream  : Ring 020 - 7946 0958 or mail [EMAIL_1]

control   : Chiamami al +39 347 1234567 …      -> Chiamami al [PHONE_1] …
control   : Rufen Sie an unter 030 12345678 …  -> Rufen Sie an unter [PHONE_1] …
```

The email in every leaking line is masked, so the tier was reached and working — this is the grammar,
not the wiring.

**The whole axis, not the phone tier.** Re-rendering each of the ten `SEPARATOR_ANSWERS` positives with
a **two-character** run built only from characters that row's own `alphabet` already contains:

| row | control | two-space run | `' - '` run |
|---|---|---|---|
| IBAN | masked | **IN CLEAR** | — |
| CreditCard | masked | **IN CLEAR** | **IN CLEAR** |
| Phone US 3-3-4 | masked | **IN CLEAR** | **IN CLEAR** |
| Phone `+CC` (both rows) | masked | **IN CLEAR** | **IN CLEAR** |
| GB NINO | masked | **IN CLEAR** | — |
| Phone `Trunk` · `TrunkPairs` · `Groups` · `LongBlock` | masked | **IN CLEAR** | **IN CLEAR** |

Ten of ten. **Their published-rendering status differs and the fix should say so**: no issuer prints an
IBAN, a card or a NINO with a doubled separator, so for those the run length is a coverage question the
maintainer may decide to leave open — exactly the distinction [M11-R48](#m11-r48) drew for the dotted
card. For the phone tier it is not a coverage question: `030 / 12345678` and `+49 30 - 12345678` are how
the numbers are published.

**Not a regression.** Every pattern has spelled a one-character gap since the tier was written; this is
byte-identical at every tag through `v1.2.1`. What round 13 changed is the *claim* — see
[M11-R54](#m11-r54).

**The fix — the chokepoint, not an eleventh hand-spelled grammar.** [M11-R49](#m11-r49) made the
separator *alphabet* a per-recognizer declaration derived from that recognizer's validator; the same
declaration should carry its **cardinality**, because a validator that discards each ignorable
character independently accepts a run. Mechanically that is one edit per recognizer — `{PGAP}` →
`{PGAP}{1,3}`, `(?:-|{GAP})` → `(?:-|{GAP}){1,3}` — and it comes with two obligations this milestone
has already paid for:

1. **The shrink obligation** ([M11-R13](#m11-r13) / [M11-R33](#m11-r33)). A repetition widens the greedy
   reach, so any recognizer with a rejecting validator and an optional or repeated trailing group must
   keep `shrink_on_reject: true`, and `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` must cut on a *run* rather than on a
   single separator.
2. **A bound, because a run is unbounded by default.** `{1,3}` rather than `+` keeps every pattern's
   longest match bounded, which is what [M4-R19](M4.md#m4-r19)'s O(n · L) argument rests on and what the
   `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST` accounting in `ARCHITECTURE.md` assumes; an unbounded run also
   multiplies the candidate count on digit-dense bodies, which is [M11-R18](#m11-r18)'s open question
   and must not be made worse silently. **Measure the added candidate count and the added corpus
   matches before shipping it** — the way `GAP_CHARS` was measured at 0 added matches over 341.7 MB.

**The test that would have caught it** is mutation C run as the guard: `SEPARATOR-01`'s matrix must
render each positive with a **run** of the row's alphabet, not one member of it — `positive.replace(' ',
&format!("{a}{b}"))` over pairs, plus at least one asymmetric run (`' - '`). Applied to the current
build it goes red on the **first** row it evaluates. Its companion is the same rule the alphabet needed:
*a guard that varies an axis must vary the axis's **cardinality** as well as its **alphabet**, and both
must come from what the validator accepts.*

**Closed 2026-09-05 (`2d52e83`).** Every placeholder expands to `[…]{1,4}` — bounded, and not out of
timidity: `Scan::Overlapping` is linear only while a match length is (M4-R19), and an unbounded `+`
would take the rescan to O(n²), which is the exact algorithmic-complexity DoS that finding is about.
Four covers every rendering the round measured; what the bound **admits** is column alignment, and
that is now published as an accepted cost with its rate rather than described as the residue
([M11-R55](#m11-r55)). The genuine residue is a run of five or more.

<a id="m11-r52"></a>
### M11-R52 — a parenthesised phone rendering is forwarded in clear: `+49 (0)30 12345678` at 0.714, `(020) 7946 0958` at 0.923; `(` and `)` are named in `PHONE_SEPARATORS`' own doc and absent from the constant — *leak, high*

**The mechanism, in one line:** `src/pii/recognizers.rs:104` —

```rust
const PHONE_SEPARATORS: &[char] = &[
    '-', '.', '/', // what `phonenumber::parse` strips beside whitespace
];
```

— while its own doc comment eleven lines above says *"the `+CC` phone arm whose own validator also
discards `-` `.` `/` `(` `)`"*. Three of the five shipped. The sentence naming the alphabet and the
constant declaring it sit in the same doc block and disagree, which is [M11-R48](#m11-r48)'s finding
surviving inside its own fix.

**And parentheses are not just two more members** — they *bracket* a group rather than sitting between
two, so `(0)` puts **two** separator characters on one side of a digit run. Adding them to the class
would not be enough on its own; the run-length coordinate of [M11-R51](#m11-r51) is a precondition,
which is why the two findings share one fix.

**Repro — the same value, one masked and one not:**

```
+1 (415) 555-2671     MASKED     -> tel [PHONE_1] end          (the US arm spells \(\d{3}\))
+49 (0)30 12345678    IN CLEAR   -> tel +49 (0)30 12345678 end
+44 (20) 7946 0958    IN CLEAR   -> tel +44 (20) 7946 0958 end
(020) 7946 0958       IN CLEAR   -> tel (020) 7946 0958 end
(0221) 1234567        IN CLEAR   -> tel (0221) 1234567 end
+44 20 7946 0958      MASKED     -> control
020 7946 0958         MASKED     -> control
```

**The repository already holds the answer.** The universal US arm (`src/pii/recognizers.rs:479`) spells
`\(\d{3}\)` because libphonenumber's own `Mode::National` for the US **is** `(415) 555-2671` — so
parentheses are a rendering this codebase has known about since M1, in one of the four recognizers of
the kind `Phone`. The other three do not know. Four recognizers of one kind, four hand-spelled
alphabets: [M11-R48](#m11-r48)'s sentence, one round later, with parentheses in the place `.` and `/`
had.

**Measured**, same generator and gate as [M11-R51](#m11-r51) (every rendering re-parsed and required to
resolve to the same number before it counts):

| rendering | leaks | rate |
|---|---|---|
| `Mode::International` — control | 0 / 13 000 | 0.000 |
| `+CC (0)<national>` — `+49 (0)30 12345678` | **5 000 / 7 000** | **0.714** |
| `+CC (<area>) <rest>` — `+44 (20) 7946 0958` | **11 000 / 12 000** | **0.917** |
| `(<area>) <rest>` domestic — `(020) 7946 0958` | **12 000 / 13 000** | **0.923** |

The `(0)` row's denominator is 7 000 because only the trunk-prefix templates have a `(0)` form at all.
`+44 (0)7911 123456` is one of the leaking rows; `+44 (0)20 7946 0958` is not — its national part is
rescued by the domestic `Groups` family, the same accidental rescue that keeps the US column out of
R51's numerator.

**Through the real `.exe`:**

```
sent      : Tel. +49 (0)30 12345678 oder mail mario.rossi@example.com
upstream  : Tel. +49 (0)30 12345678 oder mail [EMAIL_1]
sent      : Call (020) 7946 0958 or mail mario.rossi@example.com
upstream  : Call (020) 7946 0958 or mail [EMAIL_1]
sent      : Bel +31 (0)6 12345678 of mail mario.rossi@example.com
upstream  : Bel +31 (0)6 12345678 of mail [EMAIL_1]
control   : Call +1 (415) 555-2671 or mail …   -> Call [PHONE_1] or mail [EMAIL_1]
```

**Is it published?** Yes, and that is the half which makes it a leak rather than a coverage note.
`+49 (0)30 …` and `+44 (0)20 …` are what German, Dutch and British organisations print on a letterhead
or in a website footer — the parenthesised trunk digit exists precisely to tell a domestic caller to
dial it and an international caller to drop it. `(020) 7946 0958` is the same convention domestically.
And `libphonenumber`, the validator this tier is built on, prints `(415) 555-2671` from its own
`Mode::National`.

**Not a regression** — no `+CC` or domestic pattern has ever accepted a parenthesis. What round 13
changed is the **claim** that the alphabet is now the validator's ([M11-R54](#m11-r54)).

**The fix.** Add `(` and `)` to `PHONE_SEPARATORS` — the constant's own doc already names them — and
take [M11-R51](#m11-r51)'s cardinality with it, so `(0)` and `(020)` can be matched at all. Two costs to
measure before shipping, both on the precision side rather than the safety side, and both in the form
this project already uses:

1. **Added corpus matches.** A parenthesis is far commoner in source and prose than a `/`, so re-run the
   third-party-source measurement `GAP_CHARS` and the case fold were both gated on (341.7 MB /
   304.9 MB) and publish the delta. A candidate still has to satisfy `phonenumber::is_valid`, so the
   expected delta is small — but *expected* is not *measured*, which is M10-R13's rule.
2. **Over-reach into surrounding punctuation.** `(see 020 7946 0958)` must not become a candidate that
   swallows the closing parenthesis and is then **dropped** by a rejecting validator — that is
   [M11-R13](#m11-r13) / [M11-R33](#m11-r33) exactly, and the answer is the same `shrink_on_reject`.

**The test that would have caught it** is [M11-R53](#m11-r53)'s: `RENDERING_ANSWERS`' `+CC` row carries
`not_detected: &[]`, asserting that no other rendering of this value is published — and
`looks_like_a_rendering` cannot accept a token containing `(`, so the row **cannot be made to say
otherwise**. A registry structurally unable to record a limit will always report that there is none.

**Closed 2026-09-05 (`2d52e83`).** All four phone recognizers take `{PGAP}`, and there are three
separator classes, each named for the validator it is derived from: `{GAP}` for `iban_mod97`,
`{CGAP}` for `luhn_valid`, `{PGAP}` for `phonenumber::parse`. The card gained `{CGAP}` because the
widened matrix demanded it. *The repository held the answer — the characters stood in
`PHONE_SEPARATORS`' own doc comment — and three of the four recognizers did not have it: a constant
and its documentation are two places, and only one of them is compiled.*

<a id="m11-r53"></a>
### M11-R53 — the separator guards can express neither coordinate: the matrix is a 1:1 character substitution, the rendering filter's alphabet excludes `.` `/` `(` `)`, and round 13's own product fix is pinned by nothing behavioural — *guard, high*

Three mutations, one shape: **a guard's alphabet, cardinality and subject each came from whatever set
was nearest rather than from the question being asked.** This is [M11-R31](#m11-r31) (scope from the
pattern), [M11-R40](#m11-r40) (`UTF8-01`'s corpus from `CASE_ANSWERS`) and [M11-R49](#m11-r49)
(`SEPARATOR-01`'s alphabet from `GAP_CHARS`) a **fourth** time, which is the reason to fix the shape and
not the instance.

**(a) The matrix cannot render a separator longer than one character.**
`src/pii/recognizers.rs:5033` —

```rust
let rendered = answer.positive.replace(' ', &separator.to_string());
```

`separator` is a `char`, so every string this guard will ever evaluate has **exactly the same length**
as the positive it came from. A rendering one character longer is outside it by construction, and that
is where [M11-R51](#m11-r51) lives. **Mutation C** replaces the substitution with
`&format!(" {separator} ")` and the guard goes red on the **first row it evaluates** — IBAN, U+0009 —
before reaching a phone at all. That is the guard R51 asks for, already written, and it takes one line.

**(b) The rendering filter's alphabet excludes exactly the characters this round measured.**
`looks_like_a_rendering` (`src/pii/recognizers.rs:4632`) admits only
`c.is_ascii_alphanumeric() || c == ' ' || c == '-' || c == '+'`. So `.`, `/`, `(` and `)` cannot pass —
and the `why`-prose audit [M11-R34](#m11-r34) built *skips silently* what the filter rejects:

| mutation | token quoted in the `+CC` row's `why` | result |
|---|---|---|
| **D** | `+49 (0)30 12345678` | **GREEN, full suite 255 / 0 / 5** — bound by nothing |
| **E** | `+44 7911 123456` | **red**, `every_recognizer_records_the_renderings_it_detects`, naming the token |

Same field, same position, one character's difference. D's green is the filter, not a dead audit — E
proves it. The consequence is that the two renderings this round measured, and the `.`/`/` renderings
round 13 *shipped*, **cannot be written into a `RENDERING_ANSWERS` row's prose at all**; and the `+CC`
row's `not_detected: &[]` (`src/pii/recognizers.rs:4470`) therefore asserts a completeness the registry
has no way to contradict. This is verbatim [M11-R44](#m11-r44) — where the filter rejected `+`, so no
`+CC` rendering could bind on the family that had just leaked twice — with the fix having added the one
character that finding needed and no more.

**(c) Round 13's product fix has no behavioural guard.** **Mutation B** removes `'/'` from
`PHONE_SEPARATORS` *and* from `PHONE_ALPHABET` — the two sites one commit would touch to narrow the
shipped class — and the full suite is **green at 255 / 0 / 5, 0 warnings**. Nothing in the repository
asserts that `+49 30/12345678` or `+39.347.1234567` is masked; the only thing standing behind
[M11-R48](#m11-r48)'s fix is that two hand-written lists agree with each other. Note the asymmetry that
makes this fixable in one line: `RENDERING_ANSWERS`' matrix **does** assert behaviour (`detected` must
be masked as one span), and the `+CC` row's `detected` list contains no `.`, `/` or hyphenated
rendering — because (b) makes them unwritable.

**The fix, in the order that closes the class:**

1. Widen `looks_like_a_rendering` to the union of every recorded separator alphabet, **derived rather
   than listed** — it is already the union the registries hold — keeping the `false` rows that stop it
   accepting `[PHONE_1]` and `\d{9}`. Its own matrix (`the_rendering_filter_accepts_what_it_must`) gains
   a `true` row per shipped alphabet member, so the filter can never again be narrower than the code it
   is asked about.
2. Make `SEPARATOR-01`'s matrix vary **cardinality**: for each row, render the positive with a single
   member, a doubled member and a mixed pair from its own alphabet.
3. Put round 13's shipped renderings into the `+CC` row's `detected` list, where behaviour is asserted,
   so mutation B turns red.

**The rule that outlives it, and it is the fourth restatement:** *a guard has three separate decisions —
its **scope**, its **alphabet** and its **cardinality** — and each must be derived from the question it
asks, never inherited from the registry that happens to be in scope.* A guard that inherits is not a
weaker guard; it is a guard that reports the shape of what it borrowed.

**Closed 2026-09-05, in round 14's commit (`2d52e83`)** — the matrix varies cardinality, the filter's
alphabet is derived from the production constant, and the `+CC` row carries punctuated renderings.

<a id="m11-r54"></a>
### M11-R54 — the round-13 closure did not reach the docs: `TESTING.md` publishes a mutation that is now red, `ARCHITECTURE.md` still says *"any grouping"*, and the README coverage cell is on its fourth round — *docs, medium*

**Four places, and the first matters most because it is a guard's own catalogue entry.**

1. **`docs/TESTING.md`, `SEPARATOR-01`** — *"its matrix is `positive.replace(' ', separator)` over
   `GAP_CHARS`, so every substitution it will ever make is whitespace-for-space"* and *"a recognizer
   missing `-` and `.` is fully "answered" by it"*. Both were true before `2a95ecf` and are false at
   HEAD: the matrix is `answer.alphabet`. The same entry then publishes round 13's mutation E —
   *"delete `-` from the shipped `Groups` phone shape … and the whole suite stays **green at
   255 / 0 / 5**"* — which **mutation F re-ran at HEAD and it is red**, naming U+002D. A catalogue entry
   that describes a closed defect as current, and publishes a green that is now a red, is worse than
   silence: the next person reading it concludes the axis is unguarded and re-derives a fix that already
   shipped.
2. **`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:646`** — *"It now has one, over every rendering: compact E.164 and any
   grouping."* [M11-R50](#m11-r50)'s closure note states that this line was rewritten to say *"any
   grouping separated by a space, `-`, `.` or `/`"*. It was not; only the READMEs were. The paragraph
   two below does correct it in narrative, which is why this is medium and not high — but the sentence a
   reader quotes is the unqualified one.
3. **`README.md:64` and `README.it.md:67`, the *coverage* cell** — still *"`+CC` in any rendering, US
   always"* / *"`+CC` in ogni resa"*. [M11-R42](#m11-r42) found this cell overstating,
   [M11-R45](#m11-r45) found it again after the rewrite, [M11-R50](#m11-r50) a third time; each closure
   rewrote the *fourth* column and left the fifth. **Fourth consecutive round on one table cell**, which
   is no longer a typo: it is a row whose two cells make the same claim and a fix habit that only ever
   edits one.
4. **Both READMEs' fourth column** — *"any grouping separated by a space, `-`, `.` or `/`"* — is now
   measurably false in its own terms, and that part is new rather than stale: `+39 347 - 1234567` is *a
   grouping separated by a hyphen* and leaks at 0.923 ([M11-R51](#m11-r51)), and `+49 (0)30 12345678` at
   0.714 ([M11-R52](#m11-r52)).

**Also in the same family, folded here rather than filed separately:** the `+CC` row's `why`
(`src/pii/recognizers.rs:4471`) still says *"Its spaced renderings belong to the `+CC` groupings row
above"* while its own `detected` list carries four spaced renderings. [M11-R49](#m11-r49)'s finding text
named this and its closure did not reach it.

**The fix.** Say what is checked and name the residue with its measured rate, the way the domestic
tier's own table in `ARCHITECTURE.md` already does — and put the numbers in `TESTING.md` next to the
guard, with the READMEs and `ARCHITECTURE.md` linking rather than restating. Both cells of the Phone row
have to move together, or the next rewrite will leave one behind for the fifth time.

**The rule this milestone has now paid for five times** (R26 → R32 → R47 → R50 → here): *a closure note
that lists the files it changed must be checked against those files before it is written.* The cheap
mechanical form is the one this repo already uses elsewhere — **derive the coverage sentence from
`RENDERING_ANSWERS`** so a claim about renderings cannot be authored in free prose at all, which is
[M11-R53](#m11-r53)'s fix arriving from the documentation side.

**Closed 2026-09-05 — a separator is a run, and the run is bounded.**

`with_gaps` now expands every placeholder to `[...]{1,4}` instead of `[...]`. The bound is not
timidity: `Scan::Overlapping` is linear only while a pattern's match length is (M4-R19), and an
unbounded `+` would make every one of these patterns unbounded and take the rescan to O(n²) — the
algorithmic-complexity DoS that finding is about. **Four** covers every rendering this round
measured (` / ` and ` - ` are three characters); the residue is a run of five or more, which is
column alignment rather than a rendering, and it is named beside `SEPARATOR-01`.

**And the cost is measured, because M11-R38 is open on exactly this.** Re-running `DOS-BUD`'s
verdict grid against round 10's figures:

| shape | units/row before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `3XX XXX XXXX` (valid) | 8 | **9.8** |
| `0NNNN NNNN` id column (rejected) | 12 | **13.8** |

**+22% per row**, so the allowance is reached ~18% sooner; wall-clock per row is unchanged. That is
a real widening of term 1 and it belongs in the maintainer's decision rather than in a footnote —
it is added to M11-R38.

`SEPARATOR-01`'s matrix now varies **both** coordinates, substituting runs of one, two and three; a
1:1 substitution could only ever ask about the alphabet.

**Closed 2026-09-05.** `(` and `)` joined `PHONE_SEPARATORS`, and **all four** phone recognizers now
take `{PGAP}` — the universal arms, `Trunk`, `TrunkPairs`, `Groups` and `LongBlock` — rather than
three of them spelling their own narrower class.

The finding's sharpest observation is the one worth keeping: `+1 (415) 555-2671` was already masked,
because the US arm spells `\(\d{3}\)` itself. **The repository held the answer and three of the four
recognizers did not have it** — which is what a per-recognizer class costs when the classes are
written by hand instead of derived. There are three now, and each names its validator: `{GAP}` for
`iban_mod97` and `nino_prefix_valid`, `{CGAP}` for `luhn_valid`, `{PGAP}` for `phonenumber::parse`.

The card gained `{CGAP}` on the way, and it was `SEPARATOR-01` that demanded it: with the matrix
varying cardinality, `4111--1111--1111--1111` failed against a card arm that spelled `(?:-|{GAP})` —
one hyphen or a whitespace run, never a hyphen run — while `luhn_valid` filters to digits and accepts
any of them.

**Closed 2026-09-05, in all three of its parts.**

1. **The matrix varies cardinality**, substituting runs of one, two and three from each row's own
   alphabet — round 14's mutation C, which was red on the first row, is now the guard.
2. **`looks_like_a_rendering`'s alphabet is derived**, from `PHONE_ALPHABET` rather than a hand-kept
   list. Hand-listing `.` `/` `(` `)` would have fixed those four; taking the set from the constant
   means the filter can never again be narrower than the code it is asked about — which is the whole
   defect, since a row whose `not_detected` is empty was asserting a completeness the registry had
   **no way to contradict**.
3. **The `+CC` row's `detected` list carries punctuated and doubled renderings** —
   `+39.347.1234567`, `+39/347/1234567`, `+39-347-1234567`, `+39  347  1234567` — so removing `/`
   from both constants, which was **green at 255/0/5**, is now red on the span assertion.

Part 3 is the one that generalises: *a decision recorded in two lists that only check each other is
bookkeeping; it becomes a guard the moment one of them is a behaviour.* Parts 1 and 2 are the third
and fourth instances of M11-R31's shape — a guard's **scope**, its **alphabet** and its
**cardinality** are three separate decisions, and each has to come from the thing it is about.

**Closed 2026-09-05.** `TESTING.md`'s published mutation is rewritten as closed, with the four
renderings that make it red; `ARCHITECTURE.md` states the axis rule in its finished form — *an axis
is not decided until its alphabet, its cardinality and its shape are* — and both README cells name
the alphabet and the run bound instead of a universal.

**Fifth consecutive round in which that README cell overstated, and the cause is the same each
time**: the sentence was written from the fix's intent rather than from its measurement. It now
enumerates what the code enumerates, which is the only version that cannot drift ahead of it.

## Round 15 — the fix for the separator axis is correct, and it admits an IP address (2026-09-05)

**Round 14's fix works, and it does not leak. That is the first result, and it is a real one.**
The truncation class rounds 8/9/12/13 kept re-opening is closed here: 3 150 renderings (9 values ×
7 separators × runs 1–5 × 10 neighbours) driven through `Vault::mask_all` at HEAD and at `ed5214b`
under M10-R1's predicate — *no six-character window of the value survives* — give **0 leaks that
`ed5214b` did not already have**, and **1 270 renderings that leaked before and are masked now**.
Every mutation of the widening that should be red is red, and the `{1,4}` bound is real: a run of
five is not matched.

**What the round did not measure is what it cost, and the cost is a class of ordinary agent text.**
`{PGAP}` gives the three *domestic* phone shape families `.` `/` `(` `)` for the first time, and
`SEPARATOR_RUN` gives every family a run of up to four. Neither had a precision measurement attached,
and the two shapes that fall out are the two commonest digit shapes in a coding agent's `tool_result`:
**a dotted-decimal IPv4 address** and **a column-aligned numeric table** ([M11-R55](#m11-r55)).
Measured against the pre-round-14 binary on identical inputs: random public IPv4 in a sentence
**0.000 → 0.517**, `10.x.x.x` **0.000 → 0.623**, `dd.mm.yyyy` and `dd/mm/yyyy` dates
**0.000 → 0.440**, a four-column numeric row aligned with 2–4 spaces **0.000 → 0.872**. The project's
own harness agrees: `phone_eval`'s union `dates` rate is **0.270** against the **0.180**
`ARCHITECTURE.md` publishes, and the slash rendering it reports as a hit is the one both that file and
the harness's own comment say *"cannot collide at all"*.

**Through the real `.exe`, on a payload a coding agent produces every day**, an ordinary tool call
reaches the model as `ssh deploy@[PHONE_1] -p 22 && grep [PHONE_2] /var/log/app.log` and a tool result
row as `rows [PHONE_3]`; the same request through `ed5214b` is untouched. The round trip stays
byte-exact and nothing leaks — this is the *functional* harm `PHONE-OM`'s own doc names (*"hands the
model `[PHONE_1]` where it needed `8080`, and the agent then does the wrong thing"*), now eating an IP
address and two rows of a SQL result.

**Nothing could have seen it, and that is the durable half.** `phone_eval` — the only precision
harness for this tier — is `#[ignore]`d, so `cargo test` never runs it; `PHONE-OM`, the guard that
carries the *"on real agent traffic the cost is zero"* claim, runs over the M7 fixture, which contains
**zero** IPv4 addresses and **zero** multi-space-aligned digit runs
(`grep -cE "[0-9]{2,3}( ){2,4}[0-9]{2,4}" tests/common/m7_turn.rs` → 0). So the separator axis has had
four consecutive widenings guarded by *coverage* assertions only: every guard on it asserts that
**more** is detected, and not one asserts that something is **not**. M4-R13's lesson — *a corpus has a
shape, and that shape is a blind spot* — arriving on the guard written to defeat it.

**And two of round 14's own three fixes are watched by nothing** ([M11-R56](#m11-r56),
[M11-R57](#m11-r57)). `PHONE_ALPHABET` — the test-side constant the round *derived*
`looks_like_a_rendering` from — is itself a hand-copied twin of `PHONE_SEPARATORS`, and it is exactly
two characters behind it: the `(` and `)` M11-R52 added. Delete them from the production constant and
the full suite is **green at 255 / 0 / 5** while `+49 (0)30 12345678` and `(020) 7946 0958` become
complete misses and `+44 (0) 20 7946 0958` is truncated to `20 7946 0958`. Separately,
`SEPARATOR-01`'s matrix substitutes runs of `1..=3` against a pattern that declares `{1,4}`, so
`SEPARATOR_RUN` can be narrowed to `{1,3}` with the suite green — *an assertion made only where it
cannot fail*, which is M10-R13's sentence, in this milestone, on the boundary the same commit chose.

**The cost that was published is not the cost that was paid** ([M11-R58](#m11-r58)). The closure
reports *"re-running `DOS-BUD`'s verdict grid: `3XX XXX XXXX` 8 → 9.8 units/row, +22%"* and adds it to
the open [M11-R18](#m11-r18) decision. `DOS-BUD` prints **8.00 units/row at HEAD and 8.00 at
`ed5214b`** — the widening costs **zero** units on single-separator traffic, which is what a `{1,4}`
repetition over a one-character gap must cost. The term that *did* grow is the unbudgeted `free()`
validator work M11-R18 is actually open on: on 4 MiB of distinct lowercase alphanumeric groups aligned
with 2–4 spaces, HEAD costs **842–1 411 ms** against `ed5214b`'s **151–223 ms** — **4.7× to 6.5×**, at
or above the 1-space worst case that decision was measured on.

**No leak, no fail-open, no raw value in a log.** The proxy's `info` output was searched for every
value driven through it and carries none; every control masked, augmented and restored byte-exactly.

### What was verified, with the expected value written before the observed one

| Quantity | Expected (written first) | Observed | Reps |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cargo test` (default) | 255 / 0 / 5 over 24 binaries, 0 warnings — round 14's figure | **255 / 0 / 5, 24 binaries, 0 warnings** | 2, identical |
| `cargo fmt --check` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` | exit 0 | **exit 0** | 1 |
| truncation regression vs `ed5214b`, 3 150 renderings, M10-R1 predicate | **0** if round 14 only widened coverage | **0 new leaks; 1 270 renderings newly masked** | 1 (deterministic) |
| random public IPv4 in a sentence, HEAD vs `ed5214b` | **0.000** both — an IP is not a phone rendering | **0.517** at HEAD, **0.000** at `ed5214b` (5 000 each) | 1 (fixed seed) |
| `10.x.x.x` · `192.168.x.x` · `172.16–31.x.x` | 0.000 | **0.623 · 0.416 · 0.374**, all 0.000 before | 1 (2 000 each) |
| `dd.mm.yyyy` · `dd/mm/yyyy` · `mm/dd/yyyy` dates | 0.000 — `ARCHITECTURE.md` says slash *"cannot collide at all"* | **0.440 · 0.440 · 0.074**, all 0.000 before | 1 (336 each) |
| `yyyy-mm-dd` (ISO) | 0.000 — the other half of the same claim | **0.000** — that half still holds | 1 |
| four-column numeric row, gap of 1 / 2 / 3 / 4 / 5 spaces | 0.872 at gap 1 (unchanged), 0.000 above — alignment is not a rendering | **0.872 at gaps 1, 2, 3 and 4**; **0.000 at 5**. Before: 0.872 at 1, **0.000** at 2–5 | 1 (3 000 each) |
| `phone_eval` union `dates` | **0.180**, the published figure | **0.270** | 2, identical |
| `phone_eval` union recall · curated FP · `tables` | 1.000 · 0.000 · 0.375 | **1.000 · 0.000 · 0.375** — unchanged | 2 |
| the real `.exe`: IP + aligned SQL rows in `content` | forwarded — no phone number is present | **`[PHONE_1]`, `[PHONE_2]`, `[PHONE_3]`**; `ed5214b` forwards all three | 1 each |
| the real `.exe`: IPs inside `tool_calls[].function.arguments` | forwarded | **`ssh deploy@[PHONE_1] … grep [PHONE_2] …`**; `ed5214b` untouched | 1 each |
| round trip of every over-masked value | byte-exact | **byte-exact** | 2 |
| raw PII in the proxy's `info` log | none | **none** | 2 |
| `DOS-BUD` `3XX XXX XXXX` units/row, HEAD vs `ed5214b` | 9.8 vs 8 — the closure's published +22% | **8.00 vs 8.00** | 1 each (units are deterministic) |
| an independent distinct 5 000-row `3XX XXX XXXX` column, HEAD vs `ed5214b` | some increase, if +22% is real | **33 607 vs 33 607 units — identical** | 1 each |
| `DOS-BUD` `+39 3XX XXXXXXX` units/row | 1.02, the figure `ARCHITECTURE.md` publishes as *printed by DOS-BUD* | **2.02**, on **both** builds — stale since round 12, not a round-14 change | 1 each |
| M11-R18's shape, 4 MiB lowercase, 1 space, HEAD vs `ed5214b` | ~equal — round 14 changed nothing at one separator | **741–1 044 ms vs 718–860 ms** | 3 each |
| the same at 2 / 3 / 4 spaces | ~equal | **842–1 177 / 919–1 059 / 918–1 411 ms** vs **182–198 / 196–223 / 151–163 ms** | 3 each |
| uppercase control at every run length | flat — R18's mechanism is the lowercase gate | **355–628 ms throughout, both builds** | 3 each |
| longest `Iban` / `Phone` / `CreditCard` span in bytes | 44 / 20 / 19 — the figures `Scan::Overlapping`'s doc quotes | **132 / 49 / 52** | 1 |

### Mutations run in round 15

`src/pii/recognizers.rs` only. Byte substitution through a harness that refuses to write unless the
anchor matches, and that asserts the resulting sha256 **differs** — an empty substitution is a green in
disguise. After every run the file is restored from the `HEAD` blob and its sha256 re-compared
(`af87cd121c7c4b95cb4789d423e49070f3053934b83d388209a8c049bd684c30`), with `git status --porcelain`
asserted empty; verified empty again at the end of the round. All builds from PowerShell.

| # | mutation | expected | result |
|---|---|---|---|
| A | `SEPARATOR_RUN` `{1,4}` → `{1,1}` — undo M11-R51 entirely | red | **red, 2 tests** — `every_gap_bearing_recognizer_answers_the_separator_axis`, `every_recognizer_records_the_renderings_it_detects`; 156 / 2 |
| B | `SEPARATOR_RUN` `{1,4}` → `{1,3}` — narrow the bound by one | red, if the declared bound is the guarded bound | **GREEN — 255 / 0 / 5**, full suite, 0 warnings. [M11-R57](#m11-r57) |
| C | `PHONE_SEPARATORS` loses `'('` and `')'` — undo M11-R52 entirely | red, if round 14's second fix is watched | **GREEN — 255 / 0 / 5**, full suite, 0 warnings. [M11-R56](#m11-r56) |
| D | control for C: `PHONE_SEPARATORS` loses `'/'` | red — round 13's fix is watched, per round 14's mutation A | **red, 2 tests**; 156 / 2. So C's green is the paren gap, not a dead guard |
| E | control for C: `PHONE_SEPARATORS` loses `'.'` | red | **red, 3 tests**; 155 / 3 |
| F | the proposed fix for C, alone: `PHONE_ALPHABET` gains `'('` `')'` | green — the product already accepts them | **GREEN — 255 / 0 / 5**. The fix costs nothing |
| G | F **plus** C — the fix, then the narrowing it must catch | red | **red, 1 test** — `every_gap_bearing_recognizer_answers_the_separator_axis`; 157 / 1 |
| H | the proposed fix for B, alone: matrix `1..=3usize` → `1..=4usize` | green — `{1,4}` accepts a run of four | **GREEN — 255 / 0 / 5**. The fix costs nothing |
| I | matrix `1..=3usize` → `1..=5usize` | red — a run of five is the declared residue | **red, 1 test**; 157 / 1. So the bound really is 4 |
| J | H **plus** B — the fix, then the narrowing it must catch | red | **red, 1 test** — `every_gap_bearing_recognizer_answers_the_separator_axis`; 157 / 1 |

### What this round could not break

- **The truncation class, on the axis round 14 widened.** 3 150 renderings × the M10-R1 predicate,
  differential against `ed5214b`: **0** cases where HEAD forwards part of a value the previous build
  masked. Round 14 is a pure coverage gain on the safety axis — 1 270 renderings moved from leaking to
  masked, none the other way. This is the first M11 round on the separator family in which the fix did
  not relocate anything.
- **The `{1,4}` bound itself.** Mutation I is red and a five-space run is undetected, so the residue the
  constant claims is the residue it has.
- **Round 13's alphabet.** Mutations D and E are both red and name their code point, so `/` and `.` are
  genuinely watched — which is what makes C's green a gap rather than a dead guard.
- **The legitimate paths.** `cargo test`, `cargo fmt --check` and `cargo clippy --all-targets --
  -D warnings` are clean at HEAD, run the way a person runs them, the suite twice; the real `.exe`
  boots, masks, augments and restores byte-exactly, and every value over-masked by [M11-R55](#m11-r55)
  comes back to the client **byte-identical** — the client-facing round trip is untouched, only what the
  model is shown changes.
- **The log discipline.** No raw value in the proxy's own output at `RUST_LOG=info`, across both
  end-to-end drivers.

### Observations that are not findings

- **`(020) 7946 0958` is detected as `020) 7946 0958`** — the trunk family anchors on `0`, so the
  opening parenthesis falls outside the span. Every digit is covered and the round trip is exact, so
  this is cosmetic, not M11-R30's shape.
- **`WITH_HYPHEN` is the third hand-copied alphabet** and is currently correct (`GAP_CHARS` + `-` =
  `{CGAP}`). Not filed separately because [M11-R56](#m11-r56)'s fix — derive all three from the
  production constants — covers it, and filing the instance is what this milestone has twice been told
  not to do.

### Guard-vs-product tally

Round 15: **1 in the product** ([R55](#m11-r55), an over-mask class), **2 on the net**
([R56](#m11-r56), [R57](#m11-r57)), **1 on the docs** ([R59](#m11-r59)), and **1 on a published
measurement** ([R58](#m11-r58)) that is half hardening and half arithmetic.

The question that found the product finding is the one round 14 did not ask: *what does this widening
refuse?* Every guard on this axis asks what it accepts. Four rounds of widening, four coverage
measurements, no precision measurement — and the only precision harness that exists for the tier is
`#[ignore]`d, so no round has run it since M10.

<a id="m11-r55"></a>
### M11-R55 — the separator widening masks an IPv4 address, a dotted or slashed date and a column-aligned table row: 0.000 → 0.517 / 0.440 / 0.872 against the pre-round-14 binary — *precision, high*

**The mechanism, in one line.** Round 14 gave the three domestic phone shape families (`Trunk`,
`Groups`, `LongBlock`) the `{PGAP}` alphabet — `.` `/` `(` `)` on top of whitespace — and gave every
family a separator **run** of up to four. `Groups` is
`(?-u:\b)[1-9]\d{1,2}(?:{PGAP}\d{2,4}){1,3}(?-u:\b)`, so `62.30.40.50` is now a candidate whose digits
are `62304050` — a valid Latvian landline — and `170.75.154.131` is `17075154131`, a valid Chinese
mobile. CN mobiles are 11 digits beginning `1`, which is exactly what a dotted IPv4 with a `1xx` first
octet produces; that is why the rate is so high rather than incidental. `SEPARATOR_RUN` does the same
for `42   101   201   301`.

**Measured, HEAD vs `ed5214b` (the commit before `2d52e83`), identical probe binary, fixed seed:**

| pool | `ed5214b` | HEAD |
|---|---|---|
| random public IPv4 in a sentence (5 000) | **0.000** | **0.517** |
| `10.x.x.x` (2 000) | 0.000 | **0.623** |
| `192.168.x.x` (2 000) | 0.000 | **0.416** |
| `172.16–31.x.x` (2 000) | 0.000 | **0.374** |
| `dd.mm.yyyy` (336) | 0.000 | **0.440** |
| `dd/mm/yyyy` (336) | 0.000 | **0.440** |
| `mm/dd/yyyy` (336) | 0.000 | **0.074** |
| `yyyy-mm-dd` (336) | 0.000 | 0.000 |
| four-column numeric row, 2 / 3 / 4 spaces (3 000 each) | 0.000 | **0.872** |
| four-column numeric row, 1 space | 0.872 | 0.872 |
| four-column numeric row, 5 spaces | 0.000 | 0.000 |

**The project's own harness says the same thing**, which is what makes this a regression against a
published number rather than a new opinion. `cargo test --release --test phone_eval -- --ignored
--nocapture phone_precision`, run twice, identical:

```
lv   dates 0.180   fp[dates] "le 28/01/2026 signe" -> ["28/01/2026"]
it   dates 0.120   fp[dates] "le 01/02/2026 signe" -> ["02/2026"]
UNION  recall 1.000 (35/35)  FPcur 0.000 (0/20)  dates 0.270  …  tables 0.375
```

`ARCHITECTURE.md`'s domestic-phone table publishes **`dates 0.180`** for the union, and the paragraph
under it says: *"ISO (`2026-07-29`) and slash (`29/07/2026`) dates cannot collide at all — no family
accepts `/`, and a 4-digit leading group is not a candidate."* The ISO half still holds. The slash half
is false in the commit that made it false, and `tests/phone_eval.rs:109` carries the same claim as a
comment above the generator that now produces the hits.

**Through the real `.exe`** (release build, mock upstream, default configuration). An ordinary
debugging message:

```
sent      : Debug this: the upstream 62.30.40.50 refused the connection and 10.55.120.7 timed out.
            Contact mario.rossi@example.com if it recurs.
            Here is the psql output:
            id    qty   price
            101   250   1999
            205   310   2499
upstream  : Debug this: the upstream [PHONE_1] refused the connection and 10.55.120.7 timed out.
            Contact [EMAIL_1] if it recurs.
            Here is the psql output:
            id    qty   price
            [PHONE_2]
            [PHONE_3]
ed5214b   : … the upstream 62.30.40.50 … all three rows intact, only [EMAIL_1] masked
```

and the same masking path reaches tool calls, which is where it costs an agent a turn:

```
assistant -> {"cmd": "ssh deploy@[PHONE_1] -p 22 && grep [PHONE_2] /var/log/app.log"}
tool      -> "PID   RSS\n420   1024\nrows [PHONE_3]"
ed5214b   -> both byte-identical to what was sent
```

The conversation carries **no PII at all**, and it arrives at the provider with three placeholders and
an injected augmentation system message.

**Not a leak, and the direction matters.** Every over-masked value is restored byte-identically on the
response path — the client sees exactly what it sent, verified on both drivers. The harm is entirely
model-facing, and it is the one `ARCHITECTURE.md` already names as the reason the domestic-phone trade
needs a real-traffic guard: *"a masked line number or port inside `tool_use.input` hands the model
`[PHONE_1]` where it needed `8080`, and the agent then does the wrong thing."* An IP address in an
`ssh` argument and two rows of a `psql` result are a larger version of that.

**Why nothing saw it, and this is the durable half.**
1. **`phone_eval` is `#[ignore]`d.** It is the only precision harness for this tier, and `cargo test`
   never runs it. Round 14 ran the suite twice and `--features onnx` once; none of those executes the
   measurement whose published number it invalidated.
2. **`PHONE-OM`'s fixture has a shape.** It carries the *"on real agent traffic the cost is zero"*
   claim and runs over the M7 turn — which holds **no IPv4 address** and **no digit run separated by
   2–4 spaces** (`grep -cE "[0-9]{2,3}( ){2,4}[0-9]{2,4}" tests/common/m7_turn.rs` → 0). It is
   structurally unable to see either shape round 14 admitted. M4-R13's lesson, landing on the guard
   written to defeat it.
3. **Every guard on this axis is a coverage guard.** `SEPARATOR-01` asserts that a re-rendered positive
   *is* detected; `RENDER-01` asserts that a recorded rendering *is* one span. Neither can express *"and
   this is not a phone number"*. Four consecutive widenings (R25 → R48 → R51/R52) have landed against
   assertions that can only go red when coverage **shrinks**.

**The fix — the class, not the instances.** Three options; the choice is product-visible, so it is the
maintainer's:

1. **Give the un-anchored families their own separator class.** `phonenumber::parse` validates all four
   phone recognizers, so by the letter of M11-R48's rule they share an alphabet — but that rule was
   derived from the `+CC` arm, which is anchored by `+` and cannot begin inside arbitrary text.
   `Groups` and `LongBlock` have no anchor at all, so widening *their* alphabet widens the candidate
   space over ordinary punctuated prose, and that is where the whole cost lands. Keep `{PGAP}` for the
   `+CC` and US arms (`tel:+1-201-555-0123` is a published rendering) and give the un-anchored families
   `{GAP}` + `-`, the alphabet they had and the only one for which a published rendering of theirs was
   ever measured. **Cost:** `+49 (0)30 12345678` keeps working (the `+` arm owns it); `(020) 7946 0958`
   goes back to being a miss, which is what M11-R52 measured at 0.923. This is the option that answers
   *"which renderings is this value published in?"* per **recognizer anchoring**, not per validator —
   and it is the smallest change that closes the class.
2. **Refuse a candidate whose separators are all `.` and whose group count is four** — a dotted-quad
   guard. Closes the IP instance and leaves the date and alignment ones open: the enumerate-the-instances
   shape `CLAUDE.md` forbids. Recorded so it is visibly rejected.
3. **Accept it and publish it.** Update `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s table with the measured rates, add `ips` and
   `aligned` categories to `phone_eval`'s pool, and say plainly that an IP address in a tool argument is
   masked. Defensible only with the numbers in front of the operator — 0.517 of public IPv4 is not a
   footnote.

Whichever is chosen, **two things are not optional**: `phone_eval` needs an `ips` and an `aligned`
category — they are shapes the families can now reach, and its own doc says a pool must be able to
reach every family it reports on — and `PHONE-OM` needs a fixture containing them, because the M7 turn
cannot carry the claim it is being asked to carry.

**The test that would have caught it** is `phone_eval` run at all, plus `PHONE-OM` extended with a
second fixture: a real tool-result turn containing `ls -l`, `df -h`, a `psql` table and an IP address,
asserting **zero** `Phone` spans. That is M10's own construction, applied to the corpus shape M10's
fixture does not have.

**NOT closed — escalated 2026-09-05, and it is a regression my own round-14 fix introduced.**

The numbers are the finding's and they are not in dispute: random public IPv4 **0.000 → 0.517**,
`10.x` **0.623**, dotted and slashed dates **0.440**, a 2–4-space-aligned numeric row **0.872**, and
`phone_eval`'s union `dates` **0.270** against the **0.180** `ARCHITECTURE.md` publishes. No leak —
the round trip is byte-exact — but it is precisely the model-facing harm M10 spent nine rounds
bounding, on ordinary agent text with no PII in it.

**All three remedies are product-visible coverage decisions**, so none is the builder's, and they
trade against renderings this milestone closed on purpose:

1. **Narrow the un-anchored families' alphabet** (the reviewer's promoted rule: *the alphabet is the
   validator's; the reach is the anchor's*). The `+CC` arm is anchored by `+` and can keep the full
   set. Costs `030 / 12345678` (M11-R51's German business rendering) and `(020) 7946 0958`
   (M11-R52's) unless `(`/`)` are kept while `.`/`/` go — which is possible and is a fourth option.
2. **A dotted-quad special case**, which closes IPv4 and leaves dates and aligned rows.
3. **Accept and publish the rates**, which `ARCHITECTURE.md` would then have to carry beside the
   0.180 it publishes today.

**And the aligned-table row is not an alphabet question at all** — it is `SEPARATOR_RUN_MAX`. `420
1024` with three spaces is a candidate because the run is 4; before round 14 it needed exactly one.
Narrowing the bound trades against `030 / 12345678` again, which is three characters. Whichever
option is taken, that is a second, separate number to pick.

I did not take any of them, and I want to be plain about why rather than quiet: the fix that caused
this closed a leak, so undoing part of it is a trade between a miss and an over-mask on a tier whose
false-positive rate is a published promise (M8.1, M10). That is the maintainer's arithmetic.

**Closed 2026-09-06 — option 3: accepted and published (`105005c`).**

No change to the recognizers. The R48, R51 and R52 fixes stay whole and no leak is re-opened, and the
reason belongs next to the decision because **both alternatives cost a leak**: returning the narrower
alphabet to the un-anchored families re-opens `(020) 7946 0958` at 0.923, and tightening the run
re-opens the domestic half of `+39  347  1234567` at 1.000. An over-mask is restored byte-identically
on the response path; a miss is not. *Between harm aimed at the model and harm aimed at the data,
keep the data and declare the harm.*

Accepting is the easy half — the decision moves the weight onto the numbers, so it moves it onto the
things that measure them. Four, and none of them optional:

1. **`phone_eval`'s precision test is no longer `#[ignore]`d**, and its output *is* the published
   table. `ARCHITECTURE.md` carries the measurement between `<!-- PHONE-EVAL:BEGIN -->` and
   `<!-- PHONE-EVAL:END -->`; the test renders the block from a live run and asserts it byte for
   byte, printing the new one when it differs. Not two lists checking each other (M11-R53): one side
   is a measurement of the product, the other is what the operator reads, and there is no third copy
   to drift. Cost: ~13 s in the debug profile, paid on every `cargo test`. The **latency** test stays
   `#[ignore]`d — it reports milliseconds — and `TESTING.md` now declares it, with `DOS-BUD`, as a
   command run before every tag, so *"it exists and nobody runs it"* stops being a state this repo
   can be in.
2. **The pool gained the shapes the families can reach:** `ips` (public IPv4), `ips10`, `ips192`,
   `ips172` and `aligned` (2-4-space columns), from a **seeded LCG rather than `rand`**, because a
   rate asserted against a document has to be byte-identical on every box. The harness's own first
   assertion — a pool must *reach* a family before its 0.000 means "clean" — now covers both new
   shapes. Union rates, asserted: `aligned` **0.785**, `ips` **0.500**, `ips10` **0.656**, `ips192`
   **0.422**, `ips172` **0.438**, `dates` **0.270**. (The independent probe above read 0.872 / 0.517 /
   0.623 / 0.416 / 0.374 on its own pools; the published figures are the harness's, because those are
   the ones a guard keeps true.)
3. **`PHONE-OM` gained a second fixture, and its expectation is a number rather than a zero.**
   `tests/common/tool_output_turn.rs` is six fields of `ls -l`, `df -h`, a `psql` result, journal
   lines with public and RFC 1918 addresses, an `ssh` `tool_use.input` and a user message; every
   number in it is a real quantity of its own kind and none is a phone number. `PHONE-OM-TOOL`
   asserts the **11** spans it yields, exactly — six `psql` rows, three dotted quads, and two inside
   the `ssh` argument. A guard asserting *zero* here would assert the opposite of what ships.
   *(This paragraph first claimed the guard was red in both directions on both coordinates;
   [M11-R61](#m11-r61) measured which mutation each of the three guards on this axis actually
   catches, and the corrected statement lives there and in `TESTING.md`.)*
4. **The three places that said something false now say what is measured.**
   `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s *"ISO and slash dates cannot collide at all"* (the ISO half survives, the slash
   half does not), the generator comment in `tests/phone_eval.rs`, and the coverage prose in both
   READMEs — which now describe the over-mask as a cost to weigh and defer every rate to the one
   asserted place. `SEPARATOR_RUN_MAX`'s own doc said the residue *was* column alignment; column
   alignment is what the bound **admits**, and the residue is a run of five or more.
   `CHANGELOG.md` carries it under `### Changed` as a first-class entry — the same treatment Track B's
   halved thread count got — because an IP address inside a tool argument arriving as `[PHONE_1]` is a
   behaviour change, not a technical note. It deliberately carries **no rates**: a released section is
   history, and a live figure inside one ages into a lie on the next widening.

<a id="m11-r56"></a>
### M11-R56 — M11-R52's fix is pinned by nothing: `PHONE_ALPHABET` is a hand-copied twin of `PHONE_SEPARATORS`, two characters behind it, so deleting `(` and `)` is green at 255 / 0 / 5 — *guard, high*

**The mutation.** `PHONE_SEPARATORS` loses `'('` and `')'` — the whole of round 14's second fix, one
line, the exact edit a narrowing commit would make:

```
 const PHONE_SEPARATORS: &[char] = &[
-    '-', '.', '/', '(', ')',
+    '-', '.', '/',
 ];
```

**Full suite: 255 passed / 0 failed / 5 ignored over 24 binaries, 0 warnings.** Green.

**What actually changes in the product** (same build, library probe):

| input | HEAD | with `(` `)` deleted |
|---|---|---|
| `chiama +49 (0)30 12345678 subito` | `Phone:"+49 (0)30 12345678"` | **nothing** |
| `ring (020) 7946 0958 today` | `Phone:"020) 7946 0958"` | **nothing** |
| `ring (020)7946 0958 today` | `Phone:"020)7946 0958"` | **nothing** |
| `call +44 (0) 20 7946 0958 now` | `Phone:"+44 (0) 20 7946 0958"` | `Phone:"20 7946 0958"` — **truncated**, `+44 (0)` in clear |
| `call +1 (415) 555-2671 now` | masked | masked (the US arm spells `\(\d{3}\)` itself) |

So M11-R52's leak — measured at 0.714 and 0.923 one round ago — can be reintroduced in full, including
a **truncation**, with the suite green.

**The cause is one link short in the derivation chain the same commit built.** Round 14's own message
says it: *"`looks_like_a_rendering`'s alphabet is derived from `PHONE_ALPHABET` — hand-listing the four
missing characters would have fixed those four; deriving it means the filter can never again be
narrower than the code it is asked about."* The derivation is real, and it stops at `PHONE_ALPHABET`,
which is itself hand-listed in the test module:

```rust
/// The `+CC` arm's alphabet: everything `phonenumber::parse` discards, `/` included (M11-R48).
/// It is the widest because its validator is the most permissive — which is the whole rule.
const PHONE_ALPHABET: &[char] = &[
    '\u{0009}', … '\u{3000}', '-', '.', '/',
];
```

Its doc says *"everything `phonenumber::parse` discards"*; the production constant beside it says
`- . / ( )`; the two disagree by exactly the characters the round added. `SEPARATOR-01`'s matrix takes
its alphabet from this constant, so the matrix never asks about a parenthesis and the audit has nothing
to notice. `SPACE_ONLY` is written `= GAP_CHARS` — derived, and therefore correct. `WITH_HYPHEN` and
`PHONE_ALPHABET` are copies.

**This is [M11-R31](#m11-r31) → [M11-R40](#m11-r40) → [M11-R49](#m11-r49) → [M11-R53](#m11-r53) a
fifth time, and the chokepoint is now unambiguous.** Each of those rounds moved one guard input from a
hand-kept list to a derivation. The remaining hand-kept inputs are these two constants, and the rule
they need is the one this repository already applies to `SPACE_ONLY`: **a guard's alphabet must be the
production constant, not a transcription of it.**

**The fix**, verified by mutation — in the test module:

```rust
const WITH_HYPHEN:     /* GAP_CHARS ∪ ['-'] */               // {CGAP}'s alphabet
const PHONE_ALPHABET:  /* GAP_CHARS ∪ PHONE_SEPARATORS */    // {PGAP}'s alphabet
```

A `const` cannot concatenate two slices directly; the shapes this repo already uses — a small `const fn`
or a `LazyLock<Vec<char>>` beside the answers — both work. What matters is that `PHONE_SEPARATORS` is
the only place those characters are written. Measured:

- mutation **F** (add `(` `)` to `PHONE_ALPHABET` alone) → **green, 255 / 0 / 5**: the fix costs
  nothing, because the product already accepts them at every cardinality the matrix tries.
- mutation **G** (F, then delete `(` `)` from `PHONE_SEPARATORS`) → **red, 1 test**,
  `every_gap_bearing_recognizer_answers_the_separator_axis`, 157 / 1.

**The test that would have caught it** already exists and is the one round 14 wrote: `SEPARATOR-01`'s
matrix. It could not fire because its alphabet was copied rather than derived — *a guard that takes its
alphabet from a transcription of the code is asking about the transcription.*

**Closed 2026-09-05, and the first fix was not enough — which is the part worth keeping.**

The three alphabet `const`s were transcriptions of the production constants, two characters behind.
They are gone: `Alphabet::{Space, Card, Phone}` now returns `GAP_CHARS` chained with the same
`extra` slice the patterns are built from, so the guard's alphabet **is** the production constant.

**Deriving it fixed drift in one direction only, and the mutation proved it.** With the derived
alphabet, deleting `(` and `)` from `PHONE_SEPARATORS` was *still* green at 255/0/5 — because the
matrix then stops substituting the character that was removed. A derived guard cannot see its own
source being **narrowed**; it can only see the source running ahead of it.

What sees a narrowing is a **behaviour**: `+49 (0)30 12345678` is now a `detected` rendering, and the
same mutation is red on the span assertion. That is M11-R53's sentence arriving from the other side —
*two lists that only check each other are bookkeeping* — and the pair is the rule: **derive the
alphabet so it cannot drift ahead, and assert a rendering so it cannot be narrowed.**

<a id="m11-r57"></a>
### M11-R57 — `SEPARATOR-01`'s matrix substitutes runs of `1..=3` against a pattern that declares `{1,4}`, so the bound can be narrowed with the suite green — *guard, medium*

**The mutation.** `SEPARATOR_RUN: &str = "{1,4}"` → `"{1,3}"`. **Full suite green: 255 / 0 / 5.**

**Why.** The matrix added by round 14 is

```rust
for count in 1..=3usize {
    let run: String = std::iter::repeat_n(*separator, count).collect();
```

against a constant that declares four, beside a doc comment that names the residue precisely: *"Four
covers every rendering the round measured (` / ` and ` - ` are three); the residue is a separator run of
five or more."* The guard exercises 1, 2 and 3. **The one value the constant chose over its neighbour —
four — is the one value the guard never tries.**

That is M10-R13's sentence, which this repository has already paid for once: *"its own guard asserted
the superset property over 30 hand-written literals, all domestic renderings and therefore all ≤ 13
digits; the defect lived at 14-15. An assertion made only where it cannot fail is not an assertion."*

**The fix**, verified: the matrix's upper bound must come from `SEPARATOR_RUN` rather than from a
literal — parse the `{1,N}` once, iterate `1..=N`, and add a **negative** row at `N+1` so the declared
residue is asserted rather than described. Measured:

- mutation **H** (`1..=3usize` → `1..=4usize`) → **green, 255 / 0 / 5**: the widened matrix passes at
  HEAD, so the fix is free.
- mutation **I** (`1..=5usize`) → **red, 1 test**: a run of five really is outside, so the residue note
  is true and `N+1` is a usable negative.
- mutation **J** (H, then `SEPARATOR_RUN` → `{1,3}`) → **red, 1 test**,
  `every_gap_bearing_recognizer_answers_the_separator_axis`, 157 / 1.

**The rule to promote**, because this is the third registry in three rounds with the same shape: *a
guard that asserts a bounded quantity must take the bound from the constant that declares it, and must
carry one case on each side of it.* A guard whose loop bound is a literal agrees with whatever the code
said on the day it was written.

**Closed 2026-09-05, the same way and with the same second half.** `SEPARATOR_RUN_MAX` is the single
declaration; `separator_run()` formats it into the patterns and the matrix iterates `1..=` it, so the
loop can no longer run 1..=3 against a bound of 4.

And, exactly as in M11-R56, that alone was **green**: narrowing the bound to 3 narrows the loop with
it. `+39    347 1234567` — a run of exactly `SEPARATOR_RUN_MAX` — is now a `detected` rendering, and
the mutation is red against it.

Two findings, one shape, one rule: **a derived bound stops the guard drifting ahead; only an asserted
value at the bound stops it being narrowed.**

<a id="m11-r58"></a>
### M11-R58 — round 14's published cost does not reproduce on the harness it names, and the term that *did* grow is [M11-R18](#m11-r18)'s open one: 4.7–6.5× on a column-aligned body — *hardening, high*

**What is published** — the round-14 closure, `docs/DEVLOG.md:25`, and the ledger prose:

> **And the cost is measured, because M11-R38 is open on exactly this.** Re-running `DOS-BUD`'s verdict
> grid against round 10's figures: `3XX XXX XXXX` (valid) 8 → **9.8** units/row, `0NNNN NNNN` id column
> (rejected) 12 → **13.8**. **+22% per row**, so the allowance is reached ~18% sooner; wall-clock per
> row is unchanged.

**What `DOS-BUD` prints** — `cargo test --release --test complexity -- --ignored --nocapture
budget_refusal_line`, at HEAD and at `ed5214b`:

```
                 column of     spent   units/row       verdict
347 XXXXXXX (IT LongBlock)     20000        1.00  20000 masked
 3XX XXX XXXX (IT grouped)    160000        8.00  20000 masked      <- 8.00 on BOTH builds
 0X XX XX XX XX (FR pairs)     65000        3.25  20000 masked
 6XX XX XX XX (ES grouped)     65000        3.25  20000 masked
     +39 3XX XXXXXXX (+CC)     40400        2.02  20000 masked
```

An independent probe — 5 000 **distinct** `3XX XXX XXXX` rows, spend read off `Budget::spent()` — gives
**33 607 units on both builds**, byte for byte. That is what a `{1,4}` repetition over a *one-character*
gap must cost: the candidate set over single-separator text is unchanged, so term 1 is unchanged.
Neither harness reproduces +22%, and the closure names `DOS-BUD` specifically. *(The generator behind
the published figure is not in the repository, so the claim here is "does not reproduce on the named
harness", not "is arithmetically wrong" — but this file's own rule is that a number a reader can re-run
is a fact, and this one re-runs to a different value.)*

**The term that did grow is the one M11-R18 is open on.** M11-R18 is *"`free()` validator work, per
candidate and charged to nothing"* — the `shrink_on_reject` retry at every interior separator. A
separator **run** multiplies exactly that: `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` cuts at
`rfind(is_any_separator)`, so a four-character run costs four cut iterations (each with a regex `find`
over the prefix) where one character cost one. Measured on M11-R18's own shape — 4 MiB of distinct
lowercase `[a-z]{2}[0-9]{2}` groups, three reps, with its uppercase control alongside:

| separator between groups | `ed5214b` lowercase | HEAD lowercase | ratio | uppercase control |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 space | 718 / 743 / 860 ms | 741 / 718 / 747 ms | 1.0× | 400–563 ms both |
| **2 spaces** | 184 / 198 / 182 ms | **871 / 854 / 842 ms** | **~4.7×** | 480–628 ms |
| **3 spaces** | 206 / 223 / 196 ms | **1 059 / 965 / 919 ms** | **~4.8×** | 374–497 ms |
| **4 spaces** | 163 / 163 / 151 ms | **918 / 1 411 / 1 198 ms** | **~6.5×** | 355–533 ms |

So a **column-aligned** alphanumeric body — ordinary `psql`, `df`, `ls -l` output — now costs what
M11-R18's worst measured shape costs, and at four spaces exceeds it; before round 14 it cost a fifth.
The uppercase control stays flat, confirming the path is the `iban_case_gate` + `shrink_on_reject` one
M11-R18 named rather than a general slowdown.

**Not a leak, still fail-closed, still linear** — but M11-R18 is an **open maintainer decision** whose
entire content is *how much unbudgeted work a request can provoke*, and the input it was priced on is
no longer the worst one. That is the failure M10-R49 / R53 / R56 recorded three times: *a conclusion
drawn from one point of a grid is a fact about that point.*

**The fix.** Two parts, neither a change to the recognizers:
1. Correct the published cost note — replace the +22% table with what `DOS-BUD` prints, or publish the
   generator that produced 9.8 so it can be re-run.
2. Add the measured multi-separator row to [M11-R18](#m11-r18)'s option table, since it moves the
   numbers that decision rests on. `DOS-BUD` should also carry a run-separated layout in its grid: it
   varies rendering, layout and size, and now must vary **separator cardinality** — which is
   [M11-R39](#m11-r39)'s shape (*the grid does not vary the thing that changed*) one axis over.

**The test that would have caught it** is `DOS-BUD` itself, run before and after — which is what the
closure says it did.

**Partly closed, and the discrepancy is reported rather than resolved 2026-09-05.**

**The `+22%` reproduces on this machine, three times.** `DOS-BUD`'s verdict grid at HEAD spends
**49 000** units for 5 000 rows of `3XX XXX XXXX` — **9.8 units/row** — against round 10's measured
**40 000 / 8.0** on the same grid before the separator run landed. I re-ran it after this round's
edits and got 49 000 again. Round 15 reports **8.00 on both builds**, which I cannot reproduce and
cannot explain from here; the most likely reading is that the two runs did not compare the builds
they meant to, but I am not going to assert that about someone else's harness.

**What is not in dispute, and is the larger number, is round 15's own probe**: the growth it measured
on M11-R18's term is **4.7–6.5×**, an order of magnitude above the per-row figure and on a different
shape. That is the number that matters for the open decision, and it is carried into
[M11-R18](#m11-r18) and [M11-R38](#m11-r38) alongside the per-row one.

So the closure note's claim is amended, not withdrawn: **+22% per row on the verdict grid (mine,
reproduced), and 4.7–6.5× on round 15's probe (theirs, not reproduced here)** — two measurements of
two things, both of which the maintainer's decision has to be taken against.

**Closed 2026-09-06 — the disagreement is resolved by measuring against the old build, and neither
number was a per-row cost (`28f0b9e`).**

Round 14 published `3XX XXX XXXX` at *8 → 9.8 units/row, +22%*; round 15 measured **8.00 on both
builds** and could not reproduce it; the amendment above reported both and could not reconcile them.
Re-measured in a `git worktree` at `ed5214b` with the same command, `DOS-BUD`'s verdict grid:

| rows | `ed5214b` | HEAD | delta |
|---|---|---|---|
| 5 000 | 40 000 | 49 000 | **+9 000** |
| 20 000 | 160 000 | 169 000 | **+9 000** |
| 50 000 | 400 000 | 409 000 | **+9 000** |
| 5 000 (`0NNNN NNNN`) | 60 000 | 69 000 | **+9 000** |
| 20 000 (`0NNNN NNNN`) | 240 000 | 249 000 | **+9 000** |

**The delta is a constant, five rows out of five**, so the per-row *ratio* is that constant divided by
the row count: 1.8 at 5 000 rows (+22%), 0.45 at 20 000 (+5.6%), and 0.00 in the bare 20 000-row
*column* grid, which carries no non-phone text at all. Round 14 read the first, round 15 read the
third, and both were right about their own row.

**Attributed, not inferred.** Deleting the `NNN.50` money column from the body at HEAD reproduces
`ed5214b`'s figure **exactly** — 40 000 at 5 000 rows, 160 000 at 20 000 — because round 14's alphabet
made `123.50` a `Groups` candidate; and the offset is *constant* rather than per-row because the
per-scan memo collapses its 900 distinct values. **The phone column's own per-row cost did not move**,
which is what round 15's independent probe (33 607 units on both builds) was also saying.

*A conclusion drawn from one point of a grid is a fact about that point* — M10-R49 / R53 / R56 for the
fourth time, now landing on a disagreement between two reviewers rather than on a published band. The
durable half: **when two measurements of "the same number" disagree, check first whether they are the
same row.**

<a id="m11-r59"></a>
### M11-R59 — the round's own documentation: an inverted residue note, three stale figures, and no `[Unreleased]` entry for a change that eats a SQL row — *docs, medium*

Five items, four of them in the commit that introduced what they describe.

1. **`SEPARATOR_RUN`'s residue note is inverted.** It says *"the residue is a separator run of five or
   more, which is column alignment rather than a rendering."* Column alignment is **2 to 4** spaces —
   which is what the bound **admits** — and that is the whole of [M11-R55](#m11-r55)'s third pool
   (0.000 → 0.872 at gaps 2, 3 and 4; 0.000 at 5). The sentence identifies the excluded set correctly
   and describes it as the thing it is not.
2. **`ARCHITECTURE.md`'s domestic-phone table publishes `dates 0.180`**; `phone_eval` measures
   **0.270**, twice. The same paragraph's *"slash (`29/07/2026`) dates cannot collide at all — no family
   accepts `/`"* is false at HEAD; the ISO half of the same sentence still holds.
   `tests/phone_eval.rs:109` repeats the claim as a comment above the generator that produces the hits.
3. **`Scan::Overlapping`'s doc quotes match-length bounds that are ~3× off.** It says *"card ≤ 19
   digits, IBAN ≤ 44 chars, phone ≤ 20"*, and that is the bound M4-R19's O(n·L) linearity argument is
   stated in terms of. Measured at HEAD: a single `Iban` span reaches **132 bytes**, `Phone` **49**,
   `CreditCard` **52** (four U+3000 gaps of 3 bytes each, per group). L is still bounded, so the
   argument survives — the numbers it is written with do not.
4. **`ARCHITECTURE.md` publishes `+39 3XX XXXXXXX (any +CC) | 1.02` units/row** in a table introduced
   with *"Every row below is printed by `DOS-BUD`"*. `DOS-BUD` prints **2.02**, on **both** builds — so
   this one is stale since round 12's `+CC` validator rather than a round-14 change, and it is the only
   row in that table that disagrees with the harness.
5. **`CHANGELOG.md`'s `[Unreleased]` says nothing about the separator alphabet or the run.** It has
   entries for the case fold, E.164, card groupings, the Unicode digit and the non-ASCII space — and
   none for `-` `.` `/` `(` `)` becoming separators, or for runs up to four. The file's own preamble
   says the `### Changed` section *"is where a change that can surprise you is named, and it is the part
   to read before upgrading"*, and [M11-R55](#m11-r55) is exactly that: an operator upgrading gets IP
   addresses and aligned table columns replaced with `[PHONE_n]` in what the model sees. Third time in
   this milestone ([M11-R28](#m11-r28), [M11-R37](#m11-r37)) that a user-visible change landed with no
   changelog entry.

**Closed 2026-09-05** — the inverted residue note, the three stale figures and the missing
`[Unreleased]` entry are corrected, and the CHANGELOG now carries the separator work in the register
the rest of that section uses.


<a id="round-16"></a>
## Round 16 — the fix for the budget's blind term reopens M10-R29 (2026-09-06)

Target: `105005c` (M11-R55 accepted and published), `28f0b9e` (M11-R18 + M11-R38), `ef12146`
(the closure notes) and `7f2b545` (the changelog rewrite that landed mid-round).

**The headline: one finding in the product, and it is not a new one.** `iban_case_gate` leaving
`free()` is [M10-R29](M10.md#m10-r29) undone. That finding is the reason `free()` exists — *"all nine
are pure arithmetic on ≤ 18 bytes — nanoseconds, not the ~6.5 µs per region that the whole budget
exists to bound. Fifty thousand of them is five milliseconds of work refused as if it were half a
second"* — and it listed three consequences, all three of which are back at HEAD on a different
shape: a **false refusal** on legal traffic, a **confidently misdirected** refusal message, and the
phone tier's real allowance being eaten first by a cheaper recognizer that runs before it.
[M11-R60](#m11-r60).

### Numbers, expected before observed

| Quantity | Expected | Observed | Runs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `cargo test` | 258 / 0 / 4 | **258 / 0 / 4** | 2, identical |
| `cargo fmt --all -- --check` | clean | clean (exit 0) | 1 |
| `cargo clippy --all-targets -- -D warnings` | clean | clean (exit 0) | 1 |
| `DOS-BUD` `3XX XXX XXXX` (accept) µs/unit | 3.2–4.3 | 3.42 · 3.93 · 4.03 | 1 |
| `DOS-BUD` `0NNNN NNNN` (reject) | 2.9–3.5 | 2.57 · 2.83 · 3.50 | 1 |
| `DOS-BUD` `ab12 cd34` (reject) | 1.3–1.8 | 1.13 · 1.28 · 1.56 | 1 |
| `DOS-BUD` `0NNN NNNN NNNN` (accept) | 15.1–21.7 | 15.67 · 16.41 · 17.29 | 1 |
| units for 50 000 three-group rows | 487,214 | **487,214** | 1 |
| units/row: LongBlock · IT grouped · FR · ES · `+CC` | 1.00 · 8.00 · 3.25 · 3.25 · 2.02 | identical | 1 |
| refusal layouts (bare · `name,phone` · 6-column) | 813 KB · 2.1 MB · 4.7 MB | 812,500 · 2,051,390 · 4,665,280 | 1 |
| 16 MiB `347 XXXXXXX` column | 230,941 units, masked | identical | 1 |
| `PHONE-OM-TOOL` spans | 11 | 11 | 2 |

The two µs/unit cells below their published floor (2.57 against 2.9; 1.13 against 1.3) are a
rep-to-rep band on a different box, not a disagreement — every *count* in the grid reproduces
exactly, which is the half that is build-independent.

### Mutations

| # | Mutation (`src/` unless noted) | Outcome |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `iban_case_gate`: `budget.spend();` → `let _ = budget;` | **DOS-10 red**, its own message, `spent 0`; rest of `complexity` green |
| 2 | the same, release `.exe` rebuilt and driven end to end | 8 MiB and 12 MiB `xxd` go **400 → 200** — the [M11-R60](#m11-r60) repro |
| 3 | `SEPARATOR_RUN_MAX: 4 → 3` | `phone_eval` red (`aligned` 0.785 → 0.521); **`phone_overmask` green** |
| 4 | `SEPARATOR_RUN_MAX: 4 → 2` | `phone_overmask` red (`PHONE-OM-TOOL`) |
| 5 | **`SEPARATOR_RUN_MAX: 4 → 5`** | **whole suite green, 258 / 0 / 4** — [M11-R61](#m11-r61) |
| 6 | `PHONE_SEPARATORS` gains `','` and `':'` | `SEPARATOR-01` red — the *alphabet* coordinate is pinned upward |
| 7 | `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`: block between the PHONE-EVAL markers emptied | `phone_eval` red |
| 8 | `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`: `PHONE-EVAL:END` → `PHONE-EVAL:ENDX` | `phone_eval` red, *"has lost its `<!-- PHONE-EVAL:END -->` marker"* |

Every mutation was written as bytes, its sha256 compared against the `HEAD` blob before and after,
and restored; `git status` is clean at the end of the round. Mutation 6 is the one that had to be
checked for an empty substitution — the array and its doc comment both name `(` and `)`, and
substituting the comment is a green in disguise; the helper refuses any target that does not occur
exactly once.

### The legitimate paths

Real release `.exe`, mock upstream, default configuration, `/v1/chat/completions`:

* one ordinary request carrying `mario.rossi@example.com`, the **lowercase** IBAN
  `it60x0542811101000000123456`, `+39 347 1234567`, `62.30.40.50`, `10.55.120.7` and a three-row
  `psql` block → **200**. Upstream saw `[EMAIL_1] [IBAN_1] [PHONE_1..4]` and the augmentation system
  message; `10.55.120.7` went through in clear, as the published rate says it may; the round trip
  came back byte-exact. Identical on the mutated build, so nothing in this round's mutations touched
  the masking path.
* `xxd` hex dump at **4 MiB → 200** and **6 MiB → 200**.
* ordinary bodies through one request budget (`Vault::mask_all`, 4 MiB each): a JSON export with
  UUIDs **881** units, base64 **1,069**, the repo's own `recognizers.rs` repeated **883**, a
  `git log --oneline` of 40-char SHAs **0**, a log file of 4 096 cycling lines **1,483** — all
  against an allowance of 500,000. Those are wide margins and they are not in question.

### What I tried and could not break

* **The memo hole I expected to find.** `shrink_to_a_valid_prefix` runs `self.regex.find(prefix)`
  on every iteration *before* any budget check, and a repeating body makes every validator call a
  memo hit — so I predicted a body that pays the shrink's re-match at zero budget cost. Measured:
  4 MiB of one repeated lowercase group line spends **15** units in 0.33 s, against 0.31 s for the
  uppercase control that does no arithmetic at all, and 16 MiB costs 1.43 s. The unbudgeted residue
  is bounded by the linear scan `MAX_BODY_BYTES` already bounds. Hypothesis refuted.
* **M11-R38's contradiction.** The builder reported that the finding's stated mechanism — *a
  rejection costs more units than an acceptance* — does not survive measurement. It does not.
  Re-derived independently from `DOS-BUD`: the rejecting `0NNNN NNNN` column is the **cheapest**
  column but one, and the dearest shape by a factor of four is `0NNN NNNN NNNN` — **accepting**
  traffic. A verdict multiplier charges the wrong column and cannot bound the dearest one, so
  declining to add it is right. Option 1 plus a republished band is the correct call on these
  numbers.
* **The PHONE-EVAL block cannot be satisfied vacuously** (mutations 7 and 8), and it binds to the
  recognizers (mutation 3 turns it red with a printable diff).
* **The record.** 60 ledger rows resolve to 60 unique anchors, no dangling link in either direction,
  and every one of the 60 entries carries a `**Closed` note. The five notes relocated from the file
  tail appear once as a deletion and once as an addition in `ef12146` — moved, not dropped — and
  each sits under the anchor its text names. No entry was left with both an "open" and a "closed"
  copy of the same note; `M11-R38` and `M11-R55` each keep one escalation paragraph followed by one
  closure, which is chronology rather than double-recording.

### Lowest severity — named here, no ledger row, fix in the same commit

1. **The µs/unit grid exists in six places and one of them disagrees — and it is the one an operator
   reads.** `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:401-403` publishes **4.0–4.3 · 2.5–3.4 · 1.1–1.6 · 16.1–20.7**;
   `src/pii/recognizers.rs:1485`, `docs/TESTING.md:1150`, `docs/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/DEVLOG.md:30` and
   [M11-R38](#m11-r38)'s own closure table all publish **3.2–4.3 · 2.9–3.5 · 1.3–1.8 · 15.1–21.7**.
   Both are plausible rep bands; neither is asserted by anything. This is the number the republished
   ceiling is derived from, so it is the last place in the repo that should be a hand-copy — and the
   chokepoint is already built and working ten lines away: `DOS-BUD` can render its grid between
   markers and assert it against `ARCHITECTURE.md` exactly as `phone_eval` now does. *A number in
   prose is either asserted from the code, or not written.*
2. **`docs/ROADMAP.md`'s *"60 findings; six were leaks"*** does not reconcile with its own ledger,
   which carries **ten** rows labelled `**leak**` (R10, R13, R25, R30, R33, R41, R43, R48, R51,
   R52). It pre-dates these commits — present already at `aae4410` — and round 16 left the sentence
   rewritten to the ledger's number with the discrepancy flagged rather than guessing which grouping
   *six* meant.
3. **`708,648` does not reproduce with `DOS-10`'s own generator.** Running `alnum_groups(4 MiB,
   false)` — copied verbatim out of `tests/complexity.rs:528` — through `try_detect` with an
   uncapped budget measures **688,964** units here. The figure is published in
   `src/pii/recognizers.rs` (twice), `docs/TESTING.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:425` and
   [M11-R18](#m11-r18)'s closure. Either it came from a different generator, in which case that
   generator should be the one named, or it has drifted; it is not worth a row either way, but it is
   worth *one* of those two sentences.
