<a id="m11-r60"></a>
### M11-R60 — the case gate leaving `free()` refuses an ordinary hex dump at 8 MiB that was masked and forwarded at 12, and the refusal blames the phone tier — [M10-R29](M10.md#m10-r29) reopened · **correctness / availability**

**Repro, real release `.exe`, mock upstream, default configuration.** The body is one
`messages[0].content` holding `xxd`-format output — the offset column, then eight groups of four
lowercase hex digits, then the ASCII gutter. Same generator, same seed, both builds:

| body | HEAD (`7f2b545`) | the same build with `budget.spend()` removed from `iban_case_gate` |
|---|---|---|
| `xxd`, 4 MiB | 200 · 653 ms | 200 · 688 ms |
| `xxd`, 6 MiB | 200 · 1 102 ms | 200 · 1 150 ms |
| **`xxd`, 8 MiB** | **400 · 1 206 ms** | **200 · 1 811 ms** |
| **`xxd`, 12 MiB** | **400 · 1 282 ms** | **200 · 2 732 ms** |

In process, through `Vault::mask_all` with one per-request budget — the shape `PrivacyStage` drives:

| `xxd` body | units spent, HEAD | units spent, gate uncharged |
|---|---|---|
| 1 MiB | 84,579 | 48,860 |
| 4 MiB | **262,709** (53 % of the allowance) | 123,317 |
| 6 MiB | **418,260** (84 %) | 208,675 |
| 8 MiB | **REFUSED** | 246,500 |
| 16 MiB | **REFUSED** | 492,762 — accepted |

So the refusal line for this payload class moves from *outside `MAX_BODY_BYTES` altogether* to
**about 7 MiB**.

**Why nobody saw it: the sample layout cannot spell the shape it is a sample of.**
`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:426-427` publishes *"4 MiB of lowercase hex (`abcd 1234 …`) spends ~38,000"*,
and `CHANGELOG.md:117-120` turns that into the operator-facing sentence *"the shape has to be dense
to matter — two letters then two digits, repeated. Measured, **a lowercase hex dump**, a JSON
export, base64 and source code all stay far below the allowance."* The string `abcd 1234 …` is a
pure-letter group alternating with a pure-digit group, and the IBAN pattern needs two letters **and
then two digits inside one group** (`[A-Za-z]{2}\d{2}`). I measured that exact layout at 4 MiB:
**0 units.** No hex dump in the world is laid out that way — `xxd`, `od -x` and every debugger emit
uniformly-distributed hex per group, where P(two letters then two digits) = (6/16)² · (10/16)² ≈
**5.5 %** of groups — and each of those pays more than one unit, because `shrink_on_reject` retries
at every interior separator. Measured, the gate's share of a 16 MiB dump is **283,392** units
(532,278 at HEAD minus 248,886 with the charge removed). *A corpus has a shape, and that shape is a
blind spot* (M4-R13) — landing this time on the measurement the maintainer's decision was taken
against.

**Mechanism, and why this is M10-R29 rather than a new class.** M10-R29 established the rule
`free()` encodes: **one unit is one `phonenumber::parse()`; a validator materially cheaper than that
must not spend the allowance, because over-pricing cheap work refuses legal traffic.** `28f0b9e`
moves `iban_case_gate` out of `free()` (`src/pii/recognizers.rs:422`, `:2143`) on a *call-rate*
argument that is correct — `shrink_on_reject` really does retry a rejected span at every interior
separator — but prices the call at a full unit when `DOS-BUD`'s own new grid measures it at
**1.1–1.6 µs** here (1.3–1.8 published) against **3.4–4.0 µs** (3.2–4.3 published) for the phone
unit the constant was sized from — a third of a unit charged as one. The commit
names this and calls it safe: *"it over-prices that work by about 2–3× against the nominal, which
for a fail-closed bound is the safe direction."* For a DoS ceiling that is true. For availability it
is the exact direction M10-R29 measured as a defect, and all three of that finding's consequences
return:

1. **A false refusal.** The table above. Legal traffic blocked where the work being bounded is
   ~0.5 s of arithmetic.
2. **A confidently misdirected message.** `src/pii/recognizers.rs:1596-1607` still reads *"this
   request exhausted the **domestic-phone** validation budget of 500000 **number checks** … send
   less digit-dense text instead: for an oversized tool result, **add a LIMIT to the query or return
   fewer rows per call**"*, and `privacy.rs` puts it in the 400 body verbatim — I read it back off
   the wire. An agent whose hex dump was refused is told to shrink a SQL query. This is M10-R27's
   rule (*a fail-closed threshold is only as good as the failure it produces*) landing on M10-R27's
   own fix for the **second** time, and the budget now has two spenders while the sentence still
   names one.
3. **The phone tier's allowance is eaten first.** `with_shapes` builds
   `universal_recognizers()` → national IDs → VAT → `national_phone_recognizers()`
   (`src/pii/recognizers.rs:385-390`), and `try_detect` iterates that order, so on a mixed body the
   IBAN gate spends before any phone family is reached — M10-R29's third consequence, structurally
   unchanged.

**Not a leak, and the direction is right:** nothing is forwarded, the round trip on accepted bodies
stays byte-exact, and every ordinary payload I measured that is *not* a hex dump sits three orders of
magnitude below the line.

**The fix is product-visible, so it is the maintainer's.** Four options, with what each costs:

1. **Charge the gate a fraction of a unit** — e.g. spend once per *N* arithmetic calls, or once per
   rejected candidate rather than once per shrink retry. Restores M10-R29's invariant (a unit is a
   comparable quantum of work) while keeping the call rate bounded, and moves the `xxd` line back
   outside `MAX_BODY_BYTES`. Costs one number to pick and a `DOS-10` that asserts the ratio rather
   than the raw count. **This is the one I would take.**
2. **Keep the charge and correct what is published.** Cheapest, and honest, but it leaves an
   ordinary agent payload refused at 8 MiB and the release note has to say so in those words —
   naming `xxd` rather than `abcd 1234`.
3. **Raise `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST`.** Moves every line at once, including the ones the
   number was chosen for; re-prices the whole published band.
4. **Give the gate its own allowance.** Correct and the most work: a second constant, a second
   refusal message, a second row in every table.

Independent of which is chosen, **the refusal message must stop naming one spender.** The cheap
chokepoint is on `Budget`, which already tracks `spent()`: record the spend per `PiiKind` and have
the message quote the top spender, so the advice can never again be attached to the wrong term by
adding a third one.

**The test that would have caught it.** `DOS-10` asserts that the term is *charged*; nothing asserts
that an *ordinary* body stays under the allowance. `PHONE-BUD` is that guard for the M7 turn and for
nothing else. The missing case is `PHONE-BUD`'s construction over a second fixture — a uniformly
random 4-character lowercase-hex group column, i.e. what `xxd` emits — asserting the request budget
is not exhausted at `MAX_BODY_BYTES`. Note that this is the same gap `PHONE-OM-TOOL` was just added
to fill on the *precision* axis: the M7 turn is the only fixture the *cost* axis has, and it has a
shape too.

---

**Closed 2026-09-06 — option 1, at the conservative end of the measured ratio, plus the message fix
the finding makes mandatory either way.**

**The price.** `IBAN_GATE_CALLS_PER_UNIT = 2`: two arithmetic calls cost one unit. The number is
derived, not chosen — `DOS-BUD`'s µs/unit grid measures the gate's shape at **1.1–1.8 µs** and the
phone unit the allowance was sized from at **2.9–4.3 µs**, so the ratio spans ~2.1 to ~3.6 and this
takes its **smallest** value: the charge never *under*-prices the work, which is the only direction
a fail-closed bound may be wrong in. `Budget::spend_fraction` is the mechanism, and its doc says why
the accumulator is single: a second cheap validator wanting a different denominator gets its own
field rather than reusing this one.

**Verified on the finding's own repro**, real release `.exe`, mock upstream, same generator and seed,
three builds:

| `xxd` body | one unit per call (the defect) | one unit per two calls (shipped) | gate uncharged |
|---|---|---|---|
| 4 MiB | 200 | **200** | 200 |
| 6 MiB | 200 | **200** | 200 |
| 8 MiB | **400** | **200** | 200 |
| 12 MiB | **400** | **400** | 200 |

**And the residue is real, so it is stated rather than rounded off.** The 8 MiB refusal the finding
reported is gone; a **12 MiB** one is not. `DOS-BUD` now prints the line, with the per-kind split
that says who spent it:

| `xxd` body | verdict | units | of which IBAN | phone |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4 MiB | masked | 191,551 | 68,421 | 123,130 |
| 8 MiB | masked | 382,290 | 136,073 | 246,217 |
| 10 MiB | masked | 490,295 | 170,290 | 320,005 |
| **12 MiB** | **refused** | 500,000 | 204,393 | 295,607 |
| **16 MiB** | **refused** | 500,000 | 251,013 | 248,987 |

**The honest reading is that the line was never where it looked.** With the gate uncharged a 16 MiB
dump measures **492,911** units — **98.6 %** of the allowance — so this payload class already sat
within one and a half percent of refusal while roughly a third of its validation work was not being
counted. Charging it makes the bound true and makes the cost visible; it does not make the body more
expensive. What *is* a real coverage change is that the reach for this shape falls from beyond
`MAX_BODY_BYTES` to about **11 MiB**.

**So one question goes back to the maintainer, and it is a threshold rather than a defect:** restore
the previous reach by raising `MAX_PHONE_VALIDATIONS_PER_REQUEST` from 500,000 to **~800,000**
(which is what a 16 MiB hex dump honestly costs: 765,576 units, ~2.8 s of validation against the
~1.5 s the current number was sized for), or keep 500,000 and publish that hex dumps above ~11 MiB
are refused. Both are recorded in `ARCHITECTURE.md`; neither is the builder's, and nothing here is
blocked on the answer.

**The two guards.**
- **`DOS-11`** — `an_ordinary_hex_dump_stays_inside_the_request_allowance_at_max_body_bytes`. It
  asserts the **M10-R29 invariant as a ratio**: on an ordinary hex dump the cheap validator must not
  outspend the tier the allowance is named after. Measured on its 512 KiB fixture, **0.349** at the
  shipped price and **0.697** at the defect's — a factor of two either side of the bar. Verified by
  mutation in both directions. Its **first** assertion is the one the published sample failed: the
  fixture must contain ≥3 % `[a-f]{2}[0-9]{2}` groups, so a generator that stops spelling the shape
  fails loudly instead of passing for the wrong reason. The ratio is *not* scale-free (0.556 / 1.11
  at 4 MiB), so the bar travels with `SAMPLE` and the comment says so.
- **`DOS-BUD`** gains the refusal-line-by-size table above, with the per-kind split — the absolute
  line needs `--release` and multi-megabyte bodies, so it stays a declared pre-tag measurement.

**Consequence 2 is closed at the chokepoint, not at the sentence.** `Budget` now records units per
`PiiKind` (`attribute` at the two places a validator is ever called, `top_spender` to read it back),
and the 400 body quotes the top spender. The old text told an agent whose hex dump was refused to
*"add a LIMIT to the query"*; the new one is generated from what was actually charged, so a **third**
spender cannot repeat it. The label comes from the enum and never from the input, so `E2E-05`'s
value-free property is unchanged.

**Consequence 3 — recognizer order — is left as measured and not changed.** The IBAN gate does spend
before any phone family is reached, but with the charge at its measured price the two terms are the
same order of magnitude on every body tested, and reordering `with_shapes` to put the expensive tier
first would change which candidates win overlap resolution. *A fix that moves a scan order to change
a spend order is a fix with a detection side effect*, and that trade is not worth taking for a
message that now names the right spender anyway.

**What this cost, said plainly: the fix for M11-R18 shipped with a price nobody had measured against
an ordinary body, and the sample that was supposed to be that measurement could not spell the shape
it sampled.** `DOS-10` asserted the term was *charged* and stopped there. *A guard that a term is
counted is not a guard that counting it is affordable* — and the second one has to be built from
traffic, not from a string somebody wrote to illustrate the first.

<a id="m11-r61"></a>
### M11-R61 — `SEPARATOR_RUN_MAX` can be widened from 4 to 5 with the whole suite green, while three places claim the bound is red in both directions · **guard**

**The green mutation.** `src/pii/recognizers.rs:186`, `4` → `5`, `cargo test`: **258 passed, 0
failed, 4 ignored** — identical to HEAD. Nothing anywhere goes red.

**What that lets through.** The bound is not an implementation detail: it is one of the two
coordinates of the over-mask [M11-R55](#m11-r55) accepted **on purpose**, whose rates are now a
published promise asserted on every `cargo test` (`aligned` 0.785, `ips` 0.500 …). Widening it to 5
admits five-space column alignment as a phone candidate and raises that promise silently —
`SEPARATOR_RUN_MAX`'s own doc names *"a run of five or more"* as precisely the residue the bound
excludes, so the mutation moves the declared boundary of an accepted cost with nothing to say so.

**Why nothing sees it.** Both new guards take their alphabet from the constant — which is
[M11-R57](#m11-r57)'s fix and it works *downward* — but neither samples above it.
`tests/phone_eval.rs:337` generates the `aligned` pool as `for gap in 2..=4usize`, i.e. exactly the
admitted range and nothing outside it; and in the tool-output fixture the gaps that matter are **3**
(the `psql` rows, which is why 4 → 3 is invisible there too) while the only wide-column block it
carries — `free -m` — is **7 and 8** spaces, far outside 5. So a run of exactly 5 exists in no
corpus the suite owns, in either file.

**And the claim written in this commit is wrong in both directions, not one.**
`tests/phone_overmask.rs:185`, `docs/TESTING.md:400` and this file at
[M11-R55](#m11-r55)'s closure all say *"narrow the alphabet or the separator run and entries vanish;
widen either and entries appear: both directions are red."* Measured:

| mutation | `phone_overmask` | rest of the suite |
|---|---|---|
| run 4 → 3 | **green** | `phone_eval` red (`aligned` 0.785 → 0.521) |
| run 4 → 2 | red | red |
| **run 4 → 5** | **green** | **green** |
| alphabet + `,` `:` | green | `SEPARATOR-01` red |

`PHONE-OM-TOOL` is red for exactly one of the four, and the *run* coordinate has no upper pin at
all. The alphabet coordinate does — mutation 6 — so this is specifically about cardinality.

**Same family as [M11-R57](#m11-r57), and I am not proposing another guard layer.** R57 closed the
narrowing of this same constant by deriving the matrix from it; the chokepoint for the other
direction is one line in the pool that already exists: generate `aligned` over
`1..=SEPARATOR_RUN_MAX + 1` and let the extra category's measured **0.000** be the assertion that
the bound excludes it. The published block then carries the exclusion as a number, the way it
already carries the admission, and no new file or fixture is needed. Failing that, the three
sentences claiming both directions are red should say what is actually true.

**The test that would have caught it** is the pool sampling one gap wider than the constant it is
measuring — the same rule `RENDER-01` and `SEPARATOR-01` already follow on their own axes: *a guard
that never varies a quantity cannot see it* (M4's lesson 6, now on the accepted-cost side rather
than the coverage side).

---

**Closed 2026-09-06, by the one line the finding proposed — the pool is derived from the constant.**

`SEPARATOR_RUN_MAX` is now `pub`, and `phone_eval` builds **both** alignment pools from it:
`aligned` iterates `2..=SEPARATOR_RUN_MAX`, and a new `alignedwide` category holds gap
`SEPARATOR_RUN_MAX + 1`. So the constant is bound in three ways at once, and the third is the one
that was missing:

- **its declared residue is published as a number.** `alignedwide` measures **0.000** in the block
  `ARCHITECTURE.md` carries, which is the bound's exclusion asserted the same way its admission
  already was;
- **the pool sizes move with it.** Widening 4 → 5 makes `aligned` generate one more gap and
  `alignedwide` a different one, so the `generated:` line of the published block changes and the
  byte-for-byte assertion is red **before** any rate is even consulted;
- **the reach check covers it.** The harness's own first rule — a pool must be able to *reach* a
  shape before its 0.000 means "clean" — now runs for a gap outside the bound too.

Verified by mutation: `4 → 5` is **red** (it was green across the whole suite), `4 → 3` is red, and
`4 → 2` is red in two files.

**The three sentences that claimed more than was true are corrected rather than deleted**, because
the accurate version is more useful than the confident one. `tests/phone_overmask.rs`,
`docs/TESTING.md` and [M11-R55](#m11-r55)'s closure all said *"narrow the alphabet or the separator
run and entries vanish; widen either and entries appear: both directions are red."* What is actually
true, measured:

| mutation | caught by |
|---|---|
| run 4 → 2 | `PHONE-OM-TOOL` **and** `phone_eval` |
| run 4 → 3 | `phone_eval` only (the tool-output fixture's runs are 3, so it cannot see it) |
| run 4 → 5 | `phone_eval`'s `alignedwide` (nothing, before this fix) |
| alphabet + `,` `:` | `SEPARATOR-01` |

*No single guard covers both coordinates in both directions; the three together do* — and the useful
sentence is the one that says which is which, because that is what tells the next person where to
add the case they are missing.

**Same family as [M11-R57](#m11-r57), and deliberately not another layer.** R57 stopped the guard
drifting *below* the constant by deriving the matrix from it; this stops it drifting *above* by
deriving the pool from it. One constant, two directions, no new file and no new fixture — which is
the alternative the finding explicitly asked for and the one this codebase has been punished for not
taking (`CLAUDE.md`: *a guard on a guard is worth one level, not four*).
